# Bug Tracking — Archive

Audit trail for bugs that have left the live tracking pipeline. Live bugs (Critical / High / Medium / Low priority sections) remain in [bug-tracking.md](../bug-tracking.md).

Rows here are not consumed by `Update-BugStatus.ps1` for active-status transitions; they are read for `Reopened` operations (row moves back to live) and read+appended for `Closed` / `Rejected` transitions (row moved in from live).

> **ID format / column schema**: identical to the parent file — see [bug-tracking.md](../bug-tracking.md) for the canonical legend and column definitions.

## Table of Contents

1. [Closed Bugs](#closed-bugs)
2. [Rejected Bugs](#rejected-bugs)

---

| PD-BUG-035 | sed -i on Windows wipes LinkWatcher database entries | 🔒 Closed | Medium | M | 2026-03-15 | When sed -i modifies a file on Windows, it generates a modify event followed by a delayed delete event. This causes LinkWatcher to lose track of all links inside the affected file. | 1.1.1 | Source: Development; Environment: Development; Component: handler; Triage: P3 — Medium impact (database integrity loss for affected file, self-recoverable on restart) × Low frequency (sed -i uncommon in Windows/PowerShell workflow). Effort: ~4-6h; Triaged: 2026-03-16; Verification: Fix verified: 2 regression tests pass (file_replacement_retains_links, true_delete_handles_gracefully). Full suite: 448 passed, 0 failed (5 skipped, 7 xfailed). No regressions. Files changed: handler.py, test_move_detection.py.; Updated: 2026-03-16 |  | No recorded notes [details](#pd-bug-035) |

Also, process detection matches any python process running main.py, causing false positives. | 5.1.1 | Source: Development; Environment: Development; Component: start_linkwatcher_background.ps1; Expected: Script reads project root from project-config.json and passes --project-root explicitly. | PD-BUG-040 | on_moved and on_deleted handlers skip file filter check | 🔒 Closed | Medium | S | 2026-03-17 | The on_moved() and on_deleted() handlers in handler.py do not call _should_monitor_file() before processing events. Only on_created() has this check. This causes LinkWatcher to fully process every file move/delete event including .pyc files, sed temp files, pytest .tmp files, and its own log file — triggering expensive reference scanning for irrelevant files and causing constant CPU activity. | 1.1.1 | Source: Development; Environment: Development; Component: File System Monitoring; Repro: 1. Start LinkWatcher with --debug. 2. Run pytest or edit files with an editor that uses sed-style atomic writes. 3. Observe log filling with processing of .pyc, .tmp, and sed* temp files.; Expected: on_moved and on_deleted should skip files that fail the _should_monitor_file check, just like on_created does.; Actual: All file move and delete events are fully processed regardless of file extension or ignored directory, causing 7000+ log lines of wasted work in 24 hours (528 pyc entries, 422 tmp entries, 43 sed entries). Triage: P3 S — Low impact (performance/noise only, no data corruption) × High frequency (every pytest run, every atomic-write editor save). Fix: Add _should_monitor_file() early-return guard to on_moved() and on_deleted(), matching existing on_created() pattern. ~1-2h effort. Triaged: 2026-03-17.; Updated: 2026-03-17; Fix: Added _should_monitor_file() guard to on_moved (checks dest_path) and on_deleted (checks src_path after directory workaround); Root Cause: on_moved and on_deleted handlers lacked the file filter check that on_created had, causing all file events to be fully processed regardless of extension; Tests Added: Yes; Verification: 5 regression tests pass, full suite 461/462 pass (1 pre-existing failure unrelated). No regressions. Files changed: handler.py, test_service.py. |  |  | |  |

The individual file move handler calls `_update_links_within_moved_file()` but `_handle_directory_moved()` never calls this for files inside the moved directory. | 1.1.1 File System Monitoring | Source: Manual Testing. | PD-BUG-038 | Directory move does not update markdown link references in other files | 🔒 Closed | Medium | M | 2026-03-16 | When a directory is moved, `process_directory_file_move()` calls `find_references(old_file_path)` for each moved file, but the database stores references with the link target as written in the source file (e.g., `../../guides/guides/cyclical/file.md`), not as a resolved project-relative path. The lookup searches for `process-framework/guides/guides/cyclical/file.md` but the stored target is `../../guides/guides/cyclical/file.md`, so most references are not found. Log evidence: cyclical dir move updated only 5 of ~7 expected refs; 01-planning dir move updated only 1 of ~19. | 1.1.1 File System Monitoring | Source: Manual Testing. Triage: P3 M. Fix: Added `co_moved_old_paths` parameter to `process_directory_file_move()` and `skip_files` parameter to `cleanup_after_file_move()`. During directory moves, co-moved files are excluded from reference updates (they can't be opened at old paths) and skipped during cleanup rescans (they no longer exist at old paths). The final rescan is deferred to the handler's `_update_links_within_moved_file` loop. Root Cause: Two issues: (1) `cleanup_after_file_move` tried to rescan co-moved files at their old (non-existent) paths, destroying their DB entries; (2) `rescan_moved_file_links` at the end of each file's processing updated `ref.file_path` prematurely, causing stale path resolution for subsequent files. Tests Added: Yes (2 regression tests). Verification: 452/452 tests pass, no regressions. Fixed: 2026-03-16.; Updated: 2026-03-17; Verification: Unable to reproduce: _reference_points_to_file already resolves relative paths correctly. Tested with simple, co-moved, and deep relative path scenarios — all pass. Original fix code (co_moved_old_paths, skip_files) was lost and the bug cannot be reproduced in current codebase. Closing as cannot-reproduce. |  | |  |
| PD-BUG-042 | Move detection confused by rapid file creation and deletion cycles | 🔒 Closed | Medium | M | 2026-03-18 | During E2E acceptance testing (TE-E2E-005/006/007), LinkWatcher move detection misinterprets file moves when preceded by bulk file operations (workspace cleanup + fixture copy). Root cause: move detection window includes file events from the workspace setup phase. | 1.1.1 | Source: Testing; Environment: Development; Component: move_detector.py, dir_move_detector.py; Fix: Added os.path.exists() check in MoveDetector.match_created_file() — if old file still exists at original location (re-created by bulk copy), pending delete is discarded. Added os.path.isdir() check in DirectoryMoveDetector.match_created_file() — if old directory still exists, pending entry invalidated. Root Cause: Filename+size matching had no validation that the source file was truly gone; Tests Added: Yes (3 regression tests); Verification: 470 passed, 2 pre-existing failures (PD-BUG-041, lock file). No regressions. Files changed: move_detector.py, dir_move_detector.py, test_move_detection.py, test_directory_move_detection.py. Note: E2E tests TE-E2E-005/006/007 still fail due to compounding issues: PD-BUG-041 (unmonitored .conf), PD-BUG-043 (Python dot-notation), and directory move detector race condition during rapid bulk copy. Fixed: 2026-03-18; Verification: Fix verified: 3 regression tests pass (stale_delete_rejected, real_move_detected, dir_buffer_invalidated). Full suite: 470 passed, 2 pre-existing failures. No regressions. Files changed: move_detector.py, dir_move_detector.py, test_move_detection.py, test_directory_move_detection.py.; Updated: 2026-03-18 |  | No recorded notes [details](#pd-bug-042) |

Design decision needed. Path resolver also failed to match extensionless targets against .py-suffixed old paths.; Tests Added: Yes; Updated: 2026-03-18; Verification: All 472 automated tests pass. 2 new regression tests verify the fix. No regressions. |  | |  |

| PD-BUG-045 | Python import references not updated on file move — resolution uses source file directory instead of project root | 🔒 Closed | Medium | M | 2026-03-18 | When a Python file is moved (e.g., `utils/helpers.py` → `core/helpers.py`), references in other Python files using import notation (`from utils.helpers import ...`, `import utils.helpers`) are not updated. Root cause: the Python parser stores import targets as `utils/helpers` (dots converted to slashes). The database resolution in `_reference_points_to_file()` joins this with the importing file's directory (`app/`), producing `app/utils/helpers` — but the actual file is at `utils/helpers` (Python imports resolve from project root, not from the importing file's directory). The resolution needs to also try resolving relative to the project root for Python imports. Additionally, extension mismatch: stored as `utils/helpers` but file is `utils/helpers.py`. Discovered via TE-E2E-007. | 2.1.1 Link Parsing, 2.2.1 Link Updating | Source: E2E Testing (PF-TSK-070); Component: database.py `_reference_points_to_file()`, parsers/python.py; Triage: P3 Medium impact/frequency — affects any Python project with standard imports. Scope M — requires project-root resolution fallback + extension-aware matching in `_reference_points_to_file()`. Effort: Medium.; Updated: 2026-03-18; Fix: Added Phase 2 file-wide module usage replacement in updater.py for python-import references; Root Cause: Module usage sites (e.g. utils.helpers.func()) not updated when import statement changes — updater only replaced on the import declaration line; Tests Added: Yes; Verification: Fix verified: 3 regression tests pass, full suite 479/480 pass (1 pre-existing flaky test). No regressions. |  |  | |  |

| PD-BUG-054 | Markdown parser ignores paths in code blocks and backtick inline code | 🔒 Closed | Medium | M | 2026-03-30 | When files are moved, paths inside fenced code blocks, indented code blocks, and backtick-quoted inline code are not updated. Primarily cd commands and script paths in task definitions, guides, and templates. | 2.1.1 | Source: Testing; Environment: Development; Component: Parsers; Expected: Paths in code blocks and backtick inline code pointing to moved files should be updated; Actual: Paths in code blocks and backtick inline code are left unchanged after file moves. **Triage (2026-03-30):** Valid — parser has no code block state tracking (confirmed by xfail test `test_mp_004_code_blocks_fake_links`). Regression tests verify backtick-quoted paths and bare directory paths are now detected. Integration tests confirm backtick paths are updated on file move. Validator archival/table-row skip logic covers new link types. |  | |  |

| PD-BUG-069 | Outgoing link recalculation silently skips links with #anchor fragments | 🔒 Closed | Medium | S | 2026-03-31 | When a file is moved, `_calculate_updated_relative_path()` in reference_lookup.py:630 resolves the link target to an absolute path and checks `os.path.exists()` (PD-BUG-033 guard at line 664). For links with `#anchor` fragments (e.g. `file.md#section`), the fragment is included in the existence check, so `os.path.exists("file.md#section")` returns False. The method returns the original (now-incorrect) path unchanged. Links to the same file WITHOUT anchors are updated correctly. | 2.2.1 | WF-001, WF-002 | DI | Source: Development; Environment: Development; Component: reference_lookup.py; Triage: P3 confirmed (Medium impact × Medium frequency). Scope downgraded M→S: well-scoped single-function fix (strip fragment before os.path.exists, reattach after recalculation). Effort: ~1-2h. Triaged: 2026-04-01. Component: reference_lookup.py `_calculate_updated_relative_path()`; Evidence: bug-tracking.md line 292 — `[Bug Triage](../../../../...bug-triage-task.md#steps-to-reopen-a-bug)` was NOT updated when file moved from 4-level to 3-level depth, while identical link WITHOUT anchor on line 227 WAS updated correctly; Repro: 1. Create file at `a/b/c/test.md` containing `[link](../../../target.md#section)` and `[link](../../../target.md)` 2. Move file to `a/b/test.md` 3. Link without anchor updated to `../../target.md` ✓ 4. Link with anchor remains `../../../target.md#section` ✗; Fix: Strip `#fragment` before `os.path.exists()` check at line 664, reattach after recalculation at line 676; Updated: 2026-04-01; Fix: Strip #fragment from original_target before path resolution and os.path.exists() checks in _calculate_updated_relative_path(). Reattach fragment to the calculated result. Both PD-BUG-032 and PD-BUG-033 guards now work correctly with anchored targets.; Root Cause: _calculate_updated_relative_path() passed the full target including #fragment to os.path.exists(), which returns False for paths containing anchors. The PD-BUG-033 guard then returned the original stale path unchanged.; Tests Added: Yes; Verification: 2 regression tests pass (test_anchor_fragment_preserved_on_move, test_nonexistent_target_with_anchor_returns_original). Full suite: 642 passed, 2 pre-existing failures, 5 skipped, 6 xfailed. No regressions. Filed PD-BUG-070 for similar anchor issue in service.py check_links(). |  |  |  |

Effort: ~30 min.; Triaged: 2026-04-01; Updated: 2026-04-01; Fix: Strip #fragment anchor before os.path.exists() check in check_links(); Root Cause: os.path.exists() called on target_path containing #fragment anchors, which is not a valid filesystem path; Tests Added: Yes; Verification: Regression test passes, full suite 646 passed with no new failures |  |  |  |

| PD-BUG-095 | LinkWatcher false-positive corrupts regex strings and glob filters in code | 🔒 Closed | High | M | 2026-04-27 | LinkWatcher's PowerShell/Python/generic parsers classify quoted regex patterns and glob filter strings (e.g. '*.md', 'doc/foo/ART-ASS-\d+') as file paths via looks_like_file_path(). On directory moves, path_resolver.calculate_new_target() normalizes via .replace('\','/') and computes relpath, which (1) converts regex escapes like \d to /d, and (2) prepends '../' to the string. This silently corrupts source files so their regexes and globs no longer match. | 1.1.1, 2.1.1, 2.2.1 | WF-001 | CQ | Source: Refactoring; Environment: Development; Component: src/linkwatcher (parsers + path_resolver); Repro: 1. Create .ps1 with: Get-ChildItem -Filter '*.md' and [regex]::Match($x, 'doc/foo/bar-\d+').  2. Move/rename a directory (e.g. doc/foo) with LinkWatcher running.  3. LinkWatcher detects them as quoted file paths and updates.  4. '*.md' becomes '../*.md'; 'doc/foo/bar-\d+' becomes '../doc/foo/bar-/d+'.; Expected: Parser must NOT treat glob patterns (*.md, **/*.ext) or regex strings (patterns containing \d, \s, \w, \., character classes, anchors ^/$) as file paths. They should never enter the link database or be rewritten during moves.; Actual: Parser registers them; updater rewrites them. Real corruption sites found this session: Update-ScriptReferences.ps1:63 (Filter '../*.md'); Validate-StateTracking.ps1:265-267 (regex '../^## 4/. ...'); test/automated/unit/2-link-parsing-update/2-0-link-parsing-update/test_link_updates.py:1032,1074 (regexes with '/d+','/s+'); Update-TechnicalDebtFromAssessment.ps1:126,132,154 (fixed today).; Evidence: Code paths: src/linkwatcher/utils.py:189 (looks_like_file_path returns True for '*.md'); src/linkwatcher/path_resolver.py:135 (replace('\','/') unconditionally normalizes backslashes, including in regex strings).; Updated: 2026-04-28; Fix: Two-layer fix: Layer 1 adds looks_like_regex_or_glob() helper in utils.py; looks_like_file_path/looks_like_directory_path now short-circuit to False for regex/glob strings (blocks them from entering link DB via any parser). Layer 2 adds existence guard in path_resolver._calculate_new_target_relative directory-prefix early-exit (mirrors PD-BUG-033 in reference_lookup). Restored 5 corrupted regex/filter strings in Update-ScriptReferences.ps1 and Validate-StateTracking.ps1 (originals verified via git history). 23 new regression tests (17 unit + 6 integration) with negative assertions; full suite 812 passed, 0 regressions.; Root Cause: Two compounding causes: (1) looks_like_file_path()/looks_like_directory_path() in utils.py accepted regex escapes and glob meta-chars as paths via extension or path-separator heuristics, allowing strings like '*.md' and 'doc/foo/bar-\d+' to enter the link database; (2) normalize_path() in utils.py unconditionally normalizes backslash to forward slash, which when applied to regex strings turns \d into /d. The directory-prefix early-exit in path_resolver._calculate_new_target_relative had no existence guard (PD-BUG-033 fix only protected the source-file-move path in reference_lookup), so corrupted normalized regex strings matched the moved-directory prefix and were rewritten.; Tests Added: Yes; Verification: Code review approved 2026-04-28. Two-layer fix verified: Layer 1 (looks_like_regex_or_glob in utils.py) rejects regex/glob strings at parser level; Layer 2 (existence guard in path_resolver) mirrors PD-BUG-033. 28/28 BUG-095+BUG-033 tests pass; full suite 740 passed, 0 regressions. Multi-signature corruption sweep across .ps1/.psm1/.py confirmed no remaining sites. Two minor findings filed as tech debt: TD242 (hoist shared existence-check helper, also covers ungated path_resolver.py:109 first early-exit branch), TD243 (pre-compile regex patterns in looks_like_regex_or_glob). | Scope reassessed L→M after investigation; single-session fix. Investigation notes archived at [bug-fix-state-pd-bug-095.md](/doc/state-tracking/temporary/old/bug-fix-state-pd-bug-095.md). |  |  |
| PD-BUG-096 | Directory move with multiple Python imports double-prefixes import statements | 🔒 Closed | High | M | 2026-04-28 | When a directory containing multiple Python modules is moved (e.g., utils/ -> src/utils/) and a single source file imports more than one of those modules, the import statements get double-prefixed (e.g., 'import utils.a' becomes 'import src.src.utils.a' instead of 'import src.utils.a'). The usage sites elsewhere in the file are correctly updated to single-prefix form, so the result is inconsistent within the same file. | 1.1.1, 2.1.1, 2.2.1 | WF-001, WF-002, WF-005 | DI | Source: Refactoring; Environment: Development; Component: handler.py + updater.py; Repro: 1. Create utils/a.py and utils/b.py. 2. Create app/main.py with 'import utils.a' on line 1, 'import utils.b' on line 2, 'utils.a.fa()' and 'utils.b.fb()' as usage sites. 3. Move utils/ to src/utils/ via DirMovedEvent. 4. Read app/main.py.; Expected: import src.utils.a / import src.utils.b on import lines; src.utils.a.fa() / src.utils.b.fb() on usage sites.; Actual: import src.src.utils.a / import src.src.utils.b on import lines (DOUBLE-PREFIXED); src.utils.a.fa() / src.utils.b.fb() on usage sites (correct). Inconsistent within same file.; Evidence: _handle_directory_moved (handler.py:454-466) appends each moved file to move_groups twice — once as file_refs, once as module_refs. PYTHON_IMPORT references end up in both groups, producing duplicate (ref, old_path, new_path) tuples in update_references_batch -> _update_file_references_multi -> _apply_replacements. Phase 1 then iterates and calls _replace_in_line twice on the same line. _replace_at_position for PYTHON_IMPORT (updater.py:476) uses unbounded line.replace(ref.link_text, new_import_text), which substring-matches inside the already-prefixed result. Phase 2's negative lookbehind correctly protects against this, but Phase 1 has no such guard. Reproduced via debug trace showing 4 replacement_items where each ref appears twice. Test test_bug094_multi_rename_order_independent in TestBug094PythonImportDoubleApply exposes this (currently xfail-marked pending fix).; Triage: P2 High - High impact (silent file content corruption: imports double-prefixed to 'src.src.utils.x' while usage sites correctly use 'src.utils.x', leaving the file inconsistent and broken at runtime) x Medium frequency (triggers on directory moves where any single source file imports 2+ modules from the moved directory). M scope: Phase 1 sibling of PD-BUG-094 - BUG-094 fixed Phase 2 file-wide regex with negative lookbehind/lookahead, but Phase 1 (`_replace_at_position`) has no idempotency guard, and `_handle_directory_moved` (handler.py:454-466) appends each Python module twice to `move_groups` (once as file_refs, once as module_refs), causing duplicate replacement application. Two fix angles: (a) de-duplicate move_groups so PYTHON_IMPORT refs process once, (b) make _replace_at_position for PYTHON_IMPORT idempotent (bounded regex matching Phase 2's guard). Also flips xfail on test_bug094_multi_rename_order_independent (filed as TD213 during BUG-094 closure). Effort: ~3-4h. Triaged: 2026-04-29.; Updated: 2026-04-29; Fix: reference_lookup.collect_directory_file_refs filters PYTHON_IMPORT refs out of file_references for .py files. They remain only in module_references with the correct module-path target, so each ref reaches Phase 1 exactly once and the unbounded str.replace in _replace_at_position cannot substring-match inside an already-prefixed import line.; Root Cause: find_references(old_file_path) for a .py file ALSO returns PYTHON_IMPORT refs via the resolved-target index (parser registers Python imports under the slash-form module path which resolves the same way as the .py path). _handle_directory_moved appended both file_refs and module_refs to move_groups, so the same ref reached _apply_replacements twice with two different (old, new) target pairs. _replace_at_position's unbounded line.replace then double-prefixed the import.; Tests Added: Yes; Verification: Code Review (PF-TSK-005) approved on 2026-04-29. Fix in reference_lookup.collect_directory_file_refs filters PYTHON_IMPORT refs out of file_references for .py files, preventing the duplicate replacement application that produced double-prefixed imports. All 6 tests in TestBug094PythonImportDoubleApply pass (including the previously xfailed test_bug094_dir_move_multi_import_no_double_prefix regression test). 489 tests in unit + link-updates integration suite pass — no regressions. flake8 clean (no new violations). Underlying idempotency hole in _replace_at_position (unbounded line.replace for PYTHON_IMPORT) was filed as TD251 (Low priority) and resolved on 2026-04-29 in PD-REF-215: bounded regex with negative lookbehind/lookahead now makes Phase 1 idempotent for PYTHON_IMPORT, mirroring Phase 2's PD-BUG-094 guard. |  |  |  |  |

See ActualBehavior for full file list and Evidence for code samples. | 2.2.1 | WF-001, WF-002 | DI CQ | Source: Refactoring; Environment: Development; Component: src/linkwatcher (textual reference update logic); Repro: 1. Have a project with two files at different depths whose paths share a leading prefix segment, e.g. project root contains `LinkWatcherBrokenLinks.txt` and various source files reference it as just `LinkWatcherBrokenLinks.txt` (filename only, no directory prefix).; Triage: Reference-update sweep produces overlapping-substring corruption when filename appears multiple times on same line. Suspect 1: stale column positions in `_apply_replacements` after each replacement shifts line length. Suspect 2: unbounded `line.replace()` fallback at updater.py:485 replaces all occurrences. Fix is M-scope: localized to ~2 functions in updater.py, needs careful regression tests for overlapping cases. 1-2 sessions, no architectural change. Related Feature corrected from 1.1.1 to 2.2.1 (bug owned by Link Updating, not File System Monitoring).; Updated: 2026-04-29; Fix: Two-part fix in src/linkwatcher/updater.py. Primary: added _filter_contained_overlaps pre-pass to _apply_replacements that drops refs whose column range is strictly contained in another ref on the same line (with warning log). Secondary: replaced unbounded line.replace fallback in _replace_at_position invalid-position branch with occurrence-counting bounded behavior (0=no-op, 1=replace once, >1=skip with warning).; Root Cause: _apply_replacements processes refs in descending column order. When two refs on the same line overlap (inner contained in outer), inner is processed first and extends the line. Outer's original column positions become stale; line[outer_end_col:] then indexes into the freshly-inserted text, producing new_target+chopped_tail corruption like LinkWatcherBrokenLinks.txtols/linkWatcher/... The whole-line staleness check at updater.py:324 fails to catch this because link_target is still present in the line, just not at the original column.; Tests Added: Yes; Verification: Code review approved 2026-04-29. Fix is correct, well-tested (4 new regression tests + 48 unit + 41 integration tests all pass), well-logged with diagnostic context, and preserves atomic-write semantics. 4 minor follow-ups (docstring scope note, STALE-escalation suggestion, 2 E501, type annotation consistency) captured as tech debt TD252. |  |  |  |

That branch searches the raw line for the DECODED compound string, which never matches once anything is escaped, so it returns False. Two failure modes follow, both silent. When the path sits in the MIDDLE of the compound string, the value also fails whole-string classification and the reference is discarded with no warning - the path_value_line_not_located WARNING that PD-BUG-126 added is reachable only from the whole-string branch. When the path sits at the END, the compound string itself passes looks_like_file_path, falls through to _add_escaped_reference, and is registered as a reference whose link_target is the ENTIRE command string. The second mode is NEW: at HEAD both shapes were dropped, and the _add_escaped_reference fall-through arrived with the uncommitted PD-BUG-126 fix. Folded into this bug at triage (2026-08-19, human approved) because both modes share one root cause, one code path and one fix. | PD-BUG-132 | Control Panel renders a stale broken-link report as the current verdict for a validate run that never scanned | 🔒 Closed | Medium | S | 2026-08-19 | parse_validation_result maps any --validate subprocess that exits 1 without writing a report onto the stale report left by an earlier scan. _report_path falls back to the conventional path <log dir>/LinkWatcherBrokenLinks.txt when the stdout locator line is absent, so a run that never scanned is rendered as state broken with the previous count while the error text is discarded. The trigger class is every exit-1-before-report path - invalid config, invalid project root, unreadable config file, or an exception raised during the scan itself - because the --validate branch sits inside the main() outer try; the PD-BUG-128 fix widened exposure by routing every invalid config key onto it but did not create the class. The stale report is the normal condition rather than an incident: every completed run writes that report to the panel's own conventional path, so the misrender is certain for any project validated even once, and the correct failed rendering survives only for a project never validated. Where the earlier run was clean the panel renders the result line as 0 broken links, a literal violation of FDD edge case 7-1-1-EC-6 rather than merely a stale count. | 7.1.1 | WF-010 | OB DA | Updated: 2026-08-20 [details](#pd-bug-132) |

Any transient OSError from ReadDirectoryChangesW is therefore misread as a deleted watch root, making the emitter self-stop silently and permanently; the OSError itself is swallowed entirely - never logged, never re-raised - so a firing surfaces only as observer_emitter_died with no winerror. PD-BUG-139 detects the resulting blindness and stops the daemon, so nothing is silently lost; the residual cost is an avoidable daemon stop plus total loss of the diagnostic. Fix trap recorded at triage: swapping VOLUME_NAME_NT for VOLUME_NAME_DOS alone does NOT fix it, because GetFinalPathNameByHandleW returns a device-namespace-prefixed path, so the healthy case still compares unequal - a correct predicate needs prefix normalization as well. | 1.1.1 | WF-001, WF-002, WF-004, WF-005, WF-011 | DI OB ID | Verification: Code review 2026-08-27 (PF-TSK-005), re-review of the second implementation after the same-day rejection: APPROVED. BOTH rewrite paths carry it, each through its own regex anchored on a close-bracket-open-paren immediately followed by the target - replacement.replace_markdown_target (references in OTHER files, the larger exposure, since it reaches every file referencing a moved target rather than only the file being moved) and reference_lookup Phase 2 (inside the moved file). All three padding spellings fail: both-sides, leading-only and trailing-only. The updater path is silent unconditionally; the moved-file path emits link_pattern_not_found UNLESS the same target also appears unpadded in the file, in which case the target-level dedup masks it entirely and the padded occurrences are counted as updated. | PD-BUG-157 | Relaunch cannot recover a running-but-unreachable panel window | 🔒 Closed | Medium | M | 2026-08-31 | A second launch that finds a live incumbent asks it to surface and then exits. Surfacing is best-effort: the Windows foreground lock can reduce it to a taskbar flash, and a panel whose UI thread is blocked or whose window is otherwise unreachable never becomes visible at all. The banner handshake proves only that the incumbent listener thread is alive, never that its UI is, so the launcher reports success while the operator gets no window and has no route to force one. Deleting panel.port by hand is the only recovery. NOTE: the originally filed root cause (a stale panel.port trusted without a liveness check) was DISPROVEN at triage; the guard does verify liveness. ### PD-BUG-099

- **Source**: Monitoring
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Logging
- **Repro**: 1) Start a LinkWatcher daemon against a project (holds LinkWatcherLog.txt open). 2) Start a second daemon against the same project before the first releases the file (e.g. a second editor/agent SessionStart hook). 3) With --debug on, the log exceeds maxBytes; the losing instance attempts rollover, os.rename fails with WinError 32, and rollover is retried on every subsequent log record. Observed: 3 concurrent daemons against appdev, ~270 CPU-seconds, LinkWatcherError.txt 2.7MB of identical rotation-failure warnings.
- **Expected**: A failed rotation must not re-trigger on every subsequent log record. On rename failure, clear the rollover condition (truncate-in-place or copy+truncate so the base file drops below maxBytes), or back off / cap consecutive failures, or skip rotation while the target is locked, so a transient file lock cannot become an unbounded warning storm.
- **Actual**: doRollover() (src/linkwatcher/logging.py:120-160) catches the rename OSError, logs a fallback warning, and reopens the same oversized base file. Since it still exceeds maxBytes, RotatingFileHandler.shouldRollover() returns True on the next emit, so rollover repeats indefinitely - one stderr warning per record. Concurrent instances make the rename perpetually fail via the Windows file lock.
- **Evidence**: appdev 2026-06-03: 3 daemons (workers 16208/12316/31964 started 08:29/08:47/08:52) each pegging a core; LinkWatcherError.txt 2.7MB, LinkWatcherLog.txt 6.8MB and growing. Code: src/linkwatcher/logging.py doRollover (rename line 133; failure path 137-143). Contributing factors: launcher start_linkwatcher_background.ps1 hardcodes --debug and defers the singleton lock to main.py, leaving a startup race that permits concurrent instances.
- **Updated**: 2026-06-04
- **Fix**: Root cause fixed in main.py acquire_lock: replaced non-atomic exists-then-write with atomic os.open O_CREAT+O_EXCL so simultaneous starts cannot both acquire the singleton lock (loser exits loudly). Defense-in-depth in src/linkwatcher/logging.py TimestampRotatingFileHandler (feat 3.1.1): shouldRollover backs off after a failed rename and escalates one CRITICAL after 5 consecutive failures. 3 regression tests added; full unit suite 828 passed.
- **Root Cause**: TOCTOU race in acquire_lock let two near-simultaneous daemons both acquire the lock; the losing instance fought over the shared log file and doRollover reopened the still-oversized base file on rename failure, so shouldRollover re-fired every emit (unbounded rename-retry + stderr-warning storm, CPU pegged).
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review approved (PF-TSK-005, Dims OB). Full unit suite 828 passed/3 skipped/4 xfailed; 3 new regression tests pass; flake8 clean on changed code (the one E203 is pre-existing). OB dimension handled well: graduated WARNING-per-attempt to one-time CRITICAL with actionable diagnostic context; storm symptom resolved via two independent layers (atomic O_EXCL lock + rotation back-off). No Critical/Major findings. Two Minor follow-ups filed: TD255 (residual sub-microsecond create-then-write race in acquire_lock, masked by the back-off) and TD256 (TOCTOU regression test's monkeypatch is vestigial).

### PD-BUG-100

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Daemon singleton guard (acquire_lock/startup)
- **Repro**: 1. Ensure no daemon runs for a project. 2. Trigger two near-simultaneous starts of start_linkwatcher_background.ps1 for the same project (manual run plus SessionStart hook, or two starts within the same second). 3. List python processes running <bin>/main.py for that project root.
- **Expected**: Exactly one daemon per project root; when two starts race, the lock-acquisition loser exits loudly (PD-BUG-099 design).
- **Actual**: Two daemons persist per project. Snapshot 2026-06-08: appdev PIDs 33392+33124 both started 10:30:10; LinkWatcher PIDs 32932+23732 both started 10:30:40. Same-second starts, both alive. Repeated stop/start cycles oscillated 0->2->0; a single instance could not be reliably established.
- **Evidence**: Windows 11, Python 3.11.9, global install C:\Users\ronny\bin. Fix sites: main.py acquire_lock + start_linkwatcher_background.ps1. Daemon snapshot: 2 per project, same-second CreationDate. Closest prior items both CLOSED: PD-BUG-099 (log-rotation storm / atomic O_EXCL lock) and TD255 (acquire_lock settle-read, PD-REF-230). This survives both. Suspect residual race under concurrent starts, or the process backstop (Test-LinkWatcherAlreadyRunning) and the lock both passing. Surfaced during PF-TSK-008 v2.1.0 release; logged in PF-FEE-1284.
- **Triage**: P2 High - Impact High (violates single-daemon invariant; risks double-processing of move events -> conflicting/duplicate link updates + log-file contention) x Frequency Medium (race fires only on near-simultaneous starts; transient and self-resolving - 2026-06-08 ~10:51 snapshot showed exactly 1 daemon per project root after the reported 2-per-project had oscillated away). Not Critical: corruption risk is latent/unobserved and the duplicate state self-heals; the severe CPU/log-storm symptom was already fixed in PD-BUG-099. Scope M: root cause unknown across two fix sites (main.py acquire_lock + start_linkwatcher_background.ps1), timing race needs a concurrency regression harness; est ~4-8h, may span sessions. Kept as NEW (not a reopen of PD-BUG-099) per human decision - distinct symptom (duplicate instances vs log storm), likely the residual acquire_lock create-then-write race flagged by TD255. Cross-ref PD-BUG-099, TD255/PD-REF-230. Workflows WF-003 (startup race) + WF-008 (loser instance must exit cleanly). Triaged 2026-06-08.
- **Updated**: 2026-06-08
- **Fix**: Launcher Get-DaemonExitDisposition (repo mirror + blueprint canonical) preserves a live foreign owners lock (AlreadyRunning, exit 0) and removes only our own PID lock on a genuine crash; main.py release_lock made ownership-aware. Regression TE-TST-135 (5 cases) + test_lock_file release_lock test; full suite 861 passed.
- **Root Cause**: Launcher post-start cleanup removed .linkwatcher.lock whenever its spawned daemon exited non-zero, including when the daemon correctly declined because another live instance held the lock; this stripped the running owners lock so the next start spawned a duplicate. main.py acquire_lock verified sound via concurrency harness (Windows blocks deleting an open lock fd).
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review approved (PF-TSK-005), no regressions; 19/19 targeted tests + manual validation pass. DI OB dimensions verified. Coverage observation logged as tech debt TD260.

### PD-BUG-106

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Deployment
- **Repro**: Start a LinkWatcher daemon for any other project (runs from ~/bin/.linkwatcher-venv python), then run python deployment/install_global.py from the source repo
- **Expected**: Installer stops (or detects and reports) all daemons running from the install dir venv before rebuilding it, then completes
- **Actual**: Errno 13 Permission denied on .linkwatcher-venv/Scripts/python.exe; install aborts after file copy, before venv/wrappers/smoke test
- **Evidence**: v2.1.1 deploy 2026-06-10: first install run exit 1; appdev daemon PID 25200 held the venv python
- **Triage**: Installer defect: install_global.py stop_running_linkwatcher() only stops the source project daemon; daemons of other projects running from the shared ~/bin/.linkwatcher-venv lock its python.exe, so venv recreation fails (Errno 13) and install aborts mid-flow leaving partial state (files copied, no venv/wrapper/smoke-test). Bumped Medium->High: impact High (half-installed global command) and frequency real for this multi-project shared-venv setup during releases. Deployment tooling = not a product feature (RelatedFeature N/A) but a defect still gets a product bug ID. Scope S: focused fix in install_global.py daemon-stop to enumerate+stop all daemons running from the install-dir venv.
- **Updated**: 2026-06-11
- **Verification**: S-scope quick path: human-approved at checkpoint. TE-TST-139: 17 tests written first (17 failed pre-fix, 17 pass post-fix, incl. main()-wiring order + no-partial-state negative assertions); full suite 910 passed/0 failed; live read-only validation enumerated both venv-holding daemons (appdev 17404, LinkWatcher 33676) and lock gate reported locked=yes; manual validation script test/bug-validation/PD-BUG-106_installer_venv_lock_validation.py

### PD-BUG-105

- **Source**: E2ETesting
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: E2E Test Harness
- **Repro**: Run-E2EAcceptanceTest.ps1 -TestCase TE-E2E-019 -Workflow dry-run-mode-preview-without-changes with the project daemon running; compare workspace docs/readme.md against expected (unchanged)
- **Expected**: Scoped workspace daemons are the only writers inside their test workspace during E2E execution
- **Actual**: Project daemon concurrently updates workspace files with default config, overriding dry-run/ignore/backup expectations. Candidate fixes: exclude e2e workspace dirs from project daemon scope, or harness pauses project daemon during runs
- **Evidence**: logs/linkwatcher/LinkWatcherLog.txt 22:03 entries with e2e-acceptance-testing paths; TE-E2E-019 workspace link rewritten despite dry-run
- **Triage**: Product bug (kept, not reclassified): project daemon watches whole project root incl. test/e2e-acceptance-testing/*/workspace and concurrently writes over scoped per-test daemons (surfaced by PD-BUG-100 per-project-root coexistence). Live daemon ignores per-project linkwatcher-config.yaml (validate-only) so no config knob exists today. Framework runner intentionally tool-agnostic (PF-IMP-878) - central pause/resume off the table. Fix sites are product-side: per-test run.ps1 pausing the project daemon (test/) and/or a daemon runtime scope-exclude (src/linkwatcher). Root-cause note for Bug Fixing: run.ps1 pause is a workaround; deeper gap is no runtime subtree exclusion in the product daemon - may warrant a product enhancement. Scope M: multiple run.ps1 + re-verify 6 E2E cases (TE-E2E-011/015/019/023/024/025).
- **Updated**: 2026-06-11
- **Fix**: Two-layer fix: (1) per-project config now reaches the live daemon - start_linkwatcher_background.ps1 passes --config tools/linkwatcher/linkwatcher-config.yaml when present (PF-IMP-1115), and the PRJ-001 config sets ignored_directories = defaults + e2e-acceptance-testing; (2) handler.py _is_in_ignored_directory() guard at top of on_moved/on_deleted/on_created makes ignored_directories a hard boundary for live events - the PD-BUG-046 known-reference-target bypass and PD-BUG-071 directory-move path can no longer re-admit events from ignored trees (E2E audit reports referencing workspace paths were the vector). Verified: TE-E2E-019 Passed against repo-source daemon; fixed daemon logged zero e2e-tree events. Live daemon at ~/bin gets the handler fix at next release.
- **Root Cause**: Project daemon watched the whole repo incl. E2E workspaces with default config (no --config wiring), and even with a config exclusion the handler known-reference-target bypass (PD-BUG-046) skipped the only ignored_directories enforcement point, so workspace events re-entered the pipeline (deletes buffered, creates indexed, moves rewriting files) and raced the scoped per-test daemons.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review 2026-06-12 approved: _is_in_ignored_directory hard boundary correct (PD-BUG-046/071 bypasses closed), --config wiring verified, TE-TST-138 drift guards incl. over-broadness positive control, suite 923/0, lint clean. TE-E2E-019 re-verified Passed; remaining 5 cases tracked as Needs Re-execution in e2e-test-tracking.md. Move-out-of-ignored-tree edge filed separately as PD-BUG-108 (Low).
- **Updated**: 2026-06-12

### PD-BUG-103

- **Source**: TestAudit
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Documentation
- **Repro**: 1. Open doc/user/handbooks/configuration-guide.md -> 2. Find the ### Full Reference YAML block -> 3. Read the ignored_directories list -> 4. Compare against LinkWatcherConfig.ignored_directories default in src/linkwatcher/config/settings.py (lines 86-100)
- **Expected**: Guide ignored_directories list matches code default exactly: .git, .dart_tool, node_modules, .vscode, build, dist, __pycache__, tests, linkWatcher
- **Actual**: Guide lists .pytest_cache, coverage, docs/_build, target, bin, obj instead, and omits linkWatcher and tests
- **Evidence**: Test Audit report TE-TAR-075: test/audits/unit/0-system-architecture-foundation/0-0-system-architecture-foundation/audit-report-0-1-3-test-configschemadrift.md
- **Triage**: Product user-documentation drift: configuration-guide.md Full Reference ignored_directories list does not match LinkWatcherConfig.ignored_directories default in settings.py. Kept as product bug (product user docs describing product behavior) despite doc/ path; the doc/->process-improvement heuristic mis-fires for product handbooks. Low/S: single-doc sync edit. Not caught by drift-guard test (compares keys only, see TD261). No runtime workflow broken (doc-only).
- **Updated**: 2026-06-11
- **Updated**: 2026-06-12
- **Fix**: Unified to a single source of truth (scope reassessed S->M at checkpoint): dataclass defaults in settings.py now carry the full 33-extension monitored_extensions and unified 14-entry ignored_directories (union of both sources + linkWatcher, dead docs/_build entry dropped - component matching means it never matched); defaults.py reduced to DEFAULT_CONFIG = LinkWatcherConfig(). Net runtime change: linkWatcher ignored by default. Guide Full Reference now lists true full defaults; project config re-enumerates 5 promoted dirs (TE-TST-138 holds); stale '24 monitored extensions' corrected to 33 in capabilities-reference and CLAUDE.md. Tests written first: DEFAULT_CONFIG==dataclass field-for-field guard (test_config.py), guide value-compare guards for ignored_directories + monitored_extensions (test_configschemadrift.py). Full suite 923 passed.
- **Root Cause**: Two independently-authored built-in default sources: LinkWatcherConfig dataclass defaults (settings.py) and DEFAULT_CONFIG (defaults.py). Runtime (main.py) consumed DEFAULT_CONFIG, while all recent work (linkWatcher ignore addition, drift guards TE-TST-136/138, per-project config) treated the dataclass as truth. The guide's Full Reference was transcribed from defaults.py, so it matched effective runtime but not the intended source of truth; git shows the legacy dirs were never in settings.py - the sets were never copies of each other.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review 2026-06-12 approved: single-source-of-truth unification (dataclass canonical, DEFAULT_CONFIG = LinkWatcherConfig()) is a root-cause fix; field-for-field equality guard in test_config.py and value-comparing guide drift guards in test_configschemadrift.py close the TD261 key-presence gap; guide Full Reference verified in sync by passing guards; suite 923/0, lint clean.

### PD-BUG-107

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Handler/Config
- **Expected**: The daemon never monitors or indexes its own output files; log content is not parsed into the link database and is never rewritten by move updates
- **Actual**: Log files are indexed at startup/rotation; with the on_modified fix every log write triggers a full rescan of the log, which itself writes a log line, looping indefinitely
- **Evidence**: Code review session 2026-06-12 (PD-BUG-102/103/105 fix review): empirical parse of logs/linkwatcher/LinkWatcherLog.txt yielded 315 references via LinkParser; default ignored_directories verified in src/linkwatcher/config/settings.py; loop chain on_modified -> rescan_file_links -> logger.info(file_links_scanned) -> modify event (src/linkwatcher/handler.py, src/linkwatcher/reference_lookup.py). Surgical fix direction: daemon excludes its own log path (derived from --log-file) rather than relying on config lists.
- **Triage**: Validity confirmed against code: .txt monitored, no ignored_directories entry covers logs/linkwatcher (linkWatcher entry is case-sensitive basename match, handler.py _is_in_ignored_directory). Two harms: (a) LIVE - log files indexed at startup/rotation, so moves rewrite historical log lines (log corruption, 315 spurious refs in DB); (b) LATENT - with PD-BUG-102 on_modified handler, log write -> rescan -> log line -> modify event = self-sustaining loop. Priority High: blocks deployment of the finished PD-BUG-102 fix and corrupts diagnostic history today; not Critical (no product-file data loss/security/crash). Scope S: surgical single-session fix - daemon self-excludes its own log path derived from --log-file incl. rotation siblings, rather than relying on config lists. Effort ~half day incl. regression tests. Sequencing: fix BEFORE or WITH PD-BUG-102 closure - it gates that deployment. Not a duplicate of PD-BUG-105 (boundary enforcement vs ignore-set coverage gap).
- **Updated**: 2026-06-12
- **Verification**: S-scope quick path, human-approved at checkpoint 2026-06-12. Live verification: repo-source daemon against C:/tmp playground — own_output_excluded announced at startup, 0 rescans of own output (pre-fix loops), PD-BUG-102 rescan still fired exactly once for a real external edit. Validate-TestTracking.ps1 0 errors.

### PD-BUG-108

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Handler
- **Expected**: A file moved from an ignored tree into a monitored location is treated like a new file: scanned and indexed
- **Actual**: The native move event is dropped; the file is invisible to the link database until next modified event or restart
- **Evidence**: Code review session 2026-06-12 (PD-BUG-105 fix review): src/linkwatcher/handler.py on_moved drops src-ignored events before the directory/file move handling; comment documents source-side-only check as intentional for rename-TO-ignored (PD-BUG-071) but the move-OUT direction loses dest indexing
- **Triage**: Validity confirmed against code: on_moved drops src-ignored events before dest handling (handler.py PD-BUG-105 guard), so a native FileMovedEvent from ignored tree into monitored tree is dropped and the moved file's own links stay unindexed until next modify event or restart. Priority Low: rare trigger (requires native OS move event, not delete+create correlation, out of an ignored tree), self-healing - and the PD-BUG-102 on_modified handler shrinks the stale window further. No file corruption. Scope S: dest-side check + create-like indexing of dest in on_moved, single session, effort ~2-4h. Not a duplicate of PD-BUG-071 (that fixed rename-TO-ignored; this is the move-OUT direction).
- **Updated**: 2026-06-12
- **Verification**: S-scope quick path: human-approved at checkpoint 2026-06-12. Regression tests failed pre-fix for the right reason (dest absent from DB); negative assertions confirm no outside references rewritten.

### PD-BUG-102

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Handler/Database
- **Repro**: 1. With LinkWatcher running, create file A (test/automated/unit/test_configschemadrift.py). 2. Write a markdown link to A into an existing monitored .md file (test/state-tracking/permanent/test-tracking.md, written by New-TestFile.ps1). 3. A few minutes later, move A to another directory with the same basename (Move-Item). 4. Observe move_detected followed by no_references_found in the log; the link in the .md file is not rewritten.
- **Expected**: The modify event on test-tracking.md indexes the new link into the database; the subsequent move of the target rewrites the link to the new path.
- **Actual**: Move processed with references_count=0 (no_references_found warning); link left pointing at the old path; manual repair required.
- **Evidence**: logs/linkwatcher/LinkWatcherLog_20260610-215218.txt 2026-06-10 14:27:46-14:28:16 (file_created old path, file_deleted, move_detect_match result=matched, move_detected, file_moved references_count=0, no_references_found); discovered during PF-TSK-068 session for PF-STA-108; Second instance same day 14:44: a 30-minute-old link in feature-tracking.md (written 14:13 via Update-FeatureTrackingStatus) was not rewritten when its target state file moved to temporary/old/ (Finalize-Enhancement.ps1) — weakens the freshly-written-race hypothesis; modify-event rescan of externally edited .md files may be missing entirely
- **Triage**: Silent failure of core single-file-move value prop: modify-event rescan of externally-edited .md files appears missing, so freshly-written links are not indexed before a later move resolves. Reproduced twice 2026-06-10. Not a duplicate (distinct from PD-BUG-035 replacement detection). Medium (recoverable stale link, no crash/data-loss) but not lower due to silent incorrectness. Scope M: root cause unconfirmed, diagnosis+fix spans handler.py modify-event handling + database indexing. Blast radius beyond WF-001: WF-002/004/005.
- **Updated**: 2026-06-11
- **Fix**: Added on_modified handler to LinkMaintenanceHandler (handler.py): defers during initial scan (PD-BUG-053 pattern), drops directory events / ignored-directory paths (PD-BUG-105 boundary) / non-monitored / vanished files, then rescan_file_links() re-indexes the modified file (DB-only, never writes). Serial watchdog dispatch guarantees a modify is indexed before any later move event. 5 regression tests (TestModifyEventRescan in test_move_detection.py, 4 failed pre-fix reproducing the exact no_references_found log signature); full suite 926 passed/0 failed; manual validation script test/bug-validation/PD-BUG-102_modify_event_rescan_validation.py. Docs updated: FDD BR-6 rewritten, TDD dispatch tree, capabilities handbook (File Modifications trigger), implementation-state Decision 3 reversed, test spec, PD-INT-002. E2E follow-up registered as Not Created row (external-edit-then-move) in e2e-test-tracking.md.
- **Root Cause**: Handler had no on_modified hook — links written into an EXISTING monitored file by external tools (New-TestFile.ps1, Update-FeatureTrackingStatus) were never indexed; the DB only learned links at startup scan, on_created, or LinkWatcher's own rewrites. A later move of the fresh link's target ran with references_count=0 (no_references_found) and left the link stale. Documented as deliberate pre-framework Decision 3 (ignore on_modified) whose rationale missed the DB-staleness consequence.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Updated**: 2026-06-12
- **Fix**: (See Fix details already recorded in Notes, 2026-06-12 session.) on_modified handler added to LinkMaintenanceHandler — DB-only rescan_file_links() of externally edited files; 5 regression tests; full suite green. Deployment blocker PD-BUG-107 (own-log indexing / rescan loop) fixed and closed 2026-06-12, so this fix is now safe to ship — live coexistence verified (own outputs excluded, external-edit rescan fires exactly once).
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review approved 2026-06-12 (PF-TSK-005): on_modified handler verified correct and minimal; 5 regression tests; full suite 936 passed/0 failed; flake8 clean; TDD/FDD aligned; no regressions. Follow-up tech debt filed for rescan_file_links atomicity.

### PD-BUG-109

- **Source**: E2ETesting
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: handler.py/utils.py (own-output exclusion)
- **Repro**: 1. python main.py --project-root <dir>/project --log-file <dir>/lw.log (log outside watched root); 2. Move a referenced file inside <dir>/project; 3. Observe no reference updates; log shows own_output_excluded dirs=[<dir>] and scan_complete files_scanned=0
- **Expected**: Only the daemon's own output files (log + rotation siblings + colocated outputs) are excluded; the watched project tree is scanned and events are processed normally
- **Actual**: Exclusion zone = log file's parent dir, which is an ancestor of the project root; entire watched tree excluded, 0 files scanned, all events ignored, no link updates
- **Evidence**: TE-E2E-014 workspace linkwatcher-e2e.log: own_output_excluded dirs=['...workspace/te-e2e-014-directory-move-internal-refs'], scan_complete files_scanned=0; Run-E2EAcceptanceTest summary 24 failed / 5 passed (passes are no-update cases: dry-run, idle-stop, validate-only)
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-06-12: regression introduced by PD-BUG-107 fix (b741c37). compute_own_output_exclusions excludes log dir as prefix whenever it differs from project root; when log dir is an ancestor/outside the watched root the whole tree is excluded (0 files scanned, daemon inert). Priority High: impact High but trigger config non-default (--log-file outside watched root); blocks entire E2E suite. Scope S: isolated fix in one function (only apply dir exclusion when log dir strictly inside project root) + regression tests. Related feature corrected 2.2.1 -> 1.1.1 (defect site is handler/utils exclusion logic, same as PD-BUG-107). Not a duplicate; new defect, not a reopen of PD-BUG-107 (original symptom stays fixed).
- **Updated**: 2026-06-12
- **Fix**: Added missing third case in compute_own_output_exclusions (utils.py): directory exclusion now applies only when the log dir is strictly inside the project root (elif log_dir.startswith(root + os.sep)); a log dir outside the root (ancestor/sibling) yields an empty registry since the daemon never scans or receives events outside the watched tree. 3 regression tests added to TestOwnOutputPredicate (ancestor, sibling-outside, root-prefix-lookalike; verified failing pre-fix). Full suite 939 passed. TE-E2E-014 (originally failing) re-run: passed. Manual validation script test/bug-validation/PD-BUG-109_outside_root_log_exclusion_validation.py.
- **Root Cause**: PD-BUG-107 fix handled only two layouts (log dir == root -> stem family; anything else -> exclude whole log dir). When --log-file points outside/above the watched root, the excluded parent dir is an ancestor of the project root, so the is_own_output prefix match swallowed the entire tree: 0 files scanned, all events ignored.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Fix**: Review-amendment 2026-06-12: strictly-inside check changed from log_dir.startswith(root + os.sep) to log_dir.startswith(os.path.join(root, '')) — abspath keeps the trailing separator on drive roots ('C:\'), so the old form never matched there and a log dir inside a drive-root project lost its PD-BUG-107 exclusion (rescan-loop exposure). +1 regression test test_drive_root_project_keeps_inside_log_dir_excluded (verified failing pre-fix). Full suite 946 passed. Sweep: is_own_output's excluded_dir + os.sep is safe (entries are by construction strictly inside the root, never drive roots). Optional OB suggestion (info event when no exclusion zone derived) intentionally not implemented.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review approved 2026-06-13 (PF-TSK-005): fix correct at root cause (third case in compute_own_output_exclusions — dir exclusion only when log dir strictly inside project root; outside log yields empty registry, safe since daemon never scans/receives events outside watched tree). Drive-root review-amendment (os.path.join(root, '')) verified sound; is_own_output +os.sep sweep claim confirmed. 4 regression tests verified (ancestor/sibling/prefix-lookalike/drive-root, assert registry emptiness). Full suite 946 passed, coverage 92% (utils.py 90%), black/isort clean. OB dimension clean: empty registry emits no misleading own_output_excluded event; skipped no-zone INFO event agreed as correct (anti-noise). No regressions, no new findings routed; one unrouted observation: abspath does not resolve junction aliasing (pre-existing shape, contrived trigger).
- **Updated**: 2026-06-13

### PD-BUG-110

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: deployment/install_global.py
- **Repro**: 1. Have a parallel session whose SessionStart/auto-restart can relaunch a LinkWatcher daemon from the install venv; 2. Run python deployment/install_global.py; 3. Daemon restarts between the stop pass and venv creation
- **Expected**: Install either completes the venv rebuild or aborts cleanly before copying any files; the lock check should cover the venv-rebuild moment, not only the pre-copy moment (e.g. re-check/re-stop immediately before venv creation, or retry once on Errno 13)
- **Actual**: Stop pass succeeded, files copied, then venv creation failed with Errno 13 Permission denied; install exited 1 with new code copied but old venv intact
- **Evidence**: Install log /tmp/install-v212.log (first attempt, exit 1); process list showed appdev daemon pair (PID 14900/36308) from .linkwatcher-venv started after the stop pass; retry after manual Stop-Process succeeded
- **Triage**: TOCTOU race: venv_python_locked gate runs pre-copy but venv rebuild runs post-copy; an auto-restarting daemon re-locks venv python.exe in the window, failing the rebuild with Errno 13 after files are already copied (violates documented abort-before-copy guarantee). Residual race left by PD-BUG-106 fix (single pre-copy check). Fix is single-file in deployment/install_global.py; approach (reorder rebuild-before-copy / re-check before rebuild / retry on Errno 13) deferred to Bug Fixing.
- **Updated**: 2026-06-13
- **Verification**: S-scope quick path: approach human-approved at checkpoint. Regression test TE-TST-140 (4 unit tests, test-first: 3 failed pre-fix, 4 pass post-fix; incl. bounded-retry and non-lock-no-retry guards). Full unit suite 936 passed/3 skipped/4 xfailed; PD-BUG-106 installer tests still green; no sibling lock sites (venv interpreter is the only Windows-locked install resource).

### PD-BUG-112

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Reference updater
- **Repro**: 1. Repo has .py/.md files containing backslash escapes and path-like text. 2. Trigger a reference update (e.g. delete a referenced file). 3. LinkWatcher runs its reference-update pass and rewrites the touched files. 4. Inspect a touched .py: backslashes on rewritten lines are flipped to forward slashes, breaking escape sequences (e.g. SyntaxError).
- **Expected**: A reference update alters only the matched path-reference span, leaving escape sequences and other non-path text byte-identical.
- **Actual**: Backslashes across every rewritten line are normalized to forward slashes, corrupting escape sequences and stripping trailing slashes, yielding broken source (Python SyntaxError at feedback_db.py line 1137).
- **Evidence**: Filed cross-project from appdev (PRJ-000) during PF-TSK-009 work. LinkWatcherLog 2026-06-22T16:04:14: file_links_scanned + references_removed (count=50) on blueprint/process-framework/scripts/feedback_db.py. Working-tree diff vs clean HEAD = pure backslash-to-slash mangling on 9 lines; line 1137 (escaped \n \t \") became /n /t " -> Python SyntaxError. Same pass hit scripts/test/test_query.py (13) and many feedback .md files. Likely trigger: a bulk remove-references-to-deleted-files sweep (several guide files were deleted in the tree).
- **Triage**: Silent source-code corruption: reference-rewrite pass writes slash-normalized form over matched path-like spans, flipping backslash escape sequences (\n \t \") to /n /t and breaking Python source (SyntaxError). Critical per data-corruption rule (silent, already caused real cross-project damage to appdev feedback_db.py); recoverable via .bak/git only if noticed. Root cause spans parser path-detection (2.1.1) + updater span replacement (2.2.1).
- **Updated**: 2026-06-22
- **Fix**: Added PathResolver._apply_separator_style to re-apply the original separator style in the early-exit and directory-prefix match branches, so backslashes (often string-literal escape chars) survive instead of being flipped to '/'. Added a backslash-scoped disk-existence guard on the early-exit branch so non-path literals that only coincidentally match a moved dir are not relocated. 4 regression tests + manual validation script; full unit suite green (940 passed).
- **Root Cause**: path_resolver rewrote prefix/early-exit-matched references by reconstructing them from a forward-slash-normalized form, flipping every backslash in the original string — including escape sequences (\n \t \") — corrupting source (Python SyntaxError at feedback_db.py).
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review approved (read-only quality gate). Fix is byte-preserving and root-cause-targeted: _apply_separator_style re-applies original backslash style, mirroring _convert_to_original_link_type; backslash-scoped disk-existence guard prevents relocating coincidentally-matching escape literals. Full unit suite green (941 passed, 0 regressions); manual validation script all checks pass. 1 regression test added during review to close direct-match-branch existence-guard coverage gap (5 PD-BUG-112 tests total). Noted observation (not actioned, consistent with existing PD-BUG-095 guard): guard returns original silently without debug log.

### PD-BUG-116

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: reference_lookup.py update_links_within_moved_file
- **Repro**: 1. Start service with dry_run enabled (set_dry_run True). 2. Create docs/A.md containing [C](../other/C.md); create other/C.md. 3. Move docs/A.md to docs/sub/A.md and deliver the move event. 4. Read docs/sub/A.md.
- **Expected**: Dry-run mode previews all changes without modifying any file; A.md content is unchanged
- **Actual**: A.md is rewritten on disk: link becomes ../../other/C.md despite dry-run
- **Evidence**: Empirical check during PD-BUG-114 session: MODIFIED IN DRY-RUN. TE-E2E-019 passes only because its moved file has no outgoing relative links needing re-depth.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-10 (PF-TSK-041). VALID - confirmed in code: update_links_within_moved_file (reference_lookup.py:666) writes via _write_with_backup with no dry-run check, while LinkUpdater's own write paths both gate on self.dry_run (updater.py:220/257). ReferenceLookup already holds self.updater, so the flag is reachable - the gate was never added when this pass was extracted. Fix must also rescan the DB from ORIGINAL content in dry-run: the current path re-indexes the updated content, which would diverge from the unmodified disk once the write is gated. Priority Medium: safety-contract violation, but the writes are correct-content, atomic, and limited to files the user themselves moved - no corruption or data loss; dry-run workflow (WF-007) is P3; consistent with the Medium precedent for silent-behavior bugs (PD-BUG-102/114). Scope S: single gate at the sole _write_with_backup call site (the directory-move path funnels through the same function) plus original-content rescan plus regression test; est. under one session. Dims SE DI: SE - a protective mode fails open (files modified despite explicit no-modify instruction; feature dimension profile lists SE Critical because it modifies files); DI - fix must keep DB state honest against unmodified disk content. Not a duplicate: prior dry-run bugs (PD-BUG-048, PD-BUG-105) were E2E-harness issues, not a product gate bypass. TE-E2E-019 passes only because its moved file has no re-depth-needing outgoing links - regression coverage should close that gap.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-10
- **Verification**: S-scope quick path: human-approved at checkpoint. Pre-fix: 2 new tests FAILED for the right reason (on-disk rewrite despite dry-run; rescan fed rewritten content) and the validation script's dry leg FAILED through the real service. Post-fix: 44/44 in file, full suite 1085 passed / 0 failed, black clean, validation script PASS both legs. Step 20 sweep of all src write sites confirmed this was the only dry-run leak. No E2E re-execution flag: TE-E2E-019 result invariant, now conforming by design.

### PD-BUG-117

- **Source**: E2ETesting
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: In-Memory Link Database (logging)
- **Repro**: run any E2E case that moves/re-indexes a file containing no outgoing links (e.g. TE-E2E-033 directory-move-virtual-root-links) and inspect the workspace linkwatcher-e2e.log
- **Expected**: normal "nothing to remove" bookkeeping logged at DEBUG (or not at all), WARNING reserved for anomalies
- **Actual**: WARNING emitted per affected file — 10 occurrences in a single TE-E2E-033 run for blueprint/doc/guide.md, blueprint/doc/data/reference.md and blueprint/index.md
- **Evidence**: src/linkwatcher/database.py:370 (else-branch of the removed_count > 0 check); no functional impact — both WF-011 cases passed all file-state and behavioural criteria
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-10 (PF-TSK-041). VALID - reproduced directly: LinkDatabase.remove_file_links on an empty DB emits the WARNING; the site is the else-branch of the removed_count > 0 check at database.py:370, which fires whenever _source_to_targets has no entry for the file - the normal state of every link-free file. All 7 call sites (reference_lookup.py:250/261/297/370/595, handler.py:669/725) are remove-then-rescan bookkeeping; none treats a zero removal as an anomaly, and no test asserts on the event, so a level change is low-risk. Description corrected during triage: the method is remove_file_links, not remove_references_from_file (the database.py:370 line reference was correct). Evidence widened: a tally of every WARNING/ERROR across all 9 retained E2E workspace logs found 42 non-INFO records - 33 no_references_to_remove (database.py:370, feature 0.1.2), 8 file_deleted (logging.py:563, feature 3.1.1), 1 no_references_found (handler.py:493, feature 1.1.1), 1 genuine ERROR file_update_failed. The first three are all normal conditions logged at WARNING - the same defect class at three sites. Fix scope APPROVED WIDE by human partner (option b, 2026-08-10): fix all three sites under this bug, because fixing only database.py:370 leaves the 'No errors or warnings in application log during test execution' criterion (present in ~20 E2E test cases) still unsatisfiable and guarantees a follow-up. Related Feature 0.1.2 primary; 3.1.1 (logging.py) and 1.1.1 (handler.py) also touched. Priority Medium: matrix Low impact x High frequency = Medium (fires on any link-free file, on every run). Filed as Low; raised because WARNING loses its signal value for genuine anomalies and every E2E round pays the interpretation cost, while the fix is trivial. Not a duplicate - distinct from the logging-family archive bugs PD-BUG-018 (under-logging), PD-BUG-099 (rotation retry storm), PD-BUG-107/109 (own-output exclusion). Right tracker confirmed: technical debt was considered and rejected - the log-level contract is violated and a documented repo-wide test criterion is broken, so this is incorrect behavior rather than merely suboptimal code. Scope S, est. 1 session: three one-line level changes, sync the WARNING code excerpt in tdd-0-1-2-in-memory-database-t2.md:304, add a regression test asserting DEBUG-not-WARNING. Dims OB (Observability is Relevant in the 0.1.2 Dimension Profile). Workflows: all six 0.1.2 workflows fire the warning, not only WF-011 as filed.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-10
- **Fix**: Demoted four normal-condition log sites to the level their own sibling branches already use, so WARNING again means 'needs attention'. (1) database.py:370 no_references_to_remove WARNING->DEBUG (sibling success branch references_removed is INFO). (2) logging.py file_deleted() WARNING->INFO (siblings file_moved/file_created are INFO; the handler emits file_deleted for every delete including the DELETE half of every move). (3) handler.py:493 no_references_found WARNING->INFO (same if/else as file_move_completed and no_files_updated, both INFO). (4) handler.py:798 directory_deleted WARNING->INFO (on Windows every directory MOVE arrives here first as a DirDeleted) - site 4 was found during root-cause analysis, not in the original report, and folding it in was human-approved. Audited all 56 warning call sites across the full src/ tree including the parsers/ and config/ subpackages: the remaining 52 are genuine anomalies (broken_references_found, parse_error, dir_move_max_timeout, unmatched_file_truly_deleted, rescan_*_error, rotation failures, invalid config) and were left at WARNING. Docs: TDD 0.1.2 code excerpt synced; user handbook logging-and-monitoring.md console-output level table corrected (file deletions moved WARNING->INFO, WARNING row reworded to actionable anomalies) and its worked example now shows file_deleted at INFO followed by the broken_references_found WARNING, plus a note that levels reflect actionability. Verified N/A after checking: FDD 3.1.1 and TDD 3.1.1 reference file_deleted only by signature; test-spec 3.1.1 asserts structured data not level; integration narratives carry no level claims for these events; 0.1.2 and 3.1.1 state files carry no log-level claims.
- **Root Cause**: The log-level contract (WARNING = anomaly needing attention) was violated at four sites that fire during entirely ordinary operation. Two are no-op bookkeeping branches whose success siblings log INFO; two are normal domain events whose method siblings log INFO. The file_deleted case was pure redundancy: its caller never passes references_count, and the genuine 'this deletion left references dangling' anomaly is already reported separately by the handler's broken_references_found WARNING, which fires only when references actually exist. Consequence was 79 percent of all non-INFO E2E log records being expected activity, which both buried real anomalies and made the 'No errors or warnings in application log during test execution' pass criterion - present in about 20 E2E test cases - unsatisfiable as literally written.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review 2026-08-10 (PF-TSK-005): APPROVED, no findings. All four demotions are justified by a sibling branch already at INFO or DEBUG in the same if/else. Verified the load-bearing claim for site 4: genuine directory deletions still surface at WARNING downstream via dir_deletion_no_known_files (dir_move_detector.py:130) and unmatched_file_truly_deleted (dir_move_detector.py:461), so demoting directory_deleted to INFO loses no anomaly signal. Tests use negative assertions (mock_warning.assert_not_called) proving the WARNING is gone rather than merely accompanied, plus a boundary guard asserting the success branch references_removed was not demoted with it. Docs synced as claimed: TDD 0.1.2 excerpt reads logger.debug and the handbook console-output level table is corrected. Full suite green: 1194 passed, 3 skipped, 4 xfailed. No regressions.

### PD-BUG-114

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link updating - simultaneous move handling
- **Repro**: 1. In a monitored project, have file A contain a relative markdown link to file B. 2. In a SINGLE operation, move both: A into a sibling subdirectory (changing A's depth) and B into a different directory (changing B's location). 3. Let LinkWatcher settle. 4. Inspect A's link to B. Observed instance: A = process-framework-central/state-tracking/temporary/structure-change-product-founding-material.md moved to temporary/old/; B = process-framework-central/proposals/PRJ-000_product-founding-material.md moved to proposals/old/; both via one bash 'mv' pair.
- **Expected**: A's link to B is rewritten to account for BOTH changes: A's new depth and B's new directory. In the observed instance: ../../proposals/PRJ-000_product-founding-material.md should become ../../../proposals/old/PRJ-000_product-founding-material.md
- **Actual**: The link was left exactly as authored (../../proposals/PRJ-000_product-founding-material.md) - broken, resolving to neither the old nor the new location. Neither correction was applied: not the depth change, not the directory change. Meanwhile TWO other links in the same file, whose targets had NOT moved, WERE correctly re-depthed in the same pass (../../per-project-migrations/... -> ../../../per-project-migrations/..., and a blueprint link) - so the file was demonstrably processed.
- **Evidence**: Filed cross-project from appdev (PRJ-000) during Framework Extension work (PF-TSK-026 / PF-PRO-058 closure archival), 2026-07-17. Host: appdev at C:/Users/ronny/VS_Code/FrameworkBuilder/appdev, Windows 11, LinkWatcher running in background (PID 24132, started by the SessionStart hook). IMPORTANT CAVEAT FOR TRIAGE - this is a lead, not a confirmed defect: I repaired the broken link BY HAND shortly after observing it, so I never confirmed whether LinkWatcher would eventually have corrected it on a later pass. The supporting evidence that it is a real miss rather than a timing artifact is that the same file had ALREADY been processed - two of its three links were re-depthed by the time I looked - while the third (the only one whose target had also moved) was untouched in both respects. Please reproduce before investing: the trigger is narrow (both link endpoints moving in one operation) and the failure is silent (LinkWatcher reports nothing; only --validate or a manual check surfaces it).
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-09 (PF-TSK-041). VALID - reporter filed it as an unconfirmed lead; triage identified a concrete code mechanism, though a live reproduction is still pending. Suspect: the PD-BUG-033 existence guard in reference_lookup._calculate_updated_relative_path (line 776) resolves each outgoing link against the moved file's OLD directory and returns the target unchanged when that resolved path no longer exists on disk. When both endpoints move in one operation and B lands before A's outgoing-link pass runs, B's old path is already gone, so the guard fires and A's link to B is neither re-depthed nor re-pointed - while links whose targets did not move resolve fine and are re-depthed. That matches the reported two-of-three-links symptom exactly. The guard exists to reject non-path strings (regex, globs, example text); a genuine path whose target moved is a false negative it was never designed for. Second half follows from the same ordering: once A sits at its new directory its as-authored relative target no longer resolves to B's old path, so the reference lookup for B's move misses it too. Order-dependence explains the narrow, non-deterministic trigger. NOT a duplicate of PD-BUG-102 (that was DB indexing of freshly written links; this is outgoing-link recalculation). Same function family as PD-BUG-069 (silent skip) and the PD-BUG-033/095 existence-guard pair. Priority Medium: silent incorrectness in the core value proposition but hand-recoverable and detectable via --validate, no crash or data loss - consistent with PD-BUG-102. Considered High because bulk restructuring (both endpoints moving together) is LinkWatcher's primary use case; held at Medium for consistency with prior silent-stale-link bugs. Scope M, est. 1-2 sessions: fix spans two sites (existence-guard false negative plus reference matching after the source file moved) and needs a two-move-sequence regression harness. Fix must also address the silent return - the guard branch emits no log. Related Feature set to 2.2.1 Link Updating (reference_lookup.py, same ownership correction PD-BUG-098 applied).
- **Updated**: 2026-08-09
- **Fix**: Move memory + pending recalcs in ReferenceLookup (PD-BUG-114): record_move() keeps a 300s TTL map of processed moves (old->new, chain-following); the PD-BUG-033 existence guard in _calculate_updated_relative_path now consults it when a links old-resolved target is missing — memory hit with existing new target rewrites the link to the targets new location (fragment preserved, info log link_target_move_applied); guard miss registers a pending recalc keyed by resolved target, and apply_pending_recalcs() re-runs the moved-file recalc when a later move event explains the key. Handler wires record_move at top of _handle_file_moved and per-file+dir in _handle_directory_moved Phase 0; apply_pending_recalcs after single-file processing and as Phase 1.6 for directory moves. Same-directory early return in update_links_within_moved_file removed so in-place renames reach the repair path. Files: reference_lookup.py, handler.py (157 insertions/16 deletions).
- **Root Cause**: Two cooperating mechanisms, empirically confirmed in both event orders: (1) the PD-BUG-033 existence guard resolves each outgoing link of a moved file against the files OLD directory and silently skips it when that path no longer exists on disk — a false negative when the links target itself moved in the same operation (both files leave their old locations before either event is processed). (2) The targets own move event cannot repair the link afterwards: processed after the referencing file, the DB has re-indexed the stale link resolved from the NEW source dir so find_references misses it; processed before, the updater opens the referencing file at its already-vacated old path (Errno 2).
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Updated**: 2026-08-10
- **Fix**: Code review follow-up (2026-08-10), three changes all in reference_lookup.py. (1) MAJOR - authored-form preservation guard in _calculate_updated_relative_path: before recalculating, resolve the link AS AUTHORED against the moved file new directory; when that already equals the intended target, return the original string untouched. Recalculation is now semantic rather than textual, so os.path.relpath canonicalization can no longer rewrite links that were never wrong. (2) MINOR - apply_pending_recalcs now emits a pending_link_recalc_dropped warning instead of a silent continue when the recorded file moved again before its repair could run. (3) MINOR - record_move refreshes the timestamp on each chain hop so a still-moving chain is not aged from its first hop. Tests 6 to 10 in test_simultaneousmoves.py (new class TestSameDirectoryRenamePreservesAuthoredForm: dot-slash, backslash-in-ps1 and trailing-slash forms must survive an in-place rename byte-identical - all three FAILED pre-fix with the exact reformatting signature; plus a cross-directory boundary guard passing both pre- and post-fix, proving the suppression does not leak into moves that genuinely need rewriting).
