Manual validation script gained a third scenario (form preservation) alongside the two event orders. Docs: TDD 2-2-1 invariant corrected, test-spec 2-2-1 gained the four cases, 2.2.1 state file bug row records rejection plus follow-up, test-tracking row synced 6 to 10 and status corrected. Full suite 1198 passed, 3 skipped, 4 xfailed (baseline 1194, exactly +4). HEAD-baseline probe confirms in-place rename output is byte-identical to pre-PD-BUG-114 behavior. Sweep of every os.path.relpath rewrite site found no sibling instances.
- **Root Cause**: Not the early-return removal itself, which was required so a vanished target reaches the move-memory repair. The defect is that _replace_links_in_lines treats ANY textual difference as an update (if new_target == ref.link_target: continue, else rewrite) while _calculate_updated_relative_path ends in os.path.relpath, which returns a CANONICAL path. As long as the same-directory early return existed, non-canonical links never reached relpath; removing it routed every in-place rename through canonicalization, so links already resolving correctly were reformatted - dot-slash to bare, backslashes to forward slashes, trailing-slash directory links stripped. The invariant the code needed to encode is semantic (rewrite only when the as-authored link no longer resolves to its target from the new location), not textual. Confirmed by running an identical probe against a git archive HEAD baseline: HEAD left the link untouched, the working tree rewrote it and logged links_updated=1.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review (PF-TSK-005) 2026-08-17, human-approved at the findings checkpoint. Scope: reference_lookup.py and handler.py plus test_simultaneousmoves.py and the manual validation script; adjacent working-tree change sets (dry-run gate, WARNING demotions, Control Panel dependencies, resolution overrides) excluded. Dims DI OB, so performance, accessibility, security and acceptance-criteria steps are N-A. Verification: full suite 1198 passed, 3 skipped, 4 xfailed - matching the recorded baseline of 1194 plus 4; black and isort clean; flake8 clean at project settings (max-line-length 120); manual validation script PASS on both event orders plus form preservation. Both repair branches are covered by tests: target-first exercises the move-memory hit, referencing-first exercises the pending-recalc path including the DB rescan. Strengths: root cause proven in both event orders with a HEAD-baseline probe showing byte-identical in-place-rename output; the follow-up reframed the invariant from textual to semantic rather than special-casing same-directory again; five graded log events replace what was a silent skip; TDD section Simultaneous-Move Repair documents the mechanism accurately and corrects its own earlier wrong claim. Findings routed rather than blocking closure, per human decision: MAJOR thread-safety defect in the new move-memory state filed as PD-BUG-119 (unguarded dicts mutated from the watchdog thread and the DirectoryMoveDetector processing thread; RuntimeError aborts a move or a lost pending registration silently leaves a stale link). Two MINOR items filed as technical debt: no regression test for the directory-move wiring (Phase 0 record_move and Phase 1.6 apply_pending_recalcs are exercised but unasserted), and record_move chain-following is O(n squared) across a directory move. Two suggestions folded into those debt items: the authored-form guard returns without a debug trace, and the cross-directory non-canonical-form boundary case is unpinned by tests.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-17

### PD-BUG-118

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: reference-update pass
- **Repro**: 1. Have a workspace where LinkWatcher is watching. 2. Delete tracked files that many documents reference (here: 4 state trackers plus 11 placeholder files, giving 1505 broken references). 3. Let the daemon run its bulk reference-update pass (the deletions are correlated into moves). 4. Diff the working tree against HEAD: unrelated backticked path spans in files that merely mention the deleted paths have lost trailing slashes, and some have a doubled segment letter plus an eaten closing backtick.
- **Expected**: A reference-update pass either rewrites a path reference correctly or leaves the line untouched. No character outside the matched path substring is altered, and files that merely mention a deleted path in prose are not rewritten at all.
- **Actual**: Characters adjacent to the matched path are consumed and duplicated. Trailing slashes are deleted inside backticked spans; the following segment leading letter is doubled; closing backticks and sentence periods are eaten. Damage is silent: no error, no log line naming the write.
- **Evidence**: Measured in appdev: 91 files rewritten at 16:37:06.153-16:37:06.277. Repaired HEAD-anchored: 86 files / 181 slash-only lines plus 9 compound lines. Damaged shipped payload (structure-change-task.md, new-task-creation-process.md, performance_db.py) and appdev CLAUDE.md. A docstring became: Resolve database ptest/state-tracking/permanentanent/. Deployed path_resolver.py is byte-identical to release commit b91690d, so the PD-BUG-112 fix was present and running.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-17: Critical confirmed (silent corruption of user file content, no workaround, 91 files damaged including shipped payload). Scope M; escalate to L if reproduction proves elusive. Related feature set to 2.2.1 (was N/A). Mechanism re-framed at triage: the product has no reference-removal write path - _process_true_file_delete (handler.py:940) only logs broken_references_found and writes nothing; every file-writing path is move-driven (update_references, update_references_batch, update_links_within_moved_file), so the 91-file burst was a reference-UPDATE pass, most likely deletes correlated into moves by the delete+create correlator or the directory-move detector. Fixer should start at updater._replace_at_position (updater.py:601) column-span handling and the parser spans feeding it, not at a removal routine. Not a duplicate of PD-BUG-112 and 112 is NOT reopened: that fix addressed separator style of the calculated target and was verified deployed here; this one consumes and duplicates characters adjacent to the matched span. First fix step: confirm the defect still reproduces against current repo source, which carries unreleased changes, rather than only against the v2.1.3 daemon.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-17
- **Fix**: Defect A: parsers/python.py docstring extraction now computes line-absolute spans instead of matching a rebuilt inner string, unifying the triple-quote state machine with span collection so columns are correct by construction. Defect B: path_resolver.py gains _apply_trailing_separator_style applied at the calculate_new_target choke point, sibling of _apply_separator_style from PD-BUG-112. Defect C (found by the fix, not the report): new BaseParser._trim_trailing_punctuation wired into the python docstring-dir extractor and the markdown bare-path and at-prefix extractors, with column_end recomputed from the trimmed length. Verification: 17 targeted regression tests written failing-first; full suite 1239 passed / 3 skipped / 4 xfailed, identical to baseline; manual validation test/bug-validation/PD-BUG-118_path_span_corruption_validation.py 10/10 PASS driving the real service with a real directory move. Cross-parser sweep confirms 0 column drift across all 7 parsers. Committed inside sweep commit 006f5a7 (bundled with unrelated Control Panel work by a parallel session - no isolated per-bug commit exists).
- **Root Cause**: Three independent mechanisms in the reference-update write path, all confirmed by reproduction against current repo source. A: parsers/python.py recorded docstring reference columns relative to the extracted docstring text rather than the physical line; updater._replace_at_position then sliced the line at stale columns, consuming and duplicating adjacent characters (shift = length of the text before the docstring content, e.g. 7 for a 4-space-indented triple quote). B: every path_resolver rewrite returns normalize_path output and os.path.normpath strips a trailing separator, so a directory reference authored with a trailing slash was rewritten without it. C: bare-path character classes include a period, so a path at the end of a sentence was captured with its period, which the rewrite then replaced. Triage premise corrected: the trigger is an ordinary directory move via handler.py line 756, NOT a self-move - both detectors already guard same-path moves (move_detector.py:159, dir_move_detector.py:223).
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review approved 2026-08-17 (PF-TSK-005), no regressions. All three defects verified against current repo source: full suite 1253 passed / 3 skipped / 4 xfailed, manual validation script 10/10 driving the real service, black and isort clean, flake8 clean under the project pre-commit gate. Reviewer mutation-verification: neutralizing each mechanism at runtime turns the matching tests red (Defect B 1 test, Defect C 1 test, re-injected Defect A column drift 11 tests), so the new assertions are load-bearing rather than vacuous; an independent cross-parser sweep confirms line[column_start:column_end] equals link_target for every position-replaced reference across all 7 parsers, corroborating the fix session zero-drift claim. Findings routed, none blocking closure: PD-BUG-120 (High) - Defect B is not covered in the sibling write path, reference_lookup._calculate_updated_relative_path still drops an authored trailing separator via os.path.relpath once the containing file changes directory, same symptom class through a different mechanism; TD268 (High) - updater._replace_at_position substitutes at recorded columns without verifying the slice equals link_target, the amplifier that made Defect A silent across 91 files; PF-IMP-2029 - Update-TestExecutionStatus.ps1 stamps Last Executed when flagging Needs Re-execution, which falsely advanced 36 E2E rows to 2026-08-17 (corrected in-tree during this review, notes re-attributed to PD-BUG-118). Two suggestions recorded but not filed: BaseParser._trim_trailing_punctuation returns empty for a bare parent-dir token and mis-trims a parent-dir segment followed by other punctuation (both unreachable through the three current call sites), and the post-trim separator guard added to the two markdown extractors is dead code.

### PD-BUG-119

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link updating - ReferenceLookup move memory
- **Repro**: 1. Start LinkWatcher on a monitored project. 2. Move a directory containing many files and, while the DirectoryMoveDetector settle window is still open, move an unrelated single file. 3. The directory-move processing thread runs record_move per file and apply_pending_recalcs concurrently with the watchdog thread processing the single-file move. 4. The race window is the unguarded dict access in _prune_move_memory, _register_pending_recalc and apply_pending_recalcs.
- **Expected**: Concurrent move processing leaves move memory and pending recalcs consistent: no exception escapes and no deferred repair is lost
- **Actual**: Two failure modes. (1) _prune_move_memory (reference_lookup.py:412) iterates the inner entries.items() inside a list comprehension while _register_pending_recalc may insert into the same dict, raising RuntimeError dictionary changed size during iteration. Raised inside record_move it propagates to the _handle_file_moved except block and the whole move link update is abandoned; raised inside _lookup_recent_move it is swallowed by the _calculate_updated_relative_path except block and the link silently stays stale - the exact PD-BUG-114 symptom. (2) Lost update: apply_pending_recalcs pops a key while the other thread registers into it, so the registration is dropped and the deferred repair never runs.
- **Evidence**: Found during Code Review (PF-TSK-005) of the PD-BUG-114 fix, 2026-08-17, before that fix was released. Concurrency path verified in source: dir_move_detector.py _trigger_processing starts a daemon threading.Thread targeting _process_dir_move, which calls back into handler._handle_confirmed_dir_move and thence _handle_directory_moved; handler.on_moved has no serializing lock. Suggested fix: one threading.Lock around record_move, apply_pending_recalcs, _lookup_recent_move, _register_pending_recalc and _prune_move_memory, released before apply_pending_recalcs calls back into update_links_within_moved_file so it is not held across file IO. Trigger window is the bulk-restructuring scenario PD-BUG-114 triage named as the primary use case.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-17: priority raised Medium -> High. Race confirmed in source: _recent_moves and _pending_recalcs (reference_lookup.py:81/86) are unguarded dicts mutated by the watchdog thread (handler.py:449, 506) and by the DirectoryMoveDetector processing thread started at dir_move_detector.py:319 (handler.py:562-563, 610-611); handler.on_moved has no serializing lock, while _deferred_lock and _stats_lock guard other state. High rather than Medium because failure mode 1 raises RuntimeError inside record_move, which propagates to the _handle_file_moved except block and abandons the ENTIRE move link update - not a single stale link - and the trigger window is bulk restructuring, the primary use case; per-event probability is low but the event count in a bulk pass is not. Scope S: one lock around record_move, apply_pending_recalcs, _lookup_recent_move, _register_pending_recalc and _prune_move_memory, released before apply_pending_recalcs calls back into update_links_within_moved_file so it is not held across file IO. Fix before the PD-BUG-114 change set ships - it is in the same unreleased working tree. Dims extended from DI to DI OB: failure mode 1 is swallowed by the _calculate_updated_relative_path except block and leaves the link silently stale, matching the PD-BUG-114 tagging.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-17
- **Fix**: Single threading.Lock (_move_memory_lock) on ReferenceLookup guarding every read and write of _recent_moves and _pending_recalcs. record_move takes prune + chain read-modify-write + record as one critical section; _lookup_recent_move takes prune + get; _register_pending_recalc takes setdefault + store as one section with logging left outside; apply_pending_recalcs holds the lock only for the pop and releases it before the repair loop, which does file IO and re-enters the guarded methods. _prune_move_memory becomes a caller-holds-the-lock helper and deliberately does not acquire - the lock is non-reentrant and both callers already hold it. Module AI Context gains a Threading note so future cross-event state gets the same treatment. No behavior change on the single-threaded path and no new log events - OB is addressed by removing the silent-failure conditions, not by instrumentation. Verification: new regression file test/automated/unit/2-link-parsing-update/test_movememorythreadsafety.py (TE-TST-152, 5 methods), 4 FAILED pre-fix and 5 pass post-fix, with interleavings driven deterministically by hooking a callable the production code already invokes inside each vulnerable window rather than by racing; the 5th test is a design pin that fails an over-locking fix by deadlocking. Full suite 1252 passed / 3 skipped / 4 xfailed / 0 failures. Similar-issue sweep: every other module holding cross-event mutable state is already lock-guarded.
- **Root Cause**: The PD-BUG-114 fix added the first cross-event mutable state on ReferenceLookup - _recent_moves and _pending_recalcs - as plain dicts, while handler.on_moved does not serialize move processing. The watchdog observer thread (_handle_file_moved, lines 449 and 506) and the DirectoryMoveDetector worker thread (_handle_directory_moved, lines 562-563 and 610-611, started as a daemon thread at dir_move_detector.py:318) therefore mutate them concurrently. Three interleavings, all confirmed by deterministic reproduction against current repo source: (1) _prune_move_memory iterates both dicts in list comprehensions, so a concurrent insert raises RuntimeError dictionary changed size during iteration - escaping record_move it abandons the ENTIRE move link update because record_move sits at the top of the try block, and escaping _lookup_recent_move it is swallowed by the _calculate_updated_relative_path except block and leaves the link silently stale; (2) _register_pending_recalc did setdefault then store, so a concurrent apply_pending_recalcs pop between the two left the store in an orphaned dict and the deferred repair was lost with no error and no log; (3) record_move chain-following was an unguarded read-modify-write that could interleave into a torn chain update - not named in the report, found while fixing. Every other shared mutable structure in this codebase was already lock-guarded, including PerformanceLogger._timers under PD-BUG-027, the same defect class.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Fix**: Single threading.Lock (_move_memory_lock) on ReferenceLookup guarding every read and write of _recent_moves and _pending_recalcs. record_move takes prune + chain read-modify-write + record as one critical section; _lookup_recent_move takes prune + get; _register_pending_recalc takes setdefault + store as one section with logging left outside; apply_pending_recalcs holds the lock only for the pop and releases it before the repair loop, which does file IO and re-enters the guarded methods. _prune_move_memory becomes a caller-holds-the-lock helper and deliberately does not acquire - the lock is non-reentrant and both callers already hold it. Module AI Context gains a Threading note so future cross-event state gets the same treatment. No behavior change on the single-threaded path and no new log events - OB is addressed by removing the silent-failure conditions, not by instrumentation. Verification: new regression file test/automated/unit/2-link-parsing-update/test_movememorythreadsafety.py (TE-TST-152, 5 methods), 4 FAILED pre-fix and 5 pass post-fix, with interleavings driven deterministically by hooking a callable the production code already invokes inside each vulnerable window rather than by racing; the 5th test is a design pin that fails an over-locking fix by deadlocking. Full suite 1252 passed / 3 skipped / 4 xfailed / 0 failures. Similar-issue sweep: every other module holding cross-event mutable state is already lock-guarded. Docs: TDD PD-TDD-026 Simultaneous-Move Repair gains a thread-safety bullet; test spec TE-TSP gains a Move-Memory Thread Safety section.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review approved 2026-08-17 (PF-TSK-005), bug-fix review scoped to Dims DI OB. Lock placement verified complete by exhaustive grep: all four entry points guarded, all four in-place mutation sites inside the critical section, no unguarded access left in src. No lock held across file I/O and no nested acquisition, so no lock-ordering hazard against the database, handler stats or the move detectors. The similar-issue sweep claim was independently confirmed. Full suite 1258 passed / 3 skipped / 4 xfailed; reference_lookup coverage 91 percent; black clean; the single flake8 E501 is byte-identical at HEAD and therefore pre-existing. Regression tests mutation-verified: restoring the true pre-fix body turns 3 red, and moving the prune back outside the lock turns 3 red, so 4 of the 5 tests are load-bearing. Two findings routed rather than blocking closure: PD-BUG-122 (Medium, DI OB) - the check-then-register pair spans two lock acquisitions, so a registration can still be orphaned under an already-applied key, confirmed by probe, with a Scope S airtight fix suggested; TD269 (Low) - the failure-mode-2 test no longer catches a re-split of setdefault and the store, so its pin is vacuous going forward. Observation not routed: the three TTL-expiry del statements in _prune_move_memory (lines 457, 461, 463) and the pending_link_recalc_dropped warning branch (397-402) are executed by no test, pre-existing from PD-BUG-114.

### PD-BUG-120

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link updating - within-moved-file recalc
- **Repro**: 1. Create notes.md at the project root containing a directory reference written with a trailing slash (doc/state-tracking/), the target existing on disk. 2. With LinkWatcher running, move notes.md into a subdirectory (sub/notes.md). 3. Inspect the moved file: the reference lost its trailing slash. Direct probe: ReferenceLookup._calculate_updated_relative_path with target doc/state-tracking/ and move notes.md to sub/notes.md returns ../doc/state-tracking.
- **Expected**: A rewrite changes where a target points, never how it was written. The recalculated link keeps its authored trailing separator: ../doc/state-tracking/
- **Actual**: Returns ../doc/state-tracking - the trailing separator is dropped by os.path.relpath. Silent: no warning is logged, and the containing file is rewritten for the difference because _replace_links_in_lines writes on any textual difference.
- **Evidence**: Found during Code Review (PF-TSK-005) of the PD-BUG-118 fix on 2026-08-17, before that change set was released. Probe against current repo source: target doc/state-tracking/ with move notes.md to sub/notes.md yields ../doc/state-tracking; ./doc/state-tracking/ yields the same; a control target authored without a trailing slash is unaffected. Same symptom class as the 181 slash-only lines reported in PD-BUG-118, reached through a different code path - os.path.relpath in reference_lookup._calculate_updated_relative_path, not normalize_path in path_resolver. Suggested fix: apply PathResolver._apply_trailing_separator_style (or an equivalent local helper) to the recalculated result, covering both the move-memory branch and the relpath branch.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-17: High confirmed, scope S. Defect verified in current source: _calculate_updated_relative_path returns os.path.relpath output with no trailing-separator restoration at THREE return sites, not one - reference_lookup.py:935 and :937 (move-memory branch) and :975/:979 (main branch). PathResolver._apply_trailing_separator_style (path_resolver.py:288) is applied at the calculate_new_target choke point only and never reaches this path. Description corrected at triage: the report claims the containing file is rewritten for a difference that carries no meaning, which is true of PD-BUG-118 but NOT of this sibling - the PD-BUG-114 authored-form guard (reference_lookup.py:966) already returns the original whenever the authored link still resolves unchanged, so a separator-only rewrite cannot occur; the damage happens only on legitimate rewrites, when the containing file genuinely changed directory. High rather than Medium on the worst realistic manifestation: in prose and markdown the loss is cosmetic (../doc/x resolves like ../doc/x/), but a path string authored as a concatenation prefix in a moved .py/.ps1/.yaml (logs/linkwatcher/ becoming ../logs/linkwatcher) silently breaks every downstream concatenation with no warning logged - high impact x low frequency = High per the matrix. The benign cosmetic variant is the common one, recorded so a later re-triage can revisit. Secondary support: S scope at a code site already open, and this is the explicitly-named uncovered half of a Critical fix (PD-BUG-118) sitting in the same unreleased tree - shipping one half reads as trailing-separator loss being fixed. Not a duplicate and not a reopen of PD-BUG-118: different component (reference_lookup vs path_resolver) and different mechanism, so the reopen table routes it to a new bug. Workflows WF-001 and WF-002 only - WF-011 override-folder moves are NOT affected, because virtual-root /... targets are filtered out ahead of this path by _filter_relative_links (reference_lookup.py:775). The fix must cover all three return sites. Sequencing: it touches the same function the PD-BUG-119 lock fix just rewrote, which is awaiting code review - fix after that review lands to avoid churn.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-17
- **Fix**: Promoted PathResolver._apply_trailing_separator_style to shared utils.apply_trailing_separator_style and applied it at both return points of reference_lookup._calculate_updated_relative_path, covering all three producing sites (move-memory relpath branch, move-memory root-destination branch, main branch) and before the fragment is reattached. The restored separator now follows the style of the recalculated result, falling back to the authored one, so ReferenceLookup forward-slash output never gains a mixed ../doc/x backslash form; PathResolver renders results in the authored style before calling, so its behavior is unchanged. Shared rather than duplicated because this rule has now been needed on both recalculation paths.
- **Root Cause**: reference_lookup._calculate_updated_relative_path returned raw os.path.relpath output, which drops an authored trailing separator. PathResolver._apply_trailing_separator_style (the PD-BUG-118 fix) is applied only at the calculate_new_target choke point and never reaches this path. The PD-BUG-114 authored-form guard shields only links that still resolve unchanged, so the loss landed exactly on legitimate rewrites - and on a concatenation prefix in a moved .py/.ps1/.yaml it silently breaks every downstream concatenation with nothing logged.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review approved 2026-08-18 (PF-TSK-005), no regressions. Fix verified against current repo source: all three producing sites are covered by the two applications (move-memory relpath branch, move-memory root-destination branch, main branch), the restoration runs before the #fragment is reattached, and the remaining return points correctly return the authored form untouched. Full suite 1315 passed / 3 skipped / 4 xfailed; the single failure (test_bm_001_parsing_throughput) was CPU contention from concurrent reviewer probes and passes in isolation in 7s. black, isort and flake8 clean under the project pre-commit args. Manual validation script 6/6 PASS. Reviewer mutation-verification: neutralizing utils.apply_trailing_separator_style at runtime in both modules turns 8 of the 10 new assertions red, and the 2 that stay green are exactly the intended controls (no-separator-authored, file target), so the new assertions are load-bearing rather than vacuous. Refactor safety: the same mutation also turns PD-BUG-118 TestTrailingSlashPreservation red (5 of 6), proving the promotion to shared utils did not drop the older fix coverage, and a differential probe of old-vs-new selection logic over PathResolver-shaped inputs shows 0 divergences, corroborating the claim that PathResolver behavior is unchanged. Seam check against the PD-BUG-119 lock change in the same function: the helper is a pure function called outside both critical sections, no lock interaction. Findings routed, none blocking closure: PD-BUG-124 (Medium) - the restored separator is a bare backslash for backslash-authored paths, which inside a .py or .json string literal escapes the closing quote and writes an unterminated string literal (verified end to end on both paths; the class was introduced by PD-BUG-118 and its reach extended here, so it ships either way), carrying as a secondary defect the style inconsistency where a single-segment result takes the authored backslash while a multi-segment result takes the result forward slash; TD270 (Low) - the utils.py AI Context inventory does not list the newly shared function or its two callers.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-18

### PD-BUG-121

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Deployment / Installer
- **Repro**: 1. A daemon crashes, leaving .linkwatcher.lock holding PID 12345. 2. Windows recycles PID 12345 to an unrelated process (editor, pytest run, pwsh session). 3. Run python deployment/install_global.py. 4. main() calls stop_running_linkwatcher unconditionally at line 720.
- **Expected**: The installer verifies the PID is genuinely a LinkWatcher daemon (command line contains main.py and a matching --project-root) before terminating, and reports a stale lock instead of killing an unrelated process.
- **Actual**: taskkill /F terminates the recycled PID with no clean-shutdown chance, prints the false confirmation OK: Stopped running LinkWatcher (PID: 12345), then unlinks the lock, destroying the evidence.
- **Evidence**: deployment/install_global.py line 60 (stop_running_linkwatcher), called from main() line 720; lock unlinked line 72. Identity primitives already present at line 97 (project_root_from_cmdline) in the same file; panel equivalent at src/linkwatcher/linkwatcher_control_panel/discovery.py lines 148-155.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-17: High confirmed, scope S. Defect verified in current source: stop_running_linkwatcher (install_global.py:40) reads the lock PID and calls taskkill /F with no identity check - no cmdline, no main.py, no --project-root match - is called unconditionally from main() at line 720, prints OK: Stopped running LinkWatcher (PID: N) for a kill it never verified, and unlinks the lock at line 72, destroying the evidence. High on impact x frequency: high impact (hard-kills an unrelated recycled-PID process with no clean-shutdown chance, e.g. an editor with unsaved buffers or a running test session, then reports success) x low frequency (needs a stale lock AND a recycled PID AND an installer run). Not Critical: the data loss is second-order, in another process, not in the LinkWatcher data path. Related Feature corrected 7.1.1 -> 0.1.1 at triage: the Control Panel neither introduced nor owns this, its code review only made the contrast visible; deployment/ is process-owned tooling belonging to no feature, and the precedent for deployment defects (PD-BUG-104, global install omits handbooks) assigned 0.1.1. Workflows: none - installation is not a tracked user workflow, same as PD-BUG-104. Not a duplicate of PD-BUG-106 or PD-BUG-110. Root-cause note for the fixer, not decided at triage: adding identity verification may be the symptom fix. stop_daemons_using_venv (line 130, added by PD-BUG-106) already enumerates every daemon holding the install venv by executable path - exactly the Permission denied problem this function docstring claims to solve - and it runs one line later at main() line 721, so deleting stop_running_linkwatcher outright is a live candidate. The two bugs are mirror images in the same function: PD-BUG-106 was under-reach, this one is over-reach. Identity primitives already present: project_root_from_cmdline (install_global.py:97) and the three-way test is_daemon_for (control panel discovery.py:142).
- **Updated**: 2026-08-17
- **Updated**: 2026-08-18
- **Verification**: S-scope quick path: root-cause removal (Option A) human-approved at checkpoint over identity-verification (Option B). TE-TST-154 (6 tests) written fail-first: 5 failed before the fix and the behavioral failure was verified for the right reason - a real spawned bystander (PID 33948) was hard-killed and the lock file destroyed; all 6 pass after. Two behavioral tests drive main() against a sandbox project root and assert bystander survival plus lock preservation (negative assertions on the buggy outcomes); four AST-based structural pins (no lock literal, single kill path, no lock-stop call in main, surviving path still tree-kills). Full suite: 1331 passed, 1 failed - unrelated and pre-existing, filed as PD-BUG-125 (order-dependent Control Panel logging test; reproduces identically with every installer test excluded). Installer suites 40/40. flake8 clean under the pre-commit gate settings; Validate-TestTracking -Path scoped run clean. Manual validation script test/bug-validation/PD-BUG-121_installer_unverified_pid_kill_validation.py created before the fix (visible Notepad bystander); its live-daemon guard verified. No E2E group covers installation, Workflows column empty - no re-execution flagged.

### PD-BUG-124

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link updating - trailing separator restoration
- **Repro**: 1. In a watched project create a directory logs/ containing any file. 2. Create collect.py at the project root containing the line: LOG = "logs\\" (a doubled backslash, the only valid way to write a trailing backslash in a Python string). 3. With LinkWatcher running, rename the directory logs to logs2. 4. Read collect.py: the line is now LOG = "logs2\" and ast.parse reports unterminated string literal. Variant through the second path: put the line LOG = "..\\logs\\" in sub/collect.py and move sub/collect.py to the project root; the line becomes LOG = "logs\".
- **Expected**: A rewrite never leaves the containing file invalid. Where the authored path sits in an escaped-string context, the emitted separator preserves the authored escaping (a doubled backslash stays doubled), or the rewrite is skipped and logged rather than written.
- **Actual**: The emitted single backslash escapes the closing quote, so the file is written with an unterminated string literal. The daemon logs only moved_file_links_updated links_updated=1 - no warning that the write broke the file.
- **Evidence**: Found during Code Review (PF-TSK-005) of the PD-BUG-120 fix on 2026-08-18, before that change set was released. Verified end to end against current repo source by driving the real service: (a) PathResolver path - directory move logs to logs2 turned LOG = "logs\\" into LOG = "logs2\", ast.parse raises SyntaxError unterminated string literal; (b) ReferenceLookup path - moving sub/collect.py to the project root turned LOG = "..\\logs\\" into LOG = "logs\", same SyntaxError. Controls unaffected: forward-slash forms, and multi-segment backslash forms (LOG = "logs\\" moved deeper yields "../logs/", which parses). The class was introduced by PD-BUG-118 (already closed, same helper) and its reach extended by PD-BUG-120; .ps1 is not exposed because PowerShell escapes with a backtick, .json is exposed the same way as .py. Fixer note: test_backslash_target_preserves_trailing_backslash in test/automated/unit/2-link-parsing-update/2-0-link-parsing-update/test_updater.py deliberately asserts the path-level preservation, so a fix must reconcile with it - the gap is escaping-awareness, not the preservation rule. Secondary defect in the same branch: the restored separator follows the style of the result when the result contains a separator but falls back to the authored style when it does not, so one authored form yields a trailing backslash for a single-segment result and a forward slash for a multi-segment one - fix both together. Proposed scope S to M. Severity proposed Medium: impact high (a source file stops parsing), frequency low (valid Python requires the doubled-backslash form), detectability high (the file fails loudly, unlike PD-BUG-118); High is defensible on silent corruption of user source files.
- **Triage**: Priority raised from the reported Medium to High on the Priority Assignment Matrix: impact High (an automated rewrite leaves a user source file unparseable) x frequency Low = High. Aggravated by silent success reporting - the daemon logs moved_file_links_updated links_updated=1 with no warning that the write broke the file. Critical considered and rejected: one corrupted character in a file that remains present and fails loudly at next parse is corruption, not data loss. Scope M: two coupled defects (escaping-awareness in apply_trailing_separator_style, plus the result-style vs authored-style fallback) and reconciliation with test_backslash_target_preserves_trailing_backslash, which deliberately asserts path-level preservation. Release blocker - regression introduced by the still-unreleased PD-BUG-118/120 fixes. Helper verified at utils.py:226-236 during triage. Workflows include WF-005 because the defect is format-specific (.py and .json escaping).
- **Updated**: 2026-08-18
- **Fix**: Escape-aware separator emission at all three sites. New utils helpers trailing_separator_run (returns the authored trailing separator verbatim, escaping included) and authored_backslash_unit (shortest authored backslash run = the author unit for one separator). apply_trailing_separator_style now re-appends the authored run and takes an optional separator argument for callers that render in a fixed style; reference_lookup passes an explicit forward slash at both call sites, so the restored separator no longer depends on result depth. Both PathResolver body-separator sites emit the authored unit instead of a hard-coded single backslash. Single-backslash authoring (markdown prose, and the JSON parser which reports decoded values) is byte-for-byte unchanged, so test_backslash_target_preserves_trailing_backslash passes unmodified.
- **Root Cause**: Reconstructed backslash path separators were spliced back into the file raw source but emitted as a bare backslash regardless of the authored escaping. Inside an escaped string literal (.py) one separator must be authored as two backslashes, so the bare backslash became an escape character - loudly when it escaped the closing quote (unterminated string literal), silently when it formed a valid escape such as backslash-n. Three sites shared the defect: utils.apply_trailing_separator_style (the reported trailing-separator case, both recalculation paths), PathResolver._apply_separator_style (body separators, directory-prefix branch) and PathResolver._convert_to_original_link_type (body separators, match branch - the silent variant). The triaged secondary defect had the same origin: the trailing separator followed the result own style when the result carried one and the authored style otherwise, so one authored form yielded a backslash at single-segment depth and a forward slash at multi-segment depth.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review approved (PF-TSK-005, 2026-08-18). Primary defect verified fixed against a pre-fix baseline extracted from HEAD and driven through the real service: LOG = "logs\\" plus a directory rename gave an unterminated string literal pre-fix and parses post-fix, decoding to logs2\. Secondary defect (restored separator depended on result depth) fixed at the root by passing separator="/" explicitly at both reference_lookup call sites. Regression tests are load-bearing: 6 of the 7 new tests fail against verified pre-fix source, and the 7th is the intended control (unescaped single-backslash authoring must not gain an escape). Suites green - unit 1331 passed, full non-performance 1337 passed, 0 failures; coverage utils 89 percent, path_resolver 85 percent, reference_lookup 92 percent. Reconciled with test_backslash_target_preserves_trailing_backslash and the PD-BUG-120 companion test, both unchanged and passing; no new lint class (the one new E203 is the black slice style already used across the codebase). Scope correction: the .json half of this bug title is NOT substantiated - a JSON trailing separator rewrote correctly both pre-fix and post-fix because the JSON parser reports decoded values, and backslash-authored JSON paths turn out never to be detected at all (filed as PD-BUG-126, pre-existing). Minor finding routed to TD274: removing the result-style fallback exposes the PD-BUG-045 suffix-match branch, which returns forward slashes without applying separator style, so a backslash-authored target there now yields a mixed form - no corruption, narrow reachability.

### PD-BUG-123

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Configuration System
- **Repro**: 1. Write a config file containing exactly: log_level: 5 . 2. Call LinkWatcherConfig.from_file(path) then .validate(), or start the daemon with --config pointing at that file. 3. Observe AttributeError rather than an issue list naming log_level. Repeat with max_file_size_mb: big / move_detect_delay: x / monitored_extensions: 7 for the TypeError variants.
- **Expected**: validate() returns an issue list naming the offending key and its expected type, e.g. log_level must be one of DEBUG, INFO, WARNING, ERROR, CRITICAL. The daemon then logs one config_issue per entry and exits 1 with an actionable message.
- **Actual**: validate() raises AttributeError or TypeError. The daemon logs fatal_error with error_type AttributeError and the raw Python message, so the operator is never told which key is wrong. Any caller treating validate() as non-raising loses whatever work it was holding.
- **Evidence**: Reproduced across four value types 2026-08-17 during Feature Enhancement of feature 7.1.1. Sites: log_level: 5 hits self.log_level.upper() at src/linkwatcher/config/settings.py line 427; max_file_size_mb and move_detect_delay hit numeric comparisons at lines 422 and 440; monitored_extensions: 7 hits set(value) in _from_dict at line 294, before validate() is reached. Daemon call site: main.py line 453, inside the top-level except Exception handler at line 500. Surfaced as Code Review finding CR-3 on the Control Panel (feature 7.1.1), whose save pipeline was one affected caller; the panel side is fixed independently in config_edit.save_config because a pane must handle anything an external module throws. This bug is the remaining root cause and also degrades daemon startup diagnostics.
- **Triage**: Impact Medium (a function whose contract is to return an issue list raises instead, so the daemon emits a generic fatal_error with a raw Python message rather than naming the offending key) x frequency Low = Medium. Scope S for the minimum viable fix - type-guard the known-typed fields so validate() reports instead of raising, and make _from_dict tolerate a wrongly typed monitored_extensions rather than failing before validate() is reached. Escalates to M if the fixer chooses a systematic type-schema validation redesign. SE considered and excluded: the input is a config file the operator wrote, so no trust boundary is crossed - this is a validation-contract defect, not an input-security one. Sites verified during triage at settings.py:294, :422, :427, :440; daemon call site main.py:453. Workflows: WF-003 (startup calls validate) and WF-006 (configuration change).
- **Updated**: 2026-08-18
- **Fix**: Type guards in settings.py via new module helpers: _is_number (excludes bool), _is_string_collection (excludes a bare str, so a string value is reported rather than split into per-character extensions) and _wrong_type for the issue text. validate() now reports instead of raising for every checked field; _from_dict stores a wrongly typed value as authored rather than coercing it. Coverage widened to all 5 Set[str] fields (monitored_extensions, ignored_directories, validation_extensions, validation_extra_ignored_dirs, validation_ignored_patterns) - loader tolerance without matching validation coverage would trade a loud load-time failure for a later runtime one on the other four. main._apply_logging_config falls back to LogLevel.INFO on an invalid log_level so logging comes up and validate() can emit config_issue; the value stays fatal and main() still exits 1. Downstream: the Control Panel CR-3 handler no longer fires for these values - they travel the normal issues path and the operator gets the validator message naming the key instead of a wrapped TypeError; the handler stays (a pane must survive anything an external module throws) with its coverage re-pointed to a forced-raise test and its stale comment refreshed.
- **Root Cause**: validate() performed type-dependent operations - .upper(), <= 0, iteration - directly on values that arrived unvalidated from YAML, so a well-formed config carrying a wrongly typed value raised (AttributeError / TypeError) instead of appearing in the issue list the method exists to return. The unguarded set(value) in _from_dict was the same defect one layer earlier: monitored_extensions: 7 failed during loading, before validate() was reached. Two shapes were silent rather than loud: bool is a subclass of int, so max_file_size_mb: true satisfied value <= 0 as False and passed as a size of 1; and set on a bare string coerced one operator mistake into three nonsense per-character extensions. SECOND SITE, found during Step 21 verification rather than in the report: main._apply_logging_config runs before config.validate(), so LogLevel(config.log_level) raised ValueError and pre-empted the very report that names the key - leaving log_level: 5, the headline field of this bug, still dying with a raw traceback at daemon startup after validate() itself was fixed.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review approved 2026-08-18 (PF-TSK-005), no regressions. Evidence: flake8 clean on settings.py, main.py and config_edit.py; full unit suite 1365 passed / 3 skipped / 4 xfailed; config/settings.py coverage 97 percent; the PD-BUG-123 validation script passes all 19 checks including three real-daemon subprocess runs confirming a config_issue naming the key, no traceback, and exit 1. Dimension verdicts (Dims DI CQ OB): DI pass - silent corruption via a bare string or a bool is closed for every covered field; CQ pass - lint clean, helpers small and single-purpose; OB pass - operator now gets the offending key instead of a raw Python message, verified end to end. PE, AX, SE and acceptance-criteria validation N-A per bug-fix scoping. Positive: the control tests pin all seven pre-existing semantic messages byte-identically, giving the type guards a real discriminating condition; and the Control Panel test rework re-pointed the CR-3 handler to a forced-raise test after the fix removed its original trigger, rather than leaving the handler silently uncovered. Two findings routed rather than blocking closure: PD-BUG-127 (Major) - log_file_max_size_mb and log_file_backup_count are consumed by _apply_logging_config before validate() and are not type-checked by it, so log_file_max_size_mb: big still produces the raw-TypeError fatal_error this bug exists to eliminate; judged a distinct manifestation (validate() passes silently rather than raising) and out of the S scope triage fixed, so filed as a new bug instead of a rejection. TD275 (Minor) - _SET_FIELDS hardcodes field names the module already derives from type hints, so its stated invariant is not mechanically enforced.

### PD-BUG-125

- **Source**: Testing
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Test suite / Control Panel discovery tests
- **Repro**: 1. Run the full suite: Run-Tests.ps1 -All (or python -m pytest test/automated/). 2. Observe test_panel_discovery.py::test_poll_cycle_duration_is_logged FAILS. 3. Run it alone, or run its whole directory (test/automated/unit/7-operations-control/7-1-control-panel/, 314 tests), or run the whole unit tree (test/automated/unit, 1324 tests): PASSES every time.
- **Expected**: The test passes deterministically regardless of what else runs in the same pytest session - a suite-order-dependent test cannot be trusted as the CR-13 instrumentation regression pin.
- **Actual**: assert [] - caplog holds no record whose message contains poll_cycle, so the assertion fails, turning an otherwise green full-suite run red.
- **Evidence**: Full suite 2026-08-18: 1 failed, 1331 passed, 3 skipped, 6 deselected, 4 xfailed. Isolation runs: single test PASSES; panel dir 314 PASSED; test/automated/unit 1324 PASSED. Bisection: test/automated/ minus performance gives 1324 PASSED (green); performance + test_config + the single test PASSED, so the performance suite alone is not sufficient - it needs the unit tree running after it. Independent of PD-BUG-121: the same failure reproduces with every installer test excluded from the run (1 failed, 1297 passed). No logging.disable / structlog.configure / setLevel call appears in test/automated/performance or test/automated/conftest.py, so the mechanism is indirect. Test at test_panel_discovery.py:626; panel logger propagate = False at src/linkwatcher/linkwatcher_control_panel/panel_log.py:64.
- **Triage**: Impact Low (no user exposure; workaround is running the unit tree alone) x frequency High (every full-suite run) = Medium. The real cost is that it degrades the suite as a release gate and voids the CR-13 instrumentation pin the test was written to be. Scope S but diagnosis-dominated - escalate to M if the mechanism resists. Triage surfaced a candidate mechanism not in the report: caplog attaches its capture handler at the ROOT logger, setup_panel_log sets propagate = False on the panel logger permanently (panel_log.py:64), and no test restores it - so once any test in a session has called setup_panel_log, panel records never reach caplog. This alone does not explain the performance-suite ordering evidence and no random-order plugin is configured, so treat it as a lead, not a root cause. Workflows: none - test-only defect with no runtime exposure; feature 7.1.1 maps to WF-010 but that workflow is unaffected. Confirmed as a product bug per the Issue Classification and Routing Guide: the fix lands in a product test, not in framework test infrastructure.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-18
- **Fix**: Test-only fix, no production code changed. Added to the 7.1.1 panel conftest.py: panel_log_capture (collects records directly on the linkwatcher.panel logger, bypassing propagation entirely) and autouse _isolate_panel_logger (restores level, propagate, disabled and handlers after every panel test, removing the leak at source). Converted all four caplog assertions in test_panel_discovery.py to the new fixture and removed the incomplete hand-restore from the panel_log fixture in test_panel_integration.py.
- **Root Cause**: UNCONFIRMED - retained as the original hypothesis, NOT as established fact. The mechanism stated below is contradicted by the code and the failure does not reproduce: the only setup_panel_log call site (test_panel_integration.py line 768) sorts AFTER the failing test in every collection order, so the leak it describes cannot reach that test. See the VERIFICATION CAVEAT later on this row, and TD279, before relying on any of it; if panel-log capture ever goes empty again, start there rather than here. Original hypothesis as recorded at fix time: the assertions captured panel records through caplog, whose handler lives on the ROOT logger, so they required linkwatcher.panel to propagate. setup_panel_log sets propagate = False permanently (panel_log.py:64) and caplog.at_level(..., logger=...) sets only the level, never propagation. The four assertions stayed green only because the one fixture that calls setup_panel_log happened to restore propagate by hand (while leaking an INFO level into the rest of the session) and because test_panel_discovery.py sorts ahead of test_panel_integration.py. Any session order that placed a configured panel log first turned the capture silently empty.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code Review (PF-TSK-005) approved 2026-08-18. Test-only change set; no defect found in it. flake8 (max-line-length 100), black and isort clean; panel suite 316 passed; discovery.py at 95 percent; full suite 1375 passed, 0 failed. Fixture nesting verified with pytest --setup-show: _isolate_panel_logger sets up before panel_log_capture and before panel_log and tears down after both, so the snapshot precedes every mutation and the restore follows every teardown - the previous partial hand-restore (propagate only, which leaked an INFO level into the session) is now complete (level, propagate, disabled, handlers). Conversion is precisely targeted: the three caplog tests remaining in test_panel_integration.py inject their own top-level throwaway loggers and never depended on the shared panel logger. VERIFICATION CAVEAT - accepted as a structural elimination of the defect class, NOT as a reproduction-verified fix. The failure does not reproduce: on a tree differing from the working tree only by the three fix files, the primary command from the bug report (Run-Tests.ps1 -All, reproducing the original 6 deselected signature) gave 1374 passed / 0 failed, against 1375 passed / 0 failed post-fix - no red-to-green evidence for a defect reported at frequency High. The recorded Root Cause is contradicted by the code: the only setup_panel_log call site (test_panel_integration.py line 768) sorts AFTER the failing test in every collection order, and nothing anywhere calls dictConfig, fileConfig or logging.disable or mutates propagate or disabled outside panel_log.py. Mechanism remains unpinned. Findings routed as tech debt: TD277 (new regression test does not pin the regression - its propagate line is a no-op the fixture already performs), TD278 (poll_cycle substring also matches poll_cycle_slow, discovery_error also matches discovery_error_cleared), TD279 (unconfirmed root cause and unverified fix - consult if panel-log capture ever goes empty again). Framework defect found during review and filed as PF-IMP-2059: Update-BatchFeatureStatus.ps1 wrote the feature status into all 12 test-tracking rows for feature 7.1.1 and destroyed six Needs Audit signals; those 12 rows were restored during this review.

### PD-BUG-122

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link updating - ReferenceLookup move memory
- **Repro**: 1. Start from current repo source with the PD-BUG-119 fix applied. 2. Drive ReferenceLookup._calculate_updated_relative_path for a link whose resolved old target K does not exist on disk - this is the path that calls _lookup_recent_move and then _register_pending_recalc. 3. Between that internal miss and the register call, run a competing thread that executes record_move(K, new) followed by apply_pending_recalcs(K) to completion - the exact ordering handler._handle_file_moved uses at lines 449 and 506. 4. Inspect _pending_recalcs.
- **Expected**: Either the concurrent apply consumes the registration, or the link is rewritten directly from move memory. No pending entry is left under a key whose apply has already run.
- **Actual**: The link is left stale and _pending_recalcs retains an orphaned entry under K. No exception, no warning, no log line. Nothing in production calls apply_pending_recalcs(K) again, because the K move event has already been processed.
- **Evidence**: Found during Code Review (PF-TSK-005) of the PD-BUG-119 fix, 2026-08-17, before that fix was released. Confirmed by probe against current repo source driving the real _calculate_updated_relative_path: link left as ../doc/vacated.md, competing apply consumed 0 entries, K present in _recent_moves, and _pending_recalcs retaining an entry keyed doc/vacated.md holding (docs/old.md, docs/new.md). Probability is materially lower than the races PD-BUG-119 fixed: the competing thread must complete an entire move-processing pass (record_move, find_references, reference updates, update_links_within_moved_file, apply_pending_recalcs) between two adjacent statements, whereas the fixed races needed only a single-instruction interleave - Low is defensible at triage. Suggested fix (Scope S, about 6 lines): have _register_pending_recalc re-check _recent_moves under the SAME lock and return the mapped target so the caller recalculates immediately instead of deferring. That closes the window completely because record_move(K) always happens-before apply_pending_recalcs(K) on the same thread in both handler paths (handler.py 449 then 506; directory move Phase 0 then Phase 1.6). Note that the TDD thread-safety section and the test-spec Move-Memory Thread Safety section both currently assert the stronger contract that no deferred repair is lost; if this is not fixed, that wording needs a documented residual instead.
- **Triage**: Held at Medium; the Low floated in the report is declined because the TDD thread-safety section and the test-spec Move-Memory Thread Safety section both assert the stronger contract that no deferred repair is lost - not fixing obliges a documented residual, which is more work than the roughly 6-line fix. Regression introduced by the still-unreleased PD-BUG-119 fix, so it should ship with the release that introduced it. Impact Medium (silent stale link, no exception and no log line) x frequency Low (needs a competing thread to complete an entire move-processing pass between two adjacent statements) = Medium. Scope S: re-check _recent_moves under the SAME lock inside _register_pending_recalc and return the mapped target so the caller recalculates immediately. Two-acquisition window verified during triage at reference_lookup.py:420 and :428, call site :931-958. Workflows widened to include WF-002 - the racing pair includes the DirectoryMoveDetector thread (handler.py:562-563, 610-611).
- **Updated**: 2026-08-18
- **Fix**: Re-check _recent_moves inside the SAME critical section as the registration, with the same prune-then-get semantics as _lookup_recent_move. Renamed to _resolve_or_register_pending_recalc because it may now resolve rather than register: it returns the mapped target when move memory already knows it, and the caller falls through to the existing recalculation branch instead of deferring. Closes the window completely because record_move(K) always happens-before apply_pending_recalcs(K) on the same thread in both handler paths. Adds a link_recalc_resolved_in_window DEBUG line so the losing side of the race is observable (OB dimension). Rename applied across reference_lookup.py, TE-TST-152, the 2.2.1 TDD, test spec and feature state file; historical bug-tracking rows and build artifacts left unchanged.
- **Root Cause**: PD-BUG-119 made each move-memory operation atomic but not the check-then-register PAIR the link recalculation performs. _lookup_recent_move(K) missed and released _move_memory_lock, then _register_pending_recalc(K) re-acquired it. A competing thread that completed an entire move-processing pass for K in that gap (record_move then apply_pending_recalcs, the order both handler paths use) left the registration under a key already applied. Nothing pops it again, so the deferred repair never ran and the link stayed stale, with no exception and no log line.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review approved 2026-08-19 (PF-TSK-005), bug-fix review scoped to Dims DI OB. Window closure verified in source rather than assumed: record_move happens-before apply_pending_recalcs on the same thread at all four call sites (handler.py 449 then 506; 562-563 then 610-611, where all records precede all applies), so a re-check that finds no entry proves that key apply is still ahead of the registration - the window is closed, not merely narrowed. The re-check reuses the prune-then-get semantics of _lookup_recent_move, so no expired entry can be returned, and record_move chain-following means a plain get suffices. Regression test independently mutation-verified, both halves: neutralizing the in-lock re-check turns exactly 1 red on the link assertion with the reported symptom ../doc/vacated.md, and resolving while still registering turns exactly 1 red on the orphan assertion; source restored byte-identical (sha256) after each. Full suite 1381 passed / 3 skipped / 4 xfailed / 0 failures; reference_lookup coverage 92 percent; black and isort clean; the single flake8 E501 at line 31 is byte-identical at HEAD and therefore pre-existing. Similar-issue sweep: no other two-acquisition check-then-act pair in src - the other lock holders test-and-mutate inside one critical section. Rename sweep clean in src and test; only historical archive rows keep the old name. Two minor documentation findings were fixed in this review rather than routed: the test-tracking row had been bumped to 6 tests while its Last Run cell still read 5 passed on the pre-fix date, and TD269 gains a note that the stale between-setdefault-and-store hook description also survives in the test file header and the test spec. TD281 (Low, EM CQ) filed 2026-08-19 on the human partner request: the two-acquisition structure survives - a single production caller of _lookup_recent_move immediately followed by the new method, kept so the new DEBUG event still means a race actually happened - correct as shipped and tracked for a later collapse that must preserve that distinction. Observations recorded not routed: in the race window a resolved-but-missing target now takes the link_target_move_stale WARNING branch instead of being queued, matching the ordinary hit path and logged rather than silent; and a registration landing more than the 300s TTL after its target move was recorded and applied would still be orphaned, which is the pre-existing PD-BUG-114 same-operation window design.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-19

### PD-BUG-127

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Configuration System
- **Repro**: 1. Write a config file containing exactly: log_file_max_size_mb: big . 2. Start the daemon with --config pointing at that file and a log file configured. 3. Observe a generic fatal_error carrying a raw TypeError rather than a config_issue naming the key. Repeat with log_file_max_size_mb: true for the silent-acceptance variant and log_file_backup_count: x for the deferred-rollover variant.
- **Expected**: validate() returns an issue naming the offending key, for example log_file_max_size_mb must be a number (got str). The daemon then logs one config_issue per entry and exits 1 with an actionable message, matching the contract established by PD-BUG-123.
- **Actual**: validate() returns no issues at all. log_file_max_size_mb: big raises TypeError inside _apply_logging_config. log_file_max_size_mb: true is silently accepted as 1 MB. log_file_backup_count: x starts normally and fails only at the first log rotation.
- **Evidence**: Measured 2026-08-18 during Code Review (PF-TSK-005) of the PD-BUG-123 fix. Sites: main._apply_logging_config computes config.log_file_max_size_mb * 1024 * 1024 and passes config.log_file_backup_count to setup_logging, both before config.validate() at main.py line 453. validate() in src/linkwatcher/config/settings.py covers max_file_size_mb, log_level, the five Set[str] fields and four timing fields, but neither rotation field. Probed directly: validate() returns empty for both fields; RotatingFileHandler raises TypeError on a str maxBytes; str * 1024 * 1024 yields a 3 MB string rather than raising, so the failure surfaces inside the logging handler. Not a regression - pre-existing and unchanged by the PD-BUG-123 fix, which closed the sibling defect for the fields it did cover. The bool variant is the same bool-is-a-subclass-of-int trap that the _is_number helper was written for, on a field that never reaches it.
- **Triage**: Impact Medium x frequency Low = Medium, matching the PD-BUG-123 precedent this bug was split from. Validity confirmed by direct measurement 2026-08-19: validate() returns an empty list for log_file_max_size_mb set to big / true / 3.5 and for log_file_backup_count set to x. Per-variant failure points measured precisely, correcting the reported description: (A) log_file_max_size_mb: big raises TypeError in the RotatingFileHandler constructor (if maxBytes > 0), propagating through setup_logging and _apply_logging_config into the main() except Exception at main.py line 500, so startup dies with a generic fatal_error naming no key - as reported. (B) log_file_max_size_mb: true is silently accepted as 1 MB (True * 1024 * 1024), so the log rotates 10x more often than the documented default - as reported. (C) log_file_backup_count: x is NOT the fatal deferred failure the report implies: doRollover renames the log successfully, then raises TypeError at if self.backupCount > 0, which the stdlib BaseRotatingHandler.emit catches via handleError, so the daemon keeps running. Lasting consequences measured instead: the triggering log record is lost, a Logging error traceback goes to stderr on every rollover, and old rotated logs are never pruned, so the log directory grows without bound. Severity assigned from the measured behavior, not the reported one. Scope M (human decision 2026-08-19, escalated from the S the report implies): the minimum fix would add two more hardcoded field names to validate(), but the ordering defect also requires _apply_logging_config to degrade to default rotation values before validate() runs (the same second-site pattern PD-BUG-123 hit with log_level), and the drift that produced this gap is structural - validate() checks a hardcoded list, TD275 records the same hardcoding in _SET_FIELDS, and the PD-BUG-123 test named test_every_numeric_field_reports_a_string enumerates only the five fields validate() already covered, promising a completeness it never had. M scope derives the checked field set from the dataclass type hints so coverage cannot silently drift from the schema again. Dims DI CQ OB: DI for the silent wrong values (bool size, bool backup count) and unbounded log growth; OB for the raw TypeError replacing a named-key config_issue and for logging degrading itself; CQ for validation coverage drifting from the schema. SE excluded on the PD-BUG-123 rationale - the config is operator-authored, so no trust boundary is crossed. Not a duplicate: PD-BUG-123 explicitly scoped these two fields out at closure and filed this as a distinct manifestation. Related feature 0.1.3 owns validate(); the fix also touches main._apply_logging_config and the logging.py rotation handler, which belong to 3.1.1. Workflows WF-003 (startup runs both sites) and WF-006 (config change is the trigger) confirmed. Effort estimate: 3-5 hours, one to two sessions.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-19
- **Fix**: Coverage now derives from the schema instead of a hardcoded list. New _schema_fields helper in config/settings.py enumerates fields from dataclasses.fields plus get_type_hints - the same derivation _from_dict already used - and validate() iterates it for both numeric and Set[str] fields, so a field added to the schema cannot be left silently unvalidated. The type hint also carries the rule: an int-declared field requires a real int (catching 3.5 and 3.0), while float-declared fields still accept either. _NUMERIC_MINIMUMS declares the single field whose bound differs - log_file_backup_count 0 is a real setting meaning rotate but never prune, so the blanket positive rule would have rejected a value doRollover deliberately handles. main._apply_logging_config degrades an unusable rotation value to the schema default through is_usable and schema_default, sharing one rule source with validate() so the value logging starts with and the value the operator is told about cannot disagree; the value stays fatal and main() still exits 1. TD275 closed in the same change - it recorded the identical hardcoding on the Set[str] side, and its stated invariant is now mechanically enforced by a test that derives the field list independently of the production helper. Message wording for previously covered fields is unchanged; the one operator-visible behavior change is that an int-declared field now rejects a fractional value, documented in the Configuration Guide troubleshooting section. Files: src/linkwatcher/config/settings.py, src/linkwatcher/config/__init__.py, main.py, two unit test files, one manual validation script, configuration-guide.md, 0.1.3 state file. Verification: 31 new tests with 13 red pre-fix, full unit suite 1415 passed 3 skipped 3 xfailed, flake8 clean, manual validation 18 of 18 checks including two real daemon startups, and mutation verification turning the drift guard red when coverage is reverted to a hardcoded four-of-five list. E2E re-execution flagging deliberately skipped: the -WhatIf cascade covers 12 entries across 7 features that already carry the flag, no E2E case exercises the invalid-config path, and flagging would overwrite existing provenance.
- **Root Cause**: validate() iterated a hardcoded list of five numeric field names while the dataclass declared seven, so log_file_max_size_mb and log_file_backup_count were never type-checked. Nothing tied the list to the schema, and the PD-BUG-123 test named test_every_numeric_field_reports_a_string enumerated the same five names, so code and test drifted together and the omission was invisible to both. Compounding ordering defect: main._apply_logging_config consumes both fields before config.validate() runs, so even complete validation would not have prevented the startup crash - the identical second-site pattern the PD-BUG-123 fix hit with log_level. Measured consequences: log_file_max_size_mb big raised TypeError in the RotatingFileHandler constructor and surfaced as a generic fatal_error naming no key; true was silently accepted as 1 MB; log_file_backup_count x let the daemon start and then broke the first rollover inside the stdlib emit handler that swallows it, losing the record and leaving rotated logs unpruned; a fourth variant found during the fix, 3.5 and 3.0, fails at the backups slice because a slice index must be a real int.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review approved 2026-08-19 (PF-TSK-005). Scope: the PD-BUG-127 delta only - _schema_fields, _numeric_issue, _NUMERIC_MINIMUMS, is_usable, schema_default, the validate() derivation, and main._usable_or_default. The PD-BUG-123 change set is stacked uncommitted in the same working tree and was seam-checked rather than re-reviewed, its boundary reconstructed from the two separate fix entries in the 0.1.3 state file. Dims scoped to DI CQ OB, so the PE, AX and SE steps and the acceptance-criteria step are N/A. Verification: full unit suite 1416 passed 3 skipped 3 xfailed; flake8 clean at the project pre-commit args (max-line-length 120, extend-ignore E203); the manual validation script passed all 18 checks including two real daemon startups. Independently probed the central contract - validate() did not raise across 119 hostile value and field combinations. Confirmed the drift guards are load-bearing rather than tautological: the test helper derives the field list from the dataclass independently of the production helper. Confirmed _NUMERIC_MINIMUMS uses an is-None check rather than truthiness, so the legitimate minimum of 0 for log_file_backup_count works. Two Minor findings, neither blocking. Minor 1 routed to PD-BUG-128: _apply_logging_config has two callers and only the daemon one calls validate(), so on the --validate path the degradation is genuinely silent - measured exit 0 and a clean report with log_file_max_size_mb big and log_file_backup_count x - which makes the not-a-silent-fallback comment at main.py line 147 accurate at line 486 and inaccurate at line 434; the PD-BUG-123 log_level fallback shares the shape. Minor 2 routed to TD282: the strict-int rule is broader than its slice-index justification, which applies only to log_file_backup_count, and now rejects max_file_size_mb 0.5 and log_file_max_size_mb 0.5, both measured to work correctly at runtime; exposure is zero today since no config file in the repo uses a fractional value, and the narrowing is documented in the Configuration Guide. Three suggestions recorded at the review and not tracked separately: schema_default returns the dataclasses MISSING sentinel for default_factory fields and is newly exported public API; NaN and inf pass numeric validation, a pre-existing hole the old less-than-or-equal-zero check shared; validate() now costs about 1 ms from roughly ten get_type_hints calls, immaterial since its callers are daemon startup and Control Panel config save. Positive: the fix addresses the root cause rather than the reported instance, closes TD275 with mechanical enforcement, and the doc change accurately records the operator-visible whole-number rule including the timing-field exception and the backup-count-0 meaning.

### PD-BUG-126

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link parsing - JSON parser
- **Repro**: 1. In a watched project create logs/sub/x.txt. 2. Create config.json holding two values: one authored with escaped backslashes (logs\sub\x.txt, the only valid way to write a Windows-style path in JSON) and one with forward slashes (logs/sub/x.txt). 3. Start the service and run an initial scan, then inspect the link database. 4. Rename the directory logs to logs2 with LinkWatcher running. 5. Read config.json.
- **Expected**: A path authored with escaped backslashes inside a .json string is detected like its forward-slash sibling and updated when its target moves - or, if backslash paths in JSON are deliberately out of scope, the limitation is stated in the capabilities reference.
- **Actual**: Only the forward-slash value is registered as a link. The backslash value is never detected, so the rename leaves it reading logs\sub\x.txt, pointing at a path that no longer exists. Nothing is logged - the daemon reports a successful update for the sibling reference on the same line.
- **Evidence**: Found during Code Review (PF-TSK-005) of the PD-BUG-124 fix on 2026-08-18. Verified by driving the real service against a temp project: after the initial scan the link database held exactly one reference (target logs/sub/x.txt, type JSON) and none for the backslash-authored value; after renaming logs to logs2 the JSON body value was byte-identical. Behavior is identical on the pre-fix HEAD source and on the post-fix working tree, so it is pre-existing and NOT caused by PD-BUG-124. Corollary worth recording at triage: because these references are never tracked, .json was never exposed to the PD-BUG-124 corruption class, contrary to that bug title - verified separately, a JSON trailing separator rewrote correctly both pre-fix and post-fix. May be intentional: a decoded logs\sub\x.txt is a valid POSIX filename, so detection would need to be Windows-specific.
- **Triage**: Validity confirmed by driving the real parser, not by code reading. Mechanism corrected: detection SUCCEEDS (looks_like_file_path returns True); the reference dies one step later at line location - _find_unclaimed_line searches the RAW source line for the DECODED value, so when decoded differs from raw it returns 0 and the reference is silently discarded. Blast radius wider than backslashes: every JSON escape form drops the reference - backslash, escaped forward slash, and unicode escape all yield 0 refs while the plain control yields 1. The escaped-forward-slash and unicode variants decode to ordinary forward-slash paths with no Windows semantics, which refutes the reported may-be-intentional hypothesis that detection would need to be Windows-specific. Not JSON-only: YAML shares the identical code shape (_find_next_occurrence guarded by line_num greater than 0) but exposure is limited to double-quoted scalars - single-quoted and plain YAML scalars are detected normally because raw equals decoded there. JSON has no escape-free string form, so a Windows-style path in JSON is ALWAYS dropped, with no workaround other than authoring forward slashes. Known and deferred rather than intended: test_jp_004_escaped_strings carries a pytest xfail marker with reason Escaped path line-number matching fails for some decoded paths, dating to the v2.0.1 release commit; two sibling escape xfails exist in test_markdown.py. Priority Medium confirmed on the Priority Assignment Matrix: impact Medium (a reference goes silently stale so the core promise fails, but no corruption, no data loss, and the file stays valid - unlike PD-BUG-124 which left source files unparseable and was raised to High) times frequency Medium (LinkWatcher targets Windows and advertises backslash path support, so escaped Windows paths in .json config are a realistic authoring pattern). Scope M not S because of a coupling: today the invisibility of these references is the ONLY thing protecting .json from the PD-BUG-124 corruption class - that bug records its own .json half as unsubstantiated precisely because the refs were never detected. Making them detectable exposes the updater write-back path, so the parser fix and escape-aware write-back must land together; a detection-only fix would be net-negative. Dims DI plus OB: the drop is completely silent, nothing is logged at line_num 0. Fixer notes: xfail_strict is NOT set, so a fix flips test_jp_004_escaped_strings to a silent XPASS - remove the marker deliberately or the regression coverage is vacuous; that test already asserts the desired behavior so it is a ready-made load-bearing regression test and must be observed failing pre-fix. No duplicates - PD-BUG-013, PD-BUG-030 and PD-BUG-061 are JSON but different mechanisms.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-18
- **Fix**: Shared BaseParser._find_decoded_literal locates a value by DECODING candidate double-quoted source literals instead of searching the raw line for the decoded text. Both structured-data parsers now record the RAW source in link_text, the DECODED value in link_target, and a span over the raw literal - the same split PYTHON_IMPORT already used. Updater: the stale check accepts a structured ref located via link_text (without it the whole file is declared stale and every update in it abandoned, including refs that worked before), and the write-back re-escapes the replacement so a rewritten backslash separator comes back doubled, keeping the file valid. A path_value_line_not_located warning replaces the silent drop. 181 insertions, 0 deletions, purely additive.
- **Root Cause**: Line location searched the RAW source line for the DECODED value. Every JSON escape sequence makes those two differ, so the search returned 0 and the reference was discarded with nothing logged. Classification always succeeded - this was never a detection gap. JSON has no escape-free string form, so a Windows-style path, authorable only with doubled backslashes, was never tracked at all; YAML was exposed only in double-quoted scalars, where escapes are honoured.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Updated**: 2026-08-19
- **Fix**: Code Review amendment 2026-08-19 (PF-TSK-007, re-entry from PF-TSK-005): the escape-aware write-back called json.dumps at its ensure_ascii=True default, re-spelling any non-ASCII character in the target as a uXXXX escape. The value stayed valid and decoded identically, so nothing broke - but a character unrelated to the move came back in a different spelling, violating the rule PD-BUG-118/120/124 exist to enforce: a rewrite changes WHERE a target points, never HOW it was written. Now json.dumps(new_target, ensure_ascii=False)[1:-1]; backslashes and quotes are still escaped, which is what keeps the file parseable. Also closed the two documentation findings from the same review: TDD 2.2.1 now records the _replace_at_position re-escape and the stale-check escapable-types fallback (with why both halves had to land together), TDD 2.1.1 records _find_decoded_literal in the BaseParser shared-helper list plus escaped-literal location and the new warning on the Json/Yaml rows, and test-tracking.md carries a correction noting the escaped-string xfail its April audit recorded no longer exists (appended rather than rewritten, since that audit finding was true at the time). Test spec 2.2.1 gained an Escaped Structured Literals section stating the coupling contract. Feature state file 2.2.1 gained its Bug Fix History row.
- **Root Cause**: Amendment root cause: json.dumps defaults to ensure_ascii=True, which escapes every non-ASCII codepoint. The re-escape step needed only backslash and quote escaping to keep the literal parseable; escaping the character set as well was an unintended side effect of the default.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review approved (PF-TSK-005, 2026-08-19) - re-review of the 2026-08-19 amendment after the prior rejection. Delta reviewed: json.dumps(new_target, ensure_ascii=False) in _replace_at_position plus the four documentation closures; the parser, stale-check and write-back halves were seam-checked rather than re-reviewed.
