Amendment mutation-verified independently: reverting to the ensure_ascii default turns exactly test_non_ascii_authoring_survives_the_rewrite red and leaves the other three TestBug126EscapedLiteralNotStale tests green, so the regression test discriminates on the amendment itself. Seams verified by driving the real parser and updater, not by reading: (a) the PD-BUG-124 interaction - a json-dir value authored with an escaped body AND an escaped trailing separator rewrites with its separators still doubled and decodes correctly, so the two fixes do not double-escape; (b) the re-escape branch cannot misfire - all seven JSON/YAML LinkReference construction sites other than the new escaped path set link_text equal to link_target; (c) YAML write-back works end to end, although no test covers it; (d) markdown frontmatter delegation keeps columns valid under the +1 line shift. Suites green: unit 1416 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failed; coverage base 95, json_parser 90, yaml_parser 94, updater 98 percent. flake8, black and isort clean apart from the pre-existing black slice E203. Manual harness 7/7. No dependency changes. Findings, none blocking, all routed post-closure: (1) Minor - the observability half is untested and json_parser lines 171-177 are never executed by the suite, and every write-back regression test is JSON so the YAML and json-dir-with-trailing-separator paths are unpinned (tech debt); (2) Minor - path_value_line_not_located fires at WARNING on correct behavior, measured 6 warnings across all 84 repo json/yaml files and every one a YAML anchor or merge-key duplicate where dropping is right, conflated with the genuine not-found case (tech debt); (3) Minor - escaped compound strings are still dropped silently, a Bash(...) style value carrying doubled backslashes yields 0 references and 0 warnings while its plain sibling yields 1, the same line-location defect surviving in the _extract_embedded_paths branch (filed as a new bug). Observations recorded not routed: a JSON key equal to its own value takes the rewrite and leaves the value stale, measured identically on the plain pre-existing path and on the new escaped path, so this change set neither introduces nor worsens it; the new stale-check branch precedes the already-replaced branch, which would yield a false STALE for a duplicate escaped ref on one span, with no reproducer today because the parser claim set prevents duplicates within a parse.

### PD-BUG-128

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Configuration System
- **Repro**: 1. Create a config file containing log_file: logs/lw.log, log_file_max_size_mb: big and log_file_backup_count: x. 2. Run python main.py --validate --config that-file --project-root a-small-tree. 3. Observe a clean validation report and exit code 0 with no config_issue line naming either key. 4. Run the daemon with the same config and observe that it does report both keys and exits 1.
- **Expected**: The validation run reports the unusable keys, at minimum as warnings, so the operator learns of the broken config from the diagnostic command rather than from a later failed daemon start.
- **Actual**: Exit code 0, clean report, no mention of either key.
- **Evidence**: Found 2026-08-19 during Code Review (PF-TSK-005) of the PD-BUG-127 fix and reported there as Minor finding 1. Sites: _apply_logging_config has two callers - main.py line 434 on the --validate path and main.py line 486 on the daemon path - and only the latter is followed by config.validate() at line 489. The code comment at main.py line 147 states this is not a silent fallback because the value is still diagnosed by validate(), which is accurate at line 486 and inaccurate at line 434. Not introduced by PD-BUG-127 alone: the PD-BUG-123 log_level fallback at main.py line 132 has the identical shape and shipped with the same gap, so the pattern now covers three fields. No automated test exercises the --validate path. Pre-fix behavior on this path was a raw TypeError inside the top-level except Exception, so the change traded a loud but unhelpful crash for silence. Measured directly: a --validate run with log_file_max_size_mb big and log_file_backup_count x produced Broken links 0, No broken links found, exit 0.
- **Triage**: Validity confirmed by measurement: --validate with unusable log_file_max_size_mb and log_file_backup_count exits 0 with a clean report naming neither key, while the daemon path with the same config names both and exits 1. Description understates impact: the missing config.validate() call gates every key validate() checks, not only the two logging fields. Measured on a tree with a genuinely broken link, validation_extensions set to a bare string .yaml (which validate() does catch, as must be a list of strings) produces exit 0 and Files scanned 0 - a silent false-clean link-health audit that defeats automated callers including run_linkwatcher_validate.ps1. Priority raised Low to Medium (impact Medium x frequency Low; matches the PD-BUG-123 / PD-BUG-127 precedent in the same helper) - capped below High because triggering needs an already-malformed config, the daemon still refuses to start on it, and Files scanned 0 is a visible tell for a human reader. Scope M: the fix itself is small but no automated test exercises the main() --validate branch, and the exit-code semantics need a decision (validate already uses 0 clean / 1 broken links). Dims OB AC: one helper, two call sites, divergent contracts. Not a duplicate - PD-BUG-123 and PD-BUG-127 are both Closed; this is the third instance of the same pattern, at the caller rather than the callee. Separate bug filed during triage: validate() applies the dot check only to monitored_extensions, so validation_extensions with a missing dot also yields a false-clean and would NOT be fixed by this bug.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-19
- **Fix**: New _configure_logging_and_validate(args, config) in main.py runs _apply_logging_config then config.validate(), exiting 1 with each offending key named; both branches call it and the daemon branch inlined duplicate is removed, so a caller can no longer take the fallback without the diagnosis. Exit 1 rather than a new code, matching the daemon path - the bug AC dimension is about the two sites diverging, so a second convention on one path would widen that gap; warn-and-continue was rejected because it leaves exit 0 intact and the false-clean survives for automated callers. Measured after: --validate with the unusable rotation keys now names both and exits 1, identical to the daemon; the bare-string validation_extensions case exits 1 instead of reporting a clean scan of zero files; both happy paths unchanged (clean tree 0, broken link 1). Docs: exit-code table in link-validation.md and the capabilities-reference summary now say 1 also means configuration invalid; WF-006 and WF-009 integration narratives corrected. WF-009 E2E group TE-E2G-013 flagged for re-execution (4 entries) - the 0.1.3-keyed cascade was deliberately not used, as it would have flagged 12 entries across four untouched workflows.
- **Root Cause**: The fallback contract was prose, not code. _apply_logging_config degrades an unusable value to the schema default and defers naming the offending key to config.validate(); that deferral was stated in three code comments and enforced nowhere, so it held only for callers that remembered it. main() had two call sites - the daemon branch ran both halves, the --validate branch ran the first and never the second. Third instance of the PD-BUG-123 / PD-BUG-127 pattern and the first on the caller side.
- **Tests Added**: Yes - 8 tests in TestBug128ValidatePathValidatesConfig (test_main_logging_setup.py), driving main.main() end to end rather than the helper because the defect is in the wiring between the halves. Written failing-first: 5 red / 3 controls green pre-fix, all green post-fix. Mutation-verified - reverting only the validate-branch call turns exactly those 5 red and leaves the 3 controls green. Negative assertions (exit code must not be 0), since asserting only that an error appears would pass while automation still got a clean pass. Full suite 1432 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed. Tracking count synced 19 to 26.
- **Verification**: Code review approved 2026-08-19 (PF-TSK-005). Scope: the PD-BUG-128 delta only - _configure_logging_and_validate in main.py, its two call sites, the 8 tests in TestBug128ValidatePathValidatesConfig, and the doc and tracking updates. The PD-BUG-123 and PD-BUG-127 change sets stacked uncommitted in the same working tree were seam-checked rather than re-reviewed. Dims scoped to OB and AC, so the PE, AX and SE steps and the acceptance-criteria step are N/A; feature 0.1.3 has no TDD, so the state file served as the design record. Verification: full unit suite 1447 passed / 4 failed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed, where all four failures are TestBug129ExtensionChecksDeriveFromSchema - the failing-first tests of the parallel in-flight PD-BUG-129 session - confirmed independent of this change set because they exercise the validate() dot check rather than the --validate wiring; flake8 clean at the project pre-commit args; the 8 PD-BUG-128 tests pass. Mutation-verified independently, by patching the fix out at runtime rather than editing the working tree: exactly 5 red and 3 controls green, matching the claim in the fix notes. Behavioral probe confirmed both the fix and its documentation: an unusable log_file_max_size_mb exits 1 naming the key and writes no report, a bare-string validation_extensions exits 1 instead of reporting a clean scan of zero files, and both control paths are unchanged. Checked for a third unpaired caller: the Control Panel config-save path at config_edit.py line 171 already calls validate(), so main() held the only two call sites and the fix coverage is complete. Two Minor findings, neither blocking. Minor 1 routed to TD285: the log_level case carries a corrupted string literal where a newline escape was mangled into literal text; measured to still parse as an invalid level, so the test reaches validate() and passes for the reason it names - load-bearing, making this legibility debt rather than a defect. Minor 2 routed to PD-BUG-132: a --validate run aborting on an invalid config exits 1 without writing a report, and the Control Panel _report_path conventional-path fallback then adopts the report left by an earlier scan, rendering state broken with a stale count and discarding the config error - measured directly; the mechanism is pre-existing but this fix routes every invalid config key onto it. Observations recorded and not filed: the WF-006 and WF-009 narrative line anchors are stale, verified already stale at HEAD; the framework validate launcher header no longer states the exit-1 contract completely, which is framework path and routes to Process Improvement; an invalid config on the daemon path exits after acquire_lock and leaves a lock file, pre-existing and handled by the stale-lock reclaim. Positive: the fix binds the two halves into one function so a caller cannot take the fallback without the diagnosis, removing the recurrence class rather than adding a fourth reminder comment, and the tests assert negatively on the exit code, which is the correct shape for a false-clean defect.

### PD-BUG-129

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Configuration System
- **Repro**: 1. Create a tree containing a.md whose only content is a markdown link to does-not-exist.md. 2. Run python main.py --validate --quiet --project-root that-tree and observe exit code 1, the broken link is found. 3. Create a config file setting validation_extensions to a single list entry md, with no leading dot. 4. Run python main.py --validate --quiet --config that-file --project-root that-tree and observe exit code 0 with a clean report.
- **Expected**: validate() reports the dotless entry the same way it already does for monitored_extensions, so the operator learns the config is wrong instead of receiving a clean report from a scan that examined nothing.
- **Actual**: validate() returns an empty issue list, the scan examines zero files, and the run reports Files scanned 0 with No broken links found at exit code 0.
- **Evidence**: Found 2026-08-19 during Bug Triage (PF-TSK-041) of PD-BUG-128 while measuring that bug blast radius. Measured directly: a control run with no config exits 1 on the same tree; the run with a dotless validation_extensions exits 0. Distinct from PD-BUG-128 and NOT fixed by it - PD-BUG-128 is the missing config.validate() call on the --validate path, whereas this defect sits inside validate() itself, so adding that call changes nothing here. Site: the monitored_extensions dot loop at the end of validate() in src/linkwatcher/config/settings.py.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-19 via Bug Triage (PF-TSK-041). Validity confirmed by measurement on a one-file tree: the control run with no config exits 1 and finds the broken link, a dotted .md config exits 1, the dotless md config exits 0 with Files scanned 0. Direct probe of validate(): dotless monitored_extensions yields 1 issue, dotless validation_extensions yields 0. Priority Medium: a silent false-clean audit is a wrong answer from the gate, but it needs an operator misconfiguration and the project own config at tools/linkwatcher/linkwatcher-config.yaml does not set the key, so there is no live exposure here; matches PD-BUG-128, which carries an overlapping symptom. Scope S: confined to validate() in settings.py plus regression tests, one session. Dims CQ OB: hardcoded field list beside a schema-derived sibling check (CQ), failure entirely silent (OB); DI dropped versus siblings PD-BUG-123 and PD-BUG-127 because nothing stored is corrupted, the wrong artifact is a report. Description found understated in two ways. (1) Third site: parser_type_extensions values are compared at database.py line 442 against stripped_ext from os.path.splitext, which carries the dot, so a dotless value silently drops every Python or Dart import reference; being a Dict it escapes even the string-collection type check. (2) Root cause is not a forgotten field but a dot check hardcoded to one field name - the third occurrence of the PD-BUG-127 pattern (validate() iterating a hardcoded list while the schema declares more), in the check immediately below the one PD-BUG-127 fixed. Sequencing: not a duplicate of PD-BUG-128 (verified distinct sites) but coupled - the --validate path at main.py line 434 still has no config.validate() call, so this fix alone changes nothing on the reported repro path; its only immediate effect is that the daemon starts refusing a dotless validation_extensions. Approved fix scope (human, 2026-08-19): derive the dot check from the schema so every extension-bearing field is covered, including parser_type_extensions. PD-BUG-128 is being fixed concurrently in a parallel session, so main.py is off limits for this fix.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-19
- **Verification**: S-scope quick path, human-approved at the Step 23 checkpoint. 20 regression tests added (10 in test_config.py, 10 in the new TE-TST-155); 8 written failing-first and confirmed red pre-fix for the right reason. Full suite 1463 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed, no regressions. Re-measured end to end on the repro tree: a dotless validation_extensions now exits 1 naming the key (composing with the PD-BUG-128 fix that landed in a parallel session mid-work), and an uppercase .MD entry now finds the broken link where it previously scanned nothing at exit 0. No E2E re-execution flagged, human-approved: the -WhatIf cascade spans 12 entries across 7 features for a fix that is behavior-preserving on every valid config.

### PD-BUG-130

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link parsing - compound-string extraction
- **Repro**: 1. In a watched project create a config.json with two sibling values: one compound string carrying a doubled-backslash path inside a Bash(...) wrapper, and one compound string carrying the same path with forward slashes. 2. Parse the file with JsonParser (or run the service initial scan) and inspect the references. 3. Only the forward-slash sibling is registered. 4. Move the target file: the escaped compound value is left stale.
- **Expected**: An escaped path inside a compound string is located like its forward-slash sibling and updated when its target moves - or, if it is out of scope, the drop is at least logged through path_value_line_not_located like every other unlocatable value after PD-BUG-126.
- **Actual**: 0 references and 0 warnings for the escaped compound value; 1 reference for the plain sibling on the next line. The drop is completely silent, so the observability half of PD-BUG-126 does not cover this branch.
- **Evidence**: Found during Code Review (PF-TSK-005) of the PD-BUG-126 fix on 2026-08-19. Measured against the real parser: a value of the form Bash(python logs-doubled-backslash-sub-doubled-backslash-run.py *) produced no reference and no warning event, while the adjacent forward-slash spelling produced one JSON reference. Realistic on Windows, where a permission or command entry in a .json config can only spell a Windows path with doubled backslashes. Pre-existing and not caused by PD-BUG-126 - that fix simply did not reach this branch. Same code shape in yaml_parser._extract_embedded_paths. Fixer note: the natural fix reuses BaseParser._find_decoded_literal to locate the raw literal, then offsets each candidate span within the RAW text rather than the decoded text, so the span arithmetic in the compound branch has to change too - a decoded-offset span spliced into an escaped literal is the PD-BUG-124 corruption class.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-19 via Bug Triage (PF-TSK-041). Reproduced against the real parsers; report confirmed but incomplete on three points. (1) Mechanism: the fall-through is NOT blocked by a space check - _classify_path has none. Two failure modes measured, both silent: path in the MIDDLE of the compound string yields 0 refs and 0 warnings (the reported drop); path at the END passes looks_like_file_path and yields 1 ref whose link_target is the ENTIRE command string. (2) Provenance: git show HEAD confirms the silent drop is pre-existing, but the whole-string-target mode is NEW - introduced by the uncommitted PD-BUG-126 _add_escaped_reference fall-through currently in code review. Human approved folding both modes into this bug: one root cause, one code path, one fix. (3) Impact understated: also a partial false-clean in --validate. Measured on two adjacent equally-broken refs, only the forward-slash sibling was reported (links_checked=1, broken=1); the escaped sibling was invisible - same false-clean family as PD-BUG-128 and PD-BUG-129. Real population in this repo: 5 values across .claude/settings.json (drops process-framework/ai-tasks.md inside the SessionStart hook string) and .claude/settings.local.json (4). Priority Medium held: impact Medium x frequency Low - escape-free refs unaffected, audit incomplete rather than wholly false-clean. Scope M: two parsers, candidate spans must move to raw coordinates (PD-BUG-124 corruption class if wrong), plus suppressing the new whole-string fall-through, plus tests; the updater re-escape machinery (_ESCAPABLE_STRUCTURED_TYPES) already exists and is reusable. Dims DI OB confirmed. Workflows extended with WF-003 (drop happens during initial scan) and WF-009 (validate false-clean). Not a duplicate: PD-BUG-126 fixed the whole-string branch only, PD-BUG-061 created the compound branch. Effort: about one focused session.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-19
- **Fix**: Added BaseParser._build_decoded_to_raw_index, which maps decoded character offsets onto RAW offsets by decoding successively longer PREFIXES of the raw literal with the format own decoder, so every spelling the format allows (doubled backslash, escaped quote, escaped slash, uXXXX, YAML xNN) maps correctly without reimplementing escape rules. Both parsers now collect path candidates first, then branch: located value keeps the existing behaviour; unlocated value routes to a new _add_escaped_embedded_paths that locates the raw literal via _find_decoded_literal and records each candidate with link_text = RAW spelling and link_target = DECODED path, the split the updater already uses to rewrite in the author own escaping (no updater change needed). The return contract changed from found/not-found to handled/not-handled: candidates present but unlocatable now returns True AND logs path_value_line_not_located, which both suppresses the bogus whole-string reference and closes the observability half. Value with no candidates still returns False so whole-string classification keeps working for a name like file with spaces.txt. YAML multiline block-scalar branch (PD-BUG-079) preserved unchanged.
- **Root Cause**: Both parsers _extract_embedded_paths located a compound value by searching the raw lines for the DECODED whole string, and offset each path candidate span within the DECODED text. Both assumptions hold only while the source literal is escape-free. Any escape made the line search fail, so the branch returned False and discarded every candidate it had already matched; the caller then fell through to whole-string classification, which registered the ENTIRE command string as the link target whenever that string happened to end in the path. The decoded-relative spans were the second half of the defect: even with the line located they addressed the wrong characters, the PD-BUG-124 corruption class.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review approved 2026-08-19 via PF-TSK-005, reviewed jointly with PD-BUG-131 whose fix refactored the same two functions. No findings against this fix. Verification: full unit suite 1492 passed; coverage 93 percent across the three changed parser modules; 798 fuzzed spans (400 file plus 398 directory candidates over randomized escape mixes) with 0 mismatches, so the PD-BUG-124 corruption class does not reappear; mutating the index builder to an identity map reddens 8 tests, confirming the new assertions are load-bearing rather than vacuous. Reported case verified against the real file: .claude/settings.json line 17 now yields process-framework/ai-tasks.md. The validate under-report closes (links_checked 1 to 2 on an escaped and plain sibling pair) with no new false positives, since the newly registered escaped targets are gated by _should_check_target. The observability half is confirmed wider than claimed: path_value_line_not_located also fires for YAML single-quoted and folded scalars that were previously silent. Also verified the intersection nothing in the suite pins: a quoted directory inside an escaped literal parses, spans correctly and survives a real directory move with both JSON and YAML left valid. Two non-blocking observations recorded at review: YAML index building is quadratic per escaped literal (0.020s at 50 chars rising to 0.309s at 800 chars for a single scalar, JSON stays flat, the real repo file parses in 0.008s, so no exposure today), and the three return-None guards in _build_decoded_to_raw_index appear unreachable for both decoders rather than merely untested.

### PD-BUG-131

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link parsing - compound-string extraction
- **Repro**: 1. In a watched project create a config.json holding a compound value with an extensionless path, e.g. Bash([ -d /c/proj/doc/design ]). 2. Parse it with JsonParser. 3. One reference is produced whose link_target is the whole command string and whose type is JSON_DIR. 4. Move the directory: nothing is updated.
- **Expected**: The directory path inside the compound string is extracted and tracked like an embedded file path, so a directory move updates it.
- **Actual**: The whole command string is registered as a JSON_DIR target. It can never resolve, so the embedded directory is silently untracked.
- **Evidence**: Found during Bug Fixing (PF-TSK-007) of PD-BUG-130 on 2026-08-19, while verifying that fix against the real .claude/settings.local.json - lines 44 and 45 still produced whole-string targets after the fix. Measured as NOT escape-dependent, which is what separates it from PD-BUG-130: an escape-free compound value behaves identically. Bash([ -d /c/proj/doc/design ]) yields one JSON_DIR reference targeting the whole string, while the file-path control Bash([ -f /c/proj/doc/x.md ]) correctly yields /c/proj/doc/x.md. Pre-existing and unchanged by the PD-BUG-130 fix - that fix altered escaped-literal span mapping, not the extension requirement in _path_pattern. Distinct root cause, so filed separately rather than folded in. Same code shape in yaml_parser._extract_embedded_paths. Fixer note: extending _path_pattern to extensionless segments risks matching ordinary command words, so the fix likely needs a separator-anchored directory pattern plus the existing looks_like_directory_path check, and must keep the whole-string fall-through working for values like a filename with spaces.txt.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-19 via Bug Triage (PF-TSK-041). Reproduced against the real parsers escape-free, confirming the reported root cause: _path_pattern requires an extension, so a compound value whose embedded path is a directory yields no candidates, _extract_embedded_paths returns False, and whole-string classification registers the entire command as the target. Report confirmed but corrected on three points. (1) Type label imprecise: the whole string is not always a DIR type. Measured YAML value pwsh.exe -File doc/design -Recurse registers as LinkType.YAML (file) because the compound string passes looks_like_file_path on the .exe segment. The substantive claim (whole string as target, embedded directory untracked) holds in both shapes. (2) No --validate impact in either direction, unlike PD-BUG-128/129/130. Measured: the bogus whole-string target is skipped by _should_check_target so there is no false positive; and an extensionless missing directory is not reported today regardless, because _should_check_target requires a trailing separator for extensionless targets. WF-009 therefore NOT added. (3) Real population smaller than the sibling bug: scanned all 84 .json/.yaml files in this repo, 14 whole-string compound references but only 2 are this defect (.claude/settings.local.json lines 44 and 45), and both carry host-absolute /c/Users/... paths that likely would not update even once extraction works. The other 12 are prose containing a slash (Product Documentation - UI/UX Design; File/directory names to exclude) - a separate pre-existing whole-string-classification issue, not this bug. Priority Low HELD: impact Low to Medium x frequency Low, no corruption, no audit falsification, narrow shape (compound + extensionless + json/yaml only). Consistent with PD-BUG-130 at Medium, which is broader, falsifies --validate, and has 5 real values. Scope S: two parsers plus one shared pattern in base.py, the candidate-to-reference machinery is reused verbatim, and there is no raw-span arithmetic, so the PD-BUG-124 corruption class does not apply. Dims DI only - the reference is registered rather than silently dropped, so there is no warning-path gap of the kind that earned PD-BUG-130 its OB tag. Workflows WF-002 and WF-003 unchanged. Not a duplicate: direct precedent is PD-BUG-021 (same defect class, extension-required regex blocking directory detection, but in GenericParser and already fixed); distinct from PD-BUG-130 because this reproduces escape-free. Fix-scope warning for the fixer: the containment is the work, not the extraction. A separator-anchored directory pattern would start harvesting the 12 prose values above as directory candidates (UI/UX, .git/objects/, File/directory), so the fix needs looks_like_directory_path plus a rule that rejects prose, and must keep the whole-string fall-through for a value like a filename with spaces.txt. Effort: about one focused session.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-19
- **Fix**: Adopted the shared QUOTED_DIR_PATTERN in the two structured parsers, which is the mechanic the other five parsers already use for extensionless directories. New BaseParser._collect_path_candidates returns (start, end, text, is_dir) per candidate, collecting file candidates from _path_pattern as before and directory candidates from the shared quoted pattern, de-overlapping the two (a quoted path carrying an extension matches both) and ordering by position. All five record sites now choose JSON_DIR/YAML_DIR vs JSON/YAML from the is_dir flag: json plain and escaped, yaml plain, multiline block scalar and escaped. Symptom (b) is contained by _is_command_shaped, consulted from _classify_path (used only by the two structured parsers): a value carrying BOTH a space and a bracket or parenthesis is a command, not a path, so the whole command string is no longer registered as a target. A clean-path-token guard (_dir_token_pattern, the same character class the file pattern demands) was added after measurement showed the first cut harvesting the gap between two prose apostrophes.
- **Root Cause**: _extract_embedded_paths in both structured parsers reached only for _path_pattern, which requires a file extension. A compound value whose embedded path is a directory therefore produced zero candidates and returned False, and the caller fell through to whole-string classification, which registered the ENTIRE command string as the link target. The deeper cause is adoption rather than logic: QUOTED_DIR_PATTERN has existed in the shared patterns module and is used by the generic, markdown, python and powershell parsers, but json and yaml never adopted it, so they had no directory mechanic inside a compound value at all.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Fix**: Code-review round 2 (2026-08-19), three Minor findings folded in. Finding 1 - _is_command_shaped was keyed on a space plus ANY of ()[], which reads the same way to a filename: it silently dropped genuine paths such as assets/img (1).png, doc/report (final).pdf and doc/notes (draft), and a Windows (1) copy suffix is ordinary on this product declared platform, so the drop was the same silent-staleness class PD-BUG-130 exists to remove. Narrowed to command SYNTAX via _command_syntax_pattern - a parenthesis bound directly to a word (a call head like Bash(), or a bracket used as a standalone test operator ([ -d path ]) - keeping the space requirement as a cheap precondition. All three reported paths are classified again and the unquoted command is still contained; guard re-mutation-verified (disabling it reddens 4 tests). The reviewer sharpest point is recorded in the code: this guard is only REACHED when the command inner path is not extractable, because a quoted one is pulled out and _extract_embedded_paths returns handled before classification runs. Finding 3 - the QUOTED_DIR_PATTERN import sat in the wrong isort group and would have blocked commit; moved. Added 3 boundary controls (space plus parenthesis, bracketed filename tag, bare test-operator command) since both prior discriminating controls used space-ONLY values, which is exactly why Finding 1 passed the suite, plus the missing yaml prose-apostrophe test for symmetry with the json suite. One of my own new controls asserted behaviour the product never had (a bracketed filename is independently rejected by looks_like_regex_or_glob, PD-BUG-095) and was rewritten to assert the guard directly rather than pin unrelated pre-existing behaviour. Full suite 1510 pass; isort, black and flake8 clean on the touched files (two further files black would reformat are untouched drift from parallel sessions, left alone).
- **Tests Added**: Yes Verification: 20 regression tests across the two parsers (all bug tests confirmed failing pre-fix, for the right reason); full suite 1510 pass, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed. Manual validation PD-BUG-131_compound_directory_path_validation.py passes 5/5 through a real DirMovedEvent: the embedded quoted directory follows the move, the file stays valid JSON and the authored escaping is preserved. Real population verified - settings.local.json lines 44-45 now yield JSON_DIR references targeting the directory, and repo-wide bogus whole-string references fell 14 to 4 (the remaining 4 are the pre-existing prose whole-string classification, out of scope and noted at triage). ACCEPTED COST, approved at checkpoint: none remaining after the review round - the space-plus-parenthesis paths the first cut dropped are classified again. RESIDUAL, symptom (b) is contained only PARTIALLY: a bracket-free command such as pwsh.exe -File doc/design -Recurse still registers the ENTIRE string as a FILE reference in both parsers, because it carries no command syntax and its .exe segment passes looks_like_file_path. _is_command_shaped does NOT contain that shape. Closing it means touching looks_like_file_path, which all seven parsers share, so it was left out of an S-scope fix; measured residual exposure in this repo is 0 command-shaped values. The quoted-only contract is documented in the capabilities reference. DEPENDENCY for the reviewer: the real-population path rides _add_escaped_embedded_paths, which is PD-BUG-130 code. Two pre-existing defects found during this fix and filed rather than folded in: PD-BUG-133 (python parser shares the adjacent-quoted-literal defect, since fixed) and PD-BUG-134 (_find_unclaimed_line substring attribution leaves a value silently stale), the latter confirmed pre-existing by re-running the fixture with this fix emulated away.
- **Verification**: Code review approved 2026-08-20 via PF-TSK-005, a round-2 re-review scoped to the delta that folded in the three round-1 Minor findings, plus a seam check against the round-1 scope and the PD-BUG-130 machinery this fix rides. Finding 1 verified fixed: all three reported paths classify again, and reverting the pattern to the first cut reddens exactly the new boundary control. Finding 3 verified: isort, black and flake8 clean on the three touched parser files under project settings. The containment claim was verified in source rather than accepted - _classify_path has exactly two callers, both structured parsers, so the guard cannot reach the other five. Guard cost measured repo-wide at 0 dropped values across 85 json and yaml files and 2241 string values. The DI-critical intersection nothing in the suite pins was verified end-to-end by the reviewer in BOTH formats: an escaped compound value holding a quoted directory survives a real DirMovedEvent with the target rewritten, the authored doubled-backslash escaping preserved, and both documents still valid. New rewrite surface introduced repo-wide is 4 directory candidates inside compound values, none of which resolve on disk, so rewrite exposure today is 0. Mutation verification of the fix assertions, run site by site: disabling the guard reddens 4 tests, reverting it to the first cut 2, neutralising _dir_token_pattern 2 across both suites, removing directory candidates 4, forcing is_dir to False 2. Unit suite 1496 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed. Findings routed, none blocking: TD286 (Low, TST DI) for is_dir link-type selection being unpinned at 5 of the 9 record sites, mutation-verified individually, all five emitting the correct type today so coverage rather than behaviour; TD287 (Low, DI) for the documented bracket-free-command residual, which had no tracker row and would otherwise have survived only in this archived row. Stale test-specification method counts corrected in place - 9 to 12 for the json class and 8 to 9 for the yaml class, exactly the round-2 boundary controls. Two evidence-precision observations recorded but not routed: the accepted cost is a narrowed class rather than none, since a directory value carrying a bracket with adjacent whitespace such as reports/2024 [ Q1 ]/summary is still dropped (measured exposure 0); and the manual validation script prints plain_dir left stale in its own AFTER block while reporting 5 of 5, which is PD-BUG-134 and was confirmed pre-existing at HEAD, so that evidence reads stronger than it is.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-20

### PD-BUG-132

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Control Panel - validation result parsing
- **Repro**: 1. Validate a project once from the Control Panel so a report exists in that project log directory. 2. Introduce an invalid key into that project config, for example log_file_max_size_mb set to big. 3. Run validation again from the Control Panel. 4. Observe the panel showing the earlier broken-link count as the current result instead of surfacing the configuration error.
- **Expected**: A validation run that aborted on an invalid configuration is shown as failed, carrying the config_issue text that names the offending key, and never as a link-health verdict.
- **Actual**: The panel shows state broken with the count parsed from the earlier report and no error text, presenting a stale verdict as the current one.
- **Evidence**: Measured 2026-08-19 during Code Review (PF-TSK-005) of the PD-BUG-128 fix. Direct probe of parse_validation_result with returncode 1, empty stdout and a config_issue stderr: with no report in the log directory the result is state failed carrying the config error, which is correct; with a report file present holding Broken links 7, the same input yields state broken, broken_count 7, error None. Sites: _report_path conventional-path fallback and the returncode 1 branch of parse_validation_result in src/linkwatcher/linkwatcher_control_panel/validation.py. The mechanism is pre-existing and was reachable through other exit-1-without-report paths such as an invalid project root, but the PD-BUG-128 fix routes every invalid config key onto it.
- **Triage**: Validity confirmed by measurement at two levels. Unit: parse_validation_result with returncode 1, empty stdout and a config_issue stderr returns state failed carrying the error when no report is present (correct), and state broken with the stale count and error None when a report is present (defect); an exit-1 crash traceback behaves identically, and exit 2 with the same stale report correctly stays failed. End to end: a real --validate run with a good config wrote logs/linkwatcher/LinkWatcherBrokenLinks.txt and exited 1, then a second run with log_file_max_size_mb set to big exited 1, printed config_issue and wrote no report, leaving the first report on disk - and the panel log_dir is logs/linkwatcher, exactly where the validator writes, so the conventional fallback resolves every time. Description corrected in four ways. (1) The trigger class is broader than an invalid config: the --validate branch sits inside the main() outer try, so any exception exits 1 before write_report - invalid config, invalid project root, unreadable config file, or a crash during the scan itself; PD-BUG-128 widened exposure but did not create the class. (2) The stale report is the norm rather than an incident: every completed run writes the report to the panel own conventional path, so for any project validated even once the misrender is certain, and the correct failed rendering survives only for a project never validated. (3) The count-0 shape is a literal false-clean: measured through validation_display, an earlier clean run makes the panel render the result line as 0 broken links (error glyph, result kind) with the report-open affordance pointing at the stale file, which violates FDD edge case 7-1-1-EC-6 (surfaces the failure instead of silently reporting success) rather than merely showing a stale count. (4) An existing audited test, test_report_found_at_conventional_path_without_stdout_locator, pins the defective input-to-output pair; it will go red correctly during the fix and is not a regression. Priority Medium held: impact Medium x frequency Low, capped below High because the trigger needs an already-failing precondition, the panel config editor calls validate() on save, and the daemon refuses to start loudly on the same config; held at Medium rather than lowered because the count-0 case is a false-clean audit, the class already priced at Medium for PD-BUG-128 and PD-BUG-129. Scope S: one function in one file, roughly ten lines, with the test harness (make_project, write_report) already in place - about five new tests plus one re-pin, single session. Dims OB DA: OB because the config_issue diagnostic is discarded and a false verdict surfaced, DA because the fallback is documented as intended in the module AI Context block and in TDD PD-TDD-033 section 8 Q3 (checkpoint-approved 2026-08-10), so the design record must be amended with the code; AC considered and rejected - a single site, no diverging contract, unlike PD-BUG-128. Workflows narrowed to WF-010 and WF-009 dropped: WF-010 explicitly covers triggering link validation from the panel, while WF-009 is the direct-CLI audit whose tracked features are 0.1.1, 2.1.1 and 6.1.1 and which this defect never reaches; leaving WF-009 would falsely imply the CLI audit workflow needs E2E re-execution after the fix. Not a duplicate: PD-BUG-130 and PD-BUG-131 are parser defects, and PD-BUG-128 is closed and sits at a different site (main() wiring rather than panel result parsing). Effort about 1.5 to 2.5 hours. Fix-session note: the open decision is what evidence proves the run actually scanned - the stdout locator line, a report freshness check, or the Broken links summary line in stdout - and since the panel never passes --quiet the locator line is always printed on a completed run, so the conventional fallback original justification is thin; that decision reverses a checkpoint-approved TDD decision and needs its own checkpoint in Bug Fixing. Title corrected to name the mechanism (a run that never scanned) rather than the single reported trigger.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-19
- **Fix**: New _scan_completed(stdout) in validation.py gates the broken verdict on positive evidence that this run's scan finished - the Report written to locator or the Broken links : N summary, either of which --validate prints only after the scan completes. The conventional-path fallback is kept but demoted to a path resolver: it answers where the report is, never whether this run produced one. Second half, new _failure_reason(stderr, stdout): a failed state now reports the validator's own diagnosis - the config_issue payloads, else the error lines, else the raw text - instead of the first 300 characters of a stream that opens with startup logging. Without that half the run surfaced as a failure that still could not tell the operator which key was wrong, which is half of the reported Expected; it was measured on the manual validation script, not assumed, and approved at the Step 23 checkpoint as an addition to the approved approach. Measured after: an aborted run renders as Validation failed: log_file_max_size_mb must be a number (got str) where it previously rendered 1 broken link, and 0 broken links where the earlier run had been clean; a genuine broken-links run still renders its count, so the evidence rule suppresses nothing real. Alternatives rejected at the checkpoint: a report freshness check (mtime versus run start) needs a new parameter threaded through the controller and every test for a weaker guarantee, since Windows mtime granularity can pass a same-second stale report; deleting the fallback outright also strips the harmless exit-0 report link for no added safety. Sibling check: the other on-disk-artifact-as-truth sites in the panel, discovery.py status classification and lifecycle.py lock cleanup, already carry provenance checks - the lock file is explicitly diagnostic with the process table as truth - so no further instance of this class exists here. Docs: TDD PD-TDD-033 section 8 Q3 amended, test spec rows UNIT-V2, UNIT-V3 and the EC-6 traceability row updated, feature state file record added; FDD verified unchanged because the code now matches EC-6 as already written. E2E: Update-TestExecutionStatus.ps1 -WhatIf reports 0 entries, the WF-010 group being Not Created, so nothing was flagged and no cascade had to be weighed.
- **Root Cause**: _report_path answers where a report is; parse_validation_result consumed that answer as whether this run produced one. The conventional-path fallback carried no binding to the current run - no provenance, no freshness - and the validator writes exactly that path on every completed run, so a report is present for any project validated even once, while a run that exits 1 before scanning writes none. The fallback therefore supplied a stale artifact precisely when the run itself produced nothing, and the failure branch that would have surfaced the error was never reached. The trigger class is every exit-1-before-report path, because the --validate branch sits inside the main() outer try: invalid config, invalid project root, unreadable config file, or an exception raised during the scan itself. The stale count was the symptom; the missing evidence check was the defect. The design record shared it - the fallback was recorded as intended behavior in TDD PD-TDD-033 section 8 Q3 (checkpoint-approved 2026-08-10), in the module AI Context block, and in test spec row UNIT-V2, and an existing audited test pinned the defective input-to-output pair - which is why this fix is Documentation Alignment as well as Observability, and why code alone would have left the next reader with three records still describing the defect as the design.
- **Tests Added**: Yes - 6 tests in test_panel_validation.py (TE-TST-149), all written failing-first. Three for the verdict half (abort before scan with a stale report present; the count-0 false-clean asserted at the renderer; a crash-before-scan variant) went red on 'broken' == 'failed', the defect itself rather than a setup or import error; two for the reason half went red on the startup preamble dominating the message; one control stayed green throughout. All green after the fix, which is the mutation evidence - the pre-fix run turned exactly those five red and left the controls green. Assertions are negative where it matters - state != broken, broken_count is None, and 0 broken links not in the rendered text - because a positive-only assertion passes while the false-clean survives alongside it. One existing test, test_report_found_at_conventional_path_without_stdout_locator, pinned the defective input-to-output pair; it was re-pinned rather than deleted, its legitimate intent (a genuine broken run whose locator line is missing) surviving by supplying the summary line, so the original hedge keeps its coverage. Manual validation test test/bug-validation/PD-BUG-132_stale_validation_report_validation.py, 6/6, drives the real CLI twice per scenario and prints the pane line under the old and the fixed logic, including a control that a genuine broken-links run still reports its count. Full suite 1500 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed (baseline 1494 plus these 6); control-panel directory 322 passed; flake8 clean at the project pre-commit args. Test tracking synced for the touched file only, 15 to 21, via Validate-TestTracking.ps1 -Path -Fix. Note for review: test_panel_validation.py carries Audit Approved in test-tracking.md and this change adds 6 tests to it; whether that status still holds is a reviewer's call and was deliberately not taken here.
- **Verification**: Code review approved 2026-08-20 (PF-TSK-005). Scope: the PD-BUG-132 delta only - _scan_completed and _failure_reason in validation.py plus the two changed branches of parse_validation_result, the 6 new tests in test_panel_validation.py and the re-pinned test_report_found_at_conventional_path_without_stdout_locator, the TDD PD-TDD-033 section 8 Q3 amendment, test spec rows UNIT-V2 and UNIT-V3 and the EC-6 traceability row, and the feature state record. The parallel in-flight parser and config change sets stacked uncommitted in the same working tree were seam-checked rather than re-reviewed; the pyproject bump 2.1.3 to 2.2.0 belongs to another session and adds no dependency. Dims scoped to OB and DA, so the PE, AX and SE steps and the acceptance-criteria step are N/A. Verification, measured independently rather than read from the fix notes: full unit suite 1510 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed / 0 failed; control-panel directory 322 passed; flake8 clean at the project pre-commit args. End-to-end probe against the real CLI - an aborted run now renders Validation failed naming both offending keys where it previously rendered a stale count, and the control run with genuine broken links still renders its count, so the evidence rule suppresses nothing real. Mutation-verified by patching the fix out at runtime rather than editing the working tree: reverting the verdict half turns exactly 4 red, each on assert broken == failed which is the defect itself; reverting the reason half turns 2 red on the startup preamble dominating the message; union 5 distinct tests with the controls green, matching the fix notes claim. Manual validation script 6/6, including the old-logic false-clean demonstration. Sibling check independently confirmed - discovery.py states that the lock is diagnostic and the process table is truth, so the provenance pattern is genuinely present at that site. DA verified complete at all three sites the triage named: the module AI Context block, TDD section 8 Q3 amended with a dated entry rather than overwritten (which preserves the checkpoint-approved original decision), and test spec UNIT-V2 plus UNIT-V3 and EC-6; the FDD was correctly left unchanged because EC-6 as already written describes the fixed behavior. Three findings, none blocking. Minor 1 routed to TD288: the fix bound the verdict to run evidence but left _count_from_report reading the report file first, so when a scan completes and its fresh report is unavailable the stale file count wins over the count this run printed - measured, stdout carrying 2 against a stale report carrying 7 renders 7 broken links and links the stale file. Same class one step downstream, narrow trigger, and the state stays correctly broken, so it is a wrong count rather than a false verdict. Minor 2 routed to TD289: all 6 new tests drive the diagnosis through stderr while the console handler writes config_issue to stdout (logging.py line 475; measured - config_issue on stdout true, stderr empty), so they pass only because the stderr-or-stdout selection picks the non-empty stream, and no test pins the branch production takes. Suggestion recorded and not filed: _failure_reason selects one stream rather than searching both, so stderr noise would mask the stdout-borne diagnosis - attempted to trigger with PYTHONWARNINGS=always and PYTHONDEVMODE=1 and could not produce non-empty stderr, so it stays theoretical, and the precedence is pre-existing rather than introduced here. Test audit status deliberately not flipped: test_panel_validation.py carries Audit Approved and gained 6 tests, but that verdict is owned by PF-TSK-030, so it stays standing with the note that it predates this coverage, following the PD-BUG-123 precedent. Feature tracking not touched - feature 7.1.1 is implemented and held at Needs User Docs pending PF-IMP-2032, which this bug fix does not change. Positive: the fix removes the recurrence class rather than guarding the symptom, by demoting the conventional-path fallback to a path resolver; the second half naming the offending key is load-bearing rather than scope creep, because without it the failure surfaces undiagnosable; the assertions are negative, which is the correct shape for a false-clean defect; and the existing test that pinned the defective pair was re-pinned rather than deleted, so its legitimate intent keeps its coverage.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-20

### PD-BUG-133

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link parsing - python quoted-dir detection
- **Repro**: 1. Create a .py file containing a comment or docstring with two single-quoted examples separated by parenthesised prose, e.g. naming: (quote)a_test(quote) (Dart/Go), (quote)b_test(quote). 2. Parse it with PythonParser. 3. One PYTHON_QUOTED_DIR reference is produced whose target is the prose between the two quoted examples.
- **Expected**: No reference is produced: the span between two prose apostrophes is not a directory path.
- **Actual**: One PYTHON_QUOTED_DIR reference is produced with the prose fragment as its link target.
- **Evidence**: Found during Bug Fixing (PF-TSK-007) of PD-BUG-131 on 2026-08-19, at that tasks step-20 check for the same root cause in other components using the shared pattern. Four sibling parsers were probed with identical input: markdown, generic and powershell produced nothing; python produced the false positive. Pre-existing and untouched by the PD-BUG-131 fix, which changed only the structured parsers (base._collect_path_candidates plus the json and yaml record sites) and left every parser own quoted_dir_pattern attribute alone. Powershell escapes the class by using QUOTED_DIR_PATTERN_STRICT, which requires a character after the last separator; markdown and generic appear protected by their surrounding context rules rather than by the pattern. Impact is bounded: the bogus target can never resolve, so it is database noise rather than corruption, and validate skips extensionless targets without a trailing separator.
- **Triage**: Triaged inline 2026-08-19 during Bug Fixing (PF-TSK-007), human approved. Priority RAISED Low to Medium and the report corrected on two points, both by measurement against this repo. (1) Mechanism is broader than filed: QUOTED_DIR_PATTERN matches the span between two ADJACENT quoted literals, and the prose-apostrophe shape I filed is the minor case. The dominant real shape is the pathlib operator - Path(a) / (quote)b(quote) makes the pattern capture the literal string space-slash-space - 322 times in python and 384 in markdown. (2) Scale is large and not python-specific: excluding the build tree, bogus versus clean directory references measure generic 151/0 (100 percent bogus), markdown 1028/1817 (36 percent), powershell 287/176 (62 percent) and python 856/1026 (45 percent), so 2322 bogus against 3019 clean across the four parsers that use the shared pattern. Impact stays noise rather than corruption: a bogus target never resolves, and validate skips extensionless targets without a trailing separator, so no verdict is falsified in either direction. Priority Medium follows the matrix as low impact times high frequency. Scope S is for the PYTHON parser only, which is what this bug covers: the fix is the clean-path-token guard already built for PD-BUG-131 (_dir_token_pattern on BaseParser), applied in the python quoted-dir loop. Measured safe there - all 300 python bogus candidates that still look path-like were inspected and none is a legitimate reference; they are code and prose fragments, largely from test fixture strings. The guard must NOT be generalised to the other parsers: measured, it would delete legitimate references including tilde-slash-bin (10 occurrences in markdown, the documented LinkWatcher install directory), Program Files/Git in powershell, and dollar-WorkspacePath paths. A fleet-wide fix needs a different rule (for example requiring an alphanumeric segment on both sides of the separator) plus per-parser verification against each population, and is M scope; human chose option A, fix python now and file the fleet-wide gap separately so it is triaged on its own evidence. Not a duplicate of PD-BUG-131: same pattern, different parser and different trigger shape.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-19
- **Fix**: Added a clean-path-token guard to the quoted-directory loop in PythonParser, reusing BaseParser._dir_token_pattern built for PD-BUG-131: a candidate must match the same character class the file pattern already demands before the existing extension and looks_like_directory_path checks run. One guard, applied to the python parser only.
- **Root Cause**: QUOTED_DIR_PATTERN accepts any quote-delimited run containing a separator. Between two adjacent quoted literals on one line the effective delimiters are the CLOSING quote of the first and the OPENING quote of the second, so the connecting text is captured whenever it holds a slash or backslash. Dominant real shape is the pathlib operator: Path(a) / (quote)b(quote) yields the fragment consisting of close-paren space slash space.
- **Tests Added**: Yes Verification: 6 regression tests, the 3 bug tests confirmed failing pre-fix and the 3 controls (genuine quoted directory, trailing-separator directory, quoted file path) passing throughout; full suite 1510 pass. Repo measurement: python bogus directory references fell 856 to 0 while clean references were not reduced (1026 to 1032, the increase being the new test fixtures). One authoring defect caught during test writing: the pathlib test initially PASSED pre-fix because it asserted on the fragment space-slash-space when the parser actually captures close-paren-space-slash-space - the span runs from the CLOSING quote of one literal to the OPENING quote of the next - so it was tightened to assert no fragment at all and re-confirmed failing pre-fix. SCOPE, deliberate: held to the python parser because the same guard was measured to DELETE legitimate references in the siblings (tilde-slash-bin 10 times in markdown, the documented LinkWatcher install directory; Program Files/Git in PowerShell; dollar-WorkspacePath paths), so the remaining 1466 bogus references across markdown, generic and PowerShell are filed as PD-BUG-135 to be fixed on their own evidence with a different rule. Manual validation test skipped per the task criteria: no user-observable UI or filesystem symptom, since the bogus references never resolve.
- **Verification**: Code review APPROVED 2026-08-20 (PF-TSK-005), bug-fix review scoped to Dims DI. Change set: the 8-line guard in the python parser quoted-directory loop plus 6 regression tests; base.py, json and yaml belong to PD-BUG-131 and were seam-checked only. VERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY: full suite 1510 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed (matches the fix claim); coverage 92 percent on the changed module with both guard lines covered; flake8 clean under the real commit-gate arguments (max-line-length 120, extend-ignore E203), black and isort clean, the 3 bare-flake8 E203 hits being pre-existing and outside the change set; no dependency changes (only an unrelated version bump to 2.2.0). Tests mutation-verified by the reviewer WITHOUT editing source, by patching the pattern at runtime: the 3 bug tests are load-bearing (green to red when the guard is neutralised) and the 2 directory controls are discriminating (red when the guard is made maximally strict); the file-branch control is correctly unaffected. Repo measurement reproduced over 183 python files: 1472 pre-fix quoted-directory references, guard drops 918 and keeps 554. POSITIVE: the guard reuses BaseParser._dir_token_pattern rather than inventing a pattern, which aligns the quoted branch with this parser own docstring branch _BARE_DIR_RE on the same character class - the strongest justification for the fix, and not stated in the fix notes. Scope restraint confirmed correct: a boundary-shaped alternative was measured (no leading or trailing whitespace, no code punctuation, no whitespace before the first separator) and keeps 577 versus 554, of which only 2 of the 23 extra are path-shaped, so the shipped guard is strictly stronger and nothing it keeps does the alternative reject. THREE MINOR FINDINGS, none blocking, all follow-up rather than defects in this change. (1) UNDOCUMENTED ACCEPTED COST: the character class also drops quoted directories containing a space, a tilde, a dollar sign, or ANY non-ASCII character - doc/uebersicht written with an umlaut yields no reference at all. Measured 0 legitimate instances in this repo and the trade-off is favourable, but the fix notes name tilde-slash-bin, Program Files/Git and dollar-WorkspacePath only as reasons NOT to generalise to the siblings, never as a cost accepted INSIDE python, and non-ASCII is named nowhere. It is silent staleness with no validate warning, since validate skips extensionless targets without a trailing separator. No test pins it either way. Mitigating: the docstring branch already carried the same restriction and handles it worse, truncating doc/my design to doc/my. (2) VERIFICATION CLAIM OVERSTATED: bogus python directory references fell 856 to 0 holds for the adjacent-literal class but not unqualified - 67 of the 554 survivors (12 percent) are still bogus, being 55 escape fragments, 6 occurrences of N/A and 6 command switches such as /PID and /FI. Each sample was traced to source and every one is a SINGLE literal whose content merely holds a slash or backslash, a different pre-existing cause, so this is NOT a miss by this fix; the accurate phrasing is 0 of the adjacent-literal class. Per human decision at the review checkpoint this residual is routed to PD-BUG-135 triage rather than filed as a new bug. NOTE FOR THAT TRIAGE: PD-BUG-135 covers only the adjacent-literal class in the markdown, generic and PowerShell parsers, so the single-literal class in python is NOT covered by it and needs its own disposition. Recorded here because Update-BugStatus.ps1 requires a NewStatus whose ValidateSet has no NeedsTriage member, so PD-BUG-135 could not be annotated without falsely transitioning it out of Needs Triage. (3) The capabilities reference still advertises the python row as plain quoted directory paths with no mention of the clean-path-token requirement, while the sibling PD-BUG-131 fix documented its own narrowing in that same file. TWO SUGGESTIONS: the in-code comment names the captured fragment as two empty quote pairs around a slash when the real capture is close-paren space slash space, which the test file comment states correctly; and the _dir_token_pattern docstring in base.py is written wholly in PD-BUG-131 and structured-parser terms although it now has a second caller. TEST TRACKING: the test_python.py row count was bumped 26 to 32 but carries no note that the 6 new tests post-date the Audit Approved verdict, unlike the PD-BUG-118 batch which has one; routed to Test Audit (PF-TSK-030), which owns that column, rather than edited here.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-20

### PD-BUG-134

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link parsing - value line attribution
- **Repro**: 1. Create a config.json whose line 2 holds a compound value embedding a path in quotes, e.g. watched_dir set to Bash([ -d (quote)doc/technical/design(quote) ]), and whose line 3 holds that same path as a plain value, plain_dir set to doc/technical/design. 2. Run an initial scan and move doc/technical/design. 3. Line 2 is rewritten; line 3 still holds the old path.
- **Expected**: Each value is attributed to the line it actually occupies, so both lines follow the move.
- **Actual**: Both references are recorded against line 2. Line 2 is updated and line 3 is left stale with no warning.
- **Evidence**: Measured 2026-08-19 during Bug Fixing (PF-TSK-007) of PD-BUG-131, in that bug manual validation fixture. Both references parse as line 2 and after the move plain_dir on line 3 still reads doc/technical/design. Confirmed PRE-EXISTING and not introduced by the PD-BUG-131 fix: the same fixture was re-run with dir candidates and the command-shape guard emulated away (pre-fix behaviour) and the mis-attribution is identical in both runs. In isolation, with no earlier line containing the text, the same plain value updates correctly, which is what isolates the trigger to the substring match. Same helper as PD-BUG-013 (_find_unclaimed_line and its claimed set), different trigger: the claimed set does not prevent this because the value/line pair really is unclaimed. The yaml sibling _find_next_occurrence has the same substring shape and was not probed.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-20, human approved. Priority RAISED Medium to High: the only one of the four parser bugs that produces a silently stale reference, with no warning logged because a line IS found, just the wrong one (so the path_value_line_not_located warning added for PD-BUG-126 never fires). Impact High / Frequency Low per the priority matrix. Scope S: bounded to _find_unclaimed_line (json) and _find_next_occurrence (yaml); JSON can match on the quoted form it already uses for the column lookup, YAML needs a different rule because plain scalars are unquoted and that sibling was never probed - if the probe complicates, re-scope to M. Dims DI unchanged. Workflows corrected: WF-005 and WF-011 added, both being move workflows this bug breaks. Mechanism re-verified at triage: a fixture with a compound value on line 2 and the same path as a plain value on line 3 puts BOTH references on line 2. Not a duplicate of PD-BUG-013 (whole-string collision; its claimed set keys on text plus line and does not help when the pair genuinely is unclaimed).
- **Updated**: 2026-08-20
- **Fix**: JSON line lookup now matches the QUOTED form of the value, the delimiter the format guarantees for every string value and the one _find_decoded_literal has always used. YAML has no delimiter to key on, so _is_standalone_scalar requires the occurrence to be a whole value: introduced by key-colon, a sequence dash, a flow bracket or comma, or an opening quote, and ended by end-of-line, whitespace, a closing quote, a flow separator or a comment. The strict pass runs first and the plain scan is retained BEHIND it as a fallback, so tightening the lookup can only move a reference to a better line, never drop one. Block-scalar candidates opt out via whole_value=False - they are command fragments by construction. Confined to the two structured parsers: the other five derive line numbers from enumerate(lines), so the line is the unit of iteration and a value cannot be attributed elsewhere.
- **Root Cause**: Both structured parsers mapped a decoded value back to a raw line by plain substring search, never testing whether the occurrence IS the value or merely sits inside a larger literal. A value was therefore attributed to any earlier line containing it - a compound value embedding it, or a longer path it prefixes - and the line it actually occupies was never rewritten. Silent by construction: a line WAS found, so the path_value_line_not_located warning added for PD-BUG-126, which fires only when NO line is found, never triggered. The YAML sibling was defective too, contrary to the triage note that left it unprobed: its used_positions guard rejects a position another reference already took, which covers a verbatim repeat but not a span inside another value literal.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Fix**: Follow-up within the same session, driven by mutation verification: the block-scalar opt-out was found UNPINNED (mutating it reddened no test). It is load-bearing rather than redundant with the fallback - with the standalone rule applied to a block-scalar candidate, the strict pass skips the fragment on its own line and matches a LATER line where the same path is a whole value, so the candidate claims the standalone line and the standalone value is pushed onto the block scalar by the fallback: the two references swap lines. A control test was added pinning the block-scalar candidate to its own line, and it reddens under that mutation.
- **Fix**: VERIFICATION EVIDENCE (recorded under Fix because Update-BugStatus.ps1 writes VerificationNotes only on the Closed transition - it accepts the parameter on NeedsReview and discards it while reporting SUCCESS; filed as a framework IMP). 17 regression tests (7 json, 10 yaml); the 7 bug tests confirmed FAILING pre-fix for the right reason - each failure names the wrong line, not a setup error. Mutation-verified site by site: reverting the JSON quoted needle reddens 4 tests, the YAML standalone rule 3, removing the fallback 1 (the tagged-scalar control), removing the block-scalar opt-out 1. Full suite 1539 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed. Manual validation 5 of 5 through a real DirMovedEvent, including a check that the unquoted directory inside a compound scalar is NOT rewritten. Repo-wide measurement over all 85 json and yaml files: 527 references before and after, ZERO lost, 11 attributions corrected across 5 files - 6 in doc/PD-id-registry.json where directory values sat inside their longer sibling path, one in powershell-config.json where .Tests.ps1 was recorded inside a regex on a comment line, and a duplicate-span pair in sandbox-reset-registry.json which is the reported shape occurring in this repo. NOTE for the reviewer: two of the four reproduction shapes self-heal end to end, because a directory move runs two update rounds and the first removes the substring from the colliding line - the parse stays wrong for the database, for validate reporting, and for any single-round path, so the end-to-end evidence understates the defect. E2E: TE-E2E-005 and TE-E2E-006 annotated per test case; the by-feature call was deliberately NOT used - it cascades 31 entries across 7 features this fix cannot affect, and every entry already sits at Needs Re-execution.
- **Verification**: Code review approved 2026-08-20 via PF-TSK-005, first review round, scoped to the PD-BUG-134 delta only: the JSON quoted-needle lookup in _find_unclaimed_line, and the YAML standalone-scalar rule with its strict-then-fallback scan and block-scalar opt-out. The PD-BUG-126, PD-BUG-130 and PD-BUG-131 code sitting uncommitted in the same two files was seam-checked, not re-reviewed. Every claim in the fix note was re-verified rather than accepted. Mutation verification reproduces the claimed counts EXACTLY, site by site: reverting the JSON quoted needle reddens 4 tests, neutralising the YAML standalone rule 3, removing the plain-scan fallback 1 (the tagged-scalar control), removing the block-scalar opt-out 1 - and each mutation reddens the test that NAMES its behaviour, so the mapping is right rather than merely red. Repo-wide pre-fix versus shipped over all 85 json and yaml files: 527 references before and after, ZERO lost, 11 attributions corrected across 5 files, independently reproducing the fix note figure - 6 in PD-id-registry.json, one each in PF-id-registry.json, TE-id-registry.json and powershell-config.json, plus the duplicate-span pair in sandbox-reset-registry.json where two references to PF-id-registry-local.json both sat at line 31 column 70 pre-fix and the second now lands on line 33 (a line-only comparison sees 9; the pair is a column correction). End-to-end validation 5 of 5 through a real DirMovedEvent, and unlike the PD-BUG-131 script this one prints output consistent with its verdict: config.json line 3 plain_dir follows the move, which is the reported repro. Full suite 1546 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed. Coverage json 91 percent, yaml 94 percent, both above the 80 percent bar. black, isort and flake8 clean on the touched files under project settings. No new dependencies and no new imports. TDD 2.1.1 section 3.3 statelessness holds - _is_standalone_scalar is a classmethod and the delta adds no instance state. POSITIVE: the min(start_line, len(lines)) guard also removes a latent IndexError in the old wrap-around loop, which indexed past the end whenever the search offset exceeded the line count; the strict-then-fallback shape is conservative by construction, so tightening the lookup can only move a reference to a better line, never drop one, which the 527 to 527 measurement confirms; and the fixer probed the YAML sibling instead of inheriting the triage assumption that it was undefective. FINDINGS ROUTED, none blocking: TD290 (Low, DI) for three residual mis-attribution shapes the guard does not cover - a YAML hyphen or comma lead inside a plain scalar, and a key literal equal to the value in BOTH parsers, the last of which would rewrite a KEY on a move and change config semantics - all three measured PRE-EXISTING by emulating the pre-fix lookup, all three at ZERO exposure in this repo under a stricter guard, plus two unexercised branches of the new guard; TD291 (Low, TST) for a regression test named for the key-collision shape that actually pins a suffix collision, making the one still-failing shape read as covered. MEASURED AND DISMISSED, not routed: the new list-plus-list index allocation in the JSON scan costs 0.5 percent across 289 calls repo-wide, which is noise. OBSERVATION not routed: the test-tracking rows for these two files carry current method counts (54 json, 46 yaml, both verified) but stale Last Run cells from April 2026.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-21

### PD-BUG-135

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link parsing - shared quoted-dir pattern
- **Repro**: 1. In a .md file write a line containing two quoted literals separated by text holding a slash, for example a pathlib expression or two quoted naming examples separated by parenthesised prose. 2. Parse it with the matching parser. 3. A directory reference is produced whose target is the connecting text rather than a path.
- **Expected**: Only a real path token between the quotes is a directory reference; the gap between two separate literals is not.
- **Actual**: The gap is captured. Dominant shape is the pathlib operator: the fragment consisting of space slash space appears 384 times in markdown alone.
- **Evidence**: Measured 2026-08-19 during Bug Fixing (PF-TSK-007) of PD-BUG-133, at that tasks step-20 check across every parser using the shared pattern. Totals excluding the build tree: 2322 bogus against 3019 clean directory references, so roughly 43 percent of all quoted-directory references in this repo are fragments. Impact is noise rather than corruption - a bogus target never resolves, and validate skips extensionless targets without a trailing separator - but it scales with repo size. Filed separately rather than folded into PD-BUG-133 because the fix is NOT the same: the clean-path-token guard used for python was measured to delete legitimate references here, including tilde-slash-bin which appears 10 times in markdown and is the documented LinkWatcher install directory, Program Files/Git in PowerShell, and dollar-WorkspacePath paths. A safe fix needs a different rule, for example requiring an alphanumeric segment on both sides of the separator, verified against each parser own population. Human chose to fix python first and file this on its own evidence.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-20, human approved. Priority Medium unchanged: low impact but high frequency per the priority matrix - noise, not corruption. Scope RAISED to M: three parsers (markdown, generic, PowerShell via the STRICT variant), and the clean-path-token guard used for the python sibling PD-BUG-133 was measured to delete legitimate references here (tilde-slash-bin, Program Files/Git, dollar-WorkspacePath), so a new rule must be designed and re-measured against the population of each parser. State file recommended. Dims CORRECTED from DI to PE CQ: a bogus target never resolves and validate skips it, so nothing goes stale or corrupt; the cost is roughly 43 percent junk in the in-memory database, scaling with repo size, which is Performance and Scalability (Critical in the 2.1.1 Dimension Profile) plus Code Quality. Workflows NARROWED to WF-003: the junk is created at scan time and does not affect the move workflows. Mechanism re-verified at triage: markdown and generic both return the fragment space-slash-space as a directory target.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-20
- **Fix**: New shared guard BaseParser._is_paired_delimiter in parsers/base.py: the opening delimiter must really open (an even number of that same character precedes it on the line) and the closing delimiter must be the same character. Wired into 5 sites - markdown quoted-dir and backtick-dir, generic quoted-dir, PowerShell quoted-dir and the general quoted-string pass. Whole-repo effect, measured with the guard neutralised in-process so both halves see identical files: 44279 to 43581 references, 731 removed, 33 added. Removed by type: markdown-backtick-dir 606, markdown-quoted-dir 47, powershell-quoted 44, powershell-quoted-dir 24, powershell-block-comment 5 plus 3 dir, generic-quoted-dir 2. The clean-path-token guard used for the python sibling PD-BUG-133 was re-measured and rejected: it would delete 997 markdown references including tilde-slash-bin 11 times and 265 PowerShell references including Program Files/Git and every variable-bearing path.
- **Root Cause**: The directory patterns are delimiter-class regexes that require a separator INSIDE the span. When a legitimate literal holds no separator the regex fails at that start position and re-anchors on that literal closing delimiter, pairing it with the NEXT literal opening delimiter - so the text between two literals is captured whenever it holds a slash or backslash. Dominant shape is two backticked literals joined by a slash, which yielded the target space-slash-space 383 times in repo markdown alone. Second shape is a mismatched pair: a double-quoted PowerShell message containing an apostrophe closed the span on that apostrophe.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Fix**: VERIFICATION - 19 regression tests across the three parser suites, all confirmed failing pre-fix for the right reason (each names the captured fragment). Mutation-verified: neutralising the guard turns 11 of the 19 red while the 8 controls stay green, those controls asserting tilde-slash-bin, variable-bearing PowerShell paths and real quoted/backtick directories survive. Full suite 1546 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failures. Manual validation test/bug-validation/PD-BUG-135_delimiter_gap_directory_refs_validation.py scores 1/4 pre-fix and 4/4 post-fix through a real DirMovedEvent. THREE CORRECTIONS TO TRIAGE. (1) Dims restored to include DI. Triage removed DI reasoning that a bogus target never resolves so nothing goes stale; that covers only the noise half. Markdown suppresses a match falling inside an already-claimed span, so a bogus span STRADDLING a real backticked directory hid it: 6 real references were never tracked and would silently fail to follow a move, among them doc/founding/inputs/ at retrospective-documentation-creation.md line 383, plus logs/linkwatcher, services/, 1-customer-management/ and proposals/old/. (2) Workflows extended with WF-009. Measured by running validate from repo source before and after: broken links 1341 to 1344, because the 4 newly visible suppressed references are illustrative placeholders that genuinely do not exist, minus 1 dropped casualty. Small, but not zero as WF-003-only implied. An earlier analytic estimate of up to 469 fewer false positives was wrong: it ignored the validate context-aware skip rules, which were already skipping the fragments. (3) The report bogus-versus-clean split (2322 bogus, 43 percent) used the python clean-token guard as its oracle, which conflates gap-captures with prose inside a correctly paired literal. Measured by the actual mechanism the gap population is 683 across the three parsers. Generic holds 8 quoted-dir references in this repo, not 151; PowerShell holds 408 of which only 24 are gap-captures. ACCEPTED COSTS - 3 markdown references lost, all inside malformed nested backticks where markdown itself renders the text outside the code span; one of them, state-tracking/temporary/old/ at framework-extension-task.md line 268, therefore leaves the broken-link report. 27 markdown-bare-path prose targets become newly visible, pre-existing noise of a different class that the bogus spans were masking. 327 markdown and 157 PowerShell junk directory refs survive because they sit inside correctly paired spans; that is a separate defect, out of scope.
