STEP 20 - the same mechanism reaches the file patterns. The PowerShell general quoted-string pass was folded in (49 bogus removed, 0 casualties, real embedded paths verified surviving). Markdown and generic file patterns were measured and DECLINED: full parity there killed a legitimate match, and a parity-free variant was clean but netted only 2 fewer references out of 43580, not worth the extra parameter and the asymmetry. Residue is about 3 fragments.
- **Verification**: Code review APPROVED 2026-08-25 (PF-TSK-005, bug-fix mode). SCOPE: the shared guard BaseParser._is_paired_delimiter plus 5 wiring sites (markdown quoted-dir and backtick-dir, generic quoted-dir, PowerShell quoted-dir and the general quoted-string pass), 19 regression tests and the manual validation script; the other hunks in the same working-tree diff belong to the closed siblings PD-BUG-126/130/131. VERIFICATION RE-RUN INDEPENDENTLY: full suite 1546 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failures; coverage base.py 93 pct, powershell 94, markdown 90, generic 89; flake8 clean under the pre-commit gate (max-line-length 120, extend-ignore E203) and black clean; manual validation 4/4 through a real DirMovedEvent. WHOLE-REPO RE-MEASUREMENT with an independent oracle (2255 md/ps1/psm1/txt files): 620 references removed, 33 added; 611 of the 620 carry a space (the gap-capture shape), only 5 are clean space-free path tokens, and only 1 resolves on disk (../, trivial), so the accepted-costs record holds under a different method than the one the fix used. SAME-MECHANISM CHECK: every consumer of the shared directory patterns is accounted for - markdown, generic and PowerShell gated; python deliberately on its own clean-token guard; the YAML/JSON path through _collect_path_candidates already protected by _dir_token_pattern. PERFORMANCE FINDING WITHDRAWN: the per-match prefix count was expected to cost, PE being Critical for 2.1.1, but measured over the real corpus (13.7 MB) guard-on runs 7.42 s against 7.58 s guard-off (0.978x, net faster because rejected spans skip downstream work); a slice-free variant was not faster (1.019x). FINDINGS - Minor 1 (recorded, not filed): a prose apostrophe earlier on the line flips the parity and drops a genuine SINGLE-QUOTED reference, reproduced in all three parsers; measured exposure in this repo is about zero and the capabilities reference already discloses the unbalanced-line cost, so the trade stands - but the Notes characterization of the lost class as malformed nested backticks is true of this repo only, the general class being ordinary prose plus a single-quoted path, which is idiomatic in PowerShell. Minor 2 filed as TD292 (DA, Low): the handbook frames the guard as a directory rule for PowerShell while it also gates the general quoted-string pass, which classifies file paths. Suggestion: the defensive early return in _is_paired_delimiter is unreachable for all five current callers and therefore untested. No Critical or Major findings, no regressions.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-25

### PD-BUG-138

- **Source**: E2ETesting
- **Environment**: Testing
- **Component**: E2E test fixture TE-E2E-037 action.ps1
- **Repro**: Run the TE-E2E-037 action.ps1 with -WorkspacePath pointing at the seeded workspace. Wait several seconds. List project/sandbox-a/churn.
- **Expected**: A rotating set of churn markdown files is written every 400 ms for the requested duration, keeping the daemon logging so quiescence is never observed.
- **Actual**: The churn directory is created but stays empty. The recorded writer PID has already exited. The script still prints Churn writer started, so the operator believes churn is active.
- **Evidence**: Reproduced twice on 2026-08-25. Root cause: Start-Process pwsh.exe -ArgumentList joins array elements with spaces and no quoting, so the multi-line inner script is split across many arguments; pwsh gets a fragment, fails to parse and exits. WindowStyle Hidden suppresses the parse error, making the failure completely silent. Consequence in the live run at 14:22: drain completed normally in 3.22s and no forced stop was reported, so the run proves nothing about AC-9. Note this fixture carries audit status Audit Approved (TE-TAR-101), so the audit did not detect a silently no-op fixture.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-25 (PF-TSK-041), human approved. Priority RAISED Medium to High (Impact Medium x Frequency High): a silently PASSING acceptance test is worse than a failing one, and this fixture already carries Audit Approved (TE-TAR-101), so the false confidence is institutionalized rather than incidental. It gates honest AC-9 verification for feature 7.1.1, which sits at Needs User Docs in the release path, and WF-010 E2E is already Failing. Counter-argument recorded and overruled by the human partner: High nominally means within 24 hours and no user is affected by a broken test fixture. Scope S: single file, root cause fully known and confirmed by direct source inspection - action.ps1:104 passes a multi-line here-string as one element of Start-Process -ArgumentList, which space-joins elements without quoting, so pwsh receives fragments and fails to parse; -WindowStyle Hidden suppresses the parse error and the script prints Churn writer started unconditionally. Classification checked against the Issue Classification and Routing Guide (Step 1): the corrective fix lands in a project-authored E2E case fixture, not in framework-provided test infrastructure, so product bug is the correct tracker - no reclassification. Description accurate and precise as filed - no correction needed. Effort ~30min. Sequence: fix FIRST - unblocks honest AC-9 evidence for the other two.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-25
- **Verification**: S-scope quick path: human-approved at the Step 11 and Step 23 checkpoints. New regression file TE-TST-156 (3 tests) verified fail-first - 2 failed pre-fix for the right reasons (empty churn dir; success message on a no-churn run) and the boundary test passed; all 3 pass post-fix. The tests also caught a regression introduced by the first fix attempt (stream redirection killed detachment). Full suite: 1555 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, no regressions. Validate-TestTracking scoped run clean. Step 25 skipped by judgment: -WhatIf showed a 9-entry cascade across 5 features for a fix that changes no product code. Step 21 skipped: automated tests drive the real fixture. The AC-9 verdict still needs the manual TE-E2E-037 run under E2E Test Execution.
- **Fix**: action.ps1 now writes the churn payload to churn-writer.ps1 in the workspace root and launches it with Start-Process -File plus a quoted path, instead of an inline multi-line here-string via -Command. Added a parser pre-check of the generated payload, a try/catch inside the payload that writes churn-writer.err.log, and a verification gate that waits up to 5 s for a real churn file: if none appears the writer is killed, the error log is echoed, no PID file is left behind, and the script exits non-zero. Deliberately NO stream redirection on Start-Process: -RedirectStandard* switches it to UseShellExecute false and the writer then dies with its parent (measured: about 5 files then stopped), so ShellExecute detachment is a hard requirement. test-case.md step 4 now tells the operator to abandon the run on a non-zero exit, and a new first pass criterion makes churn-was-genuinely-active a precondition for every other criterion, so an inert run is inconclusive rather than passed. Files: TE-E2E-037 action.ps1 and test-case.md; test/automated/unit/7-operations-control/7-1-control-panel/test_pd_bug_138_e2e037_churn_writer.py (TE-TST-156, new).
- **Root Cause**: Start-Process -ArgumentList space-joins its elements without quoting. The multi-line payload arrived complete, but its own double quotes were consumed as argument delimiters and re-joined by the child, so the markdown link inside the churn body parsed as a type literal (Missing ] at end of attribute or type literal) and the writer exited 1 before its first write. -WindowStyle Hidden plus an unchecked exit code made that silent, and the script printed Churn writer started unconditionally. Correction to the filed root cause, established by measurement: pwsh does NOT receive a fragment - the whole payload arrives and quoting is what gets destroyed.
- **Tests Added**: Yes; Record note: Fix and Root Cause above were re-applied by hand 2026-08-25 because Update-BugStatus.ps1 -FastClose validates -FixDetails and -RootCause as mandatory and then discards them - its notes switch writes those two fields only under the NeedsReview branch, which the FastClose chain skips (NeedsFix to InProgress to Closed). The script reported SUCCESS.

### PD-BUG-137

- **Source**: E2ETesting
- **Environment**: Testing
- **Component**: Control Panel daemon lifecycle
- **Repro**: Open the Control Panel against the TE-E2E-036 sandbox registry. Start sandbox-a from the panel. Close the panel window so the shutdown drain stops the daemon. Inspect project/sandbox-a/.linkwatcher.lock.
- **Expected**: The lock file is removed when the daemon is stopped, as it is on the explicit per-row stop path where the panel logged lock_removed=True.
- **Actual**: The lock file survived, still containing PID 31272 for a dead process. A stale lock can mislead later PID-based checks.
- **Evidence**: Panel log 14:10:25.966: daemon_stopped project=SBX-001 pids=(15308, 31272) lock_removed=False. Contrast with the earlier explicit stop at 13:50:16.655: daemon_stopped project=SBX-001 pids=(15168, 21048) lock_removed=True, which did remove it. Master test pass criteria require every .linkwatcher.lock to be removed after each phase.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-25 (PF-TSK-041), human approved. Priority Medium confirmed (Impact Low x Frequency High): both consumers already defend themselves - acquire_lock reclaims a dead-PID lock automatically (main.py:318) and the panel surfaces STALE_LOCK - so this misleads rather than breaks; frequency is high because every panel close on that path hits it. Scope S: cleanup_lock (lifecycle.py:397) is ~15 lines with three silent False exits (exited False, owner_pid not in terminated, or a swallowed unlink OSError). The logged PID 31272 IS in the terminated tuple and the 3.36s stop timing rules out an exit-wait timeout, so a swallowed unlink OSError is the leading candidate; the fix should also surface which branch fired rather than returning a bare False. Related but NOT a duplicate of closed PD-BUG-121: same stale-lock hazard class, opposite side - PD-BUG-121 hardened the consumer of stale locks, this is the producer leaving one behind. Not a duplicate of PD-BUG-136 (same shutdown pass, distinct function). Description accurate as filed. Effort ~1h. Sequence: verify alongside PD-BUG-136 - same shutdown path, shared E2E run.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-25
- **Fix**: cleanup_lock backs off around the unlink (0.05/0.1/0.2/0.3/0.4 s, six attempts, about 1.05 s total) instead of making a single attempt; FileNotFoundError counts as success because the desired end state was reached. A lock that is ours and still undeletable now emits lock_cleanup_failed naming the path, owner PID, attempt count and OS error, while the two benign non-removals (no lock, foreign or corrupt lock) stay silent so a normal close does not warn every time. The bool return contract is unchanged so UNIT-L10 and UNIT-L11 are untouched; an injectable sleep keeps the new tests instant.
- **Root Cause**: Windows cannot unlink a file that another handle holds open without FILE_SHARE_DELETE, so the single unlink attempt lost to a concurrent holder, and the swallowed OSError collapsed into a bare False indistinguishable from the four other conditions that also return False. The panel races itself: the poller reads every project lock each cycle and keeps polling throughout shutdown. Measured 208 of 300 unlink failures with PermissionError WinError 32 under a concurrent reader. The poller is a confirmed holder but not necessarily the holder in the filed incident, since it reads for well under a millisecond every 2.5 s; an antivirus scan of the files of a just-terminated process fits a recurring failure better. The backoff addresses the whole class and the new warning names the actual holder next time.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review approved 2026-08-25 (PF-TSK-005), human approved close. Scope: the cleanup_lock retry plus its UNIT-L17 tests, isolated from concurrent PD-BUG-136 edits in the same file - the cleanup_lock content hash was verified unchanged across that parallel session. Evidence: full unit suite 1557 passed 0 failed; 10/10 lock tests; flake8 at the project max-line-length and isort clean (the black drift is pre-existing at HEAD); lifecycle.py coverage 88 percent. The recorded measurement was reproduced independently rather than accepted: the bundled probe under a conservative periodic reader left 5 of 150 stale locks on the pre-fix path and 0 of 150 on the fixed path, and hold mode confirmed lock_cleanup_failed naming path, owner PID, attempt count and OS error. Worst-case stop timing about 31 s still fits the 35 s watchdog. NOT verified end to end: TE-E2E-036 remains Needs Re-execution, so the incident that filed this bug has not been re-run; closure rests on unit plus probe evidence for the mechanism class, human approved at the review checkpoint. Three findings routed as implementation gaps to the feature 7.1.1 state file section 8 rather than blocking closure: (A) Minor DI - the retry widens the ownership-check-to-unlink window from microseconds to about 1.05 s, so an externally started daemon that reclaims the dead lock inside that window can have its live lock deleted; remedy is to re-read the owner PID per attempt; (B) Minor - the new FileNotFoundError branch at lifecycle.py:576 has no test coverage and is exactly the branch finding A races into; (D) Suggestion - drain_and_terminate does not forward sleep to cleanup_lock, contradicting its own every-collaborator-injected docstring.

### PD-BUG-136

- **Source**: E2ETesting
- **Environment**: Testing
- **Component**: Control Panel shutdown orchestration
- **Repro**: Open the Control Panel against the TE-E2E-036 sandbox registry. Start sandbox-a from the panel and let it settle. Move notes/setup.md to archive/setup.md via the case action.ps1. Close the panel window within 1-2 seconds.
- **Expected**: The drain lets the pending reference update complete, leaving docs/guide.md reading [Setup Notes](../archive/setup.md).
- **Actual**: Daemon terminated 3.36s into the drain with no file events logged. docs/guide.md still reads [Setup Notes](../notes/setup.md). File intact and uncorrupted, so the no-corruption clause of AC-8 holds and only the completion clause failed.
- **Evidence**: Root cause candidate: the drain treats a daemon as quiescent when it has no active work, but a daemon holding a buffered DELETE awaiting delete-create correlation is indistinguishable from an idle one. Competing reading for triage: closing 1-2 seconds after the move guarantees the correlation window has not elapsed, so no write is ever genuinely in flight and the test timing may be at fault. Triage must decide whether quiescence needs redefining or the test needs a wait past move_detect_delay. Panel log: panel_closing 14:10:22.603, daemon_stopped 14:10:25.966. Daemon log ends at monitoring_started 14:09:47 with zero file events.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-25 (PF-TSK-041), human approved. Priority High confirmed (Impact High x Frequency Medium): the BR-3 drain guarantee fails and a reference update is silently dropped, but files stay intact so the BR-7 no-corruption clause holds - not Critical. Scope M: the panel has no existing seam for the daemon correlation window (zero move_detect_delay references anywhere in linkwatcher_control_panel/), so the fix must add per-project config consumption plus unit tests plus an E2E re-run, and it carries a design choice (panel-side threshold derivation vs daemon-side pending-work signal; the FDD deferred the cooperative stop-sentinel as out of MVP scope, which points at panel-side). ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED and the competing reading REFUTED: watch_quiescence judges quiescence solely by the log (path,size,mtime) signature (lifecycle.py:308), and log_idle_threshold_seconds=3.0 (settings.py:69) is less than move_detect_delay=10s, so a buffered move-correlation can NEVER survive a drain begun inside the correlation window - deterministic, not test timing. FDD 7-1-1-BR-3 names buffered move-correlations explicitly as work the drain must let finish. EVIDENCE CORRECTION: the filed claim of zero file events is contradicted by file_deleted logging at INFO (logging.py:561) - the DELETE half was logged, and a stale or rotated log was most likely read; the observed 3.36s from panel_closing to daemon_stopped matches idle-threshold plus exit-wait. Not a duplicate of PD-BUG-137 (same shutdown pass, distinct function). Effort ~1 session (3-4h). Sequence: fix after PD-BUG-138; TE-E2E-036 is the regression oracle.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-25
- **Fix**: watch_quiescence takes a correlation_window and raises the required log-silence span to the project move_detect_delay, read from ProjectInfo.config_path by the new read_correlation_window (any unreadable or invalid config falls back to the daemon default of 10 s, never to 0). The widening applies only when the log was written inside that window, since a buffered delete expires exactly move_detect_delay after the file_deleted record that created it, so an already-idle daemon still stops in about 3 s. The span is clamped to grace_period minus 2 s and the clamp is logged, so BR-7 still bounds the drain. Read at drain_and_terminate, so the per-row stop and the close-time fan-out both inherit it. TE-E2E-036 action.ps1 and test-case.md amended from Move-Item to a delete plus 6 s delayed create: a rename is a native move event handled in about 200 ms and can never be in flight.
- **Root Cause**: watch_quiescence judged quiescence solely from the newest log (path, size, mtime) signature. A daemon holding a buffered delete awaiting move correlation writes nothing while it waits, so it is byte-for-byte indistinguishable from an idle daemon. log_idle_threshold_seconds 3.0 sits below move_detect_delay 10.0, so a drain begun inside the correlation window deterministically terminated the daemon first and the pending reference update was dropped. FDD 7-1-1-BR-3 names buffered move-correlations explicitly as work the drain must let finish.
- **Tests Added**: Yes; CORRECTION 2026-08-25: the status flip to In Progress at ~16:10 was the Code Review REJECTING this fix, not a concurrent write. The Bug Fixing session misread it and re-applied Needs Review over the reviewer verdict; the note previously here claiming a concurrent write was wrong and is withdrawn. The review findings F1-F4 and the responses to them are in the feature 7.1.1 state file section 10 (Update-BugStatus.ps1 cannot carry them on an InProgress transition - PF-IMP-2160)
- **Fix**: CODE REVIEW RESPONSE (all four findings addressed; details in feature 7.1.1 state file section 10). F1 - watch_quiescence now re-arms required_idle with log_age 0 on every observed log change, so a correlation opened DURING the drain is covered, not only one already open at entry; the reviewer probe was reproduced first at exactly 3.50 s against a correlation open to 10.50 s. This is the design call the reviewer flagged and it is user-visible: a clean stop now needs activity to have ceased by about grace minus window (10 s) instead of grace minus idle (17 s), so a daemon logging more often than move_detect_delay now ends as FORCE_STOPPED where it drained cleanly before - taken deliberately, since such a daemon is still working and the previous behaviour was a false clean. INT-5 at-boundary project re-anchored from 15 s to 8 s with the rationale in the test. F2 - log_level WARNING or ERROR disables the protection (file_deleted is INFO); recorded as a residual in TDD section 4.4 beside the PD-BUG-140 directory-move residual and in the Control Panel explanation handbook. F3 - correlation_window is now a required keyword on watch_quiescence, removing the opposite-default asymmetry; the four base-mechanics tests state 0.0 explicitly. F4 - wall_clock threaded through LifecycleController into the stop worker plus a controller-level test with a project-configured 12 s window, mutation-verified red at the 3 s fast path when the threading is removed.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code review approved with comments 2026-08-26 (PF-TSK-005), repeat review of the F1-F4 fix delta against the DI ID dimensions. All four prior findings confirmed addressed. F1 verified END TO END by the reviewer, not only at unit level: the real-daemon harness was run in mid-drain mode against a repo-source daemon with the log deliberately aged 24 s at entry, the condition that forces the fast path and makes the re-arm the only mechanism that can save the update. Drain held 16.8 s, the correlation fired, guide.md was rewritten to ../archive/setup.md, termination was clean with forced=False, file intact at 79 bytes. Pre-F1 that path resolves at about 3 s and kills the daemon before the t+6 s create, so the 16.8 s measurement is itself the discriminating condition. F2 residual confirmed in TDD 4.4 and the explanation handbook; F3 confirmed as a required keyword in source; F4 confirmed wired through LifecycleController._stop_worker, the only drain_and_terminate call site. Suites: panel 345 passed; full unit 1568 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed, with 2 failures traced outside this change set (in-flight service.py and settings.py edits); flake8 clean across the change set; lifecycle.py coverage 88 percent with every new PD-BUG-136 line covered. Four Minor findings routed, none Critical or Major: PD-BUG-142 (panel reads move_detect_delay only from the config file and misses the LINKWATCHER_MOVE_DETECT_DELAY environment override, reopening the same silent loss on a narrow path), TD293 (clamp warning now emitted per re-arm instead of once per drain), TD294 (panel default constant duplicates the daemon default with no drift guard), TD295 (validation harness can pass vacuously in mid-drain mode because its documented 5 s settle is below the 10 s window). Two Suggestions and the full review record are in the feature 7.1.1 state file section 10. Feature Tracking N/A: 7.1.1 is held at Needs User Docs for PF-IMP-2032, so Update-CodeReviewState.ps1 was deliberately not run. Sequencing: TE-E2E-036, the triage-named regression oracle, is still at Needs Re-execution, so this bug closes ahead of its E2E oracle and the reviewer real-daemon run substitutes at the mechanism level.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-26

### PD-BUG-139

- **Source**: E2ETesting
- **Environment**: Testing
- **Component**: LinkWatcher daemon file observation
- **Repro**: Not reproducible so far. Three attempts on 2026-08-25 against the identical TE-E2E-036 workspace. Attempt 1: minimal sandbox started with python main.py, move detected in 75 ms. Attempt 2: the same E2E workspace started through the project launcher start_linkwatcher_background.ps1 and the real action script, move detected in 191 ms. Attempt 3: delete plus delayed create, correlation resolved correctly.
- **Expected**: A running daemon logs file_deleted or file_moved and applies reference updates for any move inside its watched tree.
- **Actual**: The daemon log and stdout both end at monitoring_started 14:09:47 and never grow again. The panel polled the daemon as running throughout and terminated the process pair at 14:10:25. A move performed at about 14:10:21 produced no log record and no reference update.
- **Evidence**: Workspace preserved at C:/tmp/pd139-evidence/TE-E2E-036-close-panel-drains-in-flight-move (copied out of the volatile session scratchpad on 2026-08-25; the live E2E workspace has since been reused and no longer holds this state). LinkWatcherLog.txt is 7915 bytes with mtime 14:09:47.381 and holds 12 startup records only. LinkWatcherStdout.txt is 4464 bytes and also ends at monitoring_started. Exactly one log file exists, so rotation is ruled out, and the daemon flushes its JSON log per record as confirmed by reading a live daemon log during reproduction. Split out of PD-BUG-136 on 2026-08-25 after the drain mechanism filed there was shown to be a separate and independent defect that cannot explain a total absence of events.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-25 (PF-TSK-041), human approved. Validity confirmed against the preserved evidence: the move did execute (notes/setup.md to archive/setup.md, notes/ left empty) and docs/guide.md was left stale, so this is not a no-op fixture like PD-BUG-138. Description corrected: the observer was NOT inert throughout - replaying_deferred_events count=2 proves the watch was armed and delivering at 14:09:47, after which it delivered nothing for 34 s. The observer thread stayed alive: service.py line 164 polls observer.is_alive() every second and logs observer_thread_died at ERROR, and no such record exists. Event deferral ruled out: _scan_complete is a threading.Event set before replay and never re-cleared. Leading mechanism is therefore alive-but-not-delivering. Cross-reference PD-BUG-018 (Watchdog observer thread dies silently, closed 2026-02-26): same class, different mechanism - that fix detects a dead thread, not a live thread that stopped delivering; not reopened per the Reopen Workflow (different root cause, closed long ago). Priority High per matrix: impact High (core function silently absent, no user signal, updates lost), frequency Low (1 of at least 4 runs). Scope L: root cause unproven and unreproducible in 3 attempts; realistic fix is a new delivery-level liveness mechanism, so a Bug Fix State File is required. Dims add ID: we verify the watchdog dependency thread liveness but never its event delivery. Workflows widened from WF-010 alone: a blind daemon breaks every move-driven workflow. Effort: 2-4 sessions, investigation-led.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-25
- **Fix**: Added WatchLivenessMonitor (src/linkwatcher/liveness.py): emitter-liveness check plus a silence-gated end-to-end delivery canary, polled from LinkWatcherService._watch_is_healthy(); on confirmed failure the daemon logs ERROR and stops instead of running blind. Delivery is observed in handler.dispatch() above the own-output/ignored filters; the sentinel is consumed there and never parsed. Four watch_liveness_* config settings added. See state file doc/state-tracking/temporary/bug-fix-state-pd-bug-139.md.
- **Root Cause**: service.py polled observer.is_alive() only, which reports the watchdog dispatcher thread; the per-watch emitter that actually produces events can be lost silently, leaving the daemon permanently blind while reporting healthy. Gap left by the PD-BUG-018 fix.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code Review 2026-08-26 (PF-TSK-005): REJECTED, routed back to Bug Fixing. Review scoped to the PD-BUG-139 delta only (liveness.py new; service.py, handler.py, config/settings.py, configuration-guide.md; test_watchliveness.py new) and isolated from the parallel PD-BUG-129/136/138/140 edits in the same working tree. Verification clean: flake8, black and isort clean on the change set (the E203 at handler.py line 824 is pre-existing and untouched), regression suite 15/15, full automated suite 1589 passed 3 skipped 3 xfailed 0 failed. Dims scoped to DI OB ID, so Steps 16-19 (performance, accessibility, security, acceptance criteria) are N-A. MAJOR (blocking) - _liveness_sentinel_path at service.py line 186 iterates the return of compute_own_output_exclusions, which is a dict with keys dirs and file_stems rather than a list of directories, so the loop walks the two key strings, Path(dirs).is_dir() is False for both, and EVERY deployment falls through to the project-root fallback. Measured directly. Three consequences: the documented defense-in-depth (the probe cannot pollute the link database even if the dispatch drop were ever bypassed) is inoperative everywhere and is dead code; every watched project gets an unmanaged .linkwatcher-liveness-probe dotfile in its ROOT, rewritten every watch_liveness_idle_seconds while the tree is quiet and never removed on stop, untracked and absent from .gitignore, so in this repo it is exposed to Mode B remainder-sweep commits; and a root-level file whose mtime changes every 60 s is perpetual churn for anything else watching the tree such as OneDrive or Dropbox sync or a second watcher. Fix: iterate own_output dirs, gitignore the sentinel or unlink it in stop(), and add unit coverage for both branches. MINOR 1 (same session) - _liveness_sentinel_path is the only unit in the change set with no test, which is exactly why 15/15 passed while it was broken. MINOR 2 (same session) - both observer_emitter_died and watch_delivery_stalled tell the user to restart, which restores monitoring but does not repair references already left stale during the blind window; neither message nor the new capabilities-reference section points at --validate as the repair step, leaving open the DI gap the state file itself named (make the gap explicit where restoration is impossible). MINOR 3 (same session) - watch_liveness_failure_threshold of 1 passes schema validation, which only rejects values at or below zero, yet the WatchLivenessMonitor docstring says it must be greater than 1 and FDD 1.1.1-EC-11 states as feature behavior that a single missed probe never stops a daemon; either enforce a minimum of 2 or scope the claim to the defaults. SUGGESTION 1 (defer) - a permanently unwritable sentinel silently disables the canary while reporting healthy, because _probe_round_trips correctly returns True on OSError but the caller then resets consecutive_failures to zero; with the log outside the watched tree nothing refreshes the idle clock and watch_liveness_probe_unwritable repeats at 1 Hz. SUGGESTION 2 (defer) - _signal_handler only sets running False, so a signal arriving mid-probe still waits out watch_liveness_probe_timeout of up to 10 s; both real stop paths hard-kill (Stop-Process -Force and psutil terminate), so this affects only a foreground python main.py run. POSITIVE - the investigation is the strongest part of the work: three candidate triggers discriminated against the preserved evidence, two ruled out by named fingerprints, the survivor honestly labelled unfalsifiable, and the fix then designed at the outcome level rather than against a guessed cause. Delivery is recorded in handler.dispatch above the own-output and ignored-directory filters, which is the correct seam, and the sentinel is consumed there so it can never reach the parser. Silence-gated probing keeps writes at zero on active repositories. Injected clock, sleep and write_sentinel make the monitor testable without real timing. The session also found and fixed a latent logger.error TypeError that would have crashed the pre-existing PD-BUG-018 observer_thread_died path, then swept the repository for other occurrences. Route: Bug Fixing session 2 fixes MAJOR plus MINOR 1-3 and exits at Needs Review; the re-review is scoped to that delta per the repeat-review rule. Route B (approve now and split the sentinel-path defect into its own S-scope quick-path bug) was presented and the human partner chose Route A.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-26
- **Fix**: Session 2 (2026-08-27) addressed the Code Review rejection: the sentinel now resolves to the daemon own-output directory (the resolver was iterating the exclusion registry dict rather than its dirs set, so it fell through to the project root in every deployment), the path is pinned for the daemon lifetime, cleanup on stop sits outside the running guard, both ERROR messages direct the operator to a --validate repair scan, and watch_liveness_failure_threshold is floored at 2. Regression suite 15 to 25 cases. Detail in the state file doc/state-tracking/temporary/bug-fix-state-pd-bug-139.md.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Session 2: regression 25/25 (5 confirmed failing first), full suite 1610 passed 0 failed, flake8 black isort clean, manual validation exit 0 including a new sentinel-placement half, and a real python main.py daemon run confirming the probe lands in logs/linkwatcher with the project root left clean. Registry-misuse sweep found the defect was a single occurrence. Filed PD-BUG-144 for a pre-existing stop() guard defect found while verifying: stop() skips its whole body on every self-stop and signal path, so Observability is improved but not complete until that closes. E2E re-execution not flagged (human approved): no move-handling behavior changed, and the covering case cannot reproduce the blind-watch condition on demand; it already carries Needs Re-execution.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-27
- **Verification**: Code Review 2026-08-27 (PF-TSK-005): APPROVED. Re-review scoped to the Session 2 delta (service.py sentinel resolution, pinning and stop cleanup; liveness.py repair guidance; settings.py threshold floor; test_watchliveness.py 15 to 25 tests) and isolated from the parallel PD-BUG-123/127/136/140 edits in the same working tree. Verification clean: flake8 with the project args (max-line-length 120, extend-ignore E203), black and isort clean on the change set; regression suite 25/25; full automated suite 1629 passed 3 skipped 3 xfailed 0 failed. Dims scoped to DI OB ID, so Steps 16-19 are N-A. MAJOR from the 2026-08-26 review is fixed and verified beyond the unit tests: direct resolution against a real project lands the sentinel in the own-output directory and pins it on repeat call, and a real-service run of 12 seconds across several idle windows round-tripped the canary from that new location with consecutive_failures 0, the sentinel absent from the link database, the probe removed on stop and the project root clean. That end-to-end path had never been exercised with a real observer, because the Session 1 verification ran against the root fallback the defect forced. MINOR 1 (coverage) was mutation-verified rather than counted: reinstating the exact defect turns exactly one test red (test_sentinel_lands_in_the_own_output_directory), and moving cleanup back inside the running guard turns exactly test_sentinel_is_removed_even_when_running_was_already_cleared red. MINOR 2 (repair guidance) is present in both ERROR details and propagated to FDD EC-11, the TDD and the capabilities reference. MINOR 3 (threshold floor) measured: 1 and 0 rejected with must be at least 2, 2 accepted, 2.5 rejected as not a whole number, and main.py line 203 makes it fatal at startup, so the cannot-be-configured-away claim in FDD EC-11 holds. Two new non-blocking MINOR findings were filed together as PD-BUG-145 (Low, S-scope) rather than triggering a third rejection cycle: the sentinel cleanup runs only in stop(), which neither operator stop path reaches (Stop-Process -Force in stop_linkwatcher.ps1, psutil terminate in the Control Panel), so the probe outlives every normal stop and in fallback deployments is left as an untracked project-root dotfile; and the _remove_liveness_sentinel docstring plus capabilities reference line 190 both describe cleanup behavior the code no longer provides. A deferred SUGGESTION is recorded there as well: that method catches only OSError while its docstring promises a failure must never obstruct shutdown. POSITIVE: Session 2 found and fixed a defect the review did not name (the sentinel path resolving differently at write time and cleanup time, now pinned for the daemon lifetime), swept both other compute_own_output_exclusions call sites instead of fixing only the reported instance, corrected two harness defects that were manufacturing false confidence, and declined to absorb the stop() guard defect, filing PD-BUG-144 and deliberately placing the new cleanup outside that guard with a test pinning it. Five new tests were confirmed failing first.

### PD-BUG-142

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Control Panel shutdown orchestration
- **Repro**: Set LINKWATCHER_MOVE_DETECT_DELAY=30 in the environment. Start a daemon for a project whose linkwatcher-config.yaml omits move_detect_delay or sets it to the 10 second default. Delete a referenced file, close the Control Panel window within a second or two, then re-create the file at a new path 15 seconds later.
- **Expected**: The drain honours the effective move_detect_delay the daemon is actually running with (30 s), holds until the correlation resolves, and the reference update completes before termination.
- **Actual**: read_correlation_window in lifecycle.py reads only the YAML file, so the panel uses 10 seconds, reaches quiescence, and terminates the daemon while the correlation is still open. The pending reference update is silently dropped. Files stay intact, so the BR-7 no-corruption clause holds and only the BR-3 completion clause fails.
- **Evidence**: Found during Code Review (PF-TSK-005) of the PD-BUG-136 fix on 2026-08-26. main.py line 73 merges LinkWatcherConfig.from_env() over the file config, and from_env derives LINKWATCHER_MOVE_DETECT_DELAY from the dataclass field automatically, so the override is a live surface. lifecycle.py read_correlation_window reads the YAML file only. Same defect class as the accepted F2 residual (log_level WARNING or ERROR disables the protection), which was documented rather than fixed. Severity counterargument for triage: the trigger requires a deliberate non-default environment variable so frequency is Low, while the impact is the same silent loss as PD-BUG-136. Cheap root-cause fix available: os is already imported in lifecycle.py, so honouring the environment override is a few lines, which may be preferable to recording a second residual.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-27 (PF-TSK-041), human approved as recommended. Priority Medium HELD, scope M, Dims DI ID held, related feature 7.1.1, workflows WIDENED from WF-010 alone to WF-010 plus WF-006. Validity CONFIRMED by direct measurement against the real config machinery: with the file omitting move_detect_delay or setting 10 and LINKWATCHER_MOVE_DETECT_DELAY=30, the daemon resolves 30.0 while read_correlation_window returns 10.0; with file 25 and env 30 the pair is 30.0 versus 25.0. Mechanism confirmed in source - settings.py merge overrides whenever the env value differs from the dataclass default, while lifecycle.py read_correlation_window parses the YAML alone, and the panel launcher uses Popen with no env argument so a panel-started daemon does inherit the panel environment. Description corrected on three points. (a) UNDERSTATED: the divergence runs in BOTH directions - env below the file value (file 30, env 5) makes the panel over-wait 30 s where the daemon needs 5 s, which loses no data but raises the chance of a FORCE_STOPPED where a clean stop was available now that the F1 re-arm is in place. Same root cause, same fix. (b) OVERSTATED: the env surface is not fully live - merge skips any value equal to the dataclass default, so LINKWATCHER_MOVE_DETECT_DELAY=10 is a silent no-op against a file value of 25. The override works for every value except exactly 10.0. That wider defect - any LINKWATCHER_ variable set to its default value is ignored when a config file sets a non-default one, contradicting the documented precedence for every field - is spun off as its own bug against feature 0.1.3. (c) DECISIVE: the cheap fix the report proposes, reading os.environ in lifecycle.py in a few lines, is INCOMPLETE. discovery.py line 303 adopts daemons found by command line that the panel never started and drains them at close, and this project starts its own daemon from a SessionStart hook rather than from the panel, so the panel environment is not the adopted daemon environment. The daemon publishes no effective-config surface: config_loaded logs only the config file path and .linkwatcher.lock holds a bare PID. Bound established: there is NO CLI flag for move_detect_delay, so the environment is the SOLE unread surface for this field. Priority Medium held over a strict matrix reading of High (Impact High x Frequency Low), and the deviation is deliberate: PD-BUG-139 held High on the same pair but its trigger was rare and spontaneous, whereas this one requires a deliberate opt-in configuration act that nothing recommends - zero occurrences of LINKWATCHER_MOVE_DETECT_DELAY anywhere in the product, launchers, installer, docs or config examples, the only env var in real use being LINKWATCHER_PROJECT_REGISTRY - and a user who sets the value on the primary surface, the config file, is entirely unaffected. Scope M rather than the S the report implies, on the same ground PD-BUG-136 was scoped M: the fix carries a design fork. S path, about 1 hour, reads os.environ over the file value, closing panel-started daemons and leaving adopted daemons as a residual beside the accepted F2 residual. M path, 3 to 4 hours in 1 session, APPROVED: have the daemon publish its effective move_detect_delay via a log record or the lock payload and have the panel read that, closing the whole class including the F2 residual - the same shape, the panel inferring daemon behaviour from the config file when the effective config is file plus env plus CLI - and subsuming TD294. Workflows widened with WF-006 because the defect is precisely a violation of the documented precedence in FDD 3-1-1-BR-6. NOT a duplicate and NOT a reopen of PD-BUG-136: the root cause lives in read_correlation_window, code that did not exist before that fix, so it is a different root cause in a different function, the precedent approved at PD-BUG-141 versus PD-BUG-018. Effort about 1 session. Sequence after PD-BUG-141, which is In Progress.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-27
- **Fix**: read_correlation_window now resolves the window through the same configuration chain the daemon uses - LinkWatcherConfig(), then merge(from_file), then merge(from_env) - instead of hand-parsing the YAML for move_detect_delay. The file stage and the environment stage are guarded separately, mirroring load_config in main.py, so an unreadable or unparseable config can no longer swallow a live environment override. DEFAULT_MOVE_DETECT_DELAY_SECONDS is now derived from LinkWatcherConfig().move_detect_delay rather than a hand-copied 10.0, which eliminates TD294 structurally - no second copy of the default remains that could drift. The never-raises contract, the bool and non-finite and non-positive guards, and the protective fallback are retained; the yaml import is removed. SCOPE REASSESSED DOWNWARD at Step 13 with human approval: triage had approved having the daemon publish its effective value, but investigation showed neither approved channel closes the class - the lock body is parsed as a bare integer by four readers across two languages (main.py _read_lock_owner_pid, the panel read_lock_pid, and both PowerShell launchers), and a log record inherits the accepted F2 residual verbatim, since a daemon at log_level WARNING or ERROR emits no INFO record. ACCEPTED RESIDUAL, documented in the function docstring, TDD 4.4 and the explanation handbook: a daemon the panel ADOPTED rather than started may have been launched from a different environment than the panel, in which case the environment read here is not the one that daemon runs under; it needs three conjoined conditions and has zero known instances. VERIFICATION: 4 new fail-first parametrized cases plus 2 supporting cases in test_panel_lifecycle.py, observed FAILING pre-fix (5 failures, each on the negative assertion naming the stale file-derived value, so failing for the right reason) and passing after. Panel suite 356 passed; full suite 1623 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed; flake8 clean; test tracking synced 45 to 48. The TD294 property was mutation-verified rather than asserted: with the daemon default temporarily set to 12.0 the panel fallback followed to 12.0, and the source was restored with a hash check. Manual validation script PD-BUG-142_correlation_window_env_override_validation.py passes across 6 scenarios, showing the retired file-only logic wrong by -20s, -5s and +25s where the override is active. E2E deliberately NOT flagged for re-execution: the -WhatIf cascade covers 9 entries across 5 features for a fix that is behavior-preserving in every fixture (none sets the variable), and TE-E2E-036 is already at Needs Re-execution from PD-BUG-136.
- **Root Cause**: The panel re-implemented a fragment of the daemon configuration resolution instead of using it. read_correlation_window parsed only the YAML file, while main.py merges LinkWatcherConfig.from_env() OVER the file config and from_env derives LINKWATCHER_MOVE_DETECT_DELAY automatically from the dataclass field. The two therefore disagreed whenever that variable was set to a non-default value, in BOTH directions: an override above the file value made the panel terminate a daemon still holding a buffered move-correlation and silently drop the reference update (PD-BUG-136 reopened), and an override below it made the drain over-wait toward a needless FORCE_STOPPED. Measured: a config omitting the key with the variable at 30 gave daemon 30.0 against panel 10.0. There is no CLI flag for this field, so file plus environment is the whole precedence chain.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Code Review (PF-TSK-005) APPROVED 2026-08-27, no regressions. Scoped to the PD-BUG-142 delta in a mixed working tree (read_correlation_window rewrite, DEFAULT_MOVE_DETECT_DELAY_SECONDS derivation, the 6 new test cases, TDD 4.4, the explanation handbook, the manual harness); the re-arm, clamp and lock-unlink backoff are already-closed scope and were seam-checked only. Dims DI ID; PE, AX, SE and feature acceptance criteria marked N/A per the bug-fix scoping rules. CHAIN VERIFIED, not merely read: the panel resolution matches main.py load_config lines 62 to 77 stage for stage, and grep confirms there is NO CLI flag for move_detect_delay anywhere in main.py, so file plus environment really is the whole precedence chain as the fix claims. Independently corroborated by the PD-BUG-143 suite, whose _resolve helper builds the same three-stage chain and reaches the same answers. The env-equals-default case (file 25, env 10) was measured at 10.0, correct, and is pinned at the config layer by test_float_env_value_equal_to_default_wins_over_the_file, so the panel reliance on the parallel merge fix is protected. TD294 closure confirmed structurally: single call site, fallback derived from LinkWatcherConfig().move_detect_delay, no residual literal, archived with resolution date. TESTS RUN: panel suite 356 passed; full suite 1630 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed (the fix notes said 1623 - the suite has grown via parallel sessions, no failures either way); flake8 at the gate settings (max-line-length 120, extend-ignore E203) clean for the source and the unit tests. The new parametrized cases carry genuine discriminating conditions - the stale value differs from the expected value in all four rows, so the negative assertions bite rather than passing vacuously. FINDINGS 3 Minor, 4 Suggestion, nothing Critical or Major, none of them wrong behavior in the fix itself. TD297 (TST): the new environment read made 7 existing test cases environment-coupled - measured, with LINKWATCHER_MOVE_DETECT_DELAY=30 exported 7 cases fail, while the daemon config suite passes 114 of 114 under the same condition, so this is a local gap against the project own standard and the PD-BUG-142 repro steps themselves create the contaminated shell. TD298 (ID): the fix removed the hand-copied default but left a hand-rolled copy of the daemon numeric-validity rule beside it, where config.settings exports is_usable and schema_default and main._usable_or_default is the established pattern from PD-BUG-127; the rules agree today, the drift hazard is prospective. TD299 (DA): TDD section 9 still defers PD-FRQ-020 because the PD-BUG-142 approved fix builds a publication channel, which the downward reassessment explicitly abandoned - section 4.4 was updated, section 9 was missed. Suggestions not filed: config_path remains the only unannotated parameter in the module (prior review S1, and the function was fully rewritten here); the manual harness oracle re-implements the daemon chain rather than calling main.load_config, reintroducing the hand-copy hazard the fix just removed; a malformed config now emits 18 warning lines per drain, measured, because routing through _from_dict iterates a non-dict YAML value character by character; and the harness carries a 121 character line at 153 over the flake8 gate while the new test code is not black-at-100 normalized, both of which the commit gate self-heals. Also raised for decision, not filed: the accepted adopted-daemon residual has no FDD edge case, where PD-BUG-140 one day earlier established EC-12 for its accepted residual, and this one is user-visible. Feature Tracking left untouched - 7.1.1 sits at Needs User Docs, past code review, and this bug-fix review does not move its pipeline status.

### PD-BUG-144

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: LinkWatcher service lifecycle
- **Repro**: Run test/bug-validation/PD-BUG-139_blind_watch_detection_validation.py, whose POST-FIX half makes the daemon detect a dead watch and leave its monitoring loop. Grep the captured output for service_stopping, file_observer_stopped and service_stopped: all three are absent while observer_emitter_died is present. Equivalently read service.py - the loop sets self.running = False, and stop() opens with if self.running.
- **Expected**: A daemon that stops itself, or is asked to stop by a signal, runs its shutdown sequence: observer stopped and joined, final statistics printed, service_stopped logged.
- **Actual**: stop() returns immediately without doing any of it. The observer thread is left running until the process exits, statistics are lost, and the log ends at the error record with no shutdown confirmation - an operator cannot distinguish a clean self-stop from a crash.
- **Evidence**: Measured 2026-08-27 during Bug Fixing of PD-BUG-139. In a validation run whose POST-FIX half self-stopped on a killed watch: observer_emitter_died 1, service_stopping 0, file_observer_stopped 0, service_stopped 0. Confirmed pre-existing by reading git HEAD - the committed service.py has the identical guard and the identical running=False assignment on the observer_thread_died path. The PD-BUG-139 sentinel cleanup was deliberately placed OUTSIDE this guard so it does not inherit the defect.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-27 (PF-TSK-041), human approved as recommended. Priority Medium HELD, scope S, Dims OB CQ held as filed, related feature CORRECTED 1.1.1 to 0.1.1, workflow CORRECTED WF-010 to WF-008. Validity CONFIRMED and reproduced at two levels. Unit probe with a mocked observer: with running cleared before stop() is reached, observer.stop and observer.join are never called, final statistics are never printed and ZERO log events are emitted, while the control - stop() called with running still True - calls both, prints statistics and logs service_stopping plus service_stopped. Real-observer end-to-end through the actual monitoring loop and the start() finally block: after the SIGINT path completes, the WindowsApiObserver is STILL ALIVE, which is the non-mock proof that the guarded body never ran; the control run leaves it dead. Pre-existing confirmed at git HEAD - if self.running: opens stop() at HEAD line 181 and the loop clears running at HEAD line 169. Description corrected on four points. (a) DECISIVE, and it inverts the meaning of the report: the filed workflow WF-010 is wrong, because the Control Panel stops daemons with a hard TerminateProcess - lifecycle.py line 505 is psutil.Process(pid).terminate() and the module docstring states there is no graceful cross-process signal - so under the panel stop() NEVER RUNS AT ALL and this bug is invisible on WF-010. The same applies to stop_linkwatcher.ps1, which uses Stop-Process -Force. The correct workflow is WF-008 Graceful shutdown, whose documented trigger reads literally Stop the LinkWatcher process (Ctrl+C, kill). Complete affected-path inventory established: Ctrl+C on a foreground run (python main.py, the README Quick Start) AFFECTED, liveness self-stop from PD-BUG-139 AFFECTED, observer-thread-died from PD-BUG-018 AFFECTED, Control Panel and stop_linkwatcher.ps1 NOT AFFECTED (hard kill, stop() never reached), unhandled exception and explicit stop() calls NOT AFFECTED (running still True, body runs). (b) OVERSTATED: the observer thread being left running until process exit is harmless - WindowsApiObserver.daemon is True, so the process exits normally with no hang - and the lock is NOT leaked, because release_lock() sits in the main.py finally block outside the service. No data loss either, since updates are synchronous atomic writes. (c) UNDERSTATED: the real harm is not lost statistics but that this partially defeats PD-BUG-139, whose whole purpose is that a daemon going blind stops LOUDLY so the operator knows to run a validation scan. On that exact path this bug removes service_stopping and service_stopped, so the log ends at observer_emitter_died with no shutdown confirmation and the operator cannot distinguish a completed clean stop from a crash mid-work. (d) Pinned for the fix: the except KeyboardInterrupt branch in start(), and the matching one in main() at line 554, are effectively DEAD CODE - register_signals defaults True and main.py does not override it, so SIGINT reaches _signal_handler and never raises. The intuitive reading that Ctrl+C is safe because KeyboardInterrupt is handled is wrong. Priority Medium held over a defensible High reading: WF-008 is a P2 workflow whose entire purpose is graceful shutdown and whose own documented trigger never executes the shutdown sequence, which on the letter of the impact scale reads as a major feature not working, and the trigger is 100 percent deterministic on every affected path. Held at Medium because the workflow acceptance criterion is no corrupted files and that criterion HOLDS - files intact, lock released, process exits cleanly - so what fails is the instrumentation of the workflow rather than its promise, and in this project dominant deployment the daemon runs detached and is stopped by hard kill, so the affected paths are rarely reached. Consistent with PD-BUG-137, held Medium as something that misleads rather than breaks. Related feature corrected because service.py is owned by Core Architecture: the 0.1.1 state file names LinkWatcherService (service.py) as the central component and carries it in its code inventory, while 1.1.1 File System Monitoring does not own it. Scope S with no open design fork - the fix guards stop() on a dedicated idempotence flag instead of the mutable running, and the design is already constrained by existing tests: test_si_002_service_multiple_stop_calls pins that three consecutive stop() calls must not raise AND calls them on a never-started service, so the new guard must also keep a never-started stop() quiet rather than emitting three misleading shutdown records. NOT a duplicate and NOT a reopen of PD-BUG-018: that bug was about DETECTING a dead observer, this is the shutdown sequence being skipped afterward - same file, adjacent code, different mechanism, the precedent approved at PD-BUG-141 versus PD-BUG-018. Effort about 1 session. One observation recorded, not filed: the WF-008 E2E group is Covered 3 of 3 and passing while the graceful-shutdown sequence never runs on its own trigger, because the cases verify file non-corruption rather than that shutdown completed - the group name promises more than its assertions check.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-27
- **Fix**: Idempotence moved off the mutable running flag onto dedicated _started and _stopped lifecycle flags, so running keeps only its own job. _started is load-bearing rather than defensive: start() builds the observer, schedules it, then starts it, and calls stop() from its finally whatever happens, so guarding on self.observer being set would reach join() on an unstarted thread and raise RuntimeError, masking the original failure; _started is set only after observer.start() returns, and also keeps a never-started service silent, which test_si_002_service_multiple_stop_calls requires. THREE design documents described this path correctly and the code silently diverged from all of them, so no documentation text needed changing - the code was the defect: FDD 0.1.1-FR-5 and 0.1.1-FR-6 were violated while AC-4 and AC-7 were recorded as met, TDD PD-TDD-021 states that try/finally ensures Observer.stop() and Observer.join() are ALWAYS called and spells out the exact SIGINT sequence, and the graceful-shutdown Integration Narrative calls observer.join() the critical synchronization point for in-flight updates. All four are true again. IMPACT CORRECTED from the triage assessment, which said observability-only and no data loss: because the skipped join() is that documented synchronization point, a Ctrl+C landing during an active update could LOSE that in-flight update, since the process exits and the daemon observer thread is killed wherever it stands. Bounded honestly - atomic writes mean no file is ever corrupted so the WF-008 acceptance criterion held throughout, the race window is milliseconds, and it was NOT reproduced; recorded as a reasoned consequence rather than a measured one. It does not change the Medium call. Step 20 sweep found no siblings: the three other stop/cleanup methods in the package (discovery.py, lifecycle.py, logging_config.py) each already use a dedicated stop Event or claim flag plus a thread check, so service.stop() was the only one overloading a loop-control flag as shutdown idempotence. Documentation updated: the 0.1.1 state file carries the full fix record and the 0.1.1 test spec gained a Shutdown Sequencing scenario table; FDD, TDD and the Integration Narrative are N-A verified, correct as written and now satisfied. No ADR - the fix restores documented design rather than making a new architectural commitment. Qualification on the dead-code note from triage: the except KeyboardInterrupt branches in start() and main() are unreachable only in the daemon configuration, because register_signals defaults True; with register_signals=False, the option tests and library embedders use, no handler is installed, KeyboardInterrupt propagates and the branch is live and correct, so it was deliberately left in place.
- **Root Cause**: stop() guarded its whole body with if self.running, a flag every path that reaches it has already cleared. The monitoring loop clears running on a liveness failure (PD-BUG-139) or a dead observer thread (PD-BUG-018), and _signal_handler clears it on SIGINT/SIGTERM; start() then calls stop() from its finally, the guard is already False, and the entire shutdown sequence is skipped. running was overloaded as two different questions - should the loop keep looping, and has shutdown already happened - and the second answer was always wrong on exactly the paths that reach stop(). Not reached under a hard kill, which is why this is WF-008 and not the filed WF-010: the Control Panel (TerminateProcess), stop_linkwatcher.ps1 and the E2E harness (Stop-Process -Force) all skip Python shutdown entirely.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: S-scope quick path: human-approved at the Step 23 checkpoint, and criterion 2 verified rather than assumed - the WF-008 E2E harness stops the daemon with Stop-Process -Force at lw-e2e-helpers.ps1 line 109, so stop() never runs in those cases and the fix is unobservable to them before or after. 7 regression tests added to test_service_integration.py. Fail-first discipline observed and recorded: 3 failed and 4 passed PRE-fix, each failure inspected and confirmed red for the stated reason, including a real-observer assertion that the observer thread was left alive after start() returned - the non-mock proof that the guarded body never ran. POST-fix 7 of 7 pass. Full suite 1640 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failed. Manual validation test/bug-validation/PD-BUG-144_shutdown_sequence_skipped_validation.py was authored BEFORE the fix and observed reporting BUG PRESENT (observer alive, all three shutdown records absent), and now reports FIXED; its Part 2 hands the operator a real foreground Ctrl+C run. Test tracking synced; black and isort clean, with black touching no pre-existing line (deletion count against HEAD stayed at zero). No E2E re-execution flagged, human-approved: the -WhatIf radius is 14 entries across 8 features and 4 workflows for a change those hard-killing fixtures cannot observe. Two disclosures. (1) The FIRST full-suite run showed 4 failures, all in test_watchliveness.py, because a parallel session wrote its PD-BUG-145 fix into service.py DURING that run; verified rather than assumed - the file passes 32 of 32 in isolation and the clean full re-run is 1640 with 0 failures. (2) Pre-existing and not swept in: test_service_integration.py carries test_type=integration while sitting in a unit/ directory, which the tracking validator reports as a warning with the marker authoritative. Sibling defect PD-BUG-146 filed during this fix: E2E case TE-E2E-020 asserts a shutdown log message its own Stop-Process -Force harness makes impossible, and Verify-TestResult.ps1 compares only files so the criterion is never machine-checked.

### PD-BUG-145

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: LinkWatcher daemon shutdown (watch liveness)
- **Repro**: Configure log_file at the project root so the sentinel uses the project-root fallback. Start the daemon, leave the tree quiet longer than watch_liveness_idle_seconds so one probe is written, then stop the daemon with stop_linkwatcher.ps1 or the Control Panel. The .linkwatcher-liveness-probe file remains in the project root.
- **Expected**: No probe file outlives the daemon on any stop path, and the user documentation describes the actual cleanup behavior.
- **Actual**: After a Stop-Process -Force or Control Panel stop the probe file remains. In fallback deployments it remains in the project root as an untracked dotfile.
- **Evidence**: Code review of the PD-BUG-139 session 2 delta (2026-08-27). Sites: stop_linkwatcher.ps1 line 45 Stop-Process -Force; lifecycle.py line 505 psutil terminate; service.py line 294 places _remove_liveness_sentinel() at the top of stop(), while its docstring at line 309 still says removal runs after the observer has stopped; capabilities reference line 190 says the probe is removed when the daemon stops. Suggested fix: also unlink at startup (idempotent, sweeps leftovers) and correct both statements.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-27: valid, all five cited sites verified in code. Low confirmed - no functional, data or security impact; harm is a stray untracked dotfile in fallback deployments (log at project root or outside) plus two inaccurate cleanup statements. Description corrected on two counts: the probe outlives only stops that followed at least one probe write, because on an active project the silence gate never opens and no probe is written; and the service.py line 309 docstring inaccuracy concerns ordering inside stop(), not a consequence of the force-kill paths. Severity ceiling checked and ruled out - a leftover sentinel cannot cause a false stall on the next daemon, because handler.dispatch matches src_path regardless of event type, so truncating an existing sentinel still registers as observed. Dims OB DA, with DA added since two statements contradict code and one is user-facing. Scope S covering startup unlink plus two doc corrections plus one regression test. Not a duplicate, but coupled to PD-BUG-144, which is the reason cleanup was hoisted above the if self.running guard - a 144 fix must not drop the hoisted call. Fixer caution: an unlink at startup must sit after logging setup, because _liveness_sentinel_path pins for the daemon lifetime and an early call would pin the project-root fallback and relocate the probe for every deployment.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-27
- **Fix**: Two layers, neither sufficient alone. Layer 1 (liveness.py): _probe_round_trips discards the sentinel in a finally as soon as the round trip is decided, making the probe transient scaffolding rather than persistent state; added an injectable remove_sentinel seam mirroring write_sentinel, and _discard_sentinel swallows OSError at debug so cleanup can never change a liveness verdict. Layer 2 (service.py): start() sweeps a probe stranded by a hard-killed predecessor - the only layer that can cover a crash during a probe. Sweep placement is load-bearing in both directions: after logging setup, because _liveness_sentinel_path pins for the daemon lifetime and an earlier call would pin the project-root fallback and relocate the probe for every deployment; and before the observer starts, so the delete emits no event. stop() keeps its removal for the clean-stop path. Corrected three stale cleanup claims (DA): the _remove_liveness_sentinel docstring saying removal runs after the observer has stopped when it runs first, the hoist comment left stale by the parallel PD-BUG-144 guard replacement, and the capabilities reference. TDD 1.1.1 and test spec 1.1.1 updated. Rejected alternative: having stop_linkwatcher.ps1 and the Control Panel delete the sentinel - that launcher is under process-framework, rollout-managed and read-only here, the logic would be duplicated across two languages, and it would still miss crashes. E2E re-execution deliberately NOT flagged: blast radius is 34 entries across 7 workflows via cross-cutting cover, no fixture references the probe, and the fix is behavior-preserving for everything those cases assert.
- **Root Cause**: Cleanup for the liveness probe existed only in LinkWatcherService.stop(), which is reached only when Python gets to run shutdown code. Both operator stop paths are a hard TerminateProcess on Windows - Stop-Process -Force in stop_linkwatcher.ps1, psutil terminate() in the Control Panel - so the cleanup was structurally unreachable on the paths that actually stop daemons. An atexit or signal hook fails for the same reason. Cleanup-at-shutdown is the wrong lifecycle hook for a process that is normally hard-killed.
- **Tests Added**: Yes - 7 unit tests in test_watchliveness.py (suite 25 to 32), 4 written first and confirmed FAILING on the intended assertion before the fix. Startup-sweep tests assert while the daemon is still up, so stop() cleanup cannot make them pass for the wrong reason. Plus a two-half manual validation at test/bug-validation/PD-BUG-145_liveness_probe_survives_force_kill_validation.py that reproduces the strand against a real daemon killed with taskkill /F and exits INCONCLUSIVE rather than PASS if the pre-fix half fails to reproduce. Full suite: 1646 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed.
- **Verification**: Reproduced and verified against real daemons, not only the test harness. Pre-fix the probe survived a taskkill /F in both placement layouts; post-fix nothing is stranded and a planted leftover is swept at startup.
- **Verification**: Code review (PF-TSK-005) approved 2026-08-27, human approved. Verified independently rather than accepted from the fix notes: the manual validation script PASSed against real daemons (pre-fix half stranded the probe in the project root under taskkill /F; post-fix half left the tree clean and swept a planted leftover at startup), and the full suite ran 1646 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed, matching the recorded figures. Assertions mutation-verified out of tree: nulling _discard_sentinel turns exactly its 2 tests red on the intended assertions; nulling _remove_liveness_sentinel turns both startup-sweep tests red plus the 2 pre-existing stop-cleanup tests. The E2E-not-flagged decision was checked and holds - no E2E fixture or expected tree references the probe filename. Scoped to Dims OB DA; PE, AX, SE and feature acceptance criteria N/A. Findings routed, none blocking: TD300 (vacuous placement guard - test_startup_sweep_does_not_relocate_the_probe pre-creates the log directory before constructing the service, so the sentinel path is already pinned correctly with start() never called and the test would pass with the sweep anywhere, including __init__; confirmed by direct probe) and PD-BUG-147 (the capabilities reference credits stranded-probe behavior to earlier versions, but watch liveness has never shipped). Noted, not tracked: F541 at line 215 of the PD-BUG-145 validation script will fail the pre-commit flake8 gate when staged, and the PD-BUG-142 and PD-BUG-144 validation scripts carry sibling violations; _discard_sentinel logs removal failure at debug only, so a persistent failure recreates this symptom invisibly; the start() placement comment and the TDD 1.1.1 text overstate within-start() sensitivity when the real constraint is caller ordering (main.py runs setup_logging before constructing the service), and the load-bearing half of that constraint - sweep before observer.start() so the delete emits no event - has no test.

### PD-BUG-146

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: TE-E2E-020 E2E fixture
- **Repro**: Read test/e2e-acceptance-testing/graceful-shutdown-no-corrupted-files/templates/TE-E2E-020-stop-during-idle/test-case.md and note the Pass Criteria entry Application log contains a shutdown/stopping message, plus the Behavioral Outcomes entry Log shows clean shutdown message. Then read run.ps1 in the same directory, which delegates shutdown to Stop-WorkspaceLinkWatcher in test/e2e-acceptance-testing/_lib/lw-e2e-helpers.ps1 line 109: Stop-Process -Id procId -Force. Confirm no code path can emit a shutdown record under a forced termination.
- **Expected**: Either the harness stops the daemon by a route that runs its shutdown sequence, so the documented log criteria can actually be met, or the case drops those criteria and asserts only what its harness can produce - and says so, so a reader is not told the test verifies graceful shutdown when what it verifies is file non-corruption after a kill.
- **Actual**: The case asserts a shutdown log message that its harness structurally prevents. Because Verify-TestResult.ps1 compares only files, the unsatisfiable criteria are never machine-checked, and the case sits at Covered.
- **Evidence**: Found 2026-08-27 during Bug Fixing (PF-TSK-007) of PD-BUG-144, while establishing whether that fix would change any E2E outcome. Measured: Stop-WorkspaceLinkWatcher uses Stop-Process -Force, and PD-BUG-144 independently established that a hard TerminateProcess never reaches LinkWatcherService.stop(). Same family as the closed PD-BUG-138, where a silently passing acceptance test was RAISED to High at triage because it institutionalizes false confidence rather than merely failing to catch something. Note for triage: per the Issue Classification and Routing Guide the fix lands in a project-authored E2E fixture rather than framework test infrastructure, which is the same reasoning that kept PD-BUG-138 on the product bug tracker. Triage should also decide whether the sibling cases TE-E2E-021 and TE-E2E-022 carry the same unsatisfiable criteria - not checked here.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-27 (PF-TSK-041), human approved as recommended: priority Medium HELD, scope S assigned (was blank), related feature 0.1.1 held, workflows WF-008 held, Dims CORRECTED from CQ OB to CQ DA - the product logging is not defective, the defect is documented-vs-actual misalignment across test-case.md, audit report TE-TAR-048 and the tracking rows. Validity confirmed by direct inspection plus measurement. Description CORRECTED on three points: (1) TWO pass criteria are unsatisfiable, not one - Application log contains a shutdown/stopping message AND LinkWatcher process exited with code 0; measured that Stop-Process -Force yields exit code -1, never 0; both are mirrored again in Behavioral Outcomes. (2) The case is not merely recorded Covered - it is Passed AND Audit Approved (TE-TAR-048), whose report calls the four criteria 4 measurable conditions and records No issues found, so the false confidence is institutionalized exactly as in the closed PD-BUG-138. (3) The sibling check requested at filing is done: TE-E2E-022 is clean; TE-E2E-021 carries a weaker instance - its Behavioral Outcomes say process exits cleanly, which a forced kill cannot demonstrate, while its Pass Criteria only require exited without hanging, which a kill does satisfy - a one-line wording fix in the same session, not a separate bug. Key reframing fact: the harness does not deviate from the product, it mirrors it. Every operator stop path is a hard kill - lifecycle.py:8 states there is no graceful cross-process signal because terminate() is a hard TerminateProcess, service.py:142-143 says the same, and stop_linkwatcher.ps1:45 uses Stop-Process -Force - so making the harness stop gracefully would test a path only an interactive Ctrl+C user can reach. The correct fix is to align the criteria with what the harness can produce and to record that Ctrl+C shutdown is not E2E-covered. Priority rationale: Impact Low x Frequency High = Medium. The counter-case for raising to High was presented and NOT taken: unlike PD-BUG-138, whose fixture verified nothing while reporting a pass, this case does verify something real and machine-checked (file non-corruption under a kill) and merely claims more than it verifies; the unverified path is unreachable by any operator tool and the code behind it was fixed and verified under PD-BUG-144; no release path is gated (0.1.1 Completed with All Passing, WF-008 is P2). Scope S: test-case documentation only, no product code, root cause fully known. Classification checked against the Issue Classification and Routing Guide: the corrective fix lands in a project-authored E2E case fixture, not framework test infrastructure, so product bug is the correct tracker - no reclassification. Effort estimate 30-45 minutes.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-27
- **Fix**: Rewrote the TE-E2E-020 pass criteria, behavioral outcomes, steps 3-4, scripted action and verifier command so the case asserts only what a hard kill can produce: files byte-identical (machine-checked) plus no errors or stack traces up to termination. Added a scope note stating that the case verifies kill-safety rather than graceful shutdown, why the harness mirrors the product, and that the graceful Ctrl+C sequence is E2E-uncovered. Corrected the same claim in TE-E2E-021 (exits cleanly) and in master-test-graceful-shutdown.md (process exits with code 0), both found by the step-20 sweep. Appended a dated post-audit correction note to TE-TAR-048 (v1.0 to v1.1) recording that two of the four criteria it approved as measurable were unsatisfiable; the verdict was left as written and the case was NOT re-audited, per human decision. Also corrected Verify-TestResult.ps1 invocations in TE-E2E-021 and TE-E2E-022 that omitted the required -Workflow parameter and would hard-error. New regression guard test/automated/unit/0-system-architecture-foundation/test_pd_bug_146_shutdown_criteria_guard.py (TE-TST-160) fails the build if any force-killed E2E case, or any master test of an all-force-killed group, asserts an exit code of 0, a shutdown log record, or a clean exit.
- **Root Cause**: The case was written against the WF-008 documented trigger (Ctrl+C, kill) and asserted the Ctrl+C half while its harness implements the kill half. run.ps1 stops the daemon through Stop-WorkspaceLinkWatcher, which uses Stop-Process -Force - a hard TerminateProcess that runs no Python code in the target - so LinkWatcherService.stop() never executes and neither a shutdown record nor an exit code of 0 is possible (measured: -1). No product code is defective; the harness mirrors the product, where every operator stop path is a hard kill (lifecycle.py line 8, service.py lines 142-143, stop_linkwatcher.ps1 line 45). The claim survived because Verify-TestResult.ps1 compares only files under expected and reads no logs or exit codes, so the impossible criteria were manual checkboxes that nothing enforced, and the case sat at Passed and Audit Approved.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: S-scope quick path, human-approved at the step-23 checkpoint. Fail-first verified: the new guard went red on the pre-fix content, naming all 8 sites in TE-E2E-020 with correct line numbers, while 25 sibling force-kill cases stayed green; green after the fix (58 passed). Full suite 1698 passed, 3 skipped, 6 deselected, 3 xfailed, 0 failed - 1684 before, the delta being the guard cases. TE-E2E-020 executed end to end and PASSED with files matching expected, which also verified the corrected -Workflow verifier command. Run log evidence: 18 records, zero service_stopping or service_stopped records and zero ERROR-level records, confirming the corrected criteria describe what the harness actually produces. During the fix the guard flagged the fixers own explanatory bullet, which drove two hardening changes: the explanation moved out of the assertion section into Notes, and the negation check was scoped to the matched claim rather than the whole line, fixing a false negative that had let TE-E2E-021 through. Nine historical violation strings are pinned as matcher self-tests so a later regex change cannot silently blind the guard. Step 21 manual validation test skipped - documentation-only fix with no user-observable UI or filesystem behavior. Audit status left at Audit Approved with a correction note rather than re-audited, per human decision. Referred to the human, not done: cross-cutting spec PF-TSP-044 S-017 still states its Assert as Clean shutdown, no errors, the wording that seeded this defect. Observation not filed: TDD 0.1.1 section 9.2 claims clean shutdown is verified by a LinkWatcher stopped log message, but no such literal record exists (the records are service_stopped and linkwatcher_stopped_by_user).

### PD-BUG-141

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Daemon file observation (watchdog dep)
- **Repro**: Open a directory handle via watchdog.observers.winapi.get_directory_handle on any existing temp directory, then call winapi._is_observed_path_deleted(handle, that_path). It returns True for a live directory. Probe used during PD-BUG-139: scratchpad probe_observed_path_deleted.py. A second, weaker upstream defect sits alongside it: EventEmitter.run() has no exception guard, so any raise from queue_events kills the emitter too - that one at least leaves a traceback on stderr.
- **Expected**: _is_observed_path_deleted returns True only when the watched directory has actually been deleted or moved, so a transient ReadDirectoryChangesW error is propagated as an error rather than converted into a permanent silent emitter shutdown.
- **Actual**: Returns True for a healthy existing directory because it compares an NT device path against a DOS path. Any transient OSError is converted into a synthetic FILE_ACTION_DELETED_SELF, the emitter self-stops, and the watch is permanently dead while the observer reports alive.
- **Evidence**: Measured 2026-08-25. Watch path under AppData Local Temp vs GetFinalPathNameByHandleW returning a Device HarddiskVolume3 path for the same directory; directory still exists True, verdict True. Ruled out as the PD-BUG-139 trigger because that path also queues DirDeletedEvent(watch.path) before stopping, and _handle_directory_deleted logs directory_deleted at INFO - no such record exists in the preserved evidence. Residual impact: the daemon dies on a possibly recoverable transient error. Candidate responses: report upstream to watchdog, and/or a narrow local guard that re-verifies deletion before honouring the synthetic DELETED_SELF event.
