- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-26 via Bug Triage (PF-TSK-041), human approved. Priority RAISED Low to Medium; scope S; Dims DI OB ID, related feature 1.1.1 and workflows WF-001/WF-002/WF-004/WF-005/WF-011 all held as filed. Validity CONFIRMED by direct measurement against the installed watchdog 6.0.0: _is_observed_path_deleted returns True for a healthy directory, for a deleted one, AND for a moved one - it is a constant-True predicate, not merely wrong in one case. Description corrected on two points. (1) The filed root cause understates the defect in a way that invites a silent no-op fix: swapping VOLUME_NAME_NT for VOLUME_NAME_DOS alone does NOT fix it, because GetFinalPathNameByHandleW returns a path carrying the device-namespace prefix, so the healthy case still compares unequal and still returns True; a correct predicate needs prefix normalization as well. Measured at triage; the same measurement shows a correct compare IS achievable, since a deleted root resolves under the NTFS Extend Deleted namespace and a moved root resolves to its new path. (2) The underlying OSError is swallowed entirely - never logged, never re-raised - so a field occurrence surfaces only as observer_emitter_died with no winerror. That diagnostic loss is the one part of this bug the PD-BUG-139 detector does NOT bound, and it is the decisive reason for Medium rather than Low. Priority derivation: Impact Medium (monitoring halts until restart, workaround available) x Frequency Low = Medium on the matrix. Frequency Low established empirically: zero occurrences across all retained logs - the 23 directory_deleted records are genuine sub-directory deletions and none names the watch root, which is what a real firing would log. The one plausible high-frequency trigger was ruled out by measurement: a synchronous ReadDirectoryChangesW buffer overflow returns zero bytes rather than raising, so a large move burst does not take this path. The counter-case for holding Low was recorded and overruled by the human partner: never observed in months of operation, outcome already bounded to a clean stop by the PD-BUG-139 liveness detector, and the fix patches a vendor internal that must be re-verified at every watchdog bump. Scope S because the fix is confined to one Windows-only shim plus wiring plus unit tests derived from the triage probes, with no cross-component dependency and no design question that must be resolved in an FDD first. No duplicates: PD-BUG-018 (closed) is the same class - watch dies silently - with a different mechanism at the handler and observer level, so per the Reopen Workflow table this is a new bug and not a reopen; PD-BUG-139 is the detector for the consequence, not this defect. WF-010 deliberately NOT added despite PD-BUG-139 carrying it: post-139 the daemon stops rather than going blind, and the Control Panel reports that honestly. DI retained after consideration - the residual staleness window is only the 1 to 2 seconds before the 1 second liveness poll catches the dead emitter, but the window is real and the sibling PD-BUG-139 carries DI on the same surface. Effort estimate 2 to 4 hours. Sequencing: fix after PD-BUG-139 clears code review, since both change the same watch surface.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-26
- **Fix**: Added src/linkwatcher/winapi_patch.py. install_watch_root_deletion_fix() replaces the vendor predicate with a corrected one: an os.path.isdir pre-check, then a device-namespace-prefix-stripped, normcase/normpath DOS-path compare. Two signals rather than one, so the result stays right where GetFinalPathNameByHandleW legitimately resolves elsewhere (junctions, subst drives) and where a root moved away and was replaced at its old path. Installed from service.py immediately before Observer() is built; a failed install logs watch_root_deletion_fix_unavailable at WARNING. Idempotent via a marker attribute, and a documented no-op on non-Windows, without watchdog, or when the vendor symbol is absent. A transient OSError now propagates with its winerror intact instead of being rewritten into a watch-ending synthetic deletion. Retry or absorption of the error was considered and rejected as a different feature; the second upstream defect (EventEmitter.run has no exception guard) stays out of scope since it is what carries the traceback to stderr and its consequence is already detected by the PD-BUG-139 monitor.
- **Root Cause**: winapi._is_observed_path_deleted asked GetFinalPathNameByHandleW for the NT device form (VOLUME_NAME_NT) and compared it against the DOS watch path. The two forms never match, so the predicate was CONSTANT TRUE - measured to answer deleted for a healthy, a deleted and a moved directory alike. read_directory_changes consults it from its except OSError arm, so every transient error other than ERROR_OPERATION_ABORTED became a synthetic FILE_ACTION_DELETED_SELF, whereupon queue_events called stop() and permanently ended the watch while discarding the original error. Fix trap recorded: swapping to VOLUME_NAME_DOS alone leaves the predicate constant true, because the returned path carries the device-namespace prefix - normalization is required.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: TE-TST-159 (11 tests) fail-first verified: before the fix 6 failed with the defect signature (assert True is not True on healthy directories, DID NOT RAISE OSError, and the synthetic DELETED_SELF action present) while 5 passed, confirming they were not trivially red; after the fix 11 of 11 pass. Full suite 1594 passed, 0 failed (3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 6 deselected). black and isort clean. A real daemon started from repo source on an isolated temp workspace reached monitoring_started, followed a file move (the reference was rewritten to ../moved/target.md), and logged no patch-unavailable warning and no emitter death. Manual validation at test/bug-validation/PD-BUG-141_transient_watch_error_validation.py: Part 1 passes automatically, Part 2 needs a human File Explorer deletion and is pending. E2E re-execution deliberately NOT flagged: the -WhatIf cascade is 34 entries across 7 features and 7 workflows, while the changed branch runs only inside except OSError in read_directory_changes, which no E2E case induces - behavior-preserving per the task step-25 allowance and confirmed by the real-daemon run. Similar-issue sweep found no siblings: the two path-equality sites in LinkWatcher own code compare like-normalized internal keys (database.py) or already apply normcase and abspath to both sides (utils.py). Docs updated: FDD 1.1.1 gained EC-12, the 1.1.1 test spec gained a PD-BUG-141 row, the feature state file gained bug-history plus source and test inventory rows, and the capabilities handbook gained a Watch Liveness Monitoring bullet. TDD marked N/A after reading it - it scopes to the handler subsystem and lists neither liveness.py nor the observer layer. Version coupling to a watchdog internal recorded as technical debt (TD296) rather than an ADR.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-27
- **Verification**: Code review 2026-08-27 (PF-TSK-005): NOT APPROVED - returned for scoped documentation and comment corrections. The code fix itself is sound and verified for the common case (local disk, real path): the vendor predicate was measured constant-true (True for healthy, trailing-separator and upper-case spellings alike) while the patched predicate returns False for a healthy root, so the 11 tests in TE-TST-159 are discriminating rather than vacuous, and they pass 11 of 11. black and isort clean. Coverage of winapi_patch.py is 85 percent. The observability chain was verified end to end: the launcher redirects stderr to logs/linkwatcher/LinkWatcherError.txt and the Control Panel starts daemons through that same launcher, so a propagated winerror is captured on both start paths - the decisive reason for the Medium priority holds. FINDING 1 (Major) - the docstring at winapi_patch.py lines 32 to 38 claims the os.path.isdir pre-check keeps the predicate honest where GetFinalPathNameByHandleW legitimately resolves elsewhere (junctions, subst drives). That is structurally impossible: the isdir branch can only return True (deleted) and never False (healthy), so control always falls through to the path compare. Measured against the real Win32 API: with a junction root the handle resolved to a different final DOS path and the verdict was True (deleted) for a perfectly healthy directory; with a subst drive root (W: mapped to a temp directory) the verdict was likewise True; and for an unreachable UNC path os.path.isdir returns False, so the predicate answers deleted immediately. Consequence: for a junction-rooted, subst-rooted or network-share-interrupted watch the predicate remains effectively constant-true and the fix delivers no improvement. The two documented goals conflict - a path that exists but resolves elsewhere must be read as either healthy (junction) or deleted (moved-and-replaced) - and the code deliberately picks deleted, which the test at test_watchrootdeletionpredicate.py line 145 pins. That trade-off is defensible; the comment describing it is not. Exposure today is ZERO: the LinkWatcher project root was measured to resolve to itself, so no deployment triggers this now - it is a latent gap plus a false documented claim shipping in 2.2.0. FINDING 2 (Minor) - the except OSError guard at winapi_patch.py lines 103 to 106 is unreachable for the failure it was written for. watchdog wires GetFinalPathNameByHandleW to _errcheck_dword, which raises only when the return value is 0xFFFFFFFF (the WAIT_FAILED sentinel borrowed from WaitForSingleObjectEx), while GetFinalPathNameByHandleW returns 0 on failure. Measured with an invalid handle: no exception, buff.value is the empty string, which normalizes to a single dot and compares unequal, yielding deleted. The outcome coincidentally matches the guard intent so there is no behavioural defect, but the guard is dead code reasoning from a false premise. The same empty-buffer path is taken for any watch path longer than PATH_BUFFER_SIZE (2048). FINDING 3 (Minor) - test and coverage gaps: the _patch_installed fixture docstring says the predicate is installed at service import time when it is actually installed in service.start(); uncovered lines are 79 (the UNC normalization branch, which is the one path relevant to the network-share scenario the docs name), 103 to 106, 124 and 128 to 129; nothing pins the junction or subst verdict either way so the trade-off is unprotected against regression; and test_transient_error_does_not_yield_a_watch_ending_deletion_event early-returns on OSError so it can pass without evaluating its own assertion, duplicating the preceding test rather than adding independent coverage. FINDING 4 (Suggestion) - watchdog is declared as greater-or-equal 6.0.0 with no upper bound, and install_watch_root_deletion_fix returns True whenever the vendor symbol merely exists, so an upstream fix would be silently overwritten. TD296 records the coupling but nothing makes a version bump loud. Optional but recommended: a test pinning the junction verdict. SEPARATE ISSUE, not this bug: the full automated suite now reports 5 failed and 1604 passed, all 5 in test_watchliveness.py (PD-BUG-139 work, still In Progress). Attribution measured - that file passes 24 of 24 alone, 35 of 35 alongside the PD-BUG-141 tests, and the whole 1-file-watching-detection directory passes 201 of 201, so this change set is not the polluter; pytest-randomly is not installed so the ordering is deterministic and reproducible. The Fix and Verification notes recorded on this row claim full suite 1594 passed 0 failed, which is no longer reproducible.
- **Fix**: Rewrote src/linkwatcher/winapi_patch.py to decide watch-root deletion by directory IDENTITY rather than by path spelling. install_watch_root_deletion_fix() replaces the vendor predicate with one comparing the volume serial number plus file index (GetFileInformationByHandle) of the watch handle against the same pair for whatever directory sits at the watch path: equal means healthy, different means the root was replaced, nothing openable means gone. This removes _normalize, _final_path_for_handle, the VOLUME_NAME_DOS constant and the dead except OSError guard entirely - no path normalization is needed at all, and the junction and subst cases the previous approach could not reach are now correct. Installed from service.py immediately before Observer() is built, unchanged; a failed install still logs watch_root_deletion_fix_unavailable at WARNING. Idempotent via a marker attribute, and the Win32 binding now lives inside the install path so the module stays importable on non-Windows. Documentation corrected in the same change: FDD 1.1.1-EC-12 and the capabilities handbook now state the junction and subst support and name the unreachable-root case as a known limitation instead of claiming unconditional coverage; the module docstring states the trade-off the code actually makes; the manual validation script gained an automatic junction part (Part 2) and had a broken import repaired.
- **Root Cause**: Two layers. The vendor defect: watchdog winapi._is_observed_path_deleted asked GetFinalPathNameByHandleW for the NT device form (VOLUME_NAME_NT) and compared it against the DOS watch path, so the predicate was CONSTANT TRUE, and every transient ReadDirectoryChangesW error other than ERROR_OPERATION_ABORTED became a synthetic FILE_ACTION_DELETED_SELF that permanently stopped the emitter while discarding the original error. The deeper root cause, established at code review on 2026-08-27: comparing PATHS is the wrong primitive for the question being asked. A directory on Windows has several equally true spellings - reached through a junction, through a subst drive, with or without the extended-length prefix, in any case, with or without a trailing separator - and GetFinalPathNameByHandleW resolves a junction to its target, so a junction-rooted or subst-rooted watch compares unequal no matter how carefully the strings are normalized. The first fix normalized paths and therefore remained effectively constant-true for exactly those projects, while its docstring claimed an os.path.isdir pre-check protected them - a branch that can only ever return True (deleted) and so protects nothing. Comparing directory identity asks the real question directly and needs no normalization.
- **Tests Added**: Yes

The rewritten negative test (a spy on _generate_observed_path_deleted_event, replacing one that early-returned on OSError and could pass without evaluating its own assertion) was mutation-verified: green against the fix, red against a restored constant-true predicate. Measured against the real Win32 API - junction root verdict moved from True (deleted) to False (healthy); subst drive root W: likewise True to False; deleted, moved, and moved-and-replaced roots all still correctly report True. Full suite 1630 passed, 0 failed (3 skipped, 3 xfailed). ### PD-BUG-143

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Configuration System (merge / from_env)
- **Repro**: Write a project config file that sets a field to a non-default value, for example move_detect_delay: 25 (default 10) or dry_run_mode: true (default false). Set the matching environment variable to the field default: LINKWATCHER_MOVE_DETECT_DELAY=10 or LINKWATCHER_DRY_RUN_MODE=false. Start the daemon and inspect the effective configuration.
- **Expected**: The environment variable outranks the config file, so the effective value is the one taken from the environment (10, or dry run disabled), per FDD 3-1-1-BR-6.
- **Actual**: The config file value survives (25, or dry run still enabled). merge in settings.py compares each incoming value against the dataclass default and skips it when equal, so the environment value is discarded with no warning.
- **Evidence**: Found 2026-08-27 during Bug Triage (PF-TSK-041) of PD-BUG-142, while measuring how the daemon resolves move_detect_delay. Measured directly: with the config file at move_detect_delay 25 and LINKWATCHER_MOVE_DETECT_DELAY=10, the resolved effective value is 25.0, not 10.0. The mechanism is generic - merge in src/linkwatcher/config/settings.py compares every incoming value against a fresh LinkWatcherConfig instance - so all fields carry the same defect, not only move_detect_delay. The dry_run_mode case looks the most damaging: a config file that enables dry run cannot be disabled from the environment, so the daemon silently performs no reference updates while appearing configured to do so. Triage should confirm the severity and the filed Dims estimate of DA, and decide whether the fix is a sentinel-based merge that tracks which fields were explicitly set, or a from_env that returns only the fields actually present in the environment.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-27 (PF-TSK-041), human approved as recommended. Priority Medium HELD, scope S, Dims CORRECTED DA to AC OB CQ, related feature 0.1.3 held, workflow WF-006 held. Validity CONFIRMED by direct measurement across six fields spanning bool, float and str: file move_detect_delay 25 with env 10 resolves 25 (control: env 30 resolves 30.0); file dry_run_mode true with env false resolves True; file create_backups true with env false resolves True; file log_level DEBUG with env INFO resolves DEBUG; file initial_scan_enabled false with env true resolves False. Mechanism confirmed in source: merge in settings.py compares every incoming value against a fresh LinkWatcherConfig instance and skips it when equal. Description corrected on five points. (a) UNDERSTATED, and decisively: this is not a corner case. For all 17 boolean fields, 44 percent of the 39-field schema, the environment can NEVER reverse a config-file setting in either direction, because a boolean has only two values, the default and not-the-default, so whenever the file sets the meaningful one the counter-value from the environment IS the default and is discarded. Measured against the SHIPPED config-examples/production-config.yaml: LINKWATCHER_ENABLE_DART_PARSER=true, LINKWATCHER_ENABLE_GENERIC_PARSER=on and LINKWATCHER_COLORED_OUTPUT=true are all discarded, while from_env alone reads all three correctly, proving the loss is at merge and not at parsing. Non-bool fields hit the defect only on the narrow equals-default case the report describes. (b) SHARPENED: dry_run_mode is the worst field not because it is damaging but because there is no escape - the CLI --dry-run only sets True and there is no --no-dry-run, so with the environment blocked by this bug, once a config file sets dry_run_mode true, editing the file is the only way out. (c) The silent claim is CONFIRMED but for a different reason than filed: a startup banner does report dry-run, backups and initial-scan, but print_startup_info returns early when colored_output is false (main.py line 243) and the shipped production config sets exactly that, and it is a print to stdout rather than a log record. Combined with the PD-BUG-142 finding that the daemon publishes no effective-config surface, the operator has no reliable signal. (d) BOUND: the CLI layer is NOT affected, because main.py applies CLI args by direct attribute assignment and never through merge, and the defaults-to-file merge is a no-op case since the base is the plain defaults. Env-over-file is the SOLE live surface. (e) Doc side-effect to fold into the fix: the handbook troubleshooting entry Environment variable ignored blames naming and format only, so a user hitting this is sent down a dead end, and the bool type table documents only the truthy tokens and never how to set a boolean false. Priority Medium held over a strict matrix reading of High (Impact High x Frequency Low) for consistency with PD-BUG-142, a strictly narrower instance of the same class held at Medium one day earlier on the same reasoning: the environment layer is effectively unused here, with zero config-field env vars in any launcher, installer, hook or the project own config (the only two in real use, LINKWATCHER_PROJECT_REGISTRY and LINKWATCHER_INSTALL_DIR, are not LinkWatcherConfig fields at all); the primary surface, the config file, is entirely unaffected; nothing is corrupted or lost; and the workaround is free. Dims corrected out of DA because the documentation is right and the code is wrong and the fix lands in code: AC for an implementation that contradicts its own module docstring and FDD 3.1.1-BR-6, OB for a failure with no warning and no log record, CQ for a merge that conflates explicitly-set with differs-from-default. Scope S with the fix fork RESOLVED: track explicitly-set fields rather than inferring from value not-equal default, recording assignments in __setattr__ so that both _from_dict and from_env are captured automatically since each builds cls() then setattr, with merge falling back to the current rule when no record exists, which preserves behavior for programmatically constructed configs and keeps test_merge_configurations green; underscore-prefixed keys must be excluded from to_dict and from the merge loops so the tracker never leaks into a saved config file. The second candidate the report names, a from_env that returns only the fields present in the environment, changes a public return type and fixes only the env layer. No existing test pins the buggy behavior: test_merge_configurations asserts create_backups is False where both sides are already the default, a vacuous assertion that passes either way. NOT a duplicate and NOT a reopen: PD-BUG-123, 127, 128 and 129 are the same feature and the same silent-config-failure family, but all four are validate() hardcoding versus schema derivation, whereas this is merge() - a different function and a different mechanism. Effort about 1 session. SEQUENCE BEFORE PD-BUG-142: that bug approved M path has the daemon publish its effective move_detect_delay for the panel to read, and with this bug unfixed the published value is itself wrong for the env-set-to-default case, so 142 would inherit the defect. Two observations recorded, not filed: print_startup_info gates the whole config banner on colored_output, which should control colour and not whether information is shown; and the CLI layer can only ever turn booleans on, which is desired new behaviour rather than a defect.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-27
- **Fix**: Record explicit intent instead of inferring it. __post_init__ creates an empty explicit-field record and __setattr__ adds to it, so every assignment made after construction is recorded - which is exactly how _from_dict and from_env build their instances, so both configuration layers are captured with NO change to either constructor. The record deliberately does not exist during the generated __init__ (the guard is load-bearing: a record present during __init__ would mark all 39 fields explicit on a plain LinkWatcherConfig, and from_env would then override every field of the config it is merged into). merge now overrides on membership of that record, falling back to the historical value-vs-default rule when the other config recorded nothing, so programmatic LinkWatcherConfig(field=value) callers are unaffected. merge copies fields with object.__setattr__ rather than setattr, because copying is not the caller expressing intent, and sets the result record to the union of both inputs so a merged config can be merged again without overwriting its target. The record is not declared as a dataclass field, so dataclasses.fields()-derived coverage (the validation helpers, the schema drift test, the docs generator) is untouched, and to_dict plus both merge loops skip underscore-prefixed keys so it can never be written into a saved config file. Behavior-preserving for file-only configuration: a file value equal to the default was previously dropped as a no-op against a defaults base and is now applied, the same value either way. Documentation corrected: PD-INT-009 had documented this defect as a semantic subtlety of the merge algorithm; the Configuration Guide boolean type row never said how to set a boolean false; TE-TSP-037 gained unit and integration scenario rows; the 0.1.3 state file carries the full fix record. FDD and TDD are N-A, verified - Tier 1 feature, neither exists. No ADR: the change restores documented behaviour rather than making a new architectural commitment, and the mechanism is local to one dataclass.
- **Root Cause**: merge() in src/linkwatcher/config/settings.py had no record of which fields were actually set, so it inferred intent from value not-equal default. from_env builds a full LinkWatcherConfig and mutates only the fields whose environment variable is present, so by the time merge saw the object, set-to-the-default and never-set were byte-identical - the environment value was discarded and the config file won, contradicting FDD 3.1.1-BR-6 and the class docstring in settings.py. Booleans made it structural rather than a corner case: a boolean has only two values, so whenever a file set the meaningful one the counter-value from the environment WAS the default, and for all 17 boolean fields (44 percent of the 39-field schema) the environment could never reverse a file setting in either direction. dry_run_mode had no escape at all, since --dry-run only sets True and there is no --no-dry-run. Bound confirmed at fix time: the CLI layer never goes through merge (main.load_config assigns attributes directly), so env-over-file was the sole live surface, and the Step 20 sweep found merge to be the only site in the codebase inferring explicit intent from a default comparison.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: 13 regression tests added to test_config.py in TestBug143ExplicitlySetValuesSurviveMerge. Fail-first discipline observed and recorded: 6 failed and 7 passed PRE-fix, each failure inspected and confirmed red for the right reason (the config file value visibly surviving the environment, not a setup or import error), and the schema-derived breadth test named all 17 boolean fields. POST-fix 13 of 13 pass. Full suite 1623 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failed, re-run after formatting. Verified end to end through main.load_config, the real daemon entry path: file dry_run_mode true plus LINKWATCHER_DRY_RUN_MODE=false now resolves False, log_level DEBUG to INFO now honoured, and the non-default control is unchanged. Test tracking synced 93 to 106 and the scoped Validate-TestTracking run reports 0 errors and 0 warnings. No E2E re-execution flagged, human-approved: the -WhatIf blast radius is 13 entries across 8 features and 5 workflows, while the only LINKWATCHER_ variable appearing anywhere in the E2E fixtures is LINKWATCHER_INSTALL_DIR, which is not a config field - every fixture is file-driven and the fix is behavior-preserving for file-driven configs. Same reasoning as the human-approved PD-BUG-129 decision. One disclosure: black added a magic trailing comma to a pre-existing line in TestConfigurationIntegration, the only pre-existing line changed, kept because reverting would leave the file failing the lint gate.
- **Verification**: Code review 2026-08-27 (PF-TSK-005): code APPROVED, not closed - one Minor finding left in the declared documentation scope. Verified independently: fail-first record reproduced exactly (6 failed / 7 passed against the historical merge, each failure red for the stated reason and the breadth test naming all 17 boolean fields); all three load-bearing guards mutation-verified, each turning the correct test red (eager explicit record 4 red, merge recording its copies 1 red, to_dict filter removed 2 red); 29-check probe through main.load_config clean including both boolean directions, CLI precedence, DEFAULT_CONFIG non-pollution and no bookkeeping leak into saved YAML or JSON; full suite 1630 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failed; flake8, black and isort clean; no dependency changes; FDD 3.1.1-BR-6 citation confirmed; merge confirmed the only default-comparison inference site. FINDING (Minor, blocks close): doc/user/handbooks/configuration-guide.md line 419, the troubleshooting entry Environment variable ignored, still states that boolean values must be true, 1, yes or on. That contradicts the type table at line 229 which this fix corrected, and preserves the exact dead end triage folded in as documentation item (e): a user disabling a boolean from the environment is told those four are the only valid values. Remaining scope is that one sentence - state that any other value disables the setting and that false is what to use. Observations, not blocking: dataclasses.replace silently drops the explicit record (no caller today, consistent with the documented keyword-build contract); merge copies sets but aliases dicts, pre-existing; the test-tracking row for test_config.py has an accurate count of 106 but Notes, Run and Last-Updated cells unmaintained since 2026-08-18 across PD-BUG-127, 129 and 143.
- **Fix**: Follow-up 2026-08-27 completing the declared documentation scope after code review: the Configuration Guide troubleshooting entry Environment variable ignored (line 419) still claimed that boolean values must be true, 1, yes or on, contradicting the environment-variable type table at line 229 that this same fix corrected. It now states that those four tokens enable a setting and any other value disables it, and that false turns off a setting the config file turned on - wording matched to the coercion in from_env. Guide frontmatter 1.3 to 1.4, updated 2026-08-27. The 0.1.3 state file Documentation bullet was amended so it no longer claims more than was done. No code change in this follow-up.

### PD-BUG-147

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: User documentation (capabilities reference)
- **Repro**: Open doc/user/handbooks/linkwatcher-capabilities-reference.md at the Watch Liveness Monitoring section and read the sentinel-lifetime bullet. Compare against git tag --sort=-creatordate (latest v2.1.3) and the untracked status of src/linkwatcher/liveness.py.
- **Expected**: The bullet describes only behavior a reader can have observed in a shipped release, or states plainly that watch liveness is new in this release.
- **Actual**: It attributes stranded-probe behavior to earlier versions, none of which had a liveness probe at all.
- **Evidence**: Code review (PF-TSK-005) of the PD-BUG-145 delta, 2026-08-27. Site: doc/user/handbooks/linkwatcher-capabilities-reference.md line 195. Latest release tag v2.1.3; pyproject version 2.2.0 unreleased; src/linkwatcher/liveness.py still untracked. The file uses the same contrast-with-old-behavior convention elsewhere (line 48, PD-BUG-135) where the prior behavior did ship, so the convention is sound - only this instance is unfounded.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-27 via Bug Triage (PF-TSK-041), human approved as recommended: priority Low HELD, scope S assigned (was blank), related feature 1.1.1 held, Dims DA held, workflows WF-008 and WF-010 held with a caveat. Validity confirmed, all four cited facts verified - the clause exists at capabilities-reference lines 195-197; the watch-liveness feature is unreleased (latest tag v2.1.3 dated 2026-06-22, pyproject at 2.2.0, src/linkwatcher/liveness.py still untracked); a sweep of Earlier versions / older versions / used to / no longer / old behaviour across doc/user/handbooks returned exactly one unfounded retrospective; and the Issue Classification and Routing Guide line 75 confirms product bug as the tracker, since a wrong statement in a user handbook is a product-doc fix despite the doc/ path. Priority Low HELD over a defensible raise. The case for raising, recorded and rejected: this project raised PD-BUG-138 from Medium to High on the reasoning that a silently passing test is worse than a failing one, and the same false-assurance shape applies here, because a reader on v2.1.x can infer their daemon already has stall detection - the exact false assurance PD-BUG-139 exists to eliminate. Held Low because the inference chain is long and indirect (the sentence says sentinel CLEANUP differed, not that the feature existed), no behaviour is wrong (nothing corrupted, dropped or left behind), the consequence is self-correcting since a reader who goes looking finds nothing and no destructive action is available on the strength of it, and unlike PD-BUG-138 nothing is institutionalized in an approved artifact and no release path is gated (1.1.1 is Completed with All Passing). Matrix: Impact Low x Frequency Low. Description corrected on three points. UNDERSTATED, and the decisive one: the filed harm (readers told to expect a leftover they cannot have) is trivial; the consequential harm is that this clause is the ONLY version-dating statement in the whole Watch Liveness Monitoring section and it dates the feature backwards, and there is no CHANGELOG in this repo so the handbook is the only place a user learns what is new - the fix must close that reading, not merely delete a sentence. PRECISION on the supporting claim: v2.1.3 predates BOTH fixes (PD-BUG-135 landed 2026-08-19/20, PD-BUG-145 on 2026-08-27), so what separates the founded line-48 wording from this unfounded one is whether the CONTRASTED PRIOR BEHAVIOUR shipped - the buggy parser did, the sentinel never existed - not whether the fix shipped; stated explicitly so a fixer does not over-correct and strip lines 48 and 100 too. SCOPE verified as genuinely one site: the parallel passage in tdd-1-1-1-file-system-monitoring-t2.md lines 277-287 explains the same design without any retrospective version claim, and capabilities-reference lines 74 and 87 are positive capability statements rather than retrospective contrasts. Scope S: one-clause rewrite in one file, no code, no tests, effort 10-15 min. Dims DA held - the code is right and the documentation is wrong, so no code dimension applies. Related feature 1.1.1 held, consistent with PD-BUG-145 on the same subsystem (the liveness contract is FDD edge case 1.1.1-EC-11 and liveness.py sits in the 1.1.1 code inventory). Workflows WF-008 and WF-010 HELD but recorded as DOCUMENTATION SUBJECT MATTER (the stop paths the sentence describes), not behavioural blast radius: no workflow behaviour changes and NO E2E re-execution is implied by this fix. Not a duplicate and not a reopen of PD-BUG-145 - that fix is neither regressed nor incomplete in its functional intent; this is a NEW inaccuracy it introduced while correcting two other statements, so per the Reopen Workflow different-root-cause rule a new bug is correct. Next step: Bug Fixing S-scope quick path, worth landing before the 2.2.0 release since the defect is precisely a false claim about pre-2.2.0 behaviour.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-27
- **Fix**: Two edits in doc/user/handbooks/linkwatcher-capabilities-reference.md, no source code touched. (a) Section heading marked with the version the feature ships in - Watch Liveness Monitoring (v2.2) - following the version-marker convention the same file already uses at line 138 for another new capability. (b) The unfounded retrospective clause at lines 195-197 (Earlier versions removed the sentinel only on a clean stop ... surfaced as an untracked dotfile in the repository root) replaced by the design rationale it was carrying, stated without any historical claim: Cleanup cannot rest on shutdown alone for exactly that reason - a hard kill runs no Python shutdown code, so the startup sweep is the layer that covers a daemon killed mid-probe. BOTH edits were needed: deleting the false sentence alone would have removed the wrong date but left the section undated, so a v2.1.x reader would still assume a feature they do not have, which the triage correction identified as the consequential harm.
- **Root Cause**: Author frame versus reader frame. The clause was written during the PD-BUG-145 fix, where earlier correctly meant earlier in the same unreleased development cycle - the probe genuinely did survive hard kills in the pre-fix build. But a user handbook addresses users of RELEASES, and in that frame earlier versions reads as earlier releases, none of which had a liveness probe at all (latest tag v2.1.3 dated 2026-06-22, pyproject at unreleased 2.2.0, src/linkwatcher/liveness.py still untracked). Nothing in the section marked the feature as new, so nothing cued the reader to correct the misreading. Not a wording slip: the same author frame produced a CORRECT internal record in the 1.1.1 state file, where the internal audience makes the same contrast founded.
- **Tests Added**: No - deliberate human-approved exception; see VerificationNotes
- **Verification**: S-scope quick path: human-approved at the Step 11 combined checkpoint (fix approach) and again at the Step 23 checkpoint (fix results). Full regression suite run after the edits - 1698 passed, 3 skipped, 6 deselected, 3 xfailed, exit 0. The doc-only change was confirmed test-neutral by evidence rather than assumption: test_configschemadrift.py line 29 states that capabilities-reference handbooks are intentionally partial and not tested. Residue verified by reading the enclosing section (lines 164-211) end to end rather than grepping for anticipated residue - no orphaned lead-in, no stranded clause, and the bullet now flows from sentinel-per-probe through the hard-kill window and the startup sweep to why shutdown alone is insufficient. Post-fix sweep of Earlier versions / older versions / earlier release across doc/user/handbooks now returns nothing. Step 20 sibling check clean: the TDD parallel passage at tdd-1-1-1-file-system-monitoring-t2.md lines 277-287 carries no retrospective version claim, and the 1.1.1 state file entry plus FDD edge case EC-11 describe the fix and the contract rather than a shipped history. Step 21 manual validation test skipped under its own criteria - no observable UI or filesystem behaviour. Step 27 documentation updates N/A with each document checked individually (state file, TDD, test spec line 74, FDD EC-11); no ADR trigger, since no architectural behaviour, pattern or trade-off changed. Dims DA satisfied on both counts - no surviving statement is contradicted by release state, and the v2.2 marker positively dates the feature. No regression test added: the repaired artifact is prose with no executable contract, the Step 15 instruction-medium escape does not apply (feature 1.1.1 medium is code and this is not an agent-executed instruction artifact), and a guard for the class would be framework tooling rather than product code. Observation recorded but out of scope: the other unreleased sections in the same handbook carry no version marker either - the same undated-section gap, without the false claim.

### PD-BUG-140

- **Source**: E2ETesting
- **Environment**: Testing
- **Component**: Control Panel shutdown orchestration
- **Repro**: Move a directory containing referenced files inside a project managed by the panel, then close the panel window within a second or two. The daemon holds the batched directory delete awaiting matching creates under the new parent; the drain reaches its bound and terminates the daemon first.
- **Expected**: Per FDD 7-1-1-BR-3 the drain lets buffered move-correlations finish before terminating the daemon.
- **Actual**: The directory correlation window of up to 300 seconds is far beyond the 20 s grace period that FDD 7-1-1-BR-7 imposes, so the drain always terminates first and the reference updates for every file under the moved directory are lost.
- **Evidence**: Raised while fixing PD-BUG-136 on 2026-08-25. The PD-BUG-136 fix reads move_detect_delay and clamps the required idle span to grace minus headroom, which covers the per-file case at default settings but is structurally unable to cover the directory case. Options for triage: accept as a documented known limitation, surface a warning when the panel closes during a pending directory correlation, or revisit the cooperative stop sentinel the FDD deferred as out of MVP scope.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-25 (PF-TSK-041), human approved. Tracker confirmed correct per the Issue Classification and Routing Guide: broken existing behavior, since FDD 7-1-1-BR-3 names buffered move-correlations as work the drain must let finish and the directory case does not. That one candidate resolution (cooperative stop sentinel) is new functionality does not reclassify it. Priority Medium per matrix: impact Medium (updates dropped, never corrupted - atomic writes bound the damage; detectable and recoverable via a validate run, so a workaround exists), frequency Low (needs a directory move mid-correlation plus a panel close inside the grace period). Reconciles with PD-BUG-136 being High: same Medium impact there, but deterministic hence High frequency. Strict-reading counterargument (silent loss of all updates under a moved directory reads as High severity) was considered and rejected on workaround and rarity grounds. Scope M: the first move is a design decision between document, warn-on-close, and cooperative stop sentinel - not code. Dims add OB: the loss is silent today, the user gets no signal. Workflows add WF-002, the workflow whose promise actually breaks. Flag for the fix task: BR-3 and BR-7 are in genuine tension and two of the three options resolve it in the FDD rather than in code; warn-on-close is symptom mitigation, not a root-cause fix. Effort: 1 design checkpoint plus 1 session.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-25
- **Fix**: Resolved as a DOCUMENTATION CORRECTION (Option A, checkpoint-approved 2026-08-26): no product code changed, because the defect is in the specification rather than in the drain. FDD 7-1-1-BR-3 amended - draining is now stated as bounded by BR-7 and limited to work the quiescence signal can observe, with BR-7 winning where the two conflict; new FDD 7-1-1-EC-12 records the unobservable-in-flight-work edge case, its bounded damage and the validation-scan recovery path. TDD section 4.4 residual and section 9 Known Issues rewritten with the measured mechanism; new design decision D-T7 records the BR-3 versus BR-7 resolution and why warn-on-close, a daemon-side heartbeat and the cooperative stop sentinel were each rejected. User handbook how-the-control-panel-stops-daemons corrected - it had told users that every directory move was at risk - and given actionable guidance plus the validate recovery. Feature 7.1.1 Known Limitations row and the PD-BUG-136 residual note restated. New test file test_pd_bug_140_directory_correlation_drain_boundary.py (TE-TST-158, UNIT-L18, 5 tests, ALL mutation-verified) pins the corrected claims so the documents cannot drift back into fiction. Full suite 1583 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, no regressions. E2E deliberately NOT flagged for re-execution: zero behaviour delta, and the 9 affected entries across 5 features are already Needs Re-execution. TITLE CORRECTED 2026-08-26 (hand-edit, human-approved): was "Directory-move correlation window exceeds any bounded panel drain", which stated the very premise this fix refuted; the title carried the wrong mechanism to anyone scanning the tracker. Update-BugStatus.ps1 has no -Title parameter, so the row title cannot be changed through the script that owns every other field - filed as a framework improvement.
- **Root Cause**: The filed premise was wrong, and was reproduced empirically before any fix. A directory correlation that has matched at least one file settles within dir_move_settle_delay (5 s) of the last match, inside the drain required idle span of 10 s, so it IS covered - measured, a 15 s inter-create gap still held the drain to 15.6 s with the move processed. dir_move_max_timeout (300 s) bounds only the ZERO-MATCH state. The real defect is the silent window between dir_move_buffered and the first matched creation: the daemon writes nothing while it waits, so its log signature is byte-for-byte an idle daemon and watch_quiescence reports a CLEAN stop while the correlation is still open. That window opens only for a slow-STARTING move (cross-volume or network copy, very large tree, loaded machine); a genuine directory deletion also sits in that state but costs nothing, doing in-memory bookkeeping only via _process_dir_true_delete. The root cause is therefore specification-level: FDD BR-3 promised that buffered move-correlations are allowed to finish, which no drain bounded by BR-7 can guarantee for work the daemon does not make observable.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Updated**: 2026-08-26
- **Verification**: Code review REJECTED 2026-08-27 (PF-TSK-005), human-approved. Bug-fix review of the documentation-correction fix, scoped to Dims DI + OB; PE/AX/SE marked N/A. Change set reviewed in isolation from the mixed working tree: FDD BR-3 amendment + new EC-12 (the only uncommitted FDD delta), TDD 4.4 residual bullet + section 9 Known Issues + new D-T7, the Control Panel stop-daemons explanation handbook, the 7.1.1 state-file Known Limitations row and PD-BUG-136 residual restatement, and the new test file. PD-BUG-136/137/132 deltas sharing the TDD were seam-checked only, not re-reviewed. VERIFIED AND NOT TO BE REDONE: the corrected mechanism holds against source - _reset_settle_timer fires dir_move_settle_delay (5 s) after the last match, inside the drain 10 s required idle span (dir_move_detector.py:293); dir_move_max_timeout bounds only the zero-match state; _process_dir_true_delete is in-memory bookkeeping only; and every match additionally emits an INFO metric via log_metric, so a matched correlation re-arms the drain window - the coverage claim therefore holds by two independent mechanisms, not the one the TDD names. dir_move_buffered is confirmed as the terminal record before the first match. The dropped-never-corrupted claim is grounded in the atomic tempfile-plus-rename write in updater.py:743. Residue sweep for the refuted 300 s premise is clean across doc/ and test/ - the one surviving instance (7.1.1 state file line 875) is deliberately preserved under an inline correction. Tests: the 5 new tests pass, flake8 and isort clean, full suite 1600 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed with no regressions. FINDINGS TO ADDRESS. F1 (Major) - the impossibility claim in FDD 7-1-1-EC-12 and TDD D-T7 is not supported by the code. Both state that closing this edge case would require a cooperative stop contract in which the daemon reports its pending work. The daemon already reports it: dir_move_buffered is written at INFO, and the panel already reads log CONTENT rather than only the stat signature, in log_tail.py:136 (open + seek + read). A panel reading the newest record could distinguish an open zero-match correlation from an idle daemon with no daemon-side protocol, no heartbeat and no re-arming. D-T7 weighs and rejects three alternatives (warn-on-close, daemon-side heartbeat, stop sentinel) but never considers this fourth one, which is also the only one that addresses the OB half of the bug - the false-clean signal that makes the loss silent. Not verified end to end by the reviewer (log format text vs JSON, rotation, concurrent projects), so the finding is that the claim is overstated as written, NOT that a working fix exists. Resolve by either substantiating the impossibility, or rewording EC-12 and D-T7 to say the panel would have to parse log content rather than stat signatures and record why that was rejected, or reopening the option. This is in scope precisely because the fix existed to stop the documents asserting what the code does not support. F2 (Minor) - stale frontmatter on all three corrected documents. The FDD reads version 1.0 / updated 2026-06-29 while now carrying a 2026-08-26 business-rule amendment; the TDD reads 1.7 / 2026-08-18; the handbook reads 1.0 / 2026-08-18. Artifact locking is disabled in this project, so nothing auto-stamps these. F3 (Minor) - test_settle_delay_fits_inside_the_drains_required_idle_span couples only one of the two constants it claims to couple. It reads LinkWatcherConfig().dir_move_settle_delay but hardcodes CORRELATION_WINDOW = 10.0 plus both panel defaults, so lowering move_detect_delay or grace_period_seconds leaves it green while the documented guarantee silently becomes false. Both are readable defaults (LinkWatcherConfig.move_detect_delay, PanelSettings), so the fix is one line each in the style the file already uses. F4 (Minor) - test_zero_match_window_lets_the_drain_stop_cleanly stipulates the property it claims to pin. It hand-builds a frozen LogStat and injects it via log_stat; no assertion observes the detector actual log output. Its docstring claims that if the test goes red the drain has gained a way to see pending directory correlations, but it cannot go red for that reason: adding the very heartbeat D-T7 rejects would leave it green. Close with a caplog-based assertion that the detector emits nothing between dir_move_buffered and the first match. F5 (Minor) - the new test file fails the project black pre-commit hook (one wrapped call near line 168 that black joins into a single line under the configured length). flake8 and isort pass. OBSERVATION, not filed and not part of this fix: in _process_dir_move, _resolve_unmatched_files fires _on_true_file_delete BEFORE _on_dir_move, so for a slow-but-matched directory move late-arriving files may be dropped from the database before the directory-level rewrite runs. Pre-existing detector behaviour; needs verification before it earns a bug ID. POSITIVE: the correction refutes its own filed premise with measurement rather than defending it, preserves the original wrong text under an inline correction instead of quietly rewriting history, fixes a handbook that had over-warned users that every directory move was at risk, and records rejected alternatives with reasons in D-T7. Well above the bar for a documentation fix - F1 is a gap in one claim, not a verdict on the approach.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-27
- **Fix**: REWORK 2026-08-27 closing all five code-review findings (Bug Fixing PF-TSK-007, re-entry from a REJECTED review); Option A (documentation correction, no product code) re-confirmed at checkpoint. F1: the impossibility claim was corrected on its true grounds in THREE places, one more than the review named - FDD 7-1-1-EC-12, TDD D-T7, and the 7.1.1 state file Known Limitations row, which carried the identical claim. The documents had conflated REPORTING with HOLDING: the daemon ALREADY reports the pending work (dir_move_buffered at INFO with deleted_dir and known_file_count, plus terminal records dir_move_processing / directory_true_delete / dir_move_max_timeout), so the old wording - that closing the edge case needs a cooperative stop contract in which the daemon reports its pending work - was false. What survives is the DATA-LOSS half, restated correctly: BR-7 bounds the drain while an unmatched correlation is bounded by nothing inside it, so no bounded drain can wait it out however well informed; closing it needs the daemon to HOLD its stop. The OBSERVABILITY half does NOT survive: detecting the condition needs no daemon-side contract at all. D-T7 now carries it as a fourth alternative, viable and DEFERRED rather than rejected, with the wrong old reasoning quoted and corrected in place; the TDD section 9 Known Issues bullet now separates the accepted loss from the not-accepted invisibility. Two corrections to the reviewer, both verified: the terminal record before the silent window is the dir_move_batch_size performance metric (dir_move_detector.py line 122), not dir_move_buffered which fires immediately before it; and the panel reads log CONTENT only in log_tail.py, NOT in the drain, which consumes only the LogStat path/size/mtime signature - so this is real work rather than a wiring change, and D-T7 now says so. The deferred capability is filed as PD-FRQ-020 and cited by ID in the FDD, TDD and state file, per the rule that deferred work named in prose must carry a tracked row. F2: frontmatter stamped - FDD 1.0 to 1.1, TDD 1.7 to 1.8, handbook 1.0 to 1.1, test file 1.1 to 1.2, all updated 2026-08-27. F3: the test now reads PanelSettings and LinkWatcherConfig defaults instead of hardcoding 10.0 / 3.0 / 20.0. F4: the zero-match test now installs a recording logger and observes what the detector actually emits - exactly two records at buffering, then nothing during the wait - instead of asserting against a hand-built LogStat. F5: black clean under the project pre-commit args (line-length 100); the earlier E501 noise was an artifact of running flake8 without the project max-line-length of 120.
- **Root Cause**: Root cause of the REVIEW FINDINGS (the original bug root cause stands as reviewed and was not redone): the first pass reasoned about the drain surface only. Because watch_quiescence consumes just the LogStat signature, it concluded the panel had no pending-work signal at all, and never checked either whether the daemon emits one or whether the panel process reads log content elsewhere. Both were true, and the unchecked assumption then propagated verbatim into three documents. F3 and F4 share a different root cause: both assertions were written to restate a conclusion already reached rather than derived from a condition under which they would fail, so both were green by construction - F3 hardcoded the very constants whose relationship it claimed to pin, and F4 asserted against a hand-built stand-in rather than observed output.
- **Tests Added**: Yes - no new test file; the 5 existing tests in test_pd_bug_140_directory_correlation_drain_boundary.py (TE-TST-158) were strengthened, and both re-derived assertions were mutation-verified.
- **Verification**: Full suite 1630 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, exit 0, no regressions. Mutation evidence recorded in the test file header where the next reader meets it: lowering move_detect_delay to 3.0 turns test 1 RED where the old hardcoded 10.0 left it GREEN; injecting a dir_move_pending_heartbeat timer into handle_directory_deleted turns test 3 RED where the old hand-built LogStat left it GREEN. Both mutated sources were restored and verified byte-identical by diff against pre-mutation backups. Validate-TestTracking.ps1 scoped to the touched file: 0 errors, 0 warnings. black, flake8 and isort clean under the project pre-commit args. E2E deliberately NOT flagged for re-execution: zero behaviour delta, documentation and tests only. Reviewer OBSERVATION on _resolve_unmatched_files firing _on_true_file_delete before _on_dir_move remains unverified and unfiled, explicitly out of scope for this fix.
- **Verification**: Code review REJECTED 2026-08-27 (PF-TSK-005), human-approved. Re-review of the 2026-08-27 rework, scoped to the F1-F5 fix delta; the prior pass verified-and-not-to-be-redone list was seam-checked only, not re-reviewed. Dims DI + OB; PE/AX/SE and acceptance-criteria validation N/A. CLOSED AND VERIFIED, NOT TO BE REDONE: F1 corrected in three places (FDD 7-1-1-EC-12, TDD D-T7, and the 7.1.1 state file Known Limitations row), with the report-versus-hold distinction accurate against source and the wrong reasoning quoted and corrected in place rather than silently rewritten. Both counter-corrections to the reviewer were independently confirmed and are accepted: dir_move_batch_size (dir_move_detector.py line 123) is the true terminal record before the silent window, emitted at INFO through log_metric, and watch_quiescence consumes only the LogStat path/size/mtime signature while log_tail.py is a separate surface, so a targeted warn-on-close is real work rather than a wiring change. F2 frontmatter stamped (FDD 1.1, TDD 1.8, handbook 1.1, test file 1.2). F3 test 1 now couples both constants through the real _required_idle_span, so its mutation claim is derivable by inspection. F4 test 3 now observes real detector emissions through a recording logger and honestly bounds its own reach. F5 black, isort and flake8 all clean under the project pre-commit args. Full suite re-run by the reviewer: 1630 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, matching the claimed numbers exactly. Validate-TestTracking clean for the touched file (the 27 project-wide warnings are pre-existing and unrelated). FINDINGS TO ADDRESS. F6 (Major) - the deferral rationale in TDD D-T7 and in the PD-FRQ-020 Notes cell asserts that the PD-BUG-142 approved fix already builds a daemon-to-panel publication channel. It does not. The PD-BUG-142 fix notes record that its scope was REASSESSED DOWNWARD at Step 13 with human approval, precisely because neither approved channel closes the class: the lock body is parsed as a bare integer by four readers across two languages, and a log record inherits the log_level residual. The shipped read_correlation_window resolves configuration in-process (LinkWatcherConfig, then merge of from_file, then merge of from_env), and its own docstring states that closing the residual would still need the daemon to publish its effective configuration on a channel the panel can read - future tense, not built. Triage approved the channel; the fix abandoned it. This is the same class of unchecked assertion as F1, reintroduced inside the correction written to close F1. F7 (Major) - D-T7 declares viable an option that the same TDD declares non-working. The deferred option reads dir_move_buffered at INFO, but TDD section 4.4 lines 246-247 already record that a project running at log_level WARNING or ERROR emits no INFO records at all, and cite exactly this (a log record inherits the log_level residual) as the reason the log channel does not work. Confirmed at source: the logger level gates before handlers (logging.py line 469), so the record is never written, and the proposed detection would be blind precisely where section 4.4 says drain protection is already lost entirely. The deferred option write-up does not name this limitation, so two sections of one TDD contradict each other. ### PD-BUG-148

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Control Panel registry discovery
- **Repro**: 1. At FrameworkBuilder (role framework-builder, self-hosted: face at blueprint/process-framework, central registry process-framework-central/framework-registry.json with a frameworks mapping) set integration.linkwatcher_control_panel_auto_open true in doc/project-config.json. 2. Run process-framework/tools/linkWatcher/start_linkwatcher_hook_wrapper.ps1 (the SessionStart hook) - it launches control_panel.py --project-root <FB root>. 3. panel.log: WARNING registry unresolved: Could not locate project-registry.json; the panel opens in the error state.
- **Expected**: The panel resolves central through the framework canonical role-derived locator (Get-ChildRegistryInfo: framework-registry.json/frameworks at a framework-builder, project-registry.json/projects at a framework), finds the pointer under the workspace declared paths.process_framework (blueprint/process-framework at a self-hosted root, whose pointer already points at itself), and lists the launching workspace own daemon alongside its registered children.
- **Actual**: Tier-4 discovery fails at its first step (no process-framework/ directory at FB); even with --registry pointing at framework-registry.json, load_projects rejects it (no projects mapping); the FB daemon that IS running (lock-file PID verified) has no row. The three override sources (--registry, LINKWATCHER_PROJECT_REGISTRY, panel-config.yaml) are install-level/global, so pointing them anywhere would also hijack appdev zero-config launches - no clean per-workspace workaround exists.
- **Evidence**: Filed cross-project from FrameworkBuilder (FWK-FB) while re-enabling LinkWatcher autostart under PF-PRO-071 WI-11 (2026-08-28). Installed LinkWatcher v2.2.0 at ~/bin; panel.log 2026-08-28 10:22:00 WARNING registry unresolved. Code sites: linkwatcher_control_panel/settings.py _POINTER_RELPATH and _REGISTRY_RELPATH (:56) plus _discover_registry_via_pointer; discovery.py load_projects requires a projects mapping. The settings.py docstring claims it reuses the framework canonical where-is-central locator; that locator became role-derived (PF-PRO-067 Contract 4) and the panel diverged.
- **Triage**: Valid product bug, confirmed at code and environment level. Classification: fix lands in src/linkwatcher/linkwatcher_control_panel (settings.py, discovery.py) so product, not IMP; the feature-request reading was considered and rejected because the settings.py docstring and feature Decision 2 already promise to reuse the framework canonical where-is-central locator - the code hardcoded a snapshot of it, that locator became role-derived (Get-ChildRegistryInfo), so the fix repairs an existing claim rather than adding one. DESCRIPTION CORRECTION (measured 2026-08-28 with an isolated cwd so only --project-root is exercised, as the hook passes it): the blast radius is wider than filed and has TWO independent mechanisms. (1) Face location hardcoded to process-framework/ - FB declares paths.process_framework as blueprint/process-framework, so tier-4 dies at its first step (FB only; as filed). (2) The pointer is followed to the PARENT central and the registry name/shape is hardcoded to appdev: a parent-pointer names the parent, so joining process-framework-central/project-registry.json to it yields the parent registry. That is correct for a leaf (LinkWatcher to appdev list is its supervisor list) but wrong for every non-leaf - the appdev pointer names FB, and FB/process-framework-central/project-registry.json does not exist. Measured: FB UNRESOLVED, appdev UNRESOLVED, legal UNRESOLVED, LinkWatcher resolved. Net: zero-config discovery works ONLY at leaf project workspaces; the filed title is accurate, the filed description explains only the FB half. Bounding facts: row schemas are already compatible (name/path in both registries) so _project_from_entry needs no change; the no-workaround claim is right for FB (load_projects rejects framework-registry.json outright - no projects mapping) but overstated for appdev/legal, where a global panel-config override does restore function. Priority Medium not High: the impact x frequency matrix reads High, but per the severity-vs-priority rule severity is High while business urgency is not 24-hour - core link maintenance is untouched, the panel is a P3 convenience supervisor, and daemon state stays observable via the launcher scripts and logs. Scope M: spans settings.py and discovery.py, needs a design decision the panel has never faced (how it learns its own role and face - reading doc/project-config.json, which the panel does not read today), fixture work across test_panel_settings.py and test_panel_discovery.py, plus TDD 4.1 / docstring / a new Decision record. Dims: ID (central registry contract changed), EM (hardcoded snapshot instead of derived - the reason it broke), DA (docstring and TDD 4.1 claim behavior the code does not deliver). SCOPE BOUNDARY, human-approved 2026-08-28: listing the launching workspace own daemon is OUT of scope - it is free at FB via the FWK-FB self-row, but appdev and legal have no self-row in their own registries, and that is new behavior rather than a broken promise. No duplicates: PD-BUG-132/136/137/140/142 are Control Panel bugs, all unrelated.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-28
- **Fix**: Resolution is now workspace-shaped: a workspace that has children supervises from the child registry it owns under its own process-framework-central/ (either project-registry.json or framework-registry.json); a leaf owns none and resolves through the parent-pointer in its framework face to the registry its parent owns. load_projects accepts either the projects or the frameworks collection key - row schemas were already identical for the panel, which reads only name and path, so no row parsing changed. The face is read from paths.process_framework in doc/project-config.json with a process-framework fallback, never assumed. Both pointer filenames are accepted, canonical .framework-parent-pointer first, then legacy .framework-central-pointer. Own-registry lookup is deliberately confined to an explicit --project-root: a cwd walk-up would otherwise adopt a registry it merely walked past, and a producer blueprint tree carries a ZERO-ROW seed registry whose empty state reads as nothing running - worse than an error banner for a monitoring tool. SCOPE ADDED at the Step 20 sweep, human-approved at the Step 23 checkpoint: lifecycle.launcher_path carried the same hardcoded face assumption, so Start would have failed for a framework-builder workspace; latent only because such a workspace could never be listed, and this fix made it reachable, so shipping resolution alone would have delivered a panel that lists a workspace it cannot start. Both sites now resolve through one shared workspace_face_dir helper, a net REMOVAL of framework coupling. Verified against five real workspaces: FB resolves framework-registry.json (3 rows), appdev 4, legal 1, LinkWatcher 4, TimeTrackingV2 4, and every launcher path resolves to a file that exists; the LinkWatcher leaf result is identical to pre-fix, so the one path that worked is provably unchanged. Tests: 15 added across test_panel_settings.py, test_panel_discovery.py and test_panel_lifecycle.py, fail-first verified (5 failed pre-fix for the right reason, 5 guards passed pre-fix); panel suite 372 pass, full suite 1719 passed 3 skipped 3 xfailed with zero failures; flake8 and isort clean. Docs updated: TDD 4.1 resolution order, Control Panel Reference resolution table and --project-root description, control_panel.py help, and Decision 12 in the feature state file. BEHAVIOUR CHANGE recorded at the checkpoint: a manual launch with cwd inside a blueprint tree now resolves via the pointer to the producer real registry instead of showing the error banner; the zero-row seed is never adopted on any realistic path and is pinned by two tests. ACCEPTED RESIDUAL: this remains inference of a layout LinkWatcher does not own, and no registry-based approach can list a running daemon in an unregistered workspace - a feature request for tool-owned daemon self-registration is filed separately.
- **Root Cause**: Two independent hardcoded assumptions about a framework layout LinkWatcher does not own. (1) The framework face was assumed to be process-framework/, so at a workspace that declares another location (a self-hosted producer declares blueprint/process-framework) the pointer file was never found. (2) A parent-pointer names the PARENT, so joining the hardcoded process-framework-central/project-registry.json to it yielded the PARENT central - correct for a leaf, whose supervisor list genuinely lives in its parent, but wrong for every non-leaf, and the parent registry need not be a project registry at all. Mechanism (2) is why the framework-role workspaces failed and was not identified in the report. A third, secondary defect: load_projects required a top-level projects mapping, rejecting a framework-registry.json outright. Underlying category: settings.py claimed to reuse the framework canonical where-is-central locator but hardcoded a snapshot of one instance of it; the locator later became role-derived and the snapshot went stale.

- **Verification**: Code Review 2026-08-28 (PF-TSK-005): REJECTED, back to Bug Fixing. What passed: panel suite 372 pass, full suite 1719 passed 3 skipped 3 xfailed zero failures, flake8 and isort clean, and the five-workspace resolution claim reproduced independently (FB workspace/framework-registry 3 rows, appdev workspace 4, LinkWatcher pointer 4 identical to pre-fix, TimeTrackingV2 pointer 4, every launcher path resolves to an existing file). Code behavior approved as-is; two Major findings block closure. CR-1 (DA/EM): the workspace-owned tier needs an explicit --project-root, but the panel wrappers pass none (linkwatcher-panel.ps1 forwards args only), so every manual launch takes the walk-up and gets the PARENT registry. Measured at appdev: --project-root yields its own 4 projects, walk-up from the same root yields the parent 3 frameworks, with nothing in the UI to distinguish them. Control Panel Reference line 56 claims the launcher and the session hook both pass --project-root and that only a deep-subdirectory launch is affected; both clauses are false. Control Panel Operations (PD-UGD-009) was never updated: line 140 still documents the pre-fix 4-tier chain and the launch examples at lines 41-45 omit --project-root entirely. Correct both doc claims and decide whether the wrapper should default --project-root to cwd. CR-2 (DA): six stale doc claims left inside the changed modules, the same drift category the Root Cause names - settings.py:134 (source set omits workspace), settings.py:361 (order omits the workspace tier and names the legacy pointer), settings.py:408 (operator error text never mentions --project-root), discovery.py:5, discovery.py:264 and model.py:66 (all still say the central project-registry.json on the code taught to accept framework-registry.json). Minor: CR-3 test count disagrees across records - bug row says 15, Decision 12 says 10, actual is 14; test-tracking is the correct record at +7/+4/+3. CR-4 no test pins the declared face through registry resolution - face coverage exists only via launcher_path, yet the FB walk-up depends on it. CR-5 Control Panel Reference line 173 still hardcodes process-framework/tools/linkWatcher/start_linkwatcher_background.ps1 as the launcher Start runs, wrong for the very workspaces this fix enables. Suggestions: implicit-Optional body parameter in _make_own_registry; the two hardcoded enumerations (registry filenames, collection keys) fail the same silent way on the next role divergence, so consider logging when a central directory holds no recognized registry; the resolved registry source is log-only, so the own-versus-parent asymmetry is invisible in the panel.
- **Fix**: CODE REVIEW RE-ENTRY round 2 (2026-08-28): CR-1 and CR-2 Major closed, CR-3 to CR-5 Minor closed. CR-1 mechanism: the workspace-owned tier required an explicit --project-root, but the linkwatcher-panel launchers forward arguments and add none, so every manual launch fell through to the parent hop. Measured before: at appdev, --project-root yields its own 4 projects while a launch from that same root yields the parent 3 frameworks, indistinguishable in the UI; legal 1 vs 3. Fix (human-approved option C at the re-entry checkpoint): the current directory is now an implied workspace root, taken EXACTLY and never walked up, so the no-walk-up half of the original constraint still keeps a producer zero-row seed registry out of reach from any ancestor. Measured after: appdev 4, legal 1, FB 3, LinkWatcher 4 and TimeTrackingV2 4 both identical to pre-fix. Doc half of CR-1: the Control Panel Reference claim that the launcher and the session hook both pass --project-root was half false (the hook does, the launcher does not) and its deep-subdirectory qualifier was wrong; Control Panel Operations gained launch guidance plus a wrong-projects troubleshooting entry. Suggestion adopted: implicit-Optional removed from the _make_own_registry test helper. ACCEPTED RESIDUALS, recorded in the TDD and both handbooks: a launch from a deep subdirectory of a non-leaf workspace still lists the parent registry, and a launch from a blueprint tree root reaches that tree zero-row seed; both are diagnosable from the registry-resolved-via-source line in panel.log.

- **Fix**: CODE REVIEW RE-ENTRY round 3 (2026-08-28): CR-6 and CR-7 Major closed, CR-8 and CR-9 Minor closed, and both standing review suggestions adopted. CR-6: Control Panel Operations instructed launching from the install directory, which after this fix is the one recipe that cannot work - measured source=unresolved, i.e. the registry-error banner. The wrappers locate their own interpreter and entry script from their own path, so the instruction was never needed for them to run; it was needed for a bare-name invocation, because the install directory is not on PATH by default (verified absent from both User and Machine PATH; Git Bash adds HOME/bin implicitly, which is how it stayed invisible). Deleting the line as the finding literally asked would have left a recipe that fails a different way, so the section now directs the launch at the workspace root AND gives the full-path form for callers without the directory on PATH. CR-7: control_panel.py returns from the single-instance guard BEFORE resolve_registry_path runs, so a second launch surfaces the open window and re-resolves nothing - project-root, registry and cwd are all discarded; both troubleshooting entries, the launch guidance and the reference single-instance section now say to close the open panel first. The registry-error entry carried the same defect (the banner window IS the open instance) and was found by the residue sweep, not by the review. CR-8: test_panel_lifecycle.py failed the project PINNED black (23.7.0, line-length 100), not only the newer local one; reformatted, and the whole touched set is now clean under the pinned binary. CR-9: residual central project-registry.json claims corrected at settings.py:366, the reference --registry row, the Operations prerequisite, the TDD external-dependency line and both TDD diagram labels, plus the settings.py module summary line found by the sweep. SUGGESTION ADOPTED (a): the closed enumerations no longer fail silently - a process-framework-central directory holding no recognized registry now logs central_registry_unrecognized, naming the directory and what was sought, because the silent fall-through reproduces this bug's own CR-1 shape (the parent's list under an ordinary registry-resolved-via-pointer line) and its own root-cause category; the fall-through itself is deliberately unchanged. SUGGESTION ADOPTED (b): UnicodeDecodeError is a ValueError, so it escaped the OSError guard in _read_parent_pointer and propagated out of resolution, aborting the panel before any window existed (the CR-11 class); it is now caught and logged, and the rejected pointer skips to the next filename so a workspace mid-migration still resolves via its legacy pointer. DISPOSITIONED WITHOUT CHANGE: workspace_face_dir living in settings.py (a module for one helper is not warranted, and lifecycle importing settings is the smaller cost), and the resolved registry source being log-only rather than visible in the panel UI (a UI addition, out of scope for a bug fix). NO RESOLUTION BEHAVIOUR CHANGED this round - the two code edits add a diagnostic and a decode guard only. DELIBERATE NON-CHANGE: the 7.1.1 implementation plan still reads the central project-registry.json; it is a point-in-time planning record, not a claim about current behaviour.

### PD-BUG-149

- **Source**: UserReport
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link updater - moved-file rewrite
- **Repro**: 1. In a watched project create sub/note.md containing a real link [x](../README.md) AND the prose sentence: a template with 7 ../ links (the ../ inside backticks). 2. Start the v2.2.0 daemon. 3. git mv sub/note.md sub/deeper/note.md. 4. Open the moved file.
- **Expected**: Only markdown link targets are re-depthed: ](../README.md) -> ](../../README.md). Backtick code spans and other prose containing ../ are left untouched.
- **Actual**: Both the link AND the backtick-quoted prose span are rewritten: the sentence now reads a template with 7 ../../ links. The daemon logs moved_file_links_updated with a count that includes the prose spans; nothing flags them.
- **Evidence**: Observed 2026-08-29 10:36Z in appdev (FWK-APP MIG-002 Mode C session, PF-FEE-2001): git mv of 4 concept docs from process-framework-central/proposals/ to proposals/old/. PRJ-000_template-link-depth-independence.md had 3 prose spans corrupted (carries 7 ../ links; so ../../ lands; each ../ link) - repaired in appdev commit 4ba5602 (diff = exactly those 3 spans). Two sibling files with only real links (artifact-checkout-locking, scaled-control-architecture) were rewritten correctly, isolating the defect to code-span handling. Daemon log: appdev logs/linkwatcher/LinkWatcherLog.txt 2026-08-29T10:36:52-55Z. Reference updates in OTHER files (3 archived state files + 1 tracker row) were clean.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-30: High confirmed, scope S. Reproduced end to end by driving ReferenceLookup.update_links_within_moved_file on repo source v2.2.0 - one backtick span per line reproduces the filed shape exactly (7 ../ links becomes 7 ../../ links), silent, and links_updated counts the phantom spans. CORRECTED at triage on root cause: the defect is in DETECTION, not the rewrite. MarkdownParser._extract_backtick_dirs registers a bare ../ token as a MARKDOWN_BACKTICK_DIR reference; once it is in the reference list the rewrite behaves correctly by its own contract. A bare parent-directory token names no artifact - it is notation, not a reference. The guard is safe here unlike PD-BUG-135: measured across this repo markdown, 20 bare ./ and ../ repeat tokens against 1897 named backtick directories, so rejecting spans composed only of ./ or ../ repeats costs zero legitimate references. SPLIT at triage: the compounding seen in the incident (../ becoming ../../../../ where three spans shared one line) is a SECOND independent defect in the shared rewrite path, filed as PD-BUG-150 (High, M). It corrupts real links with no prose involved and affects every non-markdown link type, so it is not folded in here. Priority note: by the impact-frequency matrix this bug alone reads Medium (prose meaning corrupted, no link or parse breaks); High is assigned for consistency with PD-BUG-120, a comparably cosmetic silent rewrite inside a moved file. Effort about 1 session. Fix site: src/linkwatcher/parsers/markdown.py _extract_backtick_dirs.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-30
- **Fix**: Added utils.is_dot_only_path() and delegated to it from BOTH path predicates. looks_like_directory_path() was the obvious site, but guarding it alone fixed only markdown, generic and python: PowerShell, YAML and JSON kept emitting the token because looks_like_file_path() ALSO accepted it (a dot-only path has a dot and a separator, so it reached the slash-in-text branch of that function and was classified as a FILE path). Extracted rather than duplicated so the two predicates cannot drift. Deliberately NOT suppressed: a token in link syntax is extracted without consulting either predicate and is still detected.
- **Root Cause**: Detection, not rewrite. Both path predicates in src/linkwatcher/utils.py accepted a path built only from dot segments. Such a token names no artifact - it is notation. Once registered as a reference, the within-moved-file rewrite re-depthed it correctly by its own contract, silently changing prose.
- **Tests Added**: test_bug149dotonlypathtokens.py (TE-TST-161), 17 test functions / 30 cases, 14 verified failing before the fix; manual validation test/bug-validation/PD-BUG-149_dot_only_prose_token_validation.py, 5/5.
- **Verification**: Code review approved 2026-08-30 (PF-TSK-005). Scope: src/linkwatcher/utils.py plus TE-TST-161 and the manual validation script; the PD-BUG-150 and Control Panel 7.1.1 changes sharing the working tree were excluded. Dims DI OB reviewed; PE/AX/SE and feature acceptance criteria N/A per the bug-fix scoping rules. Full suite 1767 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed. Pre-commit on the delta: black (pinned 23.7.0), isort, flake8, pytest-quick all pass. Manual validation 5/5. Verified the fix sits at the shared choke point reaching all seven parsers via base.py; independently confirmed the non-obvious claim that looks_like_file_path was the second emitting route, and the preserved boundary that link-syntax bypasses both predicates. Regression hypothesis tested and DISPROVEN: neutralizing only the guard produced byte-identical parser output on compound structured values, so the whole-string targets there are pre-existing, not introduced here. Findings: 0 Critical, 0 Major, 1 Minor, 2 Suggestions.
