Minor - the guard is evaded by whitespace padding: a padded span is still registered and still rewritten on a move, more destructively (measured: a padded ../../ backtick span became ..). Exposure measured with the real parsers across both deployments: 0 corruptible today; 4 padded tokens exist in total, all of the inert space-slash-space shape, against 43761 and 49777 named references. Routed as PD-BUG-151 (Medium, Needs Triage) rather than back to Bug Fixing. Suggestions - the helper tests assert only on looks_like_directory_path, never directly on looks_like_file_path (class still covered indirectly by the YAML/JSON/PowerShell cases); and the capabilities-reference handbook claim is unqualified where padded spans are concerned, noted in PD-BUG-151 for the same fix.

### PD-BUG-151

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link parsing - path predicates
- **Repro**: 1. In a watched project create sub/note.md whose prose carries PADDED backtick spans: a line reading So (backtick)(space)../../(space)(backtick) lands one level up. 2. Start the daemon (or drive ReferenceLookup.update_links_within_moved_file directly). 3. Move sub/note.md to sub/deeper/note.md. 4. Read the moved file.
- **Expected**: The padded span is notation and is left byte-identical, exactly as the tight spelling (backtick)../(backtick) now is after the PD-BUG-149 fix.
- **Actual**: The padded span is rewritten. Measured: (backtick)(space)../../(space)(backtick) became (backtick)..(backtick) - the notation was destroyed, not merely re-depthed. Silent: no warning, and the span is counted in the moved_file_links_updated total.
- **Evidence**: Found 2026-08-30 during Code Review (PF-TSK-005) of the PD-BUG-149 fix, driving the real service move handler on repo source. Exposure measured with the real parser registry across both affected deployments: 0 corruptible instances today. 4 padded dot-only tokens exist in total (2 in LinkWatcher, 2 in appdev), all of the space-slash-space shape, verified inert under a move; zero instances of the destructive padded ../ shape. Against 43761 named references in LinkWatcher and 49777 in appdev. Controls that pass: tight ../ suppressed in all six parsers, link-syntax [x](../) still detected, ..hidden/x unaffected. NOTE: the capabilities-reference handbook paragraph added by the PD-BUG-149 fix claims without qualification that a bare token inside quotes or backticks is left alone - inaccurate for padded spans, tighten with the fix.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-30: Medium confirmed, scope S. Reproduced end to end against repo source by driving ReferenceLookup.update_links_within_moved_file with the real parser registry. Mechanism confirmed: utils.is_dot_only_path splits on separators and requires every segment to be one of empty / . / .., so " ../../ " yields [" ..", "..", " "] and is not recognised as dot-only. The token stays registered, and the whole captured span INCLUDING its padding is then replaced by the recalculated value, which is why the span is destroyed rather than re-depthed. THREE DESCRIPTION CORRECTIONS AT TRIAGE. (1) The affected-parser list was incomplete and the omissions are the consequential ones. Measured destructive in FIVE parsers: markdown (backtick AND quoted), YAML, JSON, PowerShell (quoted) and generic (.txt) - not the three filed. YAML and JSON put the damage in structured config VALUES (key: " ../../ " becomes key: ".."), not prose, so the filed framing as a prose-notation defect understates it. Python does not register the token at all, and PowerShell COMMENTS do not either - only quoted PowerShell strings do. (2) Only depth 2+ is destructive. " ../../ " is destroyed; " ../ " is INERT because recalculation returns the same value and links_updated is 0. The filed title is unqualified on this. (3) Exposure independently re-measured rather than inherited: zero padded dot-bearing spans in this repo, corroborating the filed measurement. The 4 tokens counted at filing are the " / " shape, which is not dot-only and is inert. PRIORITY RATIONALE: impact-frequency matrix gives Medium impact x Low frequency = Medium. Impact Medium because the corruption is silent but the YAML/JSON output stays syntactically valid, so nothing breaks loudly. Frequency Low because zero corruptible instances were measured and the trigger needs padded dot-only authoring inside delimiters AND a depth-changing move of the containing file. High was considered for consistency with PD-BUG-149 and PD-BUG-120 (same family, and this is more destructive per instance) and deliberately NOT taken: 149 earned High from a real observed incident in appdev, this has none and zero exposure, so inheriting High would label by association rather than by impact. It should still be fixed in the same cycle - the guard is fresh and the fix is one line - but that is scheduling, not priority. SCOPE S: trim the input in is_dot_only_path before the segment test, plus regression coverage across the five affected parsers, plus a correction to the capabilities-reference paragraph added by the PD-BUG-149 fix, which currently claims without qualification that a bare token inside quotes or backticks is left alone. FIX SITE - a deliberate departure from fix-the-root-cause, recorded so it is not mistaken for an oversight: the shared root cause is that parsers capture surrounding whitespace INTO the reference span. Measured at triage: NAMED padded targets such as " doc/sub " are captured with padding too (markdown, YAML, JSON, generic) and are then silently never updated on a move, because the padded string does not resolve on disk. Fixing capture across six parsers is rejected here because it changes what column_start/column_end MEAN, which PD-BUG-150 (fixed 2026-08-30) just made load-bearing for position-based replacement, and because the measured benefit is zero corruptible instances plus a silent-miss case that no instance in either repo exhibits. The padding-capture question is filed separately as TD305 (Low, Dims DI CQ) rather than folded into this fix; TD305 carries the measurement and the revisit condition. NOT A DUPLICATE, and deliberately NOT filed as a reopen of PD-BUG-149: the Reopen Workflow table leans toward reopening (fix was incomplete; discovered shortly after closure), but 149 is closed with a reviewed record and this entry carries measurements that record does not. Human partner confirmed keep-as-151 with a cross-reference at the triage checkpoint. WF-005 added at triage (multi-format move, all parsers handle) because five parsers are affected.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-30
- **Fix**: Root-cause fix at the capture side, not containment at the predicate (option C, chosen by the human partner over the one-line trim the triage prescribed; TD305 folded in and resolved by the same change). BaseParser.parse_content is now concrete and wraps a new abstract _parse_content_impl (mechanical rename in all seven parsers): it strips the target, shrinks column_start/column_end onto it, re-applies the path predicates so a token that only qualified BECAUSE of its padding is dropped, and drops references that normalization made identical. Normalization is confined to references whose recorded span is EXACTLY the target, so the two wide-span markdown types and the escaped structured types are untouched and the PD-BUG-150 column contract is unaffected. One choke point rather than the 39 LinkReference construction sites, so a parser added later cannot forget it. Files: src/linkwatcher/parsers/base.py (+114) plus a one-line rename in dart, generic, json_parser, markdown, powershell, python, yaml_parser. Public API unchanged, so all 172 direct test call sites and the three internal cross-parser delegations inherit the fix.
- **Root Cause**: The parsers captured surrounding whitespace INTO the reference span (TD305), so link_target held the padded string and the recorded columns covered the padding. utils.is_dot_only_path tested that span verbatim, so a padded dot-only token was not recognised as notation: it stayed registered, and the whole span INCLUDING its padding was then replaced by the recalculated value, destroying the notation rather than re-depthing it. Trimming inside the predicate was rejected as the fix: it would have left a tight ../../ registered, reviving the exact PD-BUG-149 symptom instead of fixing it.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Full suite 1801 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failed. Regression coverage in TE-TST-161: 28 assertions observed RED before the fix and 66 green after, each verified to fail for the right reason. Manual validation PD-BUG-151_padded_dot_only_token_validation.py went 1/6 to 6/6 driving the real service move handler. Behavioural delta measured PAIRED (each file parsed once, normalized vs un-normalized, so no time-skew) across 88359 references in LinkWatcher and appdev: 11 dropped, 62 tightened, 0 added. The 11 drops are 4 padded dot-only tokens (the corruption class), 4 padded bare separators (inert before), and 3 PowerShell padded twins deduped onto their tight originals; zero real references lost. EXPOSURE CORRECTION: triage measured zero corruptible instances, but all 4 corruptible tokens now sit in bug-tracking.md line 123 - the PD-BUG-151 entry itself - so filing the bug created the exposure, and the next depth-changing move of that file would have destroyed the shapes documenting the defect. Sibling latent defect closed by the same re-validation: a padded URL evaded the startswith guard in looks_like_file_path. A first before/after measurement was discarded after an external edit to test-spec-2-2-1-link-updating.md at 12:48:07 landed between the two snapshots and shifted line numbers; the paired method replaced it. black, isort, flake8 and pytest-quick pass. E2E groups flagged for re-execution: 31 entries across 6 workflows.
- **Verification**: Code Review 2026-08-30 (PF-TSK-005): APPROVED - 0 Critical, 0 Major, 2 Minor, 3 Suggestions. Scope corrected S to M at this closing call per the fix session reassessment. CLAIMS VERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY RATHER THAN INHERITED, by paired parse (normalization patched to identity, same content parsed twice, no time skew) across 2,686 files and 46,113 references, plus end-to-end runs of the real ReferenceLookup.update_links_within_moved_file. (a) DROP SET: exactly 11 references dropped, matching the fix measurement; all are dot-only notation or bare separators; zero real references lost. Note the review initially counted 14 and the excess three proved to be NOT dropped but returned untouched (see Minor 2), which is what reconciles the count to 11. (b) REPAIR CONFIRMED: a padded named reference now follows a move with its authored padding preserved - YAML value with padding re-depthed correctly - which is the quieter silent-skip failure the fix claimed to close. (c) NO PD-BUG-150 REGRESSION: a padded markdown reference definition re-depths with label, title and padding all intact, so the label-destruction class that rejected the first PD-BUG-150 fix does not recur here. (d) NOTATION PRESERVED: every padded dot-only shape constructed (double-quoted, single-quoted, backtick, one-level and two-level) is now suppressed at parse time and left byte-identical across a depth-changing move. (e) NO NEW SPLICE HAZARD: exact-duplicate spans measured 106 with normalization and 106 without, so the fix introduces ZERO; the 106 are pre-existing PYTHON_QUOTED plus PYTHON_COMMENT and MARKDOWN_STANDALONE plus MARKDOWN_BARE_PATH pairs, untouched by the dedup because its key includes link_type. (f) Two hypotheses raised at review were REFUTED by measurement and are recorded so they are not re-raised: normalization creating same-span different-type duplicates (zero introduced), and shrinking a span into new strict containment (the case found was already contained before normalization - a defect in the review probe, not in the fix). FULL SUITE 1808 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failed. Lint clean under the PINNED toolchain (black 23.7.0, isort 5.12.0, flake8 6.0.0), not the newer local black. base.py coverage 93 percent. POSITIVE: taking the root-cause capture-side option over the triage-prescribed one-line predicate trim was demonstrably correct - trimming inside the predicate would have left a tight parent-directory token registered and revived the PD-BUG-149 symptom; and the fix updated its own TDD (section 1.1 now states the normalization contract), notable given the PD-BUG-150 fix violated the FDD of its own feature. FINDINGS ROUTED, none blocking: TD306 (two inaccurate load-bearing claims - the zero-exact-duplicates measurement in the base.py comment, and the unconditional no-padded-target invariant in TDD 1.1, which has 3 live exceptions in MARKDOWN_QUOTED references whose span includes their quote characters); TD307 (silent drops with no logging on an OB-dimension bug, unconditional whole-content split on a hot path, and the confinement guard at base.py line 146 being uncovered by the entire suite despite being reachable and load-bearing). NEW BUG FILED: PD-BUG-152, a padded inline markdown link is silently never re-depthed on a move - verified PRE-EXISTING by paired run, not introduced here, 0 instances in this repo today.

### PD-BUG-153

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link updating - within-moved-file replacement
- **Repro**: 1. In a watched project create doc/a.md and the directory sub/deeper/. 2. Create sub/note.md containing a markdown link whose text and target are both ../doc/a.md. 3. With LinkWatcher running, move sub/note.md to sub/deeper/note.md. 4. Read the moved file.
- **Expected**: Both halves follow the move: target becomes ../../doc/a.md and the link text becomes ../../doc/a.md too, matching what the updater path does for the same link in any other file (PD-BUG-012).
- **Actual**: Only the target is updated. The link text stays at ../doc/a.md, so the rendered text names a path that no longer resolves from the new location. No warning, and the link counts as updated.
- **Evidence**: Measured 2026-08-30 during Code Review (PF-TSK-005) of the PD-BUG-150 rework, driving the real ReferenceLookup.update_links_within_moved_file on repo source: the moved-file path produced target ../../doc/a.md with text left at ../doc/a.md, while replacement.replace_in_line on the same reference updated both. Pre-existing and NOT introduced by the PD-BUG-150 rework, the same inline regex was there before, so it was excluded from that review verdict and routed here instead. Exposure in this repo: 213 markdown links whose text exactly equals their target, all non-URL; a further 4 are backtick-wrapped, where the condition does not fire.
- **Triage**: Validity CONFIRMED by driving the real ReferenceLookup.update_links_within_moved_file on repo source: the moved file came back with its target re-depthed to ../../doc/a.md while the visible text stayed at ../doc/a.md, where replace_in_line on the same reference updated both halves. The Phase 2 pattern and the shared replace_markdown_target are otherwise identical, so the PD-BUG-012 link-text update is the only divergence. Priority Low: impact Low (nothing corrupts, the link still resolves, navigation is unaffected) x frequency Medium = Low per the matrix. The Medium reading was presented and not taken: half the link is maintained against a contract PD-BUG-012 established, the damage is permanent, 258 links are exposed, and bulk restructuring is the primary use case. Priority governs urgency only - the fix is a one-line swap of Phase 2 onto the existing shared replacement.replace_markdown_target, so it proceeds immediately. Scope S: additive-only, verified that none of the 5 moved-file test files carries a self-named link, so no existing test asserts the current behavior. Dims corrected from DI to DI CQ - CQ for the duplicated format-aware regex instead of the shared helper, the same tag PD-BUG-098 carries for the same duplication shape. Related feature 2.2.1 and workflows WF-001, WF-002 confirmed as filed. No duplicate, and NOT a reopen of PD-BUG-012, whose fix landed in updater.py only and closed 2026-03-03 - different component and different root cause. Description corrected on one point and extended on two. Exposure re-measured at 258 self-named markdown links rather than the filed 213, by a parser-based sweep of all 2021 markdown files in the repo (doc 158, test 44, .claude 29, process-framework 27; all relative, zero URLs, zero absolute); the filed count of 4 backtick-wrapped inert links matched. The defect never self-heals and drifts further: a second move re-depths the target again while the text stays frozen, so the PD-BUG-012 condition is permanently dead for that link. And --validate cannot detect it, because the stale text is not registered as a separate reference, so there is no tooling signal in either direction. Observation recorded not routed: PD-BUG-152 anchors on the same bracket-paren-target shape that the shared helper also uses, so this fix will not address it and it likely reaches the updater path too; that belongs to its own triage.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-30
- **Fix**: The two-phase structure is gone. _replace_links_in_lines now makes ONE pass over every reference, ordered descending by line and column, so a replacement length change cannot invalidate a span not yet applied. Inline markdown is routed to a new shared helper replacement.replace_markdown_at_span, which confines the format-aware rewrite to the span the parser recorded for that reference and delegates to replace_markdown_target - so the PD-BUG-012 link-text rule arrives with the helper rather than being copied, and no reference can observe the output of another. The seen_markdown_targets de-duplication was removed with the content-global pass it existed to serve: each reference now writes its own span, so collapsing duplicates would strand every one after the first. import re removed from reference_lookup.py - the deleted block held its only two uses. Span confinement is safe because a MARKDOWN span exactly covers its bracket-paren construct, measured across 13 shapes with 0 exceptions; MARKDOWN_REFERENCE stays on the positional path where its label-anchored replacer already guards it. Scope reassessed S to M at the fix for the fold-in.
- **Root Cause**: Two defects in the same branch. The markdown half of _replace_links_in_lines carried its OWN copy of the markdown regex instead of calling the shared replacement.replace_markdown_target, so it never applied the PD-BUG-012 rule that updates a link text when the text is exactly the old target - the filed defect. And it substituted CONTENT-GLOBALLY, once per reference in sequence, so a reference whose OLD target equalled an earlier reference NEW target re-matched text that earlier pass had just written and re-depthed it a second time - a silent target corruption pointing a link at a different file, verified pre-existing on unmodified source and folded into this fix at the human partner direction at the Step 11 checkpoint.
- **Tests Added**: Yes - 15 new tests in test_bug153movedfilemarkdownrewrite.py (TE-TST-162), plus one existing test in test_reference_lookup.py corrected as removal residue: it asserted count 1 with the rationale one content-global rewrite covers both occurrences, which is the design removed here, and its negative assertion named link_pattern_not_found, an event this path can no longer emit and which was therefore vacuous - now count 2 against link_target_not_found_on_line.
- **Verification**: Pre-fix 5 failed and 8 passed, each failure inspected and carrying the exact defect signature rather than a setup error. Post-fix 15 of 15. Full suite 1823 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failures. Manual validation TE PD-BUG-153 script 6 of 6. Mutation-verified 1 to 1 across four mutations: removing the link-text rule reddens exactly the 4 self-named tests, removing span confinement reddens exactly the 2 reverse-chain tests plus the sibling duplicate test, reverting the ordering reddens the forward-chain tests plus 6 pre-existing PD-BUG-150 tests, restoring the de-duplication reddens exactly the 3 duplicate tests. That first mutation run corrected this work: the original chain tests went green without span confinement, being satisfied by the ordering change alone, so the reverse-chain shape was added - the case ordering cannot repair - and the manual validation was rewritten for the same reason after scoring 5 of 5 against a fix missing span confinement. It now scores 4 of 6 with the link-text rule removed and 4 of 6 with span confinement removed. E2E deliberately not flagged: 0 self-named links and no chain shapes across 1514 E2E fixture files, so the change is byte-neutral for every case. Pre-commit clean apart from pre-existing PD documentation-map drift from a deleted refactoring plan, not swept in. Validate-TestTracking scoped to both touched files reports 0 errors and 0 warnings.
- **Verification**: Code Review (PF-TSK-005) 2026-08-31: APPROVED, 0 Critical / 0 Major, 1 Minor and 1 Suggestion, neither blocking. Evidence re-measured rather than inherited. Mutation-verified reviewer-side by runtime patching with no source edits: disabling span confinement reddens exactly the 2 reverse-chain tests plus the sibling duplicate test in the reference-lookup suite; disabling the PD-BUG-012 link-text rule reddens exactly the 4 self-named tests - a 1:1 map matching the fix record on both counts. Span-shape assumption re-measured across 25 markdown shapes (images, an image nested in a link, titles in all three quotings, table cells, list items, blockquotes, bold text, padded destinations): 0 guard failures; only MarkdownParser emits LinkType.MARKDOWN, so the assumption is type-scoped. Containment probe across 20 shapes found 0 nested spans, confirming the positional-only overlap pre-pass is sound, and a hand-built nested reference driven through the real path was caught by the shape guard and degraded to a no-op with a warning rather than splicing text. E2E neutrality re-verified with a corrected scope: 39 self-named links do exist in the E2E tree but all sit in test-case and master-test documentation, 0 in the fixture directories, so the byte-neutral claim holds. Removal residue swept clean (the re import, link_pattern_not_found, seen_markdown_targets); surviving mentions are deliberate prose. Manual validation script re-run 6 of 6. Full suite on the final tree 1844 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failures - a higher baseline than the 1823 recorded at the fix because a parallel session landed the PD-BUG-152 padding fix into replace_markdown_target mid-review, at the single shared site this fix made possible. pre-commit on the four changed files: black, isort, flake8 and pytest-quick all pass; its only failure is pre-existing PD documentation-map drift from a deleted refactoring plan, unrelated and not swept in. Test-tracking validation scoped to the new test file: 0 errors, 0 warnings. MINOR (CQ, not blocking, no new artifact because PD-BUG-154 already owns the remedy): the fix pre-empts the shared dispatch at the call site - the moved-file path tests the MARKDOWN link type itself and calls replace_markdown_at_span directly - so replace_in_line, documented as the entry point every caller should use, still routes MARKDOWN to the line-scoped helper. That is PD-BUG-154; its fix should also collapse this call-site check so the two rewrite paths converge instead of re-diverging. SUGGESTION (not filed): nothing distinguishes a span-confined rewrite from the line-scoped fallback in the logs, so a future parser change invalidating the span assumption would be silent; zero exposure today. Reviewed while PD-BUG-150 is still In Progress after its round-2 rejection: scope was the 153 delta only, seam-checked against 150, whose open finding sits in the positional dispatch this fix neither introduced nor changed.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-31

### PD-BUG-154

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link updating - updater markdown replacement
- **Repro**: 1. In a watched project create index.md containing one line holding two inline markdown links: [x] -> new/a.md and [y] -> old/a.md (written here with the bracket-paren adjacency broken on purpose - a literal example link inside this tracker registers as a real reference and is reported as a broken link). 2. Drive two moves in one batch through LinkUpdater.update_references_batch: old/a.md to new/a.md, and new/a.md to final/a.md. 3. Read index.md.
- **Expected**: Each link lands on its own recalculated target: [x] -> final/a.md and [y] -> new/a.md. Every reference is rewritten at the position the parser found it, so no reference is rewritten twice.
- **Actual**: Both links land on final/a.md: [x] -> final/a.md and [y] -> final/a.md. The [y] link was correctly rewritten to new/a.md first, then the [x] pass - anchored on new/a.md and reaching the whole line - matched it a second time. No warning; both count as updated.
- **Evidence**: Measured 2026-08-30 during the PD-BUG-153 fix (PF-TSK-007) Step 20 similar-issues check, driving the real LinkUpdater.update_references_batch on repo source. The mirror shape is correct: [x] -> old/a.md and [y] -> new/a.md with the same two moves returns [x] -> new/a.md and [y] -> final/a.md, because descending column order rewrites the right link first. Pre-existing and NOT introduced by the PD-BUG-153 fix, which changed only reference_lookup. The shared helper replacement.replace_markdown_at_span added by that fix is the ready remedy - routing the updater MARKDOWN branch through it would close this - but that path was outside the fix scope and was left for this triage.
- **Triage**: VALID - reproduced 2026-08-31 by driving the real LinkUpdater.update_references_batch with references from the real MarkdownParser, using the filed repro unchanged: both links land on the same final target, reported as references_updated 2 with errors 0, silently. The mirror forward chain passes, as filed. Priority LOWERED Medium to Low and Workflows CORRECTED WF-001 WF-002 to WF-002 on one finding the filed row does not state: the defect is NOT reachable from production input. update_references_batch has exactly one production caller, _handle_directory_moved Phase 1b, and every batch it emits is uniformly prefixed - each old path lies under old_dir, computed by rewriting the new_dir prefix, and each new path lies under new_dir - so the old-path and new-path sets are disjoint. The chain condition needs one moved file to land exactly where another departed, i.e. those two sets to intersect, which requires one directory to contain the other; both such renames are refused by the OS, and the synthesized Windows detector funnels through the same single old_dir to new_dir pair. Single-file moves never reach this code at all - they use update_references with one old-new pair, where the chain cannot form - which is why WF-001 is not affected. Impact Low times Frequency Low gives Low on the matrix; priority governs urgency and there is no urgency without reachability. Fixing is still warranted on structural grounds rather than corruption: replace_in_line is documented as the entry point every caller should use, and it is now the UNSAFE markdown path while the moved-file caller reaches the safe replace_markdown_at_span - the exact divergence replacement.py was created to end, against a six-bug recurrence history (PD-BUG-098, 118, 119, 120, 150, 153). Scope S: one-line dispatch change routing the MARKDOWN branch to replace_markdown_at_span, plus regression test and mutation verification, about one session. Fixer note: line-wide reach has an accidental benefit today, in that an unregistered duplicate markdown link with the same target on the same line is rewritten for free, and confining to the span drops it; PD-BUG-153 already accepted that trade on the moved-file path, so accepting it here restores symmetry. Not a duplicate: sibling of PD-BUG-153 (same shape, different path and fix site), descendant of PD-BUG-150.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-31
- **Fix**: One-line dispatch change in src/linkwatcher/replacement.py: replace_in_line now routes MARKDOWN to replace_markdown_at_span instead of the line-scoped replace_markdown_target, confining an inline markdown rewrite to the column span the parser recorded for that reference. Both rewrite paths now get span confinement whichever entry point they use, ending the divergence in which the moved-file caller reached the span helper directly from the PD-BUG-153 fix while the shared updater dispatch did not. MARKDOWN_REFERENCE deliberately stays line-scoped and is safe there, its pattern being anchored to the label prefix and end-of-line so a definition line admits exactly one match. Documentation corrected at three code sites that described the old routing - the module AI Context block and the replace_in_line and replace_markdown_at_span docstrings - plus the feature TDD, whose note stating the defect was still live on the updater path is replaced by the fix record and its reachability finding.
- **Root Cause**: replace_in_line dispatched MARKDOWN to replace_markdown_target, which anchors on the old target and substitutes EVERY match in the string it is handed. Handed a whole line, that reach extends over neighbouring links. With two links on one line and two moves in one batch, a link whose OLD target equals a neighbour NEW target re-matched the text that neighbour pass had just written and re-pointed it at a third file, silently, both counting as updated. Descending column order does not save this shape, because it is the LEFT link pass that reaches rightward over text already rewritten - the mirror image of the forward chain that ordering does repair.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: S-scope quick path, human-approved at the Step 23 checkpoint after a recommendation to route to Code Review instead was presented and not taken. Evidence: 8 new regression cases (TE-TST-164), 2 red pre-fix and all 8 green post-fix, with the pre-fix failure output confirming the exact defect rather than a setup error. Full suite 1846 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failed. Mutation-verified 1:1 - reverting the one-line dispatch turns exactly those 2 red and leaves 1844 untouched, so the tests pin the changed mechanism and nothing else. A second mutation splicing the bare target across the span, the behaviour that got the first PD-BUG-150 fix rejected, turns 7 of the 8 red, so the title and link-text guards are load-bearing against that historical failure mode; the reference-definition boundary case correctly survives it because MARKDOWN_REFERENCE never enters the span helper. black, isort and flake8 pass under the pinned pre-commit hooks, and Validate-TestTracking scoped to the new file reports 0 errors and 0 warnings. Behaviour-preserving for all reachable input, so no E2E group was flagged for re-execution: WF-002 is covered only by cross-cutting TE-E2G-005 and flagging it would cascade across every feature that test touches for a zero behaviour delta. Manual validation test skipped per the Step 21 criteria and confirmed at the checkpoint - no filesystem action sequence can trigger the batch shape, so a human-reproducible scenario does not exist. Similar-issues sweep: the moved-file path was already routed to the span helper and is untouched by this change; replace_reference_target is safe line-scoped and is pinned by a boundary test; the PYTHON_IMPORT branch of replace_at_position carries the same class of line-wide reach behind the same reachability barrier and is recorded as an observation only.

### PD-BUG-152

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link updating - markdown replacement (both rewrite paths)
- **Repro**: 1. In a watched project create doc/sub/target.md and the directory sub/deeper/. 2. Create sub/note.md containing a padded inline link: See [the doc]( ../doc/sub/target.md ) for details. 3. With LinkWatcher running, move sub/note.md to sub/deeper/note.md. 4. Read the moved file.
- **Expected**: The link is re-depthed to ../../doc/sub/target.md, exactly as the unpadded spelling is, with the authored whitespace preserved.
- **Actual**: The line is left byte-identical. The link now points one level too shallow and is broken. Nothing is logged and the reference is not counted as skipped.
- **Evidence**: Found 2026-08-30 during Code Review (PF-TSK-005) of the PD-BUG-151 fix, driving the real ReferenceLookup.update_links_within_moved_file on repo source. Verified PRE-EXISTING, not introduced by that fix: a paired run with BaseParser normalization patched to identity produces the identical unchanged output, so the defect predates PD-BUG-151. Contrast measured in the same run - the markdown REFERENCE-DEFINITION shape with the same padding IS re-depthed correctly, label, title and padding intact, so the gap is specific to LinkType.MARKDOWN inline links. Exposure measured across this repo: 0 padded inline markdown links today. Workflow mapping is provisional pending triage.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-30: Low confirmed (human-approved at the triage checkpoint), scope S, Dims DI OB. VALIDITY CONFIRMED by end-to-end reproduction against repo source, driving the real service move handler; the filed repro reproduces exactly. Mechanism confirmed at source: MarkdownParser._extract_url_from_link_content strips the captured content (link_content.strip()), so link_target holds the tight path while the line reads "]( " - and BOTH markdown replacement regexes anchor on "](" immediately followed by the target, so neither matches and the line is returned unchanged. All THREE padding spellings fail identically (both-sides, leading-only, trailing-only); the tight control re-depths correctly in the same run. Premise verified independently rather than inherited: all three padded spellings are valid CommonMark, rendering via markdown-it to an identical anchor href, so the shape is legitimate authoring indistinguishable to a reader. Contrast control holds - a padded markdown REFERENCE DEFINITION re-depths correctly in both paths WITH its authored padding preserved, isolating the defect to LinkType.MARKDOWN inline links. THREE DESCRIPTION CORRECTIONS AT TRIAGE. (1) SCOPE WAS TOO NARROW and this is the consequential one: the entry was filed as moved-file-only (title "when its file moves", Component "moved-file rewrite"), but the UPDATER path fails identically - a padded inline link in ANY OTHER file pointing at a moved target is never rewritten and is left pointing at a path that no longer exists. Two distinct "](" -anchored regexes carry the defect: replacement.replace_markdown_target (updater path) and reference_lookup Phase 2 own inline regex (moved-file path). The updater path is the LARGER exposure - the moved-file path touches only the one file being moved, while the updater path touches every file in the project that references a moved target. Title and Component corrected accordingly. (2) The filed claim of "no warning" is INACCURATE for the filed repro and understates a worse case: measured, the moved-file path with a padded link ALONE does emit a link_pattern_not_found WARNING. It goes fully silent when the same target ALSO appears unpadded in the same file, because seen_markdown_targets dedups on the stripped target, collapsing every occurrence into one content-global pass; that pass finds the tight occurrence, reports success, and the padded occurrences vanish with no warning and are counted as updated. The updater path is silent unconditionally. (3) NEW, NOT FILED - false success count: the updater path with 3 padded plus 1 tight link reported references_updated=4 while exactly one line changed. This is what adds OB to the filed DI. EXPOSURE independently measured rather than inherited, using the parser own link regex plus its title-stripping logic across BOTH deployments: 2845 markdown files, 19415 inline links, 2 exposed, 0 organic. Both exposed instances sit in bug-tracking.md line 129 - the PD-BUG-152 entry itself - so as with PD-BUG-151 the act of filing created the entire exposure; neither resolves on disk, so 0 corruptible today. appdev: 0 exposed across 5819 links. MITIGATING FACTOR verified by running the real validator: a stale padded link IS reported broken by --validate (the validator checks the stripped target), so the failure is detectable by the project own scan rather than being undetectable. PRIORITY RATIONALE: the impact-frequency matrix gives Medium impact x Low frequency = Medium, and Low was taken DELIBERATELY against the matrix with human approval. The anchor is PD-BUG-151, triaged Medium: that bug was DESTRUCTIVE, destroying authored content in structured config values across five parsers, at the same zero exposure. This one is a NON-DESTRUCTIVE silent no-op - nothing is corrupted and the link is left exactly as authored - affecting one link type, with --validate as a detection net and zero organic instances in 19415 links. Ranking it one step below its destructive sibling is the consistent ordering. Recorded so it is not re-litigated: the counter-argument weighed and rejected is that this IS a silent failure of the product core promise and now spans both rewrite paths rather than the one filed. SCOPE S: make both markdown patterns tolerate optional whitespace around the target and PRESERVE the authored padding (matching what the reference-definition path already does, verified above), plus regression coverage for 3 padding variants x 2 rewrite paths plus the dedup-masking case. NOT A DUPLICATE BUT COUPLED: PD-BUG-151 is the same family with a different mechanism, is closed, and its capture-side fix deliberately did not reach here (MARKDOWN is a wide-span type excluded from that normalization). PD-BUG-153 targets the SAME reference_lookup Phase 2 regex - its structural fix is to make Phase 2 delegate to the shared replace_markdown_target, which would collapse this defect from two sites to one. Sequencing recommendation recorded for the fix session: fixing 153 first, or both in one change, avoids writing the padding fix twice into code that is about to be merged; SUPERSEDED SAME DAY: 153 was triaged concurrently in a parallel session to Needs Fix (Low, scope S, DI CQ) and its prescribed fix IS that swap, so it should land FIRST - after which this padding fix applies at ONE site (the shared replace_markdown_target) instead of two. WORKFLOWS expanded from the provisional WF-001 and WF-002 to match PD-BUG-150, the correct comparator (same replacement region, both rewrite paths): WF-005 added because its MD leg is exactly where this fires, WF-011 added because override-folder blueprint moves are markdown-dense and go through the affected updater path.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-30
- **Fix**: One-site fix at the shared choke point replacement.replace_markdown_target. Its pattern now captures the whitespace CommonMark permits around an inline link destination in groups of its own - (\s*) after the open paren and (\s*) before the close paren - and writes both back verbatim, replacing only the target. The title group was restructured so it always participates (empty when absent), removing the None check. ONE site was enough because the PD-BUG-153 fix had already routed BOTH rewrite paths through this function: the updater path via replace_in_line and the moved-file path via replace_markdown_at_span. That sequencing was the reason this bug waited for 153 rather than being fixed first, and it held - the two-site estimate from triage became one. Preserving the padding rather than normalizing it away follows the PD-BUG-118 / PD-BUG-120 / PD-BUG-124 rule that a rewrite changes WHERE a link points and never HOW it was written; the sibling replace_reference_target already honoured it. No other source file changed. Documentation updated in the same change: TDD 2-2-1 (both descriptions of the function), FDD 2-2-1 (new requirement BR-11 generalizing the authored-form rule to inline markdown destinations, verified unique and non-contradicting after the PD-BUG-150 duplicate-ID incident), test spec 2-2-1 (new section plus the mutation contrast), the 2.2.1 feature state file, and the user-facing capabilities reference handbook, since a padded link now following a move is a user-visible behaviour change.
- **Root Cause**: The markdown parser records link_target STRIPPED - _extract_url_from_link_content begins with link_content.strip() - while the source line keeps whatever whitespace the author wrote. replace_markdown_target anchored its pattern on two juxtapositions that therefore could never hold for a padded link: the close-bracket-open-paren immediately followed by the target, and the target immediately followed by a title or the closing paren. The substitution matched nothing and returned the line unchanged, so the reference was left pointing at a path that no longer existed. The failure is a silent no-op rather than a corruption, which is why it survived: nothing is mangled, no file becomes invalid, and in the updater path nothing is logged at all. Confirmed empirically at the function rather than inferred from the symptom - all three padding spellings (both-sides, leading-only, trailing-only) return the input byte-identical while the tight spelling and the tight-with-title spelling rewrite correctly. The premise was verified independently rather than assumed: markdown-it renders all three padded spellings to an identical anchor href, so the shape is legitimate CommonMark authoring and indistinguishable to a reader from the tight form.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Full suite 1837 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failed. New regression file TE-TST-163 (test_bug152paddedinlinemarkdownlink.py): 21 parametrized cases across 12 functions, 17 RED against fully reverted source with the 4 controls green both ways, all 21 green after. Manual validation PD-BUG-152_padded_inline_markdown_link_validation.py went 3 of 8 to 8 of 8, driving the real service move handler across BOTH paths in one run. black, isort and flake8 pass under the PINNED pre-commit toolchain (23.7.0), not the newer local binaries. MUTATION-VERIFIED 1:1 rather than by coverage, three mutations each restoring the source byte-identical afterwards: dropping the leading-whitespace group reddens 12 and leaves the trailing-only cases green; dropping the trailing group reddens 12 and leaves the leading-only cases green; and matching the padding but STRIPPING it on write-back reddens 16 and leaves only the 4 controls. That third mutation is the load-bearing one - it isolates preservation from replacement, and without it the suite would pass a fix that silently normalized away the author spacing. STEP 20 SWEEP found no companion defect: replace_reference_target, the only sibling target-anchored replacer, has carried optional-whitespace matching on both sides all along (verified across four padding variants), and the two remaining re.escape sites anchor on raw source text and module names rather than a stripped target, so the root-cause class does not reach them. E2E RE-EXECUTION DELIBERATELY NOT FLAGGED, human-approved at the Step 23 checkpoint: measured 0 padded inline links across 5,253 inline links in 1,493 E2E fixture files, so every existing fixture round-trips byte-identically and the behavioural delta on them is zero, against a 38-entry cascade the flag would trigger - the same trade-off PD-BUG-150 declined on the same grounds. INTERACTION WITH PD-BUG-154 RECORDED AT THE CHECKPOINT, not discovered later: 154 is the still-open span-reach defect in this same function, where the updater path applies it line-globally. Widening the pattern EXTENDS that defect reach to padded links, because padded links now participate in rewriting at all; measured on the 154 repro, tight links reproduce it unchanged and padded links now show the same shape. Judged correct to proceed and approved - a link that updates but inherits a known open defect is strictly better than one that silently never updates - but 154 should be triaged knowing its exposure is now wider. OBSERVATION recorded, not acted on: the updater path emits NOTHING when a markdown replacement no-ops, where the moved-file path emits link_target_not_found_on_line; that asymmetry is broader than this bug and was left untouched.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-31
- **Verification**: Code review APPROVED 2026-08-31 (PF-TSK-005): 0 Critical, 0 Major, 2 Minor, 1 Suggestion, none blocking. Scoped to Dims DI OB; PE AX SE and acceptance-criteria validation marked N/A per the bug-fix scoping rules. SEAM RE-VERIFIED rather than assumed: PD-BUG-154 was closed DURING the review session, changing MARKDOWN dispatch to the span-confined replace_markdown_at_span after this fix had been verified. Measured that the parser records the padded span INCLUDING its padding, so the shape guard passes and padded links now inherit span confinement on both paths; the fix is intact under 154. Full suite 1852 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failed (the record said 1837; the delta is the 154 tests landing since). PD-BUG-152 manual validation re-run 8 of 8 against the post-154 tree. black, isort and flake8 pass under the PINNED 23.7.0 pre-commit toolchain, not the newer local binaries. INDEPENDENT PROBE across 13 shapes: all three padding spellings re-depth, spacing preserved verbatim including tabs, all three title forms intact, self-named link text updates, two padded links on one line stay independent, and a hostile whitespace run stays linear to n=5000 so the widened pattern carries no ReDoS. MUTATION MATRIX RE-RUN INDEPENDENTLY across three suites rather than accepting the recorded counts: control 0 red, strip-the-padding 17, drop-leading-group 13, drop-trailing-group 13. The four tests reddened ONLY by the strip mutation are the padded cases on both rewrite paths, so the suite pins PRESERVATION independently of replacement; the recorded 16, 12, 12 reproduce as 17, 13, 13 at the wider scope. Documentation verified at all five claimed sites; FDD BR-11 confirmed unique and correctly sequenced (BR-1 through BR-11, no duplicates), so the PD-BUG-150 duplicate-ID incident was not repeated. SCOPING CAVEAT recorded: replacement.py is untracked, so no committed baseline existed to diff the delta against; the function was reviewed as it stands and its behaviour verified empirically instead. TWO MINOR FINDINGS ROUTED, neither blocking closure: a new bug for CommonMark angle-bracket destinations, silently never updated on either rewrite path (same symptom class, capture-side mechanism, and unlike this bug it carries live exposure of 2 resolvable references in this repo), and technical debt for the residual OB gap plus the positional-capture-group fragility.

### PD-BUG-155

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link parsing - markdown destination capture
- **Repro**: 1. In a watched project create doc/a.md and doc/a b.md, plus the directory sub/deeper/. 2. Create other.md containing [d] (<doc/a.md>) and [d] (<doc/a b.md>) alongside a tight control [d] (doc/a.md). 3. Create sub/note.md containing [d] (<../doc/a b.md>) and a tight control. 4. With LinkWatcher running, move sub/note.md to sub/deeper/note.md, then move doc/a.md and doc/a b.md into doc2/. 5. Read both files.
- **Expected**: Every angle-bracket destination follows the move exactly as its tight sibling does, with the angle-bracket form preserved as authored - the same authored-form rule PD-BUG-118, PD-BUG-120, PD-BUG-124 and PD-BUG-152 established.
- **Actual**: All angle-bracket destinations are left byte-identical on both paths while the tight controls update correctly. Nothing is logged and nothing is counted as skipped. The parser records the target as <doc/a.md> including the brackets, so it never matches the moved file in the database and no rewrite is ever attempted.
- **Evidence**: Found 2026-08-31 during Code Review (PF-TSK-005) of the PD-BUG-152 fix. VERIFIED END TO END by driving the real service move handler on repo source, not inferred from the symptom: 3 angle-bracket checks fail and both tight controls pass in the same run. Capture confirmed at source - MarkdownParser records target <a.md> for [x] (<a.md>) and < a.md > for [x] (< a.md >), so neither can match a moved file. EXPOSURE MEASURED, and unlike PD-BUG-152 it is not zero: 2 angle-bracket destinations exist in this repo and BOTH RESOLVE to a real file - doc/state-tracking/features/6.1.1-Link Validation-implementation-state.md - referenced from feature-tracking.md and from the 6.1.1 implementation plan. The angle-bracket form is used there precisely because the filename contains a space, the one case CommonMark requires it for, so if that state file is ever moved or renamed both references go stale silently. NOT INTRODUCED by the PD-BUG-152 fix and not reachable by it: that fix widened the replacement pattern for whitespace around the destination, while this failure happens earlier, at capture. The PD-BUG-152 Step 20 sweep was scoped to the whitespace class and so did not reach other CommonMark destination forms. Severity filed Medium as one notch above PD-BUG-152 on the exposure difference (2 live resolvable instances versus 0 organic); Workflows and Scope are provisional pending triage.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-31 to Needs Fix, Medium / scope M / DI OB. Validity CONFIRMED independently rather than accepted from the report: both production rewrite entry points were driven on repo source - ReferenceLookup.update_links_within_moved_file and LinkUpdater.update_references, the same harness the PD-BUG-152 regression suite uses - giving 4 of 4 angle-bracket cases left byte-identical and 2 of 2 tight controls updated. Root cause confirmed at source in MarkdownParser._extract_url_from_link_content, which never unwraps the CommonMark angle-bracket destination; because the database is keyed on target, the updater path never selects the reference at all, so the failure is silent by construction rather than merely unlogged. Filed exposure held exactly: 2 live resolvable instances, both naming doc/state-tracking/features/6.1.1-Link Validation-implementation-state.md, from feature-tracking.md line 178 and the 6.1.1 implementation plan line 229; the only other angle-wrapped destination in the repo is the repro text inside this entry. No script emits the form, so exposure is not growing on its own. The record was EXTENDED on three measured points. (1) Blast radius is three capture sites, not one: markdown reference definitions capture the brackets identically, and so does the PowerShell embedded-markdown-link path - 0 live instances each today, but the same defect, so they belong in one fix. (2) The PowerShell site will CORRUPT rather than no-op once capture is fixed: its recorded span covers the brackets, so positional replacement would write the new target over them and emit a tight destination containing a space, which is invalid CommonMark - the span must be narrowed in the same change. (3) The validate scan cannot detect this class at all: the validator placeholder filter treats any angle-wrapped token as a template placeholder, so every angle-wrapped destination is skipped; confirmed by an angle-wrapped destination naming a missing file going unreported while its tight sibling was reported. The documented recovery procedure of running a validation scan after a blind window therefore does not cover this class, and fixing capture restores validation coverage for free. Priority Medium via the matrix - Impact Medium (real link-integrity failure, workaround is avoiding spaces in filenames), Frequency Low (2 instances) - and consistent with the family: PD-BUG-152 was Low at zero exposure, this sits one notch up on non-zero exposure that no tool can find afterwards. The Low reading was presented and not taken: nothing is corrupted and the link is left byte-identical, which is the reasoning that rated PD-BUG-153 Low at 258 instances, but here the link stops resolving after the move and nothing reports it. Scope set to M from blank: 4 fix sites across 2 modules and 2 parsers - capture in the markdown and PowerShell parsers, replace_markdown_target, replace_reference_target, plus the PowerShell span - matching PD-BUG-151 at M rather than the single-function PD-BUG-152 at S. Workflows widened from WF-001 WF-002 by adding WF-005 (multi-format move, the PowerShell parser is affected) and WF-009 (link health audit, the validator false negative above). Dims DI OB confirmed as filed: references go stale silently, and nothing is logged, counted, or auditable. FIX-TIME OBLIGATION recorded at triage: markdown links are validated even inside table rows, so once capture unwraps the brackets the angle-wrapped examples already sitting in the Description cell of this entry become real broken-link findings, and the Step 20 sweep must neutralise them.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-31
- **Fix**: Unwrap at capture, preserve at replacement. New shared helper utils.unwrap_angle_destination unwraps only a well-formed angle destination (opens and closes with the brackets, neither character between) and returns whether it unwrapped. Applied at MarkdownParser._extract_url_from_link_content, which serves BOTH the inline-link and reference-definition extractors, so one edit covers two capture sites; the title is peeled first. Applied at the PowerShell embedded-link scan, where the recorded span is narrowed by the bracket widths in the same step - load-bearing, because that reference is replaced positionally and unwrapping alone would splice the new target over the brackets and emit a tight destination containing a space, turning a silent no-op into invalid CommonMark. replacement.replace_markdown_target and replacement.replace_reference_target each try an angle-wrapped pattern variant before the tight one and write the brackets back verbatim; one edit covers both rewrite paths since MARKDOWN reaches replace_markdown_target through replace_markdown_at_span. Deliberately NOT normalized in BaseParser as the PD-BUG-151 whitespace fix was: angle brackets are markdown-link syntax, and stripping them globally would turn a YAML or PowerShell placeholder value into a file reference - verified that YAML and JSON still record such a value literally.
- **Root Cause**: CommonMark permits an inline link and a reference definition to enclose the destination in angle brackets, and REQUIRES that form when the path contains a space. MarkdownParser._extract_url_from_link_content stripped whitespace and peeled titles but never unwrapped the brackets, so link_target held the bracket syntax rather than the path. Because the link database is keyed on target, a moved file never matched such a reference at all - it was not skipped, it was never selected - so the failure was silent by construction on the updater path, and on the moved-file path the bracketed path failed the on-disk existence guard. The PowerShell embedded-markdown-link scan carried the same defect independently.
- **Tests Added**: Yes - TE-TST-165, 19 unit cases across capture and both rewrite paths, 15 red pre-fix and 19 green post-fix, plus manual validation script PD-BUG-155_angle_bracket_destination_validation.py driving the real daemon service.
- **Verification**: Full suite 1879 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failures. Mutation-verified across the whole non-performance suite: reverting each mechanism alone turns red 12 (markdown capture unwrap), 3 (PowerShell unwrap), 2 (PowerShell span narrowing), 6 (inline-markdown angle replacement) and 1 (reference-definition angle replacement). No mutation is silent and nothing outside the new suite is affected. This is deliberately NOT claimed as a 1:1 map: 9 tests are turned red by more than one mutation, which is a genuine pipeline dependency rather than a mapping failure, since a rewrite test cannot pass while either capture or replacement is broken. What is established is that every mechanism is independently detected. The span narrowing being independently detected settles an open question - the concurrent BaseParser span-tightening work does NOT already cover it, so the narrowing is load-bearing. A parallel session was editing parsers/base.py during this session, so the measurement ran under a drift guard: base.py, reference_lookup.py and updater.py were byte-identical across the entire measurement window. Manual validation scores 2 of 9 against the pre-fix code (exactly the two tight controls) and 9 of 9 after. CORRECTION to the triage note, which claimed that fixing capture restores validation coverage for free: that is only half true. Coverage IS restored for angle-wrapped paths WITHOUT spaces, measured discriminating - an angle-wrapped missing file goes from silently skipped to reported. It is NOT restored for angle-wrapped paths containing a space, because the validator refuses to check ANY target containing a space, a separate pre-existing filter unrelated to this bug and unaffected by this fix. Both live instances in this repo name a file whose name contains a space, so they now follow a move correctly but remain unverified by a validation scan. Repo-wide validate delta attributable to the fix, measured by temporary revert on todays tree: 0 links checked and 0 broken, explained by that same space filter. Self-inflicted findings were defused as planned: capture unwrapping turned the illustrative angle examples in the Description cell of this entry into live references, so the bracket-paren adjacency was broken in 7 places and validate went from 1485 to 1482 broken with zero findings sourced from this row. No E2E group flagged: no E2E fixture uses the angle form (0 occurrences measured) and behaviour is unchanged for every input the existing cases exercise, while flagging 2.1.1 would cascade 31 entries across 7 features and 6 workflows for a delta none of them can observe. Docs updated: capabilities reference paragraph and parser-table rows, FDD 2.1.1 AC-1 and AC-5b, TDD 2.1.1 PowerShell parser row, FDD 2.2.1 BR-11, TDD 2.2.1 replacer descriptions, and both feature state files.
- **Verification**: Code review APPROVED 2026-08-31 (PF-TSK-005). Verified independently rather than accepted from the fix record. Full suite reproduced at 1879 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failed. Lint clean at the real commit gate (flake8 --max-line-length=120 --extend-ignore=E203), black 23.7.0 (the pre-commit pinned binary, not local 25.x) and isort clean, zero dependency changes, 93 percent coverage across the four changed modules. Mutation claims re-measured by runtime patching, because source edits were barred with parallel sessions live in src: reverting the markdown capture unwrap turns 12 red and reverting the PowerShell span narrowing turns 2 red, both matching the record exactly. Those runs initially showed 27 and 17 failures; a no-op control plugin carrying the same early import reproduced 15 of them, establishing the surplus as harness artifact rather than the change. The span narrowing is therefore confirmed load-bearing and independently detected. Behavioural probes drove the real entry points, each with a passing tight control: the live exposure shape (feature-tracking.md line 178, a table row holding a wrapped destination with a space in the filename next to a tight link) rewrites correctly with brackets intact and the neighbour untouched; the PowerShell positional path writes the new target inside its brackets as <doc2/a b.md>; outer padding composes with the PD-BUG-152 groups as [x] ( <doc2/a.md> ); and an angle-wrapped URL is now correctly skipped as external, which is a genuine bonus fix beyond the filed scope. Filed exposure and the E2E decision both re-confirmed: 2 live instances, 0 angle-form occurrences in E2E fixtures. TWO MINOR RESIDUALS recorded, neither blocking and neither reachable on a production path today. (1) replace_markdown_target returns as soon as the angle variant matches, so a string mixing a wrapped and a tight link to the same target updates only the wrapped one. Production is unaffected because replace_markdown_at_span confines each rewrite to a single construct, but the documented safety-net fallback for an invalid or stale span loses the tight link, where before this change it updated it. Running both variants instead of returning early was verified safe, since the tight pattern cannot match a wrapped destination. (2) An inner-padded destination such as [x] (< doc/a.md >) is still left silently stale on a move, verified with a passing tight control in the same run. The diagnosis in this entry named that exact spelling as a failing capture case, and the fix neither closes it nor records it as out of scope. Leaving it is defensible because CommonMark makes inner spaces part of the destination, but that choice should be stated rather than left implicit. SEPARATE BUG FILED: PD-BUG-156 (Low) for the validator skip filter that discards any target containing a space - the residual this entry documented but did not track, which is why both live instances follow a move correctly yet can never be verified by a validation scan. Test tracking corrected under Step 26: the TE-TST-165 row sat at Implementation In Progress with a blank test count, exactly what Validate-TestTracking passes over silently; set to Completed with 19 cases and a run note, matching every sibling bug-fix row. Observed while there: the status value Completed is in wide use in test-tracking.md but is absent from that file own status legend.

### PD-BUG-150

- **Source**: Development
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link updating - within-moved-file replacement
- **Repro**: 1. In a watched project create doc/a.md and the directory sub/deeper/. 2. Create sub/note.md whose single line references the same real directory twice inside backtick spans: Both ../doc/ and ../doc/ are the same real directory (each ../doc/ wrapped in backticks). 3. With LinkWatcher running, move sub/note.md to sub/deeper/note.md. 4. Read the moved file.
- **Expected**: Both spans are re-depthed to ../../doc/ - each reference is rewritten at the position where the parser found it, so no span is rewritten twice and none is skipped.
- **Actual**: The first span becomes ../../../doc/ (over-deepened, now a broken path) and the second is left at ../doc/ (stale). The daemon logs moved_file_links_updated links_updated=2 with no warning, so nothing flags either the wrong depth or the skipped span.
- **Evidence**: Verified 2026-08-30 by driving the real ReferenceLookup.update_links_within_moved_file against a temp project on repo source v2.2.0. Controls that pass: two DISTINCT nested targets (../doc/ and ../doc/sub/) rewrite correctly, because the first target does not survive inside its own replacement; duplicate markdown links rewrite correctly via the content-global regex branch. Structural precondition measured across 2612 scanned files: 1283 lines carry two or more non-markdown references that are duplicates or nested. The corrupting subset is those where the old target survives as a substring of its replacement, which is always true for the ../ depth family a move-deeper produces. Triaged inline 2026-08-30 during Bug Triage of PD-BUG-149 (PF-TSK-041): High confirmed, scope M.
- **Triage**: Sibling-path prior art added at triage: PD-BUG-098 (closed 2026-04-29) is the SAME defect class, already fixed in the OTHER rewrite path. updater.py _apply_replacements gained descending-column processing, a _filter_contained_overlaps pre-pass, and an occurrence-counting bounded fallback that skips with an ambiguous_fallback_replacement_skipped warning when the target appears more than once on the line. reference_lookup._replace_links_in_lines - the within-moved-file path - received none of that and still does a position-blind replace(target, new, 1). This is the same shape as PD-BUG-118 to PD-BUG-120: the choke-point fix landed for references in OTHER files and the sibling path inside the moved file was left uncovered. The fix should reuse the PD-BUG-098 shape rather than invent a new one; its regression tests live in test_updater.py TestOverlappingReferenceCorruption and give a model for the new ones.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-30
- **Fix**: Restructured reference_lookup._replace_links_in_lines into two ordered phases. Phase 1 rewrites every non-markdown reference at the column span the parser recorded, processed descending by (line, column) so an earlier replacement cannot invalidate the next span, and routed through the new shared helpers (contained-overlap pre-pass plus bounded ambiguous-case refusal). Phase 2 runs the markdown content-global regex last (ordering is load-bearing: its length changes would otherwise stale every Phase 1 column) and deduplicates by target, removing a spurious link_pattern_not_found warning on correctly-rewritten duplicate markdown links. New leaf module src/linkwatcher/replacement.py holds filter_contained_overlaps and replace_at_position, extracted from updater.py; LinkUpdater now delegates to it, so one hardened implementation serves both rewrite paths instead of two divergent copies. Net -240/+123 across the two existing files. Extraction was chosen over calling updater methods across the class boundary because the moved-file test fixture injects a MagicMock updater, which would have returned a MagicMock line, raised inside the caller catch-all, and silently returned 0.
- **Root Cause**: reference_lookup._replace_links_in_lines used lines[idx].replace(target, new, 1), discarding the column_start/column_end every parsed reference carries. With two or more references whose targets are duplicates or nested on one line, every replacement therefore landed on the LEFTMOST match: the first span was rewritten a second time inside its own replacement (the old target survives as a substring of the new one, always true for the ../ depth family a move-deeper produces), and the later span was never touched. Silent in both directions - the full count is logged and no warning is emitted. Same defect class as PD-BUG-098, which fixed it in the updater path only; the moved-file sibling was left uncovered, the same shape as PD-BUG-118 through PD-BUG-120.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Seven regression tests in TestBug150ColumnPositionIgnored (test_reference_lookup.py); six drive the real parser and real updater end to end, the seventh feeds hand-built contained-overlap refs. Written fail-first: 4 red before the fix with the exact filed signature, including three-copy compounding to ../../../../doc/. Full suite 1767 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failures. Manual validation script test/bug-validation/PD-BUG-150_moved_file_column_position_validation.py runs through the real service handler and scores 0 of 4 pre-fix, 4 of 4 post-fix. Mutation-verified: leftmost-replace turns exactly 3 red, ascending order 5, dropping the markdown de-duplication exactly the markdown test, dropping the contained-overlap pre-pass exactly the overlap test; no mutation leaves the set green. Assertions compare the whole rewritten line rather than substring counts, after mutation testing showed count-based assertions passing on splice-corrupted output. E2E re-execution deliberately NOT flagged (human-approved): the Update-TestExecutionStatus -WhatIf cascade is 38 entries across 8 features and 9 workflows because the covering tests are cross-cutting, while the behavioural delta is confined to duplicate or nested references on one line, which no E2E case exercises. No ADR: the shared module is an extraction of existing logic, not a new architectural pattern.
- **Verification**: Code Review 2026-08-30 (PF-TSK-005): REJECTED - 1 Critical, 1 Major. CRITICAL regression introduced by this fix: _replace_links_in_lines Phase 1 routes every non-MARKDOWN type into positional replacement, but MARKDOWN_REFERENCE records a span covering the WHOLE line, so the splice overwrites label and title. Measured by driving the real update_links_within_moved_file end to end: the line [lbl]: ../doc/a.md "Title" became ../../doc/a.md, losing both label and title, silently (links_updated=1, no warning). Confirmed a REGRESSION, not pre-existing: the replaced lines[idx].replace(target, new, 1) preserved label and title correctly. ROOT CAUSE - the irony of the fix: updater.py never calls _replace_at_position raw, it dispatches through _replace_in_line (MARKDOWN to _replace_markdown_target, MARKDOWN_REFERENCE to _replace_reference_target) before falling through to positional replacement (updater.py near line 477). Phase 1 adopted the guarded primitive but not its guard, so a fix whose stated purpose was ending the divergence between the two rewrite paths reintroduced a narrower one. BOUNDED EXACTLY: probing all 18 link-type shapes for spans wider than their target found only three - markdown (safe, routed to Phase 2) and the two markdown-reference forms (both corrupted). Every other type is exact or handled by the quote/escape branches, so MARKDOWN_REFERENCE is the only affected type. EXPOSURE: 24 relative reference definitions in LinkWatcher, all in E2E fixtures under single-file-move-links-updated, which are moved during E2E runs; 0 in production documentation; 0 in FrameworkBuilder. Blast radius exceeds the rewritten line - destroying the label orphans every [text][label] usage in that file. MAJOR: the full suite is green (1767 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed) with replacement.py at 100 percent line coverage while this regression sits in the delta; the seven new tests cover duplicate, nested, three-copy, markdown-duplicate and overlap cases but never a reference definition, so line coverage was actively misleading here. REQUIRED REWORK: add the MARKDOWN_REFERENCE branch to Phase 1, reusing the _replace_reference_target shape and preferably moving the type dispatch itself into linkwatcher.replacement so the guard travels with the primitive; add a regression test for the reference-definition shape (with and without a title). SUGGESTIONS: ambiguous skips in the moved-file path log twice (ambiguous_fallback_replacement_skipped then link_target_not_found_on_line) and pass no on_ambiguous_skip callback, so there is no counter; links_updated counts 1 for two duplicate markdown links (deliberate and documented in the test). POSITIVE: leaf-module extraction with a documented ordering contract is the right structural call, and the rationale for extraction over cross-class calls (MagicMock updater in the fixture) is sound; the new_target == link_target guard placed before Phase 1 correctly prevents spurious warnings on no-op rewrites; tests assert whole rewritten lines rather than substring counts, with the rationale recorded. Lint clean under the pinned toolchain (black 23.7.0, isort, flake8). Pre-existing and untouched by this change, observation only: Phase 2 does not carry the link-text update that _replace_markdown_target performs, and the MP-002_reference_links.md E2E fixtures already contain historical corruption.
- **Fix**: Rework after Code Review REJECTED the first fix. Moved the link-type dispatch out of LinkUpdater into linkwatcher.replacement, beside the primitives it guards: a new replace_in_line entry point routes MARKDOWN to replace_markdown_target and MARKDOWN_REFERENCE to replace_reference_target, and everything else to replace_at_position. Both format-aware replacers moved to the shared module along with the TD142 bounded regex cache, now an lru_cache. LinkUpdater keeps five thin delegators, so the updater path behaviour is unchanged and its tests were untouched. reference_lookup Phase 1 now calls replace_in_line instead of replace_at_position, which is the whole fix for the regression. The reviewed observability suggestion was fixed in the same change on human approval at the Step 23 checkpoint: an ambiguous PD-BUG-098 refusal in the moved-file path was reported twice, the second time as link_target_not_found_on_line naming a wrong cause, and was never counted; it is now reported once by the primitive and tallied into a single moved_file_ambiguous_skips warning carrying the count.
- **Root Cause**: updater dispatches on link type in _replace_in_line BEFORE falling through to positional replacement. The first fix extracted the primitive without that guard, so Phase 1 routed every non-MARKDOWN reference into the column-span splice, including MARKDOWN_REFERENCE, whose recorded span covers the WHOLE definition line. Splicing the bare target across that span destroyed the label and the title. Measured broader at the rework than filed at review: all three reference-definition shapes lose the entire label prefix, not only the title - with title, without title, and with a directory target. Silent, and counted as a success. A fix whose stated purpose was ending the divergence between the two rewrite paths had reintroduced a narrower one, because the guard did not travel with the primitive it guards.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: REWORK verified 2026-08-30. Five regression tests added to TestBug150ColumnPositionIgnored, 12 total: reference definition with title, without title, with a directory target, alongside positional refs, plus ambiguous-skip reporting. Written fail-first - 4 red with the exact filed signature before the fix and the 7 existing tests green throughout; the observability test red with both warnings present. Bound measured independently rather than inherited from the review: an end-to-end sweep of 33 link-type shapes across seven parsers found MARKDOWN and MARKDOWN_REFERENCE to be the only types whose recorded span is wider than their target - 30 shapes already clean, all 33 clean after. Mutation-verified: dropping the MARKDOWN_REFERENCE branch turns exactly the 4 new tests red and nothing else, as does reverting Phase 1 to call replace_at_position directly, which is the rejected fix behaviour exactly; dropping the MARKDOWN branch turns 2 updater tests red, so the moved dispatch is load-bearing for both paths; leftmost replace 4 red; ascending order 6 red; each of the three ambiguous-skip sub-mechanisms turns exactly its own test red. No mutation leaves the set green. Manual validation extended to 5 checks through the real service handler: 5 of 5 post-rework and 3 of 5 against the rejected fix - the rejected fix passed every check the original fix shipped with, and only the whole-file comparison and the new label-and-title check caught it. Full suite 1808 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failures. Lint clean under the pinned toolchain (black 23.7.0, isort, flake8). E2E re-execution again NOT flagged: the six real reference definitions in the E2E fixtures were measured to round-trip byte-identically to the last-closed behaviour, so the rework removes an unintended delta rather than adding one, and the fixtures on disk are untouched. Documentation defects found in the rejected fix own FDD edit and corrected here: duplicate requirement IDs (two FR-6, two AC-6) and a new FR-6 contradicting the existing FR-5, which already mandated the type dispatch - the fix violated the FDD of its own feature; renumbered to FR-12, AC-9, AC-10. FOR THE REVIEWER: a parallel session landed the PD-BUG-151 parser fix into this working tree at 12:55 mid-rework, and it mutates column_start and column_end in a shared base-parser pass; the 33-shape bound and the full suite were both re-measured after it landed and both hold, and the MARKDOWN_REFERENCE span is still wide, so the dispatch remains load-bearing rather than redundant.
- **Verification**: Code Review 2026-08-30 (PF-TSK-005), round 2 on the rework: REJECTED - 1 Major. ROUND-1 CRITICAL IS FIXED, verified independently rather than taken on trust: an oracle requiring that rewriting one reference change ONLY that reference (mapping a sentinel new target back must reproduce the line byte for byte) reports zero damage under the fix and turns exactly the three reference-definition shapes red under the rejected behaviour; mutation-verified by reverting Phase 1 to replace_at_position at runtime, which turns exactly the 4 reference-definition tests red and nothing else, while the leftmost mutation turns 5 red - so the new tests are load-bearing for the mechanism they name. MAJOR - THE WIDE-SPAN BOUND IS WRONG. The fix rests on MARKDOWN and MARKDOWN_REFERENCE being the only link types whose recorded span exceeds their target, stated as load-bearing truth in FDD FR-12, in the TDD and in the replacement.py docstring ("those two branches are the whole guard"). POWERSHELL_QUOTED_DIR is a third. powershell.py line 209 sets potential_dir = match.group(1).strip(), so the TARGET is stripped while column_start/column_end keep the UNSTRIPPED span; the PD-BUG-151 normalizer early-returns on exactly that shape (stripped == target, nothing to do), so the wide span reaches the Phase 1 positional splice. Measured end to end through the real move handler: a PowerShell assignment of a quoted extensionless directory written with padding, " doc/target ", comes back as "../doc/target" - the author whitespace destroyed, silently, and counted as links_updated=1. CONFIRMED A REGRESSION, not pre-existing: emulating the pre-fix branch reproduces the PD-BUG-150 filed defect exactly (compounded ../../../doc/a.md alongside a stale ../doc/a.md), and under that same emulation the padding survives. BOUND RE-DERIVED INDEPENDENTLY rather than inherited from the 33-shape sweep: a span-width census over 2612 files and 44239 real references gives MARKDOWN 13594 wide, MARKDOWN_REFERENCE 25 wide, PYTHON_IMPORT 104 handled by its own branch, POWERSHELL_QUOTED_DIR 16 wide, and every other type exact or quote-spanning or escaped. BLAST RADIUS is exactly the stripped whitespace by construction, since target equals span.strip(), so no link is broken. EXPOSURE: 16 such references in this repo, 0 of them resolvable on disk, therefore 0 corruptible today - all 16 are prose misdetected as directories, for example a Write-Host message with leading indentation. NOT A NEW PARSER PROPERTY: TestPowerShellLeadingWhitespace (PD-BUG-065) already records that the strip fixed the target and left the span, and it asserts only the target, so no test anywhere covers the span. Severity Major rests on the false documented bound rather than on the whitespace: the data loss alone is Minor-grade with zero exposure, but shipping a known-false completeness claim inside the fix whose stated purpose is to stop this class recurring is precisely the pattern that produced PD-BUG-098, then PD-BUG-118 through PD-BUG-120, then PD-BUG-150, then the rejected first fix. REQUIRED REWORK, narrow - not a redesign: (1) extend base.py _normalize_reference to also shrink a span that is wider than an already-tight target (the span.strip() == target case), which by construction excludes both markdown types, whose spans do not strip to their target, and the escaped structured types - one choke point serving every parser, the same shape PD-BUG-151 established; NOTE base.py belongs to PD-BUG-151, which is In Progress in a parallel session, so coordinate rather than editing blind. (2) Add a regression test for the padded PowerShell quoted directory driven through a real move, since none exists. (3) Correct the bound in FDD FR-12, in the TDD and in the replacement.py docstring so the next reader is not told the guard is complete when it is not. MINOR, pre-existing and untouched by this change, route as technical debt rather than blocking: Phase 2 keeps its own inline markdown regex instead of the shared replace_markdown_target, so it omits the PD-BUG-012 link-text update - measured, the moved-file path rewrites the target but leaves link text that equalled the old target stale, where the shared replacer updates both; while that second copy remains, the claim that one hardened implementation serves both rewrite paths is overstated.
