SUGGESTION: links_updated counts 1 when two duplicate markdown links are rewritten (deliberate and documented in the tests). POSITIVE: moving the link-type dispatch beside the primitives it guards is the right structural call, and the guard now travels with the primitive; the observability fix is real and properly covered, with a single moved_file_ambiguous_skips tally and no second warning naming a wrong cause; full suite 1808 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, read from the log summary line rather than from the exit code; lint clean under the pinned hooks (black 23.7.0, isort 5.12.0, flake8 6.0.0); coverage 98 percent on replacement.py, though coverage is again not the evidence here; and the FDD, TDD and docstring record of the round-1 failure is unusually honest about what went wrong and why.
- **Fix**: Round 3 after Code Review round 2 rejected the rework. Completed the parser-side span invariant at its existing choke point: BaseParser._tighten_span (11 lines of logic in parsers/base.py) shrinks a recorded span that is wider than an already-tight target and strips to it. _normalize_reference enforced only the mirror case (a padded target) and early-returned whenever the target was already tight - exactly the state the powershell.py quoted-directory scan leaves, where potential_dir = match.group(1).strip() sits beside column_start=match.start(1). Keyed on span.strip() == target, so construct-spanning markdown spans and escaped structured spans are excluded by construction rather than by an enumeration of link types. Chosen over fixing powershell.py directly, which would have left the class open for the next parser that strips a target. Repairs BOTH rewrite paths from one place: a regression in the moved-file path and a pre-existing defect in the updater path. Round-2 required-rework item 3 was inverted on human approval at the Step 14 checkpoint: the falsified bound is REMOVED rather than corrected, at all four sites (2.1.1-FR-9, 2.2.1-FR-12, both TDDs, the replacement.py module docstring), each now resting on the enforced invariant plus a type-agnostic sweep test, because correcting an enumeration only resets the clock until the next parser falsifies it.
- **Root Cause**: PD-BUG-151 installed the invariant that link_target carries no surrounding whitespace and that the column_start to column_end span covers exactly that target, but enforced only the first half of it. _normalize_reference early-returns on stripped == target, which is precisely the state a parser leaves when it strips the captured text INTO link_target while the columns keep the raw match extent. The powershell.py quoted-directory scan does exactly that, so the recorded span still covered padding the target no longer named, and positional replacement spliced the bare target across the whole span and destroyed the authored whitespace - silently, and counted as a successful update. The invariant was asserted in the parse_content docstring and left unenforced in the code.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Updated**: 2026-08-31
- **Fix**: See the round-3 Fix entry immediately above; this update carries the round-3 verification record, which the script cannot append without re-supplying FixDetails.
- **Verification**: ROUND 3 verified 2026-08-31. 22 new tests written fail-first: 10 red pre-fix with the exact filed signature, tight-spelling controls green throughout. Damage oracle over the real repo - rewrite every reference with a sentinel, map it back, require the line byte for byte - reports 16 damaged of 47057 before the fix and 0 of 47231 after, every one POWERSHELL_QUOTED_DIR. The bound was re-derived by DAMAGE rather than by a shape census, which is how rounds 1 and 2 got it wrong. Mutation-verified 1:1 with the FULL suite per mutation, since a scoped run gives a false map: removing the fix turns exactly the 10 padding tests red and nothing else, leading-edge-only turns 7, removing the span.strip guard turns exactly the 2 indented reference-definition tests. Full suite 1893 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, read from the log summary line rather than the exit code. Manual validation through the real service handler scores 6 of 6 post-fix and 5 of 6 against the defective build, with only the new check failing - checks 1 to 5, which the previous rounds shipped with, all pass against the defect, so the prior validation could not have caught this. THREE CORRECTIONS, each measured rather than inherited from the review. (1) SCOPE IS BOTH PATHS, not moved-file-only as framed at review: the updater path carries the same damage and there it is PRE-EXISTING, confirmed at HEAD where powershell.py already stripped the target and updater.py already spliced at columns; one choke point repairs both. (2) THE GUARD IS NOT LOAD-BEARING. With the span.strip guard removed the oracle still reports zero damage, because MARKDOWN_REFERENCE is rewritten by a pattern-anchored replacer that never reads the span. The new test therefore pins what the guard actually guarantees - that normalization never moves a span it does not understand - rather than a corruption it does not prevent. Claiming otherwise would repeat the false-completeness pattern that got rounds 1 and 2 rejected. (3) MY FIRST ORACLE WAS WRONG: it flagged 21, of which 5 were an oracle blind spot, since the escaped-structured branch legitimately re-spells the target through json.dumps; corrected before reporting. E2E re-execution again NOT flagged, measured for THIS delta rather than inherited: 0 affected references across the E2E fixtures (8884 references), the whole test tree and src, while all 16 instances sit under process-framework/scripts and none resolve on disk. No ADR: completing an existing invariant at an existing choke point is not a new architectural pattern. Lint clean under the pinned toolchain (black 23.7.0, isort 5.12.0, flake8). FOR THE REVIEWER: a parallel session landed PD-BUG-155 work into this working tree at 14:23, editing powershell.py - the file this fix depends on. Its fail-first test was briefly red in my pre-commit run; confirmed not caused by this change (it passes with and without it), and the oracle, the regression tests and the full suite were all re-measured against the post-155 tree rather than trusting the earlier numbers.
- **Verification**: Code Review 2026-08-31 (PF-TSK-005), round 3 on the parser-side fix: APPROVED - 0 Critical, 1 Major routed to TD311 rather than blocking (human decision at the Step 22 checkpoint), 1 Minor, 2 observations. THE ROUND-2 MAJOR IS FIXED, verified by independent measurement rather than from this record. Two damage oracles were built for this review - identity rewrite must be a byte-for-byte no-op, and sentinel rewrite must be exactly reversible - and run over 44673 real references through replace_in_line: 0 damaged. With _tighten_span disabled the same oracles report exactly 16 damaged, all POWERSHELL_QUOTED_DIR, all under process-framework/scripts and none in src, test or the E2E fixtures, independently reproducing the recorded 16. A span-relation census over 46251 references finds 0 wide-by-whitespace spans; every remaining wide span belongs to a class the module type sets already name (markdown construct 13624, quote-spanning 221, PYTHON_IMPORT 107, escaped JSON 57, MARKDOWN_REFERENCE 25). A synthetic sweep of 38 constructed padded shapes across all seven parsers, plus angle-bracket, tab, NBSP and quote-spanning variants, finds 0 damaged. Mutation-verified 1:1 with the FULL suite per mutation: disabling _tighten_span turns exactly 10 red and nothing else; dropping the span.strip guard turns exactly the 2 indented reference-definition tests. Full suite 1893 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, read from the log summary line rather than the exit code. Lint clean under the pinned hooks (black 23.7.0, isort 5.12.0, flake8 6.0.0). Manual validation through the real service handler 6 of 6. The no-E2E-re-execution call was re-measured for this delta rather than inherited: 0 of 8881 E2E fixture references have a recorded span this fix changes. MAJOR, routed to TD311: the falsified bound survives verbatim in the replace_in_line docstring near replacement.py line 487, citing the same 33-shape measurement round 2 rejected, and contradicting both its own module docstring (Do not read that list as a complete inventory of wide spans) and the type sets declared about 400 lines above it. The record states the enumeration was removed at all four sites; this function docstring is an uncovered fifth, so that completeness claim is itself incomplete - the third consecutive round in which this fix overstates its own completeness. No data is at risk and the code is correct, which is why it was routed rather than blocking. MINOR, not filed: one of the eight rows in test_no_parser_records_a_whitespace_wide_span is vacuous - the m.py row (a padded quoted path in a .py file) parses to ZERO references, so the assertion passes for free and proves nothing; confirmed under mutation, where only the m.ps1 row goes red. That test carries the type-agnostic guarantee which replaced the removed enumeration, so a free-passing row inside it matters more than it would elsewhere; the sibling PowerShell test already has the presence assertion that would close it. OBSERVATIONS, pre-existing and outside this delta, recorded not filed: the Python parser emits no reference at all for a padded quoted path, unlike every other parser, so such a reference is silently untracked and goes stale on a move; and HTML_ANCHOR retains padding in link_target because its recorded span includes the quotes, the same silent-stale class. Exposure for both was measured at effectively zero - 3 padded targets across 46251 repo references, all three inside an E2E fixture that already carries historical corruption. POSITIVE: fixing at the parser-side choke point rather than in powershell.py repairs both rewrite paths from one place and covers a parser added later that nobody remembers to list; keying the condition on a property rather than an enumeration survived a third independent falsification attempt, by damage, by census and by constructed shapes; the updater-path test drives LinkUpdater end to end on a real temp project and compares whole lines; and the record explicitly declines to claim the span.strip guard is load-bearing for corruption, which mutation 2 confirms - its two red tests pin the recorded columns, not a corruption it prevents. That restraint is the opposite of the pattern that got rounds 1 and 2 rejected.

### PD-BUG-156

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link validation - target skip filters
- **Repro**: 1. Create doc/a b.md and a markdown link to it written in the CommonMark angle form. 2. Delete doc/a b.md so the link is stale. 3. Run the validate launcher and read the report.
- **Expected**: The stale link is reported as broken, exactly as an equivalent link to a space-free filename is.
- **Actual**: Nothing is reported. The target is discarded before the on-disk check by the contains-whitespace skip predicate, so the scan reports clean.
- **Evidence**: Measured 2026-08-31 during Code Review (PF-TSK-005) of PD-BUG-155 on repo source. LinkValidator._should_check_target returns skip reason contains whitespace for target doc/a b.md, while the same target without a space returns CHECKED ON DISK - a discriminating control. Repo exposure: 2 live angle-wrapped links, both naming doc/state-tracking/features/6.1.1-Link Validation-implementation-state.md, from feature-tracking.md line 178 and the 6.1.1 implementation plan line 229. Pre-existing and unrelated to the PD-BUG-155 fix, which cannot reach it: PD-BUG-155 corrected capture, this is a separate skip filter applied later. Recorded in the PD-BUG-155 verification notes as a known residual but not previously tracked.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-31 to Needs Fix, Low / scope S / OB. Validity CONFIRMED independently rather than accepted from the report, with a discriminating control on the same line: at feature-tracking.md line 178 the space-containing target is refused by the contains-whitespace predicate at validator.py line 160 while its space-free sibling on that same line is checked. THREE CORRECTIONS to the filed record, all measured repo-wide on repo source. (1) Live exposure is 3 references, not 2, and the class is NOT the CommonMark angle form: doc/PD-documentation-map.md line 70 carries a TIGHT markdown destination with a raw space naming the same state file, alongside the two angle-wrapped instances at feature-tracking.md line 178 and the 6.1.1 implementation plan line 229. All three resolve on disk today. The affected population is any explicit markdown link whose target contains a space. (2) The obvious fix is wrong. Removing the predicate outright was measured at plus 60 references checked and plus 54 reported broken, with ZERO of the 54 genuine: all are backticked shell commands, code snippets and YAML or JSON description prose. The predicate is load-bearing noise suppression and must be narrowed, not deleted. A narrow variant exempting the existing _EXPLICIT_LINK_TYPES frozenset measures plus 5 checked and plus 2 reported, both of them illustrative examples in the capabilities handbook, and none of the 68 noise items resurface. The existing suite does not block that variant: the single test pinning whitespace-skip uses link type generic-unquoted, a non-explicit type. (3) Context the report omits, which argues against raising priority: the baseline scan already reports 1482 broken links of 7432 checked, so one unreported link is not the binding constraint on link health here. Priority Low CONFIRMED via the matrix, Impact Low times Frequency Low: nothing corrupts, all 3 references resolve, and after PD-BUG-155 they follow moves correctly, so the gap bites only in the blind-window or manually-stale case. Consistent with the family, where PD-BUG-153 was Low at 258 instances on the same nothing-corrupts reading while PD-BUG-155 earned Medium because links actually went stale. Scope set to S from blank: one predicate line plus defusing 2 handbook examples plus regression tests, against PD-BUG-155 at M with 4 sites across 2 modules and 2 parsers. Dims OB confirmed as filed and DI deliberately excluded: the 6.1.1 Dimension Profile marks DI Not Applicable because validation is read-only scanning. Workflows WF-009 confirmed as the only workflow running --validate. No duplicates: zero prior mentions of this filter in the bug archive and no technical debt item covers it. FIX-TIME OBLIGATION recorded at triage: capabilities-reference lines 41 and 49 carry doc/a b.md and path/file with space.md as illustrative markdown links, which become live broken-link findings the moment the fix lands and must be neutralised in the same change, the same obligation PD-BUG-155 triage recorded for its own description-cell examples. OBSERVATION routed separately, not filed: doc/PD-documentation-map.md is generated by Build-DocumentationMap.ps1, which emits a tight destination containing a raw space and is therefore not valid CommonMark, a framework-side generator defect belonging in IMP tracking rather than here.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-31
- **Fix**: Narrowed the contains-whitespace skip predicate in validator.py to exempt explicit link types: the lambda now reads space-in-target AND link-type not in _EXPLICIT_LINK_TYPES. That frozenset was already defined 30 lines above in the same module for the sibling bare-filename predicate, where BUG-088 drew the identical explicit-versus-heuristic line, so the fix applies a decision the file had already made rather than inventing one. Deleting the predicate outright was measured and REJECTED: plus 54 broken-link reports repo-wide, zero of them genuine. Two scoped rules were added to tools/linkwatcher/.linkwatcher-ignore for the capabilities-reference illustrative examples that the fix turned into live findings, discharging the fix-time obligation recorded at triage; suppression was chosen over the PD-BUG-155 precedent of breaking bracket-paren adjacency because that paragraph teaches the correct CommonMark syntax and mangling the example would teach the wrong form. Repo-wide effect: 7432 to 7438 links checked, broken unchanged at 1482, and all 3 live space-containing references now verify.
- **Root Cause**: The contains-whitespace entry in _TARGET_SKIP_PREDICATES was applied link-type-BLIND. For heuristically detected targets - backticked commands, bare prose paths, YAML and JSON description strings - a space almost always means the string is not a path, which is why the predicate exists and why it must stay. Applied to explicit link syntax it discarded deliberately authored destinations, including the CommonMark angle form that exists precisely for paths containing a space. Because the reference was dropped before the disk check it was never selected at all, so the failure was silent by construction: no warning, no count, no skipped-link entry, and absent from links_checked.
- **Tests Added**: Yes - 4 end-to-end tests and 11 parametrize cases added to test_validator.py (5 accept, 6 skip-guard), tracked count synced 90 to 94; plus manual validation script test/bug-validation/PD-BUG-156_validation_whitespace_skip_validation.py
- **Verification**: S-scope quick path: human-approved at the Step 11 and Step 23 checkpoints. Reproduced end to end BEFORE fixing with two passing space-free controls: 4 links all naming missing files, the 2 space-free controls reported and the 2 space-containing ones silently skipped, links_checked 2 of 4. Full suite 1901 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failed - 1893 plus the 8 new tests exactly, so no parallel-session drift. Lint clean at the real gate using pre-commit pinned black 23.7.0 rather than local 25.x, plus flake8 and isort. MUTATION-VERIFIED 1:1 WITH A CONTROL: a no-op plugin carrying the same early import gave 1901 passed and 0 failed, establishing no harness artifact, while reverting the predicate to link-type-blind turned exactly 8 red - precisely the 8 assertions pinning the fixed behaviour, nothing outside the new set. The 7 guard assertions correctly stay GREEN under mutation because the mutation restores what they pin. Runtime-patched by plugin rather than source edit, so a parallel session in src could not contaminate the measurement. Manual validation discriminates: 4 of 6 pre-fix and 6 of 6 post-fix, both controls passing in both columns, links checked 3 pre-fix versus 8 post-fix. STEP 20 SWEEP measured all 12 skip predicates for the same shape - references uniquely suppressed whose target still resolves on disk - and found NO sibling defect: bare filename has 114 such hits but is ALREADY link-type-aware by design (BUG-088), so those are intended heuristic prose mentions, and the 2 residual hits under whitespace are anchor-stripping artifacts where a target beginning with a hash splits to an empty prefix that _target_exists treats as a pure anchor. NO E2E GROUP FLAGGED, measured not assumed: the WhatIf blast radius is 5 entries across 4 features and 2 workflows, which would drag in TE-E2E-038 (WF-010, currently Passed), yet of 7368 references across 1339 E2E fixture files the 24 affected all sit in TE-E2E-004, a move test this validator-only fix cannot reach, and the WF-009 group has zero space-containing targets and is already at Needs Re-execution. Docs updated: the capabilities reference statement that validate still does not check ANY target containing a space was FALSIFIED by this fix and rewritten, a target-filtering bullet was added to its Validation Mode section, the link-health-audit integration narrative now records which two predicates are link-type-aware, and the 6.1.1 state file Issues and Resolutions Log carries the entry. TDD, FDD and test specification are N-A, verified by checking rather than assumed: feature 6.1.1 is Tier 1 and has none of the three. OBSERVATION recorded not filed: Build-DocumentationMap.ps1 generates PD-documentation-map.md line 70 with a tight markdown destination containing a raw space, which is not valid CommonMark, so that generated link does not render as a link - a framework-side generator defect belonging in IMP tracking rather than here.

### PD-BUG-158

- **Source**: Deployment
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Deployment install_global.py
- **Repro**: 1. Have at least one LinkWatcher daemon and the Control Panel running from the install venv. 2. Run python deployment/install_global.py. 3. Observe it stop each process, then abort with the venv python.exe still locked message before any files are copied. 4. Re-run the same command immediately; it completes normally.
- **Expected**: After stopping the processes it identified, the installer waits briefly and retries the lock check, so a normal release run completes in one invocation.
- **Actual**: The installer aborts before copying, reporting that the install venv python.exe is still locked by a running process, even though every process it stopped is already gone.
- **Evidence**: Release v2.2.1 deploy on 2026-08-31. Install run 1 stopped PIDs 9632, 23128, 18032 and 18960, then aborted on the lock check. A Win32_Process query immediately afterwards showed zero processes with an ExecutablePath under bin/.linkwatcher-venv. Install run 2, started about a minute later with nothing left to stop, copied all files and completed successfully.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-31 (PF-TSK-041), human approved. Valid, verified in source: stop_daemons_using_venv (line 97) issues taskkill /F /T and returns without waiting, and venv_python_locked (line 124) runs on the next statement in main() (lines 705-707), so the gate can observe a process tree that has been signalled but has not yet exited. Mechanism corrected during triage: the report attributed the failure to a file handle lingering after process exit, but the supporting Win32_Process query ran seconds later, after the abort. The simpler and likelier cause is that the killed processes had not finished exiting when the gate ran (four PIDs, each daemon a two-process pair, tree-kill). Fix direction is therefore the root cause rather than a settle window: stop_daemons_using_venv should poll until the PIDs it killed are gone (bounded), with a bounded re-check in venv_python_locked kept only as a backstop, which also covers the PD-BUG-110 auto-restart case. Priority Medium (Impact Low x Frequency High): the abort is clean, firing before any file copy, so the existing install is untouched and the workaround is a re-run. Scope S: one function plus a backstop, test precedent in test_pd_bug_106_installer_venv_daemon_stop.py and test_pd_bug_110_installer_venv_rebuild_retry.py, about 1-2 hours. Dims ID OB: ID for the broken contract with asynchronous OS process termination, OB for an error message that asserts a running process which does not exist and misdirects diagnosis. DI deliberately not tagged: unlike PD-BUG-106 and PD-BUG-110 nothing is half-written. Workflows none: WF-001 to WF-011 are all runtime workflows and none covers installation. Not a duplicate. PD-BUG-106 (other-project daemons never stopped), PD-BUG-110 (daemon auto-restart between stop and rebuild) and PD-BUG-121 (unverified PID from a stale lock) are three distinct mechanisms in the same stop and verify path; the recurrence pattern is a tech-debt candidate, not part of this fix. Description overstatement trimmed: every normal release was extrapolated from a single observation.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-31
- **Fix**: deployment/install_global.py: added wait_for_venv_python_release (bounded poll, 15 s / 0.25 s, of the exact condition the gate tests rather than a proxy such as process disappearance); stop_daemons_using_venv now waits for that release before returning and warns on genuine timeout, and returns the stopped PIDs; venv_lock_holder_report replaces the abort message that asserted a running process with one that names the surviving venv PIDs or states plainly that the holder is outside the venv (the OB half). create_linkwatcher_venv inherits the wait for free, strengthening the PD-BUG-110 defence. Step 20 sweep found no sibling defect: stop_linkwatcher.ps1 already has a settle loop and the Control Panel already has wait_for_exit, so the installer was the only termination path without a wait. Live verification of the fixed path against both real daemons: stop returned at 2146 ms, gate clear, install proceeds. doc/ci-cd/release-process.md updated to describe the wait.
- **Root Cause**: stop_daemons_using_venv issued taskkill /F /T per PID and returned without waiting, while main() ran the venv_python_locked pre-flight gate on the very next statement with zero tolerance. Killing is asynchronous, so any residual lock at that instant aborted the release before a single file was copied. REPRODUCED 2026-08-31 by replaying the production sequence against two live daemons: the gate read locked=True the moment stop returned and the lock cleared 52 ms later with no surviving venv process. An identical second run did not reproduce, so the window is real but intermittent. Two synthetic probes (one process; one parent plus three children) did NOT reproduce it - taskkill blocked until the image was writable in both - which is why the fix waits for the condition rather than sleeping a fixed amount. Whether the processes were still terminating or already gone with the handle outstanding at the failing instant remains unpinned; the fix is indifferent to which. Both filed mechanism claims were wrong: the report blamed a handle lingering after exit, and triage substituted processes still exiting, neither of which the probes support as a general property.
- **Tests Added**: Yes - TE-TST-166, 8 cases (test_pd_bug_158_installer_venv_release_wait.py). Fail-first verified at the real seam: both kills executed, then the gate read True. Mutation-verified 1:1 - removing only the wait turns exactly 2 red and leaves 43 green; breaking only the holder report turns exactly 1 red; source restored and SHA-verified after each. Full suite 1919 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed (of the +18 over the v2.2.1 baseline of 1901, 8 are these and 10 belong to a parallel session). flake8 clean, pinned black 23.7.0 and isort clean. Manual validation authored at test/bug-validation/PD-BUG-158_installer_venv_release_wait_validation.py; both its phases were exercised by hand rather than running the script end to end, because it stops the running daemons four more times.
- **Verification**: Code Review (PF-TSK-005) 2026-08-31: REJECTED. 0 Critical, 1 Major, 1 Minor, 3 Suggestions. MAJOR - the OB half of the fix is not wired into production. venv_lock_holder_report (install_global.py line 202) is defined and unit-tested but never called, and the abort message in main() at line 787 is byte-identical to HEAD, so the installer still prints the exact message this report named as the OB defect: still locked by a running process. Verified three ways rather than assumed: a repo-wide grep finds the function only in its own def and in the test file, git show HEAD confirms the message is unchanged, and no test under test/ asserts on that message text. The worst case is the very scenario in this report - when find_venv_daemon_processes returns nothing, stopped is empty, so there is no WARNING either and the misleading ERROR is the only output. All three verification layers were blind at the same seam: the 8 unit tests call the helper directly and none exercises main(), so the recorded mutation result of exactly 1 red pins a function that production never invokes; and the manual validation Phase B checks only the gate verdict, its failure branch telling the operator to read the message the installer now prints, which was never changed. Exposure measured: 1 abort path reachable on every install run; 0 files at risk and 0 data corruptible, because the gate fires before any copy, so this is diagnostics only; measured coverage of the abort block is not zero but is vacuous - 1 test executes it (test_main_aborts_before_copy_when_venv_still_locked in test_pd_bug_106_installer_venv_daemon_stop.py, which forces the gate True and asserts only that no copy and no venv rebuild followed), and 0 tests assert anything about the message text, so the message could say anything and stay green; that same test is a ready home for the rework, since the seam is already exercised; 3 documents already assert delivery (this Fix cell, doc/ci-cd/release-process.md with the phrase naming what actually holds it, and the TE-TST-166 Notes cell in test-tracking.md); 1 dead function and 1 dead return value, since the stopped PID list is discarded at both call sites (lines 465 and 783), which is evidence the wiring was designed and then dropped. MINOR - the wait is conditional on the installer having done the killing. The if stopped guard at line 149 leaves the same defect class uncovered when a lock is still dissolving from a process killed externally moments earlier, for example a Control Panel closed by hand before an install, which step 3 of release-process.md invites. The stated rationale, that a clean install must not pay for the wait, does not hold: with nothing locked the unguarded call costs one open in append mode, not 15 s. The real cost appears only in the genuine-external-holder case. Triage directed a bounded re-check in venv_python_locked kept only as a backstop; it was not implemented, and it is the gap that would have closed this. SUGGESTIONS - the WARNING at line 151 interpolates the module constant VENV_RELEASE_TIMEOUT rather than the effective timeout the wait actually used, visible in the timeout test which prints 0s for 0.05; a stray f prefix sits on the placeholder-free first segment at line 151; and the documented return value of stop_daemons_using_venv has no consumer, so either wire it into the report or drop the claim. POSITIVE, and not in question - the ID half is sound: polling the actual condition rather than a proxy such as process disappearance is the right design and is reasoned honestly in the docstring, the loop is correctly bounded (always checks once, returns on the first clear reading, honours the deadline), create_linkwatcher_venv inherits the wait and genuinely strengthens the PD-BUG-110 defence, and the comments record measured evidence including the probes that did NOT reproduce rather than asserting a mechanism. The review re-measured the record rather than accepting it: the full suite reproduced at 1919 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed; flake8 is clean under the project args (max-line-length 120, extend-ignore E203); pinned black 23.7.0 from the pre-commit cache and isort are clean; and the Step 20 sweep claim holds, with one kill site in install_global.py and a settle loop present in stop_linkwatcher.ps1. REWORK NEEDED: call venv_lock_holder_report from the main() abort path and cover that path with a test, so the OB half is pinned where it is reached rather than in isolation; then reconsider the if stopped guard and the triage-directed backstop in the gate. Closing on the ID half alone was presented and not taken, because OB sits inside the Dims declared on this bug and three documents already claim it was delivered, so approval would require correcting all three anyway while the remedy is about three lines.
- **Fix**: Rework after Code Review rejection 2026-08-31; all five findings closed. MAJOR (the OB half was never wired): venv_lock_holder_report is now called from the main() abort path, and that message no longer asserts a running process - it names the surviving venv PIDs, or states plainly that the holder sits outside the venv. MINOR (the wait was conditional): the if-stopped guard is removed, so the wait runs whether or not this pass did the killing, covering a lock left dissolving by a Control Panel closed by hand before an install; a clean install still pays nothing because the missing-venv early return precedes the wait. Suggestion 1: the warning reports the effective timeout from a local rather than the module constant and renders it with :g, so a 0.05s wait no longer prints 0s. Suggestion 2: the stray f prefix is gone, flake8 F541 clean. Suggestion 3: the documented return value now has a consumer - main() prints the stopped count, which separates a teardown that outran the wait from a holder the installer never stopped; the create_linkwatcher_venv call site still discards it deliberately, being a retry loop rather than a diagnostic path. doc/ci-cd/release-process.md rewritten to describe the unconditional wait and the new message. DEVIATION from the triage direction, presented and approved at the Step 23 checkpoint: triage directed a bounded re-check inside venv_python_locked, but that function is the pure predicate the wait loop itself polls, so nesting a wait there compounds two timers and charges every caller; the same condition is covered by making the upstream wait unconditional - one bound in one place - and the main() gate now always runs after a completed wait. Accepted cost, also approved: a genuine external holder now waits the full 15s before the clean abort.
- **Root Cause**: Rework root cause: the first fix built the OB half as a standalone helper and never called it, and all three verification layers were blind at the same seam - the 8 unit tests called the helper directly, none exercised main(), and the manual validation checked only the gate verdict while its failure branch pointed the operator at a message that had never changed. The rework pins the message where production reaches it.
- **Tests Added**: Yes - TE-TST-166 now 12 cases, was 8. Three main()-level tests drive the abort path: a negative assertion that the old string is absent, surviving-PID naming, and stopped-count reporting. Two stop-wait tests cover the unconditional wait - it runs when the installer killed nothing, and costs exactly one lock check when the interpreter is free; the test that pinned the removed if-stopped guard was replaced. Fail-first verified: 6 red before the fix, each for its named reason, with production printing the literal filed defect while zero venv processes were alive. Mutation-verified 1:1 across all 8 suites importing install_global, the rejected round having scoped too narrowly: M1 remove the wait = 4 red; M2 restore the guard = 2 red; M3 unwire the holder report from main() = 3 red, where the rejected round turned only 1 and on a function production never called; M4 drop the stopped-count line = 1 red; M5 old .0f format = 1 red. Source restored byte-identical after every mutation, SHA-verified. Full suite 1923 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed. Pinned black 23.7.0, flake8 and isort clean. Test count synced with Validate-TestTracking.ps1 -Fix -Path. Manual validation gained a Phase C printing the old and new abort messages side by side against the live machine, closing the finding that Phase B pointed at an unchanged message; rendered live and correctly named the two running venv PIDs.
- **Verification**: Rework re-presented at the Step 23 checkpoint 2026-08-31 and approved by the human partner, including the deviation from the triage-directed backstop and the accepted 15s wait for a genuine external holder. Live re-verification against real daemons was not run because it stops the running daemons; the manual validation script covers it on demand. Returns to Code Review for re-review.
- **Verification**: Code Review (PF-TSK-005) re-review 2026-09-01: APPROVED. 0 Critical, 0 Major, 2 Minor, 4 Suggestions. All five findings from the rejected round were verified closed independently rather than accepted from the record: the holder report is called from the main abort path and the old sentence survives only in this bug row and in the deliberate old-versus-new block of the manual validation script; the if-stopped guard is gone; the warning renders 0.05s via :g; the stopped count reaches the operator. Mutation re-run by this review using runtime patches with no source edit, across all 7 suites importing install_global: removing the wait turns 4 red, restoring the guard turns 2 red, reverting the holder report turns 4 red of which 2 are main-level - the seam that was blind last round is now pinned where production reaches it. Full suite 1932 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed (the recorded 1923 no longer reproduces because a parallel Control Panel session added tests, not because of this change); TE-TST-166 12 of 12; flake8 under the project args, pinned black 23.7.0 and isort all clean; coverage of the fix delta is complete except line 205. NEW MINOR FINDINGS, both filed as technical debt rather than blocking closure - TD313 and TD314: (1) the returned PID list is unpinned end to end - removing only the stopped.append call leaves all 62 installer-suite tests green, so the reported count could silently read 0 forever; this does not contradict the recorded M4, which mutated the print statement in main and is genuinely pinned, the unpinned part is the seam between recording and printing; (2) venv_lock_holder_report treats a failed process query as no process found, because find_venv_daemon_processes returns empty on any query failure, so the abort can assert that nothing runs from the venv without evidence - the same class as the filed OB defect, inverted, partly mitigated by the trailing clause about an unreadable executable path. SUGGESTIONS recorded, not filed: the timeout local in stop_daemons_using_venv can never differ from the module constant, and passing it explicitly is exactly what leaves line 205 uncovered; the warning reads awkwardly in the no-kills branch; Phase C of the validation script hardcodes the surrounding layout of the main abort message and can drift from it; and the release-process freshness stamp now reads verified against 2.2.1 for a wait that has never run in a release. POSITIVE: the ID half re-inspected and sound, the unconditional wait genuinely covers an externally killed Control Panel and strengthens the PD-BUG-110 retry defence, the negative assertion on the removed string is the right way to pin a message removal, and the two PD-BUG-106 suite edits correctly keep that suite hermetic against the new contract.
- **Updated**: 2026-09-01

### PD-BUG-157

- **Source**: E2ETesting
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Control Panel single-instance guard
- **Repro**: 1. Launch the Control Panel and confirm the window opens. 2. Kill the panel process instead of closing the window (any hard kill), leaving <install>/panel.port on disk. 3. Launch the panel again by any route: python control_panel.py, the launcher, or the SessionStart hook. 4. Watch the terminal and the desktop.
- **Expected**: Either a panel window opens, or the panel states why it declined to start. The guard should verify that a process is actually listening on the port recorded in panel.port before treating it as a live instance, and should recover on its own from a stale file.
- **Actual**: No window opens, nothing is printed to the terminal, no error is raised, and the process exits. The only trace is <install>/logs/panel.log recording single_instance surfaced_existing port=NNNNN with no matching panel_started. The panel stays unusable until panel.port is deleted by hand.
- **Evidence**: Observed 2026-08-31 during E2E execution of TE-E2E-034 and TE-E2E-035 under PF-TSK-070. C:\Users\ronny\bin\logs\panel.log shows surfaced_existing port=55310 at 08:48, 09:12, 09:17, 09:19, 14:20, 15:01, 15:25 and 16:32 with no panel_started entry all day. C:\Users\ronny\bin\panel.port held 55310 with no listening process. After deleting panel.port the next launch logged single_instance acquired port=61255 then panel_started, and the window opened. A normal close does remove panel.port (panel_exit shutdown_complete at 17:09:51 and 17:23:31, file gone both times), so a stale file originates from a killed panel. The TE-E2E-034 preconditions already tell the operator to delete a stale panel.port by hand, so the behavior was known but never filed.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-08-31 (PF-TSK-041). Filed root cause DISPROVEN: the guard does verify liveness. surface_existing_instance requires the LINKWATCHER-PANEL OK banner, so a dead or recycled port yields stale_port_file (taking over) and a fresh bind; the deployed copy is byte-identical to repo source, and stale_port_file appears 0 times in the entire panel.log. Evidence shows a LIVE incumbent instead: acquired port=55310 at 08:48:00,032 then panel_started at 08:48:00,632, and every surfaced_existing line is followed by surface_requested, which only the incumbent listener thread emits. That panel had registry unresolved, hence no poller, which is why it logged no poll_cycle_slow all day unlike the 17:41 and 19:18 panels. The no-terminal-output claim is also false: running the deployed control_panel.py against the live incumbent prints the already-running message and exits 0. REAL DEFECT (rescoped): a running-but-unreachable panel window has no recovery path and no honest feedback. The handshake proves the listener thread is alive, never that the UI is, and surfacing is best-effort because the Windows foreground lock can reduce it to a taskbar flash (TDD 4.5). Priority lowered High to Medium: the panel is a supervisor UI and daemons keep maintaining links without it, plus a workaround exists (delete panel.port); the High reading assumed any hard-killed panel permanently bricks the panel, which is false. Scope S: confined to single_instance.py and control_panel.py plus unit tests. Dims OB UX retained: the log asserted surfaced_existing while nothing surfaced (OB), and the operator had no window and no recovery (UX). Estimated effort 2 to 4 hours. Approved fix direction: the incumbent acks only after surface has actually run on the UI thread, so a dead or blocked mainloop falls through to opening a real window, rather than adding a force flag that leaves the bad default in place.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-31
- **Fix**: Order inverted: _handle now surfaces first and acks only on confirmation. New _surface_and_confirm runs the hook and returns its result within SURFACE_CONFIRM_TIMEOUT_SECONDS (1.5 s, deliberately under the client 2 s handshake timeout so a declining incumbent is still heard); no confirmation means silence, which the client reads as no-live-panel and opens a real window. The same budget WAITS for a not-yet-wired hook rather than dropping the request, because a second launch can arrive while the panel is still building its window (observed 337 ms after acquire, window ready at 632 ms) and refusing it would open a duplicate panel. control_panel.py now wires on_surface to a closure that posts the raise and waits for the UI thread to report back. Contract change: on_surface returns True only once the window has actually been raised, and anything falsy means not surfaced. Updated alongside the code: TDD section 4.5, test spec row UNIT-I5, control-panel-operations.md and control-panel-reference.md.
- **Root Cause**: The ack preceded the action it claimed to have performed. _handle sent PANEL_BANNER before invoking the surface hook and independently of it, so the banner proved only that a listener THREAD was alive, never that a Tk mainloop was running, that the dispatcher queue was ever drained, or that a window existed at all. callback() is app.post(app.surface), which merely enqueues onto the queue the mainloop drains. An incumbent whose UI was dead, wedged or not yet wired therefore absorbed every relaunch: the launcher was told already-running, exited 0, and no window appeared, leaving hand-deletion of panel.port as the only recovery. That recovery is what made the defect look like a stale-port-file bug when it was filed.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Full suite 1911 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failed. Panel single-instance suite 33 passed. Test delta verified as exactly plus 4 by collecting the HEAD copy of the changed file (29 tests) against the current one (33) rather than inferring it; the 1901 baseline quoted in the v2.2.1 release note is stale by 6 against HEAD, a pre-existing record discrepancy not caused by this fix and not swept into it. 6 tests added, 2 superseded tests deleted because they pinned the unconditional ack, and 5 further tests updated because their callbacks did not satisfy the new contract (I had estimated 2 at the checkpoint and reported the correction). Manual validation PD-BUG-157_surface_confirmation_validation.py drives real loopback listeners against both the old and the shipped _handle and prints the columns side by side: checks 1 to 3 flip FAIL to PASS, and guards 4 to 6 pass in BOTH columns, confirming the fix does not over-reach on a healthy panel, on the startup race, or on a genuinely stale port. Step 20 similar-issue sweep found no sibling: sendall occurs only in this module, and the other cross-process claim path (lifecycle.py daemon start/stop) is observational by design and already logs daemon_exit_unconfirmed. Residual limitation recorded honestly: confirmation proves the UI thread EXECUTED the raise, not that Windows honoured the activation, so a pure foreground-lock case would still present as a taskbar flash rather than a raise.
- **Verification**: Code Review 2026-08-31 (PF-TSK-005): REJECTED. 0 Critical, 2 Major, 3 Minor, 3 Suggestions. Full suite 1919 passed 0 failed; the record said 1911 and the delta is exactly plus 8, all from the out-of-scope PD-BUG-158 test file, so nothing is unexplained. Lint clean under the pinned pre-commit binaries (black 23.7.0, isort 5.12.0, flake8 6.0.0). The validation script reproduced independently: checks 1-3 flip FAIL to PASS, 4-6 pass in both columns. The Step 20 sweep claim was re-verified: sendall occurs only in this module. MAJOR 1: the confirm budget is NOT bounded by the client handshake timeout, contrary to what single_instance.py lines 85-87 and TDD 4.5 both assert as a designed property. _surface_and_confirm waits up to SURFACE_CONFIRM_TIMEOUT_SECONDS (1.5 s) for on_surface to be wired and THEN calls the hook, which waits its own full 1.5 s in control_panel.py, so the two waits are sequential and total up to 3.0 s against the client 2.0 s HANDSHAKE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS. Measured: a hook wired at 1.2 s plus a raise taking 0.9 s, each inside its own budget, makes the client time out at exactly 2.00 s and open a second window while the incumbent DOES surface. The serialized accept loop amplifies this onto the healthy path with no wedged UI needed: a healthy incumbent with a 0.8 s raise and 3 simultaneous launches leaves 1 of 3 declined and opening a duplicate. Second-order consequence, also measured: the declined launch rewrites panel.port and logs stale_port_file taking over, so the LIVE incumbent becomes unreachable to every future launch, which is the same running-but-unreachable class this bug was filed about, now reachable from the decline path this fix introduces. The release() self-owned-port check was verified intact, so the port file itself is never corrupted. Remedy direction: compute one shared deadline in _surface_and_confirm and pass only the remaining budget to the hook, so wire-wait plus confirm stays under SURFACE_CONFIRM_TIMEOUT_SECONDS and therefore under the client timeout; consider also moving the confirm off the accept loop. Add a test pinning total server response below HANDSHAKE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS, because nothing pins that bound today. MAJOR 2: the module still documents the pre-fix contract in two places. Line 180 Typical use still shows on_surface wired as a lambda returning app.post of app.surface; post returns None, so that example run verbatim is DECLINED (measured) and every relaunch would open a duplicate. Line 197 still annotates on_surface as Callable of no args returning None while the class docstring 8 lines above says it returns True. Zero runtime impact today; rated Major because it is the site a maintainer reads and it teaches the defect just removed. MINOR 1 (OB): a live incumbent that declines is logged client-side as stale_port_file taking over, which is false on both counts and is the same class of untrue log claim this bug names; nothing in either log records that two panels now exist. MINOR 2: test-tracking row TE-TST-151 records 27 tests but the file collects 33 (verified); the plus 4 delta was applied to a stale 23 baseline rather than the measured 33, and Last Run still reads Run 2026-08-10 29 passed dated 2026-08-14. Correct it during rework rather than now, since the file changes again. MINOR 3: the contract is untested at the site that must satisfy it. surface_and_confirm in control_panel.py has no test and the unit suite uses a hand-built analogue, so regressing that closure to return None leaves all 33 tests green. The structural half is routed to technical debt (extract a testable factory); add closure coverage in rework if that extraction lands. SUGGESTIONS: control-panel-reference.md still opens with the claim that only one panel window exists at a time and then describes the second one, unlike the how-to which qualifies it; the queued raise_window is not cancelled on timeout, so a slow incumbent raises after the launcher already gave up; TE-TST-151 sits at Audit Approved over a file that changed by plus 6 minus 2, which Test Audit owns rather than this task. POSITIVE: root-cause fix rather than a force flag, release() ownership verified intact under the newly reachable duplicate path, the two tests that pinned the unconditional ack were deleted rather than left to pass vacuously, and the docs record the foreground-lock residual honestly.
- **Fix**: REWORK after Code Review rejection 2026-08-31. R1: _surface_and_confirm computes ONE deadline and hands the hook only the budget that remains, so the wire-wait and the confirm can no longer be additive. R2: the listener ALSO bounds its own wait (_run_within), because a property the class advertises must not depend on a caller-supplied closure honouring it - the same shape of mistake this bug is about; an overrunning hook is abandoned and suppresses its own late raise. R3: each request is served on its own short-lived thread, capped at the listen backlog, so simultaneous launches are answered in parallel instead of queueing behind the raise ahead of them. R4: surface_existing_instance returns a three-way SurfaceOutcome (SURFACED, DECLINED, ABSENT) keyed on whether the TCP connect COMPLETED rather than on exception type, because Windows reports a closed loopback port as TimeoutError, not ConnectionRefusedError - classifying by type read "nothing there" as "a live peer declined". acquire now logs surface_declined for a port a live process still holds, naming that a second window exists and the incumbent has lost its route back, instead of the false stale_port_file (MINOR 1). R5 (MAJOR 2): module docs corrected - the Typical use example taught the pre-fix lambda that measurably declines, and the on_surface annotation still read Callable of no args returning None. R6 (MINOR 3): the production hook moved out of control_panel.main into make_surface_hook(app) in app.py, closing TD312, and it now suppresses a raise whose budget has already expired (Suggestion 2). Docs updated: TDD 4.5, test spec UNIT-I5, control-panel-reference.md (Suggestion 1 - it opened by claiming one window at a time then described the second), control-panel-operations.md, feature state file section 10.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: Rework verified 2026-08-31. Full suite 1932 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failed (run twice); panel single-instance suite 42 of 42, stable across 8 consecutive randomized runs. Lint clean under the pinned pre-commit binaries with the project flake8 args (black 23.7.0, isort 5.12.0, flake8 6.0.0, max-line-length 120). Delta from the review record of 1919 is plus 13: plus 9 mine, plus 4 from a PARALLEL SESSION that modified test_pd_bug_158_installer_venv_release_wait.py at 22:59 while this session ran - nothing unexplained. Seven regression tests written BEFORE the fix, each confirmed red for its own reason (server answering at 2.02s against a 2.0s client timeout; hook never handed a budget; healthy panel declining 1 of 3 simultaneous launches; a live decline indistinguishable from a dead port; stale_port_file logged for a live panel; factory absent). Every mechanism was then mutation-verified separately and each mutant turned red exactly the test that names it, including the TD312 regression (shipped hook returning None turns all three factory tests red). That pass caught two defects I would otherwise have shipped: a mutant collapsing DECLINED into ABSENT turned NOTHING red, exposing an uncovered classification path - a peer that accepts and never answers - which now has its own test; and the late-raise test was flaky by construction on a sub-millisecond margin, surfaced by a post-restore run rather than by the eight green runs before it. Manual validation script rewritten into two sections: checks 1-3 flip FAIL to PASS against the original ack-first code, 4-6 pass in BOTH columns, and new checks 7-9 flip FAIL to PASS against the REJECTED implementation (2.03s to 1.51s answer, 2 of 3 to 3 of 3 absorbed, stale_port_file to surface_declined). Step 20 sweep found no sibling: no other module classifies socket errors by exception type, there is one accept loop, sendall occurs only here. CORRECTION to review MINOR 2: it measured tracker drift against pytest COLLECTED count, but the column counts test FUNCTIONS (36) while pytest collects 42 because one test is parametrized seven ways; the column now reads 36 via Validate-TestTracking.ps1 and Last Run reads 42 passed. Beyond the review remedy: passing the remaining budget alone is NOT sufficient, because a hook that ignores it still overruns the client - hence R2. FDD checked and needs no change (AC-11 and EC-9 already carry the confirmation contract and the bounded wait). No ADR - per-connection dispatch and the tri-state are local refinements inside an existing component, covered by TDD 4.5. Residual unchanged and documented: confirmation proves the UI thread EXECUTED the raise, not that Windows honoured the activation, so a pure foreground-lock case still degrades to a taskbar flash.
- **Updated**: 2026-09-01
- **Verification**: Code Review 2026-09-01 (PF-TSK-005): APPROVED. 1 Major, 3 Minor, 1 Suggestion - all routed, none blocking. Every number re-measured independently rather than read off the record. Full suite 1932 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failed, matching the rework record exactly. Panel single-instance suite 42 passed across 6 randomized runs, no flakes. Lint clean under the pinned pre-commit binaries (black, isort 5.12.0, flake8 6.0.0, max-line-length 120, extend-ignore E203). Coverage: single_instance.py 88 percent, make_surface_hook fully covered. BOTH PRIOR MAJORS MEASURED CLOSED: worst-case server response is 1.52 s against the 2.0 s client timeout, including against a hook that ignores its budget entirely (10 s sleep), which is exactly what R2 exists to survive; three simultaneous launches on a healthy panel all acked at 0.80 s. Manual validation script reproduced independently: checks 1-3 and 7-9 flip FAIL to PASS, guards 4-6 pass in both columns. MUTATION MATRIX re-run independently via a pytest plugin (runtime patch, no source edits) - six mechanisms, each turning red exactly the tests that name it, baseline 42 green: R1 separate budgets 2 red (both TestResponseBudget), R2 trust-the-hook 1 red, R3 inline accept loop 5 red (incl. the healthy-concurrency test), R4 classify-by-exception 4 red (all TestTakeoverIsReportedHonestly), R6/TD312 hook-returns-None 3 red (all TestSurfaceHookFactory), core ack-before-surface 6 red. The tests are load-bearing, not vacuous. CORRECTION to the 2026-08-31 review: its MINOR 2 was WRONG. Verified independently - the file defines 36 test functions and pytest collects 42 because one test is parametrized seven ways; the tracker column counts functions, so 36 is correct and there was never any drift. The rework correction stands. MAJOR (routed as PD-BUG-159, Low): make_surface_hook sets the surfaced event unconditionally after calling app.surface(), which swallows every exception, so a raise that throws on the UI thread still returns True and the banner is sent while the same panel logs surface_failed. Measured using the real ControlPanelApp.surface bound onto a destroyed root. Rated Major on the calibration the previous review used for its own MAJOR 2 - an untrue claim at the site a maintainer reads - but explicitly NOT blocking: exposure is the millisecond race between root.destroy() and guard.release(), and it is not a regression, since before this fix every such case acked unconditionally. MINOR 1 (routed as PD-BUG-160, Low): guard.release() runs only after app.run() returns, so throughout the drain-then-terminate shutdown (grace_period_seconds, default 20 s) the listener still answers and surface() still works; a launch in that window is told already running and the panel then exits, leaving no window. Self-healing on one retry and pre-existing, but it is the last path where a truthful ack yields no usable window. MINOR 2 (routed as TD315): the R3 saturation branch is uncovered and its comment understates the cost - measured with 12 simultaneous launches, the 4 over the cap get a connection reset rather than silence and the takeover rewrites panel.port over a healthy incumbent, the same class as the previous review MINOR 1. MINOR 3 (routed as TD316): the budget-exhausted branch of the shared deadline is uncovered. SUGGESTION routed to Test Audit (PF-TSK-030): TE-TST-151 still reads Audit Approved dated 2026-08-14 over a file that has gone 23 to 36 test functions across two cycles, while sibling rows in the same tracker were flipped to Needs Audit on material change; audit status is owned by PF-TSK-030 and was deliberately not touched here. POSITIVE: R2 is the load-bearing choice - bounding the wait inside the listener rather than trusting the hook to honour the budget it is handed is the only reason the budget-ignoring probe stays inside the client timeout; the tri-state is keyed on connect completion rather than exception type, which is correct for Windows reporting a closed loopback port as TimeoutError; and the docs record the foreground-lock residual honestly instead of overclaiming. Feature Tracking not touched: 7.1.1 is past implementation and held at Needs User Docs pending PF-IMP-2032, so the bug-fix review rule applies and only Bug Tracking is updated. TE-E2E-041 remains flagged for re-execution.

### PD-BUG-159

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Control Panel single-instance guard
- **Repro**: 1. Bind ControlPanelApp.surface onto a root whose deiconify raises (the state of a root after root.destroy()). 2. Build the hook with make_surface_hook(app). 3. Call the hook and drain the dispatcher queue so the raise runs on the UI thread. 4. Read the return value.
- **Expected**: The hook reports False when the raise did not succeed, so the listener declines the ack and the launching process opens a real window. That is what the on_surface contract in single_instance.py states: True only once the window has actually been raised.
- **Actual**: The hook returns True and the banner is sent. ControlPanelApp.surface catches the exception, logs surface_failed and returns None, so raise_window cannot tell success from failure and sets the surfaced event either way. The panel records the failure in its own log while telling the launcher the opposite.
- **Evidence**: Measured 2026-09-01 during Code Review (PF-TSK-005) of the PD-BUG-157 rework, using the real ControlPanelApp.surface bound onto a destroyed root: hook returned True. Sites: app.py make_surface_hook raise_window (surfaced.set after app.surface()), app.py ControlPanelApp.surface (bare except that swallows), single_instance.py class docstring stating the contract. Exposure is narrow because the raise only throws when Tk itself fails, chiefly the millisecond race between root.destroy() and guard.release(); it is not a regression, since before PD-BUG-157 every such case acked unconditionally. Remedy: have surface() report success and gate surfaced.set() on it, plus a test with a raising root.
- **Triage**: Triaged 2026-09-01 (PF-TSK-041). Validity confirmed against source, not the report alone: raise_window in app.py calls app.surface() and then sets the surfaced event regardless of outcome, while ControlPanelApp.surface wraps the raise in a bare except, logs surface_failed and returns None - so a failed raise still returns True and the single_instance.py contract docstring (banner sent only after the window has actually surfaced) is violated. No existing test covers it: the FakeApp stand-in in test_panel_single_instance.py never raises. Priority raised from the filed Low to Medium via the matrix (Medium impact x Low frequency). Two failure profiles: the dominant post-destroy race is benign because the panel is exiting and a relaunch succeeds, but a live panel whose Tk is wedged absorbs every relaunch with no window and no error, reproducing the PD-BUG-157 dead-end this rework was meant to remove. Scope S: have surface() report success, gate the surfaced event on it, add a test with a raising root, and sync the two docstrings that state the contract. Dims widened from OB to CQ OB UX - CQ is the root cause (a bare except swallowing an outcome the caller needs, against the feature Dimension Profile rule that no external surface fails silently), OB for the contradicting log-versus-wire signals, UX for the operator-visible no-window outcome, consistent with PD-BUG-157 and PD-BUG-160. Not a duplicate: the PD-BUG-160 ack is truthful during the shutdown grace period, this one is false; the two are complementary windows.
- **Updated**: 2026-09-01
- **Fix**: Two hunks in app.py. (1) surface() now returns bool: a raised flag is set after deiconify/lift/focus_force succeed and returned at the end; the except still logs surface_failed and now yields False. The flag is set BEFORE _request_foreground deliberately - a refused foreground grab is the documented floor (TDD 4.5), the window is up and only the activation was denied, so it must not read as failure; the call stays inside the try so the never-kill-the-panel guarantee does not depend on _request_foreground swallowing internally forever. (2) raise_window now sets the surfaced event only when app.surface() returns True. Docstrings on make_surface_hook and surface updated to state what confirmation does and does not prove. Alternatives rejected: letting surface() propagate and catching in raise_window (moves the never-kill guarantee onto every future caller), and re-checking window state via winfo_viewable (heavier, needs live Tk in tests, and leaves the swallow still lying to any other caller). Test-side correction: FakeApp.surface in TE-TST-151 returned None and so read as failure under the new gate - it now returns True. That stand-in reporting nothing is why the bug was invisible, since through it a hook gating on the outcome and one ignoring it look identical.
- **Root Cause**: The outcome of the raise was discarded at the boundary, not swallowed by accident. ControlPanelApp.surface returned None on both paths - its bare except is required, since surfacing must never kill the panel - and raise_window in make_surface_hook called it and then set the surfaced event regardless. The failure information existed (logged as surface_failed one line earlier) and was thrown away one frame up, so the hook returned True, the listener sent the LINKWATCHER-PANEL OK banner, and the launching process exited with no window. Everything downstream was already correct and was verified rather than assumed: _handle already declines the ack and logs surface_unconfirmed on a False, and _run_within already bounds its own wait. One link in an otherwise sound chain, which is why scope S held.
- **Tests Added**: Yes - 5 regression tests added to TE-TST-151 (test_panel_single_instance.py) in a new TestSurfaceReportsItsOutcome class, written before the fix. They carry the REAL ControlPanelApp.surface over a scripted root rather than the FakeApp stand-in that cannot fail. Coverage: surface reports success on a healthy raise (the discriminating control, without which always-False would also pass), surface reports failure and still logs surface_failed on a throw, surface still succeeds when the foreground grab is refused (pins the documented floor), the hook declines when the raise throws, and the hook confirms when it does not. The declining tests assert root.attempts == 1 so a pass cannot come from the budget path instead of the failing-raise path.
- **Verification**: Pre-fix: 4 failed 1 passed, each for the right reason - assert None is True on the three surface cases, assert True is False on the hook case with surface_failed in the captured log. Post-fix: 47 passed in the single-instance file; full suite 1937 passed, 3 skipped, 3 xfailed, 0 failed, which is exactly the recorded 1932 baseline plus the 5 new tests with no unexplained drift. Lint clean under the pinned pre-commit binaries (black, isort, flake8, pytest-quick all Passed). Mutation-verified 1:1 by runtime patch with a no-op control that turned nothing red: reverting surface to report nothing turns exactly the 4 return-value tests red, and restoring the unconditional surfaced.set() turns exactly the 1 hook-declines test red - disjoint sets covering all 5 new tests. Reproduction was empirical with a control before any fix: healthy root and destroyed root both returned True pre-fix, so the hook answer carried no information. Step 20 sweep read all 8 other swallow sites in the panel package rather than grepping for a shape: none share the defect - each either has no outcome-consuming caller or already propagates the outcome, and single_instance._run_within already implements this exact pattern. Step 21 manual validation skipped: the trigger needs Tk to fail a raise while the process lives (a millisecond race between root.destroy() and guard.release()), which is not stageable through UI or filesystem actions, and a script fabricating a raising root would be programmatic and duplicate the new tests. Human-approved at both checkpoints.
- **Fix**: Annotation only - no code change since the entry above. Update-BugStatus has no amend-only mode, so recording a Step 25 finding requires re-passing a mandatory FixDetails.
- **Verification**: Step 25 (E2E re-execution flag): deliberately NOT run, measured rather than assumed. The -WhatIf preview showed the flag would touch 9 entries across 5 features (7.1.1, 2.2.1, 0.1.2, 6.1.1, 0.1.3) because the WF-010 cases are cross-cutting - exactly the cascade the task warns about. Three reasons not to: TE-E2E-041, the only case covering the changed component, is ALREADY at Needs Re-execution from the PD-BUG-157 fix, so the flag would add nothing; the fix is behavior-preserving on every path an E2E case exercises, since a healthy raise returns True and sets the event exactly as before; and no E2E case stages a failed Tk raise, which is the only path whose behavior changes. Flagging would have dragged four features unrelated to this fix into re-testing.
- **Verification**: Code review (PF-TSK-005) 2026-09-02: APPROVED. Scoped to the app.py surface-outcome delta and its 5 tests; the single_instance.py rework (PD-BUG-157) sharing this working tree was seam-checked, not re-reviewed. Verified rather than accepted: the two scoped test files 58 passed, full suite 1948 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed / 0 failed matching the recorded baseline, pre-commit clean under the pinned binaries at exit 0, no dependency changes. The gating chain was re-traced end to end - surface() reports the outcome, raise_window gates the event on it, _handle declines the ack, _run_within bounds the wait. Three findings, none blocking. (1) MINOR, unpinned ordering claim: raised = True sits before _request_foreground and the fix record documents that placement as deliberate, but _request_foreground swallows every exception after its platform guard - measured on win32 by invoking it against a root whose winfo_id raises, it returned normally. Moving the flag after the call would therefore behave identically, and test_surface_still_succeeds_when_the_foreground_grab_is_refused passes with either ordering: it pins the internal swallow, not the ordering its docstring credits, and the recorded 1:1 mutation matrix contains no ordering mutation. The ordering stays correct as defence in depth against a future change to _request_foreground; only the test's stated pin overstates what it discriminates. (2) MINOR, stale tracker row: the TE-TST-151 row carried count 41 with result "Run 2026-08-31: 42 passed" and last run 2026-08-31, while the file now collects 47 cases from 41 functions and the feature state file already records 47 - the count column was advanced for the +5 tests and the result and date columns were not. Corrected by this review. (3) MINOR, stale audit status: TE-TST-151 remains Audit Approved while carrying 5 tests added after that audit. Not flipped here, since the audit verdict is owned by Test Audit (PF-TSK-030), and routed there instead.
- **Updated**: 2026-09-02

### PD-BUG-160

- **Source**: CodeReview
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Control Panel single-instance guard
- **Repro**: 1. Open the Control Panel with at least one managed daemon running, so closing it has something to drain. 2. Close the panel window; the drain-then-terminate progress dialog appears. 3. While that dialog is up, launch the panel again (linkwatcher-panel.bat or python control_panel.py). 4. Watch the terminal and the desktop.
- **Expected**: A panel that is committed to exiting should stop claiming the single-instance slot, so the launch opens a usable window rather than being sent away by a panel that is about to vanish.
- **Actual**: The second launch prints the already-running message and exits 0. The incumbent raises its closing window, finishes the drain and exits, so no panel remains. Relaunching once more works, because release() has removed panel.port by then, so the condition is self-healing rather than a lockout.
- **Evidence**: Found 2026-09-01 during Code Review (PF-TSK-005) of the PD-BUG-157 rework, by reading the ordering in control_panel.main: the guard is released in the finally after app.run(), and _on_close starts ShutdownOrchestrator while the mainloop and the dispatcher pump keep running. Window is bounded by grace_period_seconds (default 20.0 in settings.py). Pre-existing rather than introduced by PD-BUG-157: before that fix the listener acked unconditionally in this window too. It is the one remaining path where a truthful ack still yields no usable window, so it belongs to the same class the PD-BUG-157 contract was written to close. Likely remedy: clear on_surface or set the stopping event when _on_close begins.
- **Triage**: Validity CONFIRMED by driving the real ControlPanelApp (real Tk mainloop, real dispatcher pump, real ShutdownOrchestrator) against the real SingleInstanceGuard rather than accepting the filed repro: a second launch arriving 1.44 s into the drain was told already running and acked, and 0.6 s later no panel existed; the probe after exit opened a real window, so the self-healing claim is confirmed. The mechanism is structural rather than timing-dependent - control_panel.py is the ONLY site where the guard and the app meet and the reference is one-directional, so no shutdown path can reach the guard at all. Pre-existing rather than introduced by PD-BUG-157 was verified against git HEAD, whose pre-fix _handle acked before surfacing and independently of it, so the shutdown window acked there too. The record was corrected on three measured points. First, the window is NOT bounded by grace_period_seconds: a run whose drain never resolved measured exit at 35.1 s after close, the AC-9 watchdog at grace plus WATCHDOG_MARGIN_SECONDS 15, so worst-case exposure at defaults is about 35 s rather than 20 s, with a typical drain of a few seconds. Second, Dims corrected UX OB to UX ID - nothing the panel LOGS is untrue here, since surfaced_existing and surface_requested are both accurate, which is exactly what separates this from PD-BUG-159 where the panel logs surface_failed while telling the launcher the opposite; the real second dimension is the interface-contract gap between two components that hold no reference to each other. Third, what the incumbent raises is the modal shutdown progress dialog, so a watching operator does see the state and the misleading half is the launcher terminal message - a mitigation that disappears entirely if hook auto-open lands. Priority Low CONFIRMED on the matrix, impact Low and frequency Low: the panel is a supervisor UI whose absence does not stop link maintenance, and recovery is a single relaunch. The case for accepting it as a documented residual instead of fixing was presented and not taken; the fix is small, reuses machinery that already exists, and the exposure turns fully silent if PF-IMP-2032 hook auto-open lands, which is the very gate feature 7.1.1 is currently held on. Scope S: signal the guard when close begins, noting that release is already idempotent and self-owned-port-safe. Fix-site constraint recorded at triage: the natural site, ControlPanelApp._on_close, sits in the Tk-owning class the panel suite never constructs, the same untested-site shape as TD312, so the change must land where a Tk-free test can reach it or it ships unpinned. NOT a duplicate of PD-BUG-159: that one acks FALSELY, this one acks TRUTHFULLY but doomed. Fixing this one removes the only documented reachable path of PD-BUG-159, leaving it a contract defect with no production exposure, so fix ordering matters. PD-BUG-159 is claimed by a parallel session and was already at In Progress when this triage was recorded; the shared edit surfaces if both fixes run concurrently are app.py, test_panel_single_instance.py and the TE-TST-151 test-tracking row, plus contamination of any full-suite baseline or mutation matrix either session runs.
- **Updated**: 2026-09-01
- **Fix**: ControlPanelApp now exposes on_shutdown_begin, fired by _release_instance_claim as the FIRST statement of _on_close; control_panel.main wires it to guard.release. A launch arriving during the drain then finds no published port at all, so it takes neither the stale_port_file nor the surface_declined branch and simply binds and opens a window - measured at 0.0 s with no message and no warning. Dropping the claim is best-effort: a raising callback is logged as shutdown_claim_release_failed and never blocks the close, because a window the operator has asked to close must close regardless. release was already idempotent and self-owned-port-checked, so the finally in main stays correct and a successor that has already taken the file over is left alone. single_instance.py was NOT changed. Chosen over a stopping flag in the listener, which would have avoided a thread join on the UI thread but sent every such launch down the surface_declined branch, logging that the incumbent is unreachable - false here, and the client has no channel to learn why it was declined. Accepted consequence, recorded as the second bounded exception on FDD 7-1-1-EC-9: for the duration of a drain two panel windows may exist, the departing one and the new one.
- **Root Cause**: The guard promise to a launching process has two halves - a window exists now, and it will still exist when you exit. PD-BUG-157 made the listener verify the first. Nothing verified the second and nothing could: ControlPanelApp commits irreversibly to exiting at _on_close but held no reference to the guard, control_panel.py being the only site where the two met and the reference running one way, so guard.release could only run in the finally of main after app.run returned - after the entire drain. Throughout that drain the mainloop and the dispatcher pump keep running, so the raise genuinely succeeded and the ack was truthful at the instant it was sent, about a window that was seconds from being destroyed.
- **Tests Added**: Yes
- **Verification**: 11 regression tests TE-TST-167, 6 red pre-fix each for its own reason; the other 5 pass on both sides and are recorded as contract cover rather than claimed as pins. Full suite 1948 passed 0 failed; the plus 11 delta was MEASURED by collection count, 1954 with the file against 1943 without, not inferred. Every mechanism mutation-verified 1:1 against the FULL suite with a no-op control at 0 red: removing the release call turns 6, unwiring the entry script exactly 1, removing the try/except exactly 1, and releasing after the drain dispatch turns 3 including the ordering test. That last mutation had to be RE-RUN - the first attempt moved the call to just above begin, which is still before dispatch, so it turned 0 red and would have been reported as an unpinned mechanism. Manual validation test/bug-validation/PD-BUG-160_shutdown_window_ack_validation.py drives a real Tk window and reports BUG PRESENT exit 1 before and FIXED exit 0 after. Tk-free coverage of _on_close was obtained by invoking the unbound method against a stand-in self, so the Tk-owning class the panel suite never constructs is now exercised for real on both close paths; the entry-script wiring is pinned on the AST rather than on source text, so a comment naming it cannot satisfy the test. E2E: only TE-E2E-041 was flagged for re-execution rather than the feature-wide cascade, measured with -WhatIf at 9 entries across 5 features, because the behavioral delta reaches only a CLOSING panel while every E2E case exercises a healthy one. Docs updated: FDD EC-9 second bounded exception, TDD 4.5, feature state file, test spec UNIT-I6. black isort flake8 clean; the 7 state-tracking gate errors are pre-existing in Surfaces 20 and 21 and touch no file this fix changed. Observed not filed: the daemon lock has the same shape, release_lock only in the finally around a blocking service.start, but both the launcher and the panel hard-kill daemons so no teardown window exists in practice.
- **Verification**: Code review (PF-TSK-005) 2026-09-02: APPROVED. Scoped to the on_shutdown_begin release path in app.py, its wiring in control_panel.py, TE-TST-167 and the manual validation script; the PD-BUG-157 and PD-BUG-158 change sets sharing this working tree were excluded as closed. Verified rather than accepted: the 11 scoped tests plus the 47 in the single-instance file all passed, full suite 1948 passed / 0 failed, pre-commit clean under the pinned binaries at exit 0. The parallel-session seam with PD-BUG-159 was traced rather than assumed: both fixes edit app.py, and the one real interaction is that guard.release now runs on the UI thread while a surface confirm may be in flight - release joins only the accept thread and is bounded at 1.0 s, which test_release_from_the_ui_thread_returns_promptly already pins. Release ordering confirmed safe by reading: the early release zeroes self.port, so the finally in main cannot delete a successor's port file. Two findings, neither blocking. (1) RESIDUAL, documented rather than fixed: a request already accepted when _on_close fires still receives a truthful but doomed ack. release closes the listening socket and joins only the accept thread; the per-request handler threads spawned by _dispatch are neither joined nor gated, and _handle never checks _stopping. Such a handler runs to completion - the hook posts raise_window, the mainloop resumes once _on_close returns and pumps the dispatcher throughout the drain, surface() succeeds on a window that still exists, the banner is sent, the launching process exits, and the panel then vanishes. Exposure is narrowed from about 35 s to the handshake window of roughly 1.5 to 2 s, not eliminated. Accepted on the same reasoning that made the parent defect Low: self-healing on one relaunch, and far narrower than what was fixed. Closing it would be a one-line _stopping check at the top of _handle and belongs to a separate Bug Fixing task, not to this review. (2) MINOR, record correction: the fix record states that the 7 state-tracking gate errors are pre-existing in Surfaces 20 and 21 and touch no file this fix changed. Measured this session, all 7 errors are in Surface 15 (test status aggregation) while Surfaces 20 and 21 emit warnings rather than errors, and the 7.1.1 error does involve a file this fix changed, since the new TE-TST-167 row at Needs Audit feeds its Needs Audit count. The substantive claim holds - the mismatch pre-dates the fix, because 7.1.1 already carried Needs Audit rows at HEAD - but the attribution is wrong on both halves.
- **Updated**: 2026-09-02

### PD-BUG-115

- **Source**: UserReport
- **Environment**: Development
- **Component**: Link updating
- **Repro**: Two candidate mechanisms, both consistent with the capabilities reference. (1) The links already existed on disk when the move happened - then the documented behavior 'updates all references pointing to the moved file across the entire project' did not fire for them, which is a defect. (2) The links were typed after the move, from a path the author had read before it - then the documented modification behavior applies ('a modification never rewrites files; only moves/renames trigger reference updates'), making this a gap rather than a defect: the daemon knows the old-to-new mapping from a move it performed minutes earlier, but a newly written link matching that old path is only re-indexed, never rewritten. Case (2) is the more general hazard in workspaces running parallel sessions - any content authored from a stale path reading breaks silently, and the daemon already holds the mapping needed to correct it.
- **Expected**: A link whose target the daemon itself relocated during its lifetime resolves correctly, whether the link was present at move time or written afterwards.
- **Actual**: Three links pointing at the pre-move path survived uncorrected until a manual check found them.
- **Rejected**: Rejected 2026-08-09 at Bug Triage (PF-TSK-041) - not a product bug; reclassified to feature request PD-FRQ-018 per the Issue Classification and Routing Guide (desired new behavior, not broken existing behavior). The report named two candidate mechanisms and asked triage to distinguish them. Case 1 (links present on disk at move time were not updated) would be a defect, but it is exactly what the PD-BUG-102 on_modified fix addressed; that fix shipped in v2.1.2 on 2026-06-13, five weeks before this 2026-07-22 report, and its 5 regression tests remain green. No log, no reproduction, and no evidence of regression were supplied. Case 2 (links typed after the move, from a path the author had read before it) is documented intended behavior: handler.py on_modified (line 385) indexes modified files database-only and never rewrites, and the capabilities reference states that a modification never rewrites files - only moves and renames trigger reference updates. Case 2 also fits the reported situation best (author read a path, a concurrent session moved it, links were typed afterwards). Making the daemon rewrite freshly authored stale paths is new behavior, so it belongs in the feature request queue. PD-FRQ-018 is deliberately scoped to detect-and-warn rather than auto-rewrite: rewriting content on modify events is the behavior class that produced the PD-BUG-095, PD-BUG-098 and PD-BUG-112 corruption bugs, and would rewrite paths in files that legitimately record historical paths (bug archive, release notes, test fixtures, migration ledgers). If a reproduction with logs ever demonstrates case 1, file it as a fresh bug against the on_modified fix rather than reopening this row.
- **Updated**: 2026-08-09
